# Firmware internals

How the scroll-wheel payload hooks the stock Shanling Q2 firmware. For the user-facing behavior it produces, see the [README](../README.md); build and release steps are in [building.md](building.md) and [releasing.md](releasing.md).

`release/bin/demo`, `usr/bin/hciplayer` (the PEQ filter, one branch, [Large MP3s](#large-mp3s), two calls, [MP3 ReplayGain](#mp3-replaygain), and the MQA gate and probe length, [MQA detection](#mqa-detection)), `usr/bin/bluealsa` (AAC capability and Bluetooth DROP handling), its launcher `usr/bin/bluealsa2` ([keep-alive](#bluetooth-keep-alive)), `usr/bin/checkappprocess.sh` (one sleep, [Battery](#battery)), the boot logo, `home_page.bin` (the Coverflow and Shortcut cards; the Home lists) and the artist page inside `rootfs.squashfs` change, and the stock EQ preset page and the images only the stock EQ pages show (`STOCK_EQ` in `tools/build.py`) are removed. The build also changes the other audited assets in `patch/ipod.json`: local browsing, settings and streaming pages, the equalizer page, the status bar, Now Playing and the theme (`styles/default.bin`). It adds the Coverflow and Rockbox card icons for the carousel. The build adds `usr/bin/q2video` ([Videos](#videos)), the new Local Music icons and Streaming's Spotify icon. The kernel is byte-identical, and the builder checks every other inode's name, type, mtime, mode, uid and gid against stock.

## Hooks

Checked MIPS prologues redirect into a payload at `0xb00000`, using the final unused `PT_NULL` program header. demo's `.pdr` section, outside every segment, is zeroed for rootfs space ([building.md](building.md)). Trampolines restore the stock GOT base and resume each original function after its PIC setup. Writable input and position state is mapped at `0xbc0000`, a `NOLOAD` section (with any stray `.bss`): the segment's memory size covers it, the file does not.

`HOOKS`, `GRADIENT_LEAF` and `WM_PAINT_LEAF` in `tools/build.py` are the hooked entry points.

| Stock callback                        | Address    | Purpose                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------------- | ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

Single checked instruction words are patched as well. `mclNextSong`'s shuffle pick (`0x5addf0`, [Queue menu](#queue-menu)), the long-Return Home call (`0x4e8924`, [ipod.md](ipod.md#hold-return)), `check_mem_thd`'s entry (`0x512050`, returns at once, [Battery](#battery)) and `device_standby`'s screen wake (`0x4f7d24`, [Wake](#wake)). From `tools/ipod.py`: the row pitch, artwork and Now Playing bar immediates and the row-layouter calls listed in `patch/ipod.json`, the folder rebind's resize call (`0x522410`, removed), the boot resume call (`0x523de0`, [Boot resume](#boot-resume-ipod)) and one data word, the `list_view` children layouter's layout slot (`0x926930`, stock `0x5e9eb4`), which becomes `ipod_list_layout` for the settings rows ([ipod.md](ipod.md#settings)). The manifest's `compact_code` lists every changed word.

Stock V1.32 turns the touch wheel into key events 172/173: the encoder thread (`0x6258e0`, unnamed in the symbol table, after `encoderknob_thread_run` at `0x6256a0`) reads tslib samples (x, 1-200 a turn, and pressure) and calls `get_direction` (`0x62587c`), but only once per 80 ms gate (200 ms after touching), at most one tick per look, dropping any travel past its threshold. That cost more than half the travel (27 units per tick against a threshold of 12), a single tick for a fast half turn and nothing in a touch's first 200 ms. So the build replaces the tick logic: the thread's `ts_read` call (`WHEEL_TS_READ` in `tools/build.py`) goes through `ringnav_ts_read`, and `wheel_sample` (`patch/playback.c`) makes a tick of every `WHEEL_TICK_UNITS` (28, a seventh of a turn) of travel at the default Wheel Sensitivity (below), carrying the rest; a reversal starts the count again and a lift drops it. It posts each tick with stock's poster (`WHEEL_POST`, `0x6257f0`) as a key-down (`0x110`) then a key-up (`0x114`), and hands stock the touch-down x, so stock's own logic sees a still finger and posts nothing. The payload moves on the release, at `on_wm_keyup_before_fun`; iPod also sees the press ([iPod row clicks](#ipod-row-clicks)). These events are not fixed physical detents. Below, a tick is one such event and a step is a row move the payload makes.

**Input queue.** The wheel, the touchscreen and the buttons all post to AWTK's main-loop queue (`main_loop_simple_init`, `0x68b70c`: `event_queue_create(20)` at `loop+0x38`), which drops its oldest event when full. A tick is two events, so a fast turn while the UI was busy could evict a touch's release: the window manager then kept the pointer pressed (a row stuck in its pressed shade), and `ringnav` dropped every tick until the next tap. `wheel_post` reads the queue's `r`, `w`, `full` and `capacity` (unlocked; off by one at worst) through the poster's main loop (`WHEEL_INFO_LOOP`, `LOOP_QUEUE`) and drops a tick that would leave fewer than `WHEEL_QUEUE_FREE` (8) slots, so a busy UI loses ticks it could not have shown yet instead of a touch or a key, and nothing replays later. Checks in `test/playback.py`. A release could still go missing: on 1.6.x the wheel and Centre went dead on a list, the bar gone, until a restart, while Return and touch still worked, which is what a pressed pointer the window manager never saw released does through these gates (`ringnav` drops ticks and Centre, `paint_selection` hides the bar, `quick_stuck` sees a touch down). The touch thread posts that release and the window manager never sees it, so the loss is in this queue. The touch thread (`tslib_run`, `0x622d70`) turns every tslib sample into an event: pressure with no finger held is a down, pressure while held a move, none while held the release, and none while no finger is held posts an event of type 0 (EVT_NONE) all the same, which the loop hands `widget_dispatch` for nothing. Twenty of them while a touch's release sat in the queue evicted it (inferred). `main_loop_queue_event` (`0x648ea8`, `QUEUE_LEAF`, a leaf the payload does whole: `ringnav_queue_event` in `playback.c`) is the queue's one way in, for the input threads, SyncLink's `main_loop_post_key_event` and `idle_queue`/`timer_queue` alike; it drops a type 0 request and hands the rest to the loop's `queue_event` slot (`LOOP_POST`). Limit: a release can still be evicted by twenty events of other kinds posted behind it within one loop pass (keys, SyncLink's), so the backstop stays. `stale_heal`, first in `ringnav_sleep`: pressed, and no pointer event (`EVT_POINTER_FIRST` 0xff to `EVT_POINTER_ABORT`) reached the window manager within `STALE_TOUCH_MS` (5 s; `ringnav_input` times it once past the gates that keep an event from the window manager, the video player's and a card game's). It sends the top window's targets `EVT_POINTER_ABORT` (`widget_dispatch_event_to_target_recursive`), so a pressed row drops its press and cannot click, then gives `stock_input_trampoline` the up (0x103) stock never got, at the touch's last place: only that path clears the flag (`input_device_status_on_input_event`, `0x69ff64`; `input_device_status_abort_keys_and_mouse`, `0x6a0870`, does not). So the wheel and Centre work again, the bar paints, and the row's pressed shade goes too. A real finger resting that long with no event is lifted as well: a still scrub or lyric hold ends after 5 s, and a slider held that still drops its drag at the abort (inferred from AWTK's source) instead of applying it at the lift, whose real release finds the flag clear and does nothing (`0x69fe30`). ponytail: `STALE_TOUCH_MS` is a guess, and two things come from AWTK's source, not traced in demo: that `window_manager_get_pointer_pressed` (vtable +0x44) reads that flag, and that widgets drop a press on the abort. `stale_heal` stamps the time, so a wrong guess costs one try per `STALE_TOUCH_MS`. `--dev` builds only (`Q2POD_DEV`): each heal, `STALE_NOTES` (8) a boot, is appended to `/mnt/data/q2-wheel.log` (`stale_note`), and the next boot to the card's `q2-wheel.log` (`tools/build.py` `WHEEL_LOG_HOOK`): the page, `g_dropdown_menu_state`, its `timer_idex` timer, the touch thread's own down flag (`main_loop() + LOOP_PRESSED`: set, the release was lost before the thread posted it; clear, between it and the window manager), the last pointer event's type and age.

Stock drops wheel ticks for 800 ms after Centre or Return (`KEY_LOCKOUT`, 8, counted down per 100 ms) so a press that brushes the wheel does not scroll; `ringnav()` keeps only its first `LOCKOUT_KEEP` (2) x 100 ms. A tick that lands while a page slides, or before the new page's list loads, is held (`hold_wheel`) instead of dropped: up to `HOLD_MAX` (8) of them, a reversal cancelling, play into the page one per `HOLD_POLL_MS` (16 ms) once it is ready, unless Centre or Return comes first or `HOLD_MS` (1.2 s) passes. So a turn begun on Back or Select carries on into the page.

**Runaway wheel.** A report of the UI scrolling one way for good from the moment a finger lifts, menus and volume alike, with only a restart to clear it: no source is shown. What one tick stream does is: every way out looks dead with no other fault. On Home the wheel's next only selects row 0, so Return opens nothing; on Now Playing a tick opens `volume_dialog`, Return closes that (`0x4a2134`) and the next tick reopens it; Centre needs a 250 ms gap (`DOUBLE_CLICK_MS`); and AWTK's one long-press timer (`input_device_status`, `+0x2c`) is removed by any key's release and restarted by the next press, so Hold Centre and Hold Return never fire with ticks under 1 s apart, and work again once they stop. Return recovers by itself 1.5 s after the stream stops. Run on stock's code, none of these starts a stream: a lost wheel release gives one long press, which `on_wm_keylong_fun` ignores (only Centre's repeats, once a second, its Shut Down); `0x66b9ac` is one focus move per event; `replay_wheel` ends at `HOLD_MS`; the payload's repeating timers post no key. Read, not run: an animator ends at its duration. `wdt_poll` only rules out a hung UI thread: a live loop feeds it.

`tick_gate`, first in `ringnav_input`, drops a tick (and its release, whatever the frames did since) with no ring frame with pressure in `WHEEL_FRESH_MS`, stamped by `wheel_sample` (`wheel_frame_at`), so a software stream from anywhere but the ring stops within about a second. `replay_wheel` calls `ringnav` itself and is not gated. A real turn makes its tick from the frame that stamps it, so it can only fail the gate if the UI stalls that long with the finger gone: a flick's tail then goes, the `ponytail:` at `tick_gate`.

Return can stop a run, on the second press. The first does only its normal job: Return is a rim button, a thumb can slide from turning to Return with no lift (`chord_gate`, `KEY_LOCKOUT`), and a turn begun on Back carries on. A press `WHEEL_ARM_MS` (2 s) or more after the first is a new first. A second Return before that with `WHEEL_STOP_TICKS` ticks since the first, a tick within `WHEEL_ESCAPE_TICK_MS` and a fresh frame, stops ticks (`wheel_mute`, `ringnav_queue_event`) until a real lift or a `WHEEL_FRESH_MS` gap in frames, measured from the last frame. Two rim presses within `WHEEL_ARM_MS` in one unbroken turn also stop it until the lift; nothing else limits a turn. Under `wheel_raw` 1 (Vortex, the lyrics) there are no ticks and so no stop; Cover Flow's thumb, 2, [counts its travel](#coverflow-thumb).

A source that reports pressure and a moving x with no lift (a ghost) passes the gate; only the two Returns stop it. If it never lifts, the wheel stays stopped and the buttons and screen work. The clock (`CLOCK_MONOTONIC`) stops across a suspend, so Standby is no gap: the stop outlasts it unless frames then rest a second. Volume Guard is no shield from a ghost: it holds the first half turn of a touch with the screen off, then ticks run on with no lift; the stop works dark as well. There's no range check on x: the driver's is one byte, 0-255, 1-200 rests on stock's wrap and [the remote's notes](remote.md), so a unit reporting past 200 keeps its arc, at the cost of a bounded burst on a bad read.

Pre-existing, seen running the real `pthres.so` with two instances, as its statics are shared between the wheel's thread and the touch screen's: a thumb on the ring and a screen tap drop the ring's lift, and a finger resting on the screen with the ring turned and lifted drops the screen's release. It fits the `thread=1` stale-press note (inferred) and can't make a stream.

A normal build leaves no trace of any of this; a trace needs a `--dev` build, which keeps the last 64 ring frames (x, pressure, ms) in RAM, and writes them with the counts of gate drops and stops to `/mnt/data/q2-wheel.log`, synced, off the wheel's thread: when the stop fires, when a run passes 30 s with no 1 s gap in ticks, and at the first tick of a run the gate drops, with its own line, so a stream from outside the ring shows apart from a ghost. Eight a boot, never past 32 KB, and the next boot moves it to the card's, up to 256 KB (`WHEEL_LOG_HOOK`). It's temporary (`ponytail:` in `patch/playback.c`), removed once the report is explained.

**Wheel Sensitivity.** **System settings → Controls → Wheel Sensitivity**, the first row (`ringnav_display`, the `system_display` icon), reads `Wheel Sensitivity: 50%` and sets the travel per tick: 50-200% in tens, `WHEEL_TICK_UNITS` x 50 / sensitivity, so 50% is 28 units, 100% 14, 130% 10.75, 150% 9.3 and 200% 7 (3.5 to 14 ticks a turn). `wheel_sample` counts in sixteenths of a unit (`WHEEL_FINE`), so the carry keeps the fraction and every step differs. It is `Q2POD WHEELSENSITIVITY` in `config.ini`, read on the first UI pass (`playback_poll` calls `wheel_pct`); a missing key or anything but a canonical ten in 50-200 is 50%. Classic's Cover Flow covers read the raw travel instead (see Coverflow thumb), and it scales their travel per album the same way. That is 1.0.6's key and scale (1.0.6 rounded the travel to whole units), so a value saved in 1.0.6 comes back as it was. A value saved before 1.0.6 comes back with the same label but about twice its old speed: the old engine's threshold scaled the same way but lost about half the travel to stock's 80 ms gate (27 units a tick at 100%), so its 100% felt like today's 50%. 1.0.7 removed the setting (fixed at 28) after 1.0.6 had kept those pre-1.0.6 values under the new meaning. `wheel_units` is one word the UI thread writes and the encoder thread reads, 0 until read, which `wheel_sample` takes as 50%. A change applies on the next sample: when the travel changes mid-turn, the carry is scaled to keep its share of a tick (`wh_acc * new / old`), so it stays under the new travel and fires no burst. Only the travel changes: `LOCKOUT_KEEP`, `hold_wheel`, the list and scrub ramps (`WHEEL_RUN_MS`, `WHEEL_RAMP_MS`, `LIST_FIRST_MS`) and every consumer (rows, volume including screen-off, scrub, Coverflow, Books, Videos) count ticks and time, so they just get more or fewer ticks a turn. iPod's lyrics page takes the raw travel instead and moves a row per tick's worth of it ([Lyrics wheel](#scrub-ipod)). The row opens [Crossfade](#crossfade)'s re-dressed Boot volume page (`xfade_open` with a nonzero context, `xf.wheel` in `patch/crossfade.c`): titled Wheel Sensitivity, the switch hidden, its slider 50-200 in steps of 10 (a drag snaps to the nearest ten), +/- and the wheel a ten a tick (`xfade_key`), and Centre going back as on Brightness (`edit_key`). Each change is saved with `write_int_config` (a drag at its release, as Crossfade below) and the Display row's label (named `label_wheel`) follows.

## Supported screens and panes

Navigation requires a supported top-window name from `patch/contexts.inc`, screen-on and no lock/test/guide/power-off/USB-link/Bluetooth-receive screen, and a navigable pane: a vertical `scroll_view`, a `table_client`, a `slide_menu` or, in iPod, a dialog or page flagged `BUTTONS`, whose buttons are its rows and which never scrolls ([ipod.md](ipod.md#pop-ups)). Horizontal and page-snapping scroll views are not candidates, so they cannot make a page look like it has two panes. When two panes are visible and neither owns the selection, the first wheel turn chooses the first pane in UI order. Later turns follow the selected pane, and tapping a row switches ownership. If both panes already hold a selection, the wheel event is left alone. Only active `pages` children are searched. The bounded walk collects at most 512 targets in a non-virtual list; virtual music tables navigate by total logical row count instead.

A native click chooses its pane from the actual target before walking up to the nearest collected ancestor. A successful selection clears the other pane’s selection and invalidates both panes, so the drawn selection, wheel and centre follow the row that owns the tap. Hidden, disabled and inactive-page panes remain excluded.

## Selection

Selection is stored in widget-owned integer properties on the navigation surface, independently of AWTK's focused flag. The drawn selection (outline or bar) and centre action resolve that same logical selection against the current entries. Centre dispatches a synchronous native `EVT_CLICK`, so a queued click cannot hit a row rebound between selection and delivery. It never dereferences the target after delivery.

## Wheel movement

Lists with at most `SHORT_LIST_MAX` (16) logical entries always move one row per step. Virtual tables use their total logical count, not the recycled row pool size. Longer lists use a continuous ramp: a same-direction tick spaced at most `WHEEL_RUN_MS` (140 ms) from the previous one extends a spin run, and the step grows with the run's duration, capped at `WHEEL_MAX_STEP` (8) rows. It is a duration ramp, not a measure of the wheel's angular speed. `ramp()` takes the curve from its caller:

| Caller                                                | One row while the run is under | Then                                      | Eight rows from |
| ----------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------- | --------------- |
| iPod's pixel-scroll fallback and [scrub](#scrub-ipod) | `WHEEL_RAMP_MS` (100 ms)       | `1 + run / WHEEL_RAMP_MS`                 | 700 ms          |
| iPod row lists and button dialogs                     | `LIST_FIRST_MS` (300 ms)       | `2 + (run - 300) / LIST_RAMP_MS` (200 ms) | 1500 ms         |

The pixel-scroll fallback is a pane without clickable rows, which scrolls `RING_STEP` pixels per step. A pause, reversal, touch, native click, centre press, list boundary or rejected navigation resets the run. Acceleration belongs to the current window, pane and browsing scope; a list resize also resets it.

Wheel movement cancels existing momentum and uses the stock `scroll_view_set_offset` / `table_client_set_yoffset` setters immediately, reloading table row references after synchronous rebinding. Each wheel step then dispatches `EVT_SCROLL_END` (`0x14f`), which touch momentum sends when it settles: the setters send only `0x14e`, which the album page takes as pause, and stock lists queue their visible covers on scroll end (album page `0x4a440c`). Remembered-position restoration retains its separate glide. This follows the row-based approach of Rockbox's [H2/Eros Q wheel mapping](https://github.com/Rockbox/rockbox/blob/master/apps/keymaps/keymap-erosq.c) and [list implementation](https://github.com/Rockbox/rockbox/blob/master/apps/gui/list.c).

Selected and restored rows use a `SCROLL_MARGIN` of 12 pixels above and below, reduced to half the spare viewport height in tight lists and clamped at content boundaries. Reaching the first row also shows whatever heads the list above it (the EQ page's graph) when both fit. Rows taller than the viewport align their top edge when selected or restored, keeping their title visible.

## List ends

The local file and music lists, the hold menu (`sortselect_dialog`, also the sort picker), Now Playing's More page, Playback settings and System settings carry over at their ends, and so does the Library's Albums list (`album_page` with class `0xf003`, which `ring_list` checks by class, since the same page shows an album's songs at `0xff10`, and those keep hard ends) and Library's Games chooser. `patch/contexts.inc` marks those audited row lists with `ring`, and iPod Home does too, while other grids, other settings menus, other dynamic pages, and the Stock build's Home carousel keep hard ends. The first tick past an end nudges only the drawn selection by `BUMP_PX` for `BUMP_MS`, without moving the viewport. Further ticks hard-stop there, bumping again, while the wheel keeps turning: each one re-arms. Only the first same-direction tick at least `EDGE_PAUSE_MS` (300 ms) after the last stopped one selects the opposite end of the same list and reveals it immediately. The existing boundary rule already reset the spin run, so the wrap lands at one row per tick. The arm is cleared by a move off the boundary row, a reversal, touch, a native click or a centre press.

Every tick against an end bumps and re-arms, so the pause is measured between ticks. Reaching an end, a bump and the wrap end the run and leave no speed; the next tick is a first one.

## Key Tone

The click is the MCU's buzzer, the device's own speaker: `buzzeer_switch` (`0x4f3cc8`) runs `system("cmd_mcu write_str buzzer")` when the byte `g_keytone_flag` (`0xa38c31`, the Key Tone setting) is set, and nothing else; stock sends it whatever plays and whatever is plugged in. It is hooked (`ringnav_buzzer`, stock resumed at `0x4f3cd4`), so every click, stock's three call sites and the iPod row click below, passes one gate: none while headphones or another output listen: either jack (`g_po_status` `0xa38af3`, 3.5 mm, GPIO `PA07`; `g_bal_status` `0xa38af2`, 4.4 mm, `PA08`; 1 plugged, kept by `check_headset_status` at `0x4e8e14`) or an output way other than the DAC (`mclGetOutputWay`, `0x5ac484`: 1 Bluetooth, 2 USB DAC, as `config_outputchannel` sets it). Otherwise stock runs, so with nothing plugged in the buzzer clicks as before, music playing or not, and Key Tone off is still silent. **System settings → Controls → Click With Headphones** (`IPOD KEYTONEHP`, off unless chosen; `st.keytone_hp`; under Key Tone and shown only while it is on: `pod_click` shows or hides it as Key Tone changes) lifts the headphone and output part of the gate, so the buzzer clicks there too, as stock does: the speaker's tick, heard or felt through the case, never in the headphones. Key Tone off, `tone_busy` and the row latch still apply. Stock runs on a detached worker (`tone_worker`), not the UI thread: with PWM clock gating, `cmd_mcu`'s write wakes the MCU, waits out the buzzer and parks it again (`tools/mcu_pwm_clock.c`), which stalled the UI loop on every click of a fast scroll. A click while the last one still sounds (`tone_busy`) is dropped, as the buzzer could not play it yet; Key Tone off and the iPod row latch are still checked on the UI thread, before the worker starts. There is no headphone click: a paused hciplayer keeps its PCM (`hw:1,0`, or `bluealsa`, one client per PCM, no `dmix`) open and stock's pause mutes the DAC (`mclSetMute`, `player_play_pause` `0x5157d8`), so a click has no free output; silence is the fallback, never the speaker.

### Wake

**System settings → Controls → Wake** (`Q2POD WAKEDOUBLE`, read with Charge Limit). With it on and the screen off (`g_backlight_status` clear), `wake_gate` in `ringnav_input` (`window_manager_dispatch_input_event`, before the window manager and every key hook) drops a centre (218, the power key) `EVT_KEY_DOWN` (`0x110`, as `widget_on_keydown` tests at `0x6627c4`; `0x111` is its long press) and its release, and records the press. A second press within `WAKE_MS` (500 ms, looser than `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` since it is done blind) passes, press and release, so stock wakes the screen on it, and holding that accepted press still reaches stock's long-press power-off. Return (170) is dropped while dark in either mode, press, long press (`0x111`) and release, so a bump of Back leaves the page alone. Every other key passes, so the wheel's volume, the media buttons and Spotify's keys work dark; with the screen on nothing is touched and a pending pass is cleared, and with the setting off a centre press passes at once. Another centre press within `WAKE_MS` of the one that woke the screen (`woke_at`, either mode), or of the first UI loop pass with the screen lit (`ringnav_sleep` restarts `woke_at` then), is dropped with its release (`wake_eat`), the blind double press's window: turning the screen on (stock's `on_wm_keyup_fun`) and that pass's paint stall the loop about that long, and `wake_gate` times presses as they are dispatched, so a second press made during the stall arrives after it. So a quick double press from dark only wakes: it neither clicks the selected row nor, as a screen-on double press, turns the screen straight off. Centre's repeated downs (first at about 258 ms, then every 50 ms) share their original press's fate, so holding a waking press keeps the release that lights the screen. A dropped press stays dropped with its repeats and release; holding it does not add a wake gesture. A passing hold keeps stock's long-press power-off. AWTK generates the long event downstream of this input hook. Stock's Standby (`check_to_poweroption` calls `device_standby`, `0x4f7a2c`, which suspends in `toolsIntoStandby`) wakes the screen once any key resumes it, a `bal awake_screen` (`0x4f7d24`) whatever the key; that call becomes `li $v0, 1`, skipping it in both modes, so the resuming key acts as it does dark: Play/Pause plays, a centre press wakes (Single Press) or counts as the first of two (Double Press). Stock then sets `BOOT_KEY_GUARD` (`0xa37c8a`), which drops the next centre release so the resuming press cannot turn the screen straight off again; the first centre down while dark consumes it and resets held-key tracking, so repeats keep that press's fate even if Standby lost the preceding release.

Before `wake_gate`, `chord_gate` drops a whole centre press (down, repeats, long press and release) whose first down arrives while an edge button (Return, Play/Pause, Previous or Next, `edge_held`) is held, within `CHORD_MS` (500 ms) of that button's press: a loose wheel, or a thumb on the rim between a button and the centre, can close both switches on one press, which then only does the edge button's job. The edge button itself always passes. A centre press already held when an edge button goes down passes too, but an edge press within `CHORD_MS` of it removes its long-press timer (`centre_hold`, kept by `ringnav_hold_timer`), so a rim press held near a corner never reaches stock's centre hold, the Shut Down dialog; its release still clicks. The time limit covers an edge release that never arrives (Standby while held); once the button is up, Centre works at once. A Return press that starts within `RETURN_GHOST_MS` (135 ms) of a Return hold's release (at least `KEY_HOLD_MS`) is dropped whole: letting go of Hold Return could send a second press. A centre down more than 300 ms after the last centre event is a new press, not a repeat, so a dropped press whose release was lost does not drop the next one, and a passing one takes a fresh time for the edge press after it. An edge button's down more than 300 ms after its last press or repeat (`edge_seen`) is likewise a new press with a fresh `edge_at`, so a lost edge release does not cost the next press its guard.

### Volume guard

**System settings → Controls → Volume Guard** (`Q2POD VOLGUARD`, off by default, read with Wake into `wheel_guard` in `patch/playback.c`), against pocket brushes moving the volume (#46). With it on and the screen off, `wheel_sample` (the encoder thread, before any tick is posted) counts a touch's travel one way in `wh_guard`, in sixteenths of a unit, and posts no tick until `WHEEL_DARK_GUARD` units (100, half a turn of the 200-unit wheel, whatever Wheel Sensitivity is). That travel is dropped, the dead zone, and the tick carry starts from zero there. The wheel is then open: it ticks as lit, reversals and new touches included, until it has gone `WHEEL_DARK_IDLE_MS` (1.5 s, `time_now_ms`) without travel. A reversal, a lift or that pause before the guard is passed starts the count again, and so does the screen coming on, where the wheel is never guarded. Nothing is held for later, so `hold_wheel`, the volume overlay and Bluetooth volume see only the ticks that pass; with the setting off the dark wheel is stock's. Both constants are in `patch/offsets.inc`.

### Standby limit

**System settings → Power → Standby Limit** (`Q2POD STANDBYLIMIT`, Off by default, read with Wake): **1 h**, **8 h** or **24 h** (`STANDBY_LIMIT_1` to `_3` in `patch/offsets.inc`) powers the Q2 off once one Standby has lasted that long, so a Q2 left asleep for days does not run flat. Stock's Standby (`check_to_poweroption` calls `device_standby` once its timer runs out with nothing playing and no cable in) stops the player, powers the DAC down, stops Wi-Fi and Bluetooth, unmounts the card and unloads the Wi-Fi and card drivers; then `toolsIntoStandby` (`0x5be57c`) writes `mem` to `/sys/power/state`, which returns when a key resumes it. Nothing else ends it: stock never sets the RTC's wake alarm. `ringnav_standby` takes `toolsIntoStandby`. With a limit it reads the RTC (`/sys/class/rtc/rtc0/since_epoch`, UTC as [the clock](#clock) keeps it), sets `wakealarm` that far ahead (after writing `0`, since rtc-sysfs refuses a new alarm while one is set), suspends through stock and clears the alarm on the resume. A resume at or past the alarm is the alarm's: the next UI loop pass (`standby_poll`, in `power_poll`) opens `poweroff_page`, as `check_to_poweroption`'s Auto power-off does, unless a USB cable is in (`g_usbdet_value`) or local music plays. Anything that resumes it sooner, a key or the charger, leaves it on, and the next Standby counts from zero. With the RTC unreadable or the alarm refused, Standby is stock's. The alarm's resume runs all of stock's resume first (the drivers, and Wi-Fi if it was on) and stays dark (`STANDBY_WAKE`, [Wake](#wake)), so the Q2 powers off a few seconds after the alarm. On a Q2, a wake alarm set before Standby resumed it 5 s after the alarm time, dark and paused, with no key pressed.

Standby's own card reload no longer toasts. The resume reloads `soc_msc`, whose `mdev` script mounts the card and then touches `/tmp/mmc_add`, while `device_standby` waits at most 31 × 50 ms for the mount; a slower card reads to `check_cardmount_status` (`0x4e9428`, every UI pass) as pulled and put back, and on the next pass `on_wm_timer_dialog` would toast `msg_tfeject` and `msg_tfinsert`. `ringnav_cardmount` runs stock's check, so the mount and unmount still happen, and clears its toast request (`CARD_TOAST`) for `CARD_QUIET_MS` (15 s) after a resume. ponytail: a real swap in those 15 s shows no toast either, and a mount slower than that still shows its inserted toast; raise `CARD_QUIET_MS` if that shows.

### iPod row clicks

Stock clicks on the press. `on_wm_keydown_before_fun` (`0x4e8424`) calls `buzzeer_switch` (the exported spelling; its only three call sites). A latch (`0xa37c90`), set after the call and cleared by `on_wm_keyup_before_fun`, keeps it to one click a press. Every wheel tick is a press and a release, so stock clicks on every tick, whether or not a row moves.

iPod hooks that callback (`ringnav_keydown`, stock resumed at `0x4e8430`). For a wheel press that the release will take as row navigation it saves `g_keytone_flag`, clears it, runs the whole stock body and restores the byte before returning stock's result: the power timer, the lock gates and both latches run as stock, and only the click is missing. Besides `buzzeer_switch`, the byte is read by `config_init` and by the System settings page's Key Tone row and its click handler (`0x4cc0a0`, `0x4cc2dc`), all on the UI thread, and the stock body calls nothing back into the UI, so nothing else sees it cleared. Whether a press is such a press is decided from the widget tree alone (screen usable, a `contexts.inc` window, a pane that is not a `slide_menu` and holds a clickable row); nothing is loaded, selected or restored. Every other press runs stock unchanged: the buttons, the volume, scrub, carousels and other slide menus, the pixel-scroll fallback, and the wheel while the screen is off or locked.

The press leaves its key in `tone_key`. Every key-down rewrites it and every key-up clears it, so a dropped release cannot carry it into a later input. On the release, `ringnav()` calls `buzzeer_switch(1)` once when that tick's step changes the selected row, with the setting as it is then: one click for a step of any size and for a wrap (a held tick's replay included), and none for a hard end, a bump, a rejected tick (a pointer down, stock's wheel lockout after a button) or a release whose press stock already clicked. If the page stops being a list between the press and the release, that tick is silent and the next press is stock's again. The call is in the wheel path, not in `select()` or the paint hooks, which also run for restoration, touch and repaints. The Stock build has no key-down hook and clicks as stock.

## Scrollbar

Wheel motion also wakes the native scrollbar of the controlled surface. `native_scrollbar()` finds the surface's `scroll_bar_m` sibling under its `list_view` or `table_view` parent and calls the stock `scroll_bar_scroll_to` with the bar's current value; the stock bar keeps ownership of the thumb value and runs its own show, wait and fade lifecycle. An initially transparent bar is still woken, and a surface without a native bar is unchanged. The payload retains no bar pointer and no fade timer, so page destruction and widget recycling need no extra handling. The end bump's `fx_token` widget property and its `fx_expire` timer remain separate from scrollbar visibility: the token rejects a recycled surface, and the timer repaints the selection at the `BUMP_MS` (120 ms) deadline.

## Centre button

A centre release arms a stock UI timer for `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` (250 ms). A second release before expiry on the same live selection cancels confirmation and passes through the stock downstream key-up handler to turn the screen off. On a listed page where no row confirmation is pending (an empty list, nothing selected, a page still sliding in, the Books reader whose Centre is its caption), `ringnav` times the presses itself (`dc_top`, `dc_at`): a second within `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` on the same window replays the release to `on_wm_keyup_fun`, so a double press turns the screen off on every page. Videos keep Centre for seeking. A single release dispatches exactly one synchronous click at expiry. Stock's key-up filter rejects wheel releases while a centre-key lockout (`KEY_LOCKOUT`, `0xa37c89`) is non-zero: the centre key sets it to 8 and `on_wm_timer_setup_state` counts it down every 100 ms, so a page opened by the centre button would ignore the wheel for up to 800 ms. The click clears it just before dispatch, so the opened page scrolls at once; a double press keeps stock's lockout. Touch, wheel input and invalid navigation state cancel pending confirmation. The timer resolves the target from the live menu and requires the original window, surface, content scope, logical selection, row count and row-text identity to match. Widget-owned tokens also reject reused window/surface addresses, and an ordinary list requires the armed row widget's own token; virtual tables resolve recycled pool widgets by logical index. An overdue confirmation runs before the power and lock gates that follow it. No delayed row pointer is retained; pending state clears before dispatch. Timer allocation failure consumes the press without activating anything.

## Setting editors

Stock's setting pages without a list take the wheel and Centre themselves (`edit_key`), ahead of list navigation. **Brightness**, **Maximum volume**, **Startup volume** and **Balance** (`backlight_page`, `maxvol_page`, `bootvol_page`, `balance_page`): the wheel dispatches a click to the page's own `img_add` or `img_dec`, so each tick is one stock step with stock's limits and saving; Centre goes back. The pull-down's brightness slider (`statusbar_dialog`) has no +/-, so the wheel sets its `value` by one within its `min`/`max`, which fires stock's change callback; Centre there stays stock's. While the pull-down slides (`g_dropdown_menu_state` 1 or 2, or its `timer_idex` slide timer running) the wheel does nothing there, in brightness and volume mode alike (`edit_key`), so a tick during the swipe or the slide moves neither: only the open, rested panel takes it. Stock's slide timer (`0x4a099c`, 10 ms, 80 px a step, added by the dialog's pointer-up handler at state 1 or 2) sets `timer_idex` back to -1 and the state to 3 once the dialog is down, and ends a push up in `dialog_quit`, whose quit handler sets the state to 0, so an open panel at rest reads 3 and -1. Its swipe up to close may start on that slider, which then moved the brightness before stock took the swipe. `ringnav_dispatch` keeps the slider's pointer-down back (Quick Settings' only, under `statusbar_dialog`; Settings' Brightness page is stock's): the slider gets it, at the touch's start, only once a move goes `SLIDE_PX` (10 px) more sideways than up or down, or with a tap's release. A touch that first moves `SLIDE_PX` up or down, or any while stock slides the pull-down or it follows a swipe (`g_dropdown_menu_state` 1 or 2), never reaches the slider, and a press it already has is aborted (`EVT_POINTER_ABORT`). A withheld event returns 0 rather than `STOP`, which would also end its parents' handling, so the window manager's pointer-down still records the touch's start (`g_pressdown_y`) that stock's swipe measures from. A drag that set the brightness before the pull-down moved keeps it. A held Play/Pause in the pull-down (`ringnav_keylong`, `quick_wheel_toggle`) switches its wheel to stock's volume and back (no toast: its `msginfo_dialog` would cover the pull-down and take the wheel for 2 s) and saves the choice as `Q2POD` `QSVOLUME` (0 brightness, 1 volume); in volume mode `edit_key` leaves the wheel to stock, and the release is swallowed as the queue menu's (`qm_press`). Stock's swipe down (`on_wm_tsmove_fun`) sets `g_dropdown_menu_state` 1 and opens the dialog off screen; only the touch's release reaching the dialog starts its slide (its pointer-up handler adds the `timer_idex` timer, which ends at 3, open). The dialog is modal: its init leaves `to_modal` on, so `navigator_to` runs `dialog_modal` inside that pointer move, and the nested loop drains the input queue before its first timers and idles. Above the screen the dialog catches a touch only once opened (`window_manager_find_target`, stage 3, set by `EVT_WINDOW_OPEN` in the window manager's open idle `0x688900`). So a release already queued goes to the page under it: a flick quicker than the dialog loads, or any swipe queued behind a blocked loop, as during a track start. That left the dialog on top and invisible at 1, with the page under it sent to the background by that same idle, so Now Playing's 250 ms timer (cleared by its background handler `0x52c710`) stopped and the time, bar, title and art froze while the music played, until a key closed the dialog or a touch showed it ([#189](https://github.com/DiamondBond/q2-pod/issues/189)). `quick_stuck` treats it at 1 or 2 with no touch down and no `timer_idex` timer as stuck, and `quick_poll`, in every `ringnav_sleep` right after `stale_heal`, then hands the dialog a pointer-up through `ringnav_dispatch`: the release it missed, late, so stock's handler (`0x4a08bc`) slides it the way the swipe asked (`g_slide_direction`). That handler ignores the event's place and, at states 1 and 2 with `timer_idex` -1, always adds its slide timer (`0x4a099c`), which always ends, so one pass does it. A stale press holds it for at most `STALE_TOUCH_MS`, as `stale_heal`'s release (the input queue paragraph under [Hooks](#hooks)) reaches the same handler; a finger resting that long on a half-pulled panel also lets it slide. That a flick can beat the dialog's load is inferred from the code's order; the load time is not measured. **Set date and time** (`manualtime_page`: year, month, day, hour, minute) and the **sleep timer** (`sleepshutdown_page`: hour, minute) edit one `text_selector` at a time, framed in two white pixels by the border hook: the wheel steps its selected index (`SELECTOR_INDEX`, set through stock `text_selector_set_selected_index`) within `text_selector_count_options`, Centre moves to the next field, switching the date page's `slide_view` to the time page after the day, then to OK (`btn_enter`), drawn while it is next as a selected row (Minimal's off-white `MINIMAL_FILL` card, `SEL_RADIUS` corners, its label dark at `EDIT_OK_PX`) rather than framed, which the next Centre clicks; a turn on OK goes back to the last field (Previous) or the first (Next). A swipe between the date and time pages moves the field to that page's first. The side keys (Next, Previous) move between the fields and OK the same way, stopping at the ends, ahead of stock's track skip (`field_key`). A side key also drops a Centre still waiting for its double (`stop_timer(&st.edit_press)`, the editor's own press; `cancel_center` stops only a list row's), so only the key acts: the Centre would step once more after it, or press OK from the last field. Under Classic with the Sleep Timer switched off, the press it drops is the switch's flip. A Centre pressed again inside the dropped one's `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` does nothing: `center_press` arms no single that soon after a press. On the date and time page OK sets the clock (stock's `set_systime`, which stays on the page) and then goes back to the Time page (`time_ok`, `navigator_back`), after a tap (`ringnav_dispatch`) or Centre's click (`edit_single`); the sleep timer's OK stays stock's. Centre waits `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` (`center_press`) and clears `KEY_LOCKOUT` as a list's click does, and a double press is stock's screen toggle. The field lives in the page's `_edit_field` property, so it dies with the page.

Pickers flagged `CHOICE` in `patch/contexts.inc` (Play mode, Filter, Language, Sort and the like) open on the row holding stock's `select` check image instead of the remembered position, so the bar starts on the current setting. In Minimal, stock's white radio images (`select`, `unselect`) would vanish on the selected row's off-white card, so the border hook draws them again over it in the card's dark ink (`paint_radio`): the 26-pixel ring and, when checked, the 12-pixel dot. The Wi-Fi, Bluetooth and Tidal rows' white delete mark (`navbar_delete`) is drawn again there as `list_delete`, a build-added copy that loads in `list_into`'s grey; `navbar_delete` itself stays white, as the playlist page's Delete wants it.

USB mode's exit asks whether to scan for music in a `confirminfo_dialog` before it clears `g_usblink_status`; with the cable already out (`g_usbdet_value` 0), that prompt alone counts as usable, so its buttons take the wheel and Centre.

## Queue menu

Holding Play/Pause (key 171) fires the stock long press once per press, after 700 ms (`KEY_HOLD_MS`, as Return's hold does; every other key keeps stock's 1000 ms, 500 for key 232, unused on the Q2): `HOLD_KEYS` turns the key-down's `timer_add` into `ringnav_hold_timer`, with the key in `$a3`, and the timer's `input_device_status_find_key_long_press_info` into `ringnav_hold_info`, so both the timer and its threshold give those two keys `KEY_HOLD_MS`: `input_device_status` (`wm+0x98`, via `window_manager_get_input_device_status` at `0x66e734`) dispatches `0x111` to the window manager, where stock `on_wm_keylong_fun` has no action for 171. `ringnav_keylong` takes it only with the navigation gates, AirPlay off (`airplayGetFlag() != 2`), no batch-select mode (`g_navbar_status`) and the row a centre press would open, on a `CTX_LOCAL`/`CTX_FOLDER` page, an artist's page (`artistinfo_page`, whose tabs fill the showlist) included. There the row's class is `g_local_classinfo_save+0`, which its tabs' `load_localartist_list` sets (`0xff07` songs, `0xff01` albums); `g_class_type` keeps the class the page opened or was last returned to with. The page's row count must equal `deque_size(*p_deque_showlist)`: these lists bind row _i_ to showlist record _i_ (Local Songs `table_row_of` → `deque_at` at `0x4a8838`), and a grid or extra clickable row fails the check. Everything else tail-calls the stock body.

The release still reaches `playpause_quick_click` (`on_wm_keyup_fun` `0x4e8b7c`), which has no long-press check. The hold stores the key record's u64 press time (record `+8`; 16 records of `0x18` from `+0x30`); the key-up hook swallows a Play/Pause release carrying that time and clears the latch on every Play/Pause release. AWTK drops the release when it aborts pressed keys on a window change; a later press has a new time, so a stale latch cannot eat it.

The target is the showlist index plus hashes of the record strings (`+8` name, `+0xc` path, `+0x10`, `+0x14` album, `+0x18` artist) and of the browsing state (`g_folder_path`, `g_class_type`, `g_local_classinfo_save`, `g_artist_type`); the dialog open and the action both re-check it. A type-8 record is one song. In `g_class_type` `0xf003` a row is an album: `getMusicByAlbum(id == -1 ? NULL : album)`, as `load_album_detaillist` → `load_localclass_list(0xff10)` does. In `0xff01` (an artist's albums) it is `getMusicByAlbumAndAlbumSonger` when `g_artist_type == 1`, otherwise `getMusicByAlbumAndSonger`, with `(album, classinfo[0xa] ? NULL : artist, id == -2)` (`0xff11`). A composer's (`0xff02`) or genre's (`0xff03`) albums use `getMusicByAlbumAndComposer`/`getMusicByAlbumAndGenre` with `+0x20` composer or `+0x1c` genre and `classinfo[0xb]`/`[0xc]`. An artist row (`0xf004`) is `getMusicBySonger(+0x18)`, or `getMusicByAlbumArtist` when `g_artist_type == 1`; a composer (`0xf005`) `getMusicByComposer(+0x20)`, a genre (`0xf006`) `getMusicByGenre(+0x1c)`; an id of -1 (Unknown) passes NULL. These are the expansions `batch_add_file` (`0x4f4be8`) runs for each class. A folder row (type 4) calls `toolsLoadDirectory(g_folder_path + "/" + name)` like `folder_enter`, then the same for each type-4 subfolder in that listing's order (`folder_tracks`: names starting with a dot skipped, 8 levels deep at most). ponytail: one directory load per folder on the UI thread, so a large tree blocks for a while; a database path-prefix query would be quicker but misses unscanned files. Queries fill `tools_pdeq_directory`; its type-8 records join in order and the staging deque is restored.

Coverflow album holds resolve the live `SLIDE_INDEX` against the current sorted album deque, without opening its tracks. Sort, Refresh, preparation, empty screens and pending screen changes are excluded. Both the deferred open and action revalidate the selected record. Play Next, Add to Queue and Shuffle use `coverflow_album_tracks`, shared with opening an album, so disc/track/path/CUE ordering and staging restoration are identical. Go to Artist is available when the album has an artist; playlist actions remain omitted.

A playlist row (Library → Playlists; its Add to Playlist picker, `PLAYLIST_ADD_MODE` set, keeps stock's hold) is the header at the index its `img_more<n>` dots name in `*p_deque_playlist`, checked against that deque's size and the header's hash. Its songs are `getMusicByPlayList(cd_num)`, the query the playlist opens with, less spoken tracks and anything not a song, as its opened list (`0xff20`, `music_records`) shows them. The rows are Play Next, Add to Queue and Shuffle (an empty queue loads them as class `0xf001`, a song list, as Shuffle's `play_folder` does), then More…, which opens `localmusic/playlistsmore_page` with that index, as the dots do.

The menu is the stock `dialog/sortselect_dialog`: its init is synchronous and clears `to_modal`, so `navigator_to` returns with it on top, and it is already a navigation context. The payload replaces its five sort rows with the row's own, titles it with the item (a song by its title tag, the player's parsed title while it plays an untagged folder record, else its file name less the extension), and swaps its key-up (`0x49f3e0`) and back arrow (`0x49f3c0`) handlers, both of which set `g_sort_changeflag`, which makes Local Songs reload and scroll to the top on return. The open is deferred out of the long-press dispatch and the action out of the row click; successful queue, favourite and rating actions are silent; failures use a stock `dialog/msginfo_dialog` toast (`{1, 2000, text}`). The menu has no `anim_hint` in any theme, so it opens in one frame and a pick's close leaves the page on top at once (stock's close keeps an animated window on top until its animation ends, `0x689db8`, and the rows read the page under it). Classic's 300 ms dialog fade once applied here, and to the confirms and Add to Playlist, and showed black before the menu: the dialog's background is clear, and by our reading the animator's snapshot of it has those pixels black; that was not traced in stock's code. Stock's own dialog assets carry no `anim_hint` and its code sets none, so no theme's dialog animates.

The rows are stock actions. Every row has Play Next and Add to Queue. A song (and a Coverflow track) adds or removes a favourite, labelled from `checkFavExist`, as Now Playing's heart (`manage_myfav`): Add selects the row alone in the batch record (`batch_init_selectrecord(rows)`, `batch_set_selectitem(index)`, as `player_addplayList` does) and runs batch-select's Add to My Fav, `batch_add_file(class, 0xf00a, list, *p_vector_select_record, 0)`, which reads a folder file's tags first; Coverflow passes its own deque as class `0xf001`. Remove is `deleteMusicFromFav`, and on My Fav (`0xf00a`) it sets `g_delete_flag` so the list reloads, as the heart does. Albums, artists, composers, genres, folders and playlists get Shuffle instead: their songs, the play mode saved as shuffle, from a random song, as Shuffle Songs. Everything but Coverflow and a playlist gets Add to Playlist: the row selected the same way, then `localmusic/playlist_page` with context `1 << 16 | class`, the page's add mode, whose pick runs `batch_add_file(class, 0xf00b, showlist, ...)` for the playlist and shows its own toast. A song gets Go to Album and a song or a row of all albums Go to Artist, when the record has one: Now Playing More's Album info (`0x5283b0`: `classinfo` `+0` = `0xff10`, `+9` = 0, `+0xd` album, `+0x10d` artist, then `playerjumpinfo_page`) and Artist info (`localmusic/artistinfo_page` with `{class, record}`). Both pages reload from `g_local_classinfo_save`, and so do the pages underneath when they come back, so the payload copies it before and writes it back on the opened page's `EVT_WINDOW_CLOSE`. Go to Album is left out on an album's own list (`0xff1x`) and either is left out while its page is already open (`navigator_window_is_exist`): both keep single-instance state, which stock's More closes first. With a `coverflow_page` open (the song was played from Coverflow) or Display → Coverflow → Artist View set to Cover Flow, Go to Album opens the album's tracks in Coverflow instead (`coverflow_goto_album`, see [Artist Albums in Coverflow](#artist-albums-in-coverflow)), falling back to `playerjumpinfo_page` when the album has no tracks; it is left out while that Coverflow already shows this album (`coverflow_shows_album`). Now Playing keeps its stock heart and More page; Photos, Books and Videos have no menu.

A song whose path is on the card (`/mnt/mmc/`) gets **Go to Folder** after Go to Artist, also on Now Playing, when `song_folder` accepts its parent: it fits `g_folder_path` (1024 bytes with its terminator), has no empty, `.` or `..` name (stock's `folder_enter` never builds one) and is still a directory (`__xstat`, checked again at the pick: "Folder not found"). The pick stores the path and its layer (the card 2, each folder below one more, as `folder_enter` counts) and opens `folder_page`, whose synchronous init, `ringnav_folder`, opens it through the [Folder Root](#folder-root) block, the card present; the pending folder is cleared either way. Stock `folder_back` then climbs to the card and out, as from Folder Root. The path is copied, never formatted. The page opens at the folder's top, not on the song. ponytail: the row is left out while a `folder_page` is open (stock keeps one), so Now Playing after a folder play has none; Return reaches that page, which shows the previous folder once the play has moved on.

The queue is `*mcl_pdeqplaylist` (`0xa269a4`) with static state at `0xa3bdb0` (shuffle pool, a deque of unplayed indices), `0xa3be90` (shuffle previous index), `0xa3be94`/`0xa3be98` (gapless preloaded index/flag), `0xa3bea0` (queue class), `0xa3bea4` (position), `0xa3bea8` (mode) and `0xa3beb4` (player socket). Only a local queue (class 1 or `0xfxxx`) grows. An empty queue is loaded by `mclLoadPlayList(tracks, 0, class)`, which does not start playback. Otherwise Add appends with `_deque_push_back`, and Play Next appends too, then turns the new rows into place at position+1 by swapping rows' bytes (`REC_SIZE`, three reversals). `deque_at` points into the deque's own storage and stock's `stSongInfo` copy (`0x5b3b1c`) gives every string its own buffer, so a row moves whole and only the new rows are copies. Rebuilding the deque (`deque_clear` + `deque_assign`, as stock loads it) held a library-wide queue twice, as `staged` once did ([Play counts](#play-counts), "`staged`'s memory"). Crossfade's fade key holds the rows' paths as well as their addresses for this reason: after the move, the next row's address holds the Play Next song. `playback_queue_insert` (`patch/playback.c`) owns every queue index fix-up for both: the pool entries, the last pick and the previous index (`back_to`) at or after the insert shift, the new indices join the pool, and then advanced play's seen, history and pending list follow (`playback_insert`, a no-op when it is off). Gapless preloads only pos+1 (modes 0 and 3), so a set preload flag is closed exactly when the tracks land there, with the `mclSetPlayMode` sequence (`{mcl-closegapless\null}` to the socket, flag -1); advanced play then closes any preload as its own (flag 0, index -1). In shuffle the checked call at `0x5addf0` in `mclNextSong` (`bal 0x5a8120`, delay slot `a0 = 1`) becomes `jal ringnav_shuffle`: it runs the stock pick, then makes the first Play Next track the position once, if the queue still holds it there. Repeat-one repeats the current track by design.

Now Playing holds capture the queue pointer, length, index, song identity (including CUE offsets) and an ordered queue hash. Deferred opening and actions revalidate this target. View Queue opens stock's `playerqueue_page` after closing the hold dialog, so its rows track the live playing queue. Stock marks the playing row with the style `s_scrlabel_red20l`, which the build turns white, so `stock_row_style` also puts ▶ (U+25B6) before that row's title; stock binds the title just before the style, each time, so it's never added twice. Stock's `default.ttf` has the glyph and Classic's two faces had none, so that row's title fell back to stock's face, larger and lighter than its neighbours: the build draws one into `classic-light.ttf` and `bold.ttf` (`play_mark` in `tools/build.py`, a triangle on the middle of the capitals, listed in `CLASSIC_LIGHT_RANGES` and `BOLD_RANGES`), so the title keeps the face `stock_row_text` gave it. That is list order, not play order, so while advanced shuffle is on (`playback_option(0)`) View Queue opens the payload's Up Next list instead (`upnext_page` in `patch/upnext.c`, titled View Queue, `playback_upcoming`): the successor walk of the shuffle's order, as a snapshot. A View Queue row plays through stock's `player_start_queue` (`0x5160dc`, which `mclSetPlayPos`es and starts the player); a row of the shuffle list calls it with its snapshot index and closes the page. A Play/Pause hold on one of its rows (`ringnav_keylong`'s `un_hold` → `un_move`) plays that occurrence next (`playback_move`), ahead of any pending Play Next. Only the pending list changes: the occurrence goes to its front, out of its old place there, so the queue, its indices, the order and every album or folder group stay as they were. Play Next's list is queue indices in play order, at most 64 (`PEND_LIMIT`); a hold or Play Next past that drops the list's last entry. Playing one takes its order entry to the cursor, as a Shuffle Songs pick does (`commit`'s `play_now`). The gapless preload is closed and the rows are retaken with the pick at row 0, the selection there. A successful hold is silent, including a row already next; one no longer up next (played since) or a queue whose `queue_hash` changed since the rows were taken retakes the rows from the live queue and toasts "Queue changed"; a page gone or a row out of range does nothing. ponytail: a row from the next pass (Repeat All) still plays in that pass too. Its hook (`ringnav_start_queue`, `patch/playback.c`) commits the pick in advanced play first, so Previous returns to it. Under Shuffle Songs it goes through Play Next's path, moving it right after the cursor, so the order carries on after it: the pick does not play again later in the pass, and the tracks it jumped stay up next. An ordered queue (or Shuffle Albums, Folders, a category) moves the cursor to it, so the tracks after it follow. A pick inside a pending Play Next takes only its entry off the list; the rest stay pending. Stock's own View Queue delete under advanced play is followed on the UI thread (`follow_queue`: each UI pass, a View Queue row, and Next, Previous or a preload when they run there): `rebuild` keeps each entry's identity (its path's hash and CUE start, `ids`), so the entries left keep their seen marks, history and Play Next places, shifted down as `playback_insert` shifts them up, and the order is drawn again over them as an insert's is. The playing entry deleted, stock's position names the one after it, which joins the history, so Previous goes back to the track before the deleted one; with none after it (the last row), Previous goes there too and Next ends the queue. A size change that isn't a deletion takes a new order as a new load gets, Play Next and the history dropped. Off the UI thread (headset keys, a natural change) a resized queue takes stock's way for that one Next or Previous, and the next UI pass follows. ponytail: only a size change is seen (a delete always makes one); a same-size swap would need the identities compared every pass. ponytail: the shuffle list's rows are indices from when it opened; a queue replaced meanwhile (a folder play's next folder) makes them name other tracks. Stock Shuffle draws each next track at random, so it has no order to list: Shuffle Songs and a collection's Shuffle save stock's Shuffle mode, then hand the loaded queue to advanced Shuffle Songs with Repeat All (`playback_shuffle`). View Queue does the same on a stock Shuffle chosen elsewhere (Now Playing's mode button; `playback_listable`, only while advanced play is off), then opens Up Next, a Play Next stock's Shuffle was holding (`qm_forced`) moved to the front of the pending list; ponytail: the order is fresh, so songs stock's shuffle already played can come round again. On Now Playing's hold menu a last row, More…, sends Now Playing's own `img_more` a click (stock's handler opens `playermore_page`), only while that page is open (`np_more`). Playlist add mode 2 uses the playing queue; its existing row click handlers are guarded again before the stock callback, with the captured row selected. Browsing state is independent.

## Home carousel

Home is an ordinary list ([ipod.md](ipod.md#home)) that bumps and wraps like a ring list. In Home: Carousel (below) the carousel uses `HOME_SLIDE_MS` (200 ms) for isolated ticks and `HOME_FAST_SLIDE_MS` (120 ms) when consecutive same-direction ticks arrive at most `HOME_FAST_WINDOW_MS` (200 ms) apart. There is no extra home input gate; stock filtering and audible ticks stay stock-controlled. Each accepted tick retargets one animator from its live offset, extending the intended destination by one icon. Reversal discards that destination and heads toward the adjacent card in the new direction at normal speed. Stock completion handles wrapping and card selection; centre confirmation follows the intended final icon. Touch, native clicks, centre presses, leaving home and unavailable navigation reset speed tracking; wheel input cancels pending centre confirmation. Allocation failure settles on the requested icon without retaining an animator.

### Home: Carousel

Display's Home: Carousel (`IPOD/HOME=2`) shows stock's carousel in place of Home's lists. `ipod_home` (`tools/ipod.py`) keeps stock's `slide_menu` and its `img_left`/`img_right` arrows in `home_page.bin`, after the lists, hidden and insensitive, with clones of the Local Music card for Coverflow and the Shortcut (`home_card`). Stock binds the stock cards and arrows by name; `coverflow_home` binds the carousel's own Coverflow and Shortcut images, as `widget_lookup` from the window finds the lists' rows first. `coverflow_home_layout` swaps the carousel and the lists at runtime (`home_show`: visible and sensitive together, since hidden widgets keep their taps), hides the art, and on leaving Carousel shows the main list. The wheel, Centre and touch take the carousel path above (`home_step`); Return keeps stock's. The cards' art (stock's, and `assets/icons/menu_coverflow*` and `menu_rockbox*`) keeps its own colours: the image hook leaves every `menu_*` image but the white arrows (`menu_left`, `menu_right`, which flip in Light Mode as other glyphs do) alone, so no Theme, accent or Light Mode reaches the cards. ponytail: the Shortcut card stays when Home's Shortcut row hides (Shortcut: Rockbox with no Rockbox on the card); its tap toasts that.

## Coverflow

Coverflow (`patch/coverflow.c`) follows Rockbox PictureFlow: albums and tracks come from the stock library queries, never from tags, and the payload owns only a thumbnail cache. Audit (V1.32, 2026-09-28); every entry point is exported, and the builder pins the executable through the stock ZIP's SHA-256:

| Stock entry                                       | Address                 | Size      | Prologue                                                |
| ------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- | --------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| `home_page_init` (hook)                           | `0x523c84`              | 1028      | `50001c3c3c309c2721e09903`                              |
| `getAllAlbum`                                     | `0x4fceb0`              | 800       | `53001c3c109e9c2721e09903`                              |
| `getMusicByAlbum`                                 | `0x4ff7d8`              | 480       | `52001c3ce8749c2721e09903`                              |
| `toolsThumbSpecCover`                             | `0x5c4550`              | 264       | `46001c3c70279c2721e09903`                              |
| `toolsGetAlbumCover`                              | `0x5c4444`              | 268       | `46001c3c7c289c2721e09903`                              |
| `window_create`                                   | `0x66a274`              | 72        | `3c001c3c4cca9c2721e09903`                              |
| `widget_factory` / `widget_factory_create_widget` | `0x666c68` / `0x666f04` | 24 / 188  | `3c001c3c58009c2721e09903` / `3c001c3cbcfd9c2721e09903` |
| `image_create` / `image_set_draw_type`            | `0x614cfc` / `0x61527c` | 112 / 116 | `41001c3cc41f9c2721e09903` / `41001c3c441a9c2721e09903` |
| `image_base_set_image`                            | `0x643794`              | 228       | `3e001c3c2c359c2721e09903`                              |
| `widget_load_image` / `widget_unload_image`       | `0x65c1ec` / `0x65c884` | 196 / 124 | `3d001c3cd4aa9c2721e09903` / `3d001c3c3ca49c2721e09903` |
| `bitmap_create_ex` / `bitmap_destroy`             | `0x62f9c4` / `0x62f574` | 248 / 256 | `3f001c3cfc729c2721e09903` / `3f001c3c4c779c2721e09903` |
| `bitmap_lock_buffer_for_read` / `_for_write`      | `0x630000` / `0x630078` | 120 / 120 | `3f001c3cc06c9c2721e09903` / `3f001c3c486c9c2721e09903` |
| `bitmap_unlock_buffer` / `bitmap_get_line_length` | `0x6300f0` / `0x62fabc` | 60 / 88   | `3f001c3cd06b9c2721e09903` / `3f001c3c04729c2721e09903` |
| `canvas_draw_image`                               | `0x635254`              | 260       | `3f001c3c6c1a9c2721e09903`                              |
| `slide_menu_set_spacer` / `widget_to_local`       | `0x5f53e0` / `0x65a9b4` | 116 / 220 | `43001c3ce0189c2721e09903` / `3d001c3c0cc39c2721e09903` |

Data: `tools_pdeq_directory` (`0xa269bc`, 4), `parse_cover_mutex` (`0xa37cb8`, 24) and `g_playcover_mutex` (`0xa39c18`, 24) are size-checked `CONTEXT_DATA`; the scan thread handle `0xa271d8` and done flag `0xa271d4` have no symbol (`offsets.inc`).

**Albums.** `load_localclass_list(0xf003)` (`0x5088cc`) is `getAllAlbum()` followed by `deque_assign(*p_deque_showlist, *tools_pdeq_directory)`, so the payload calls `getAllAlbum` itself through `staged`, into a deque of its own: no private state, and a Local Music page underneath keeps its showlist. `getAllAlbum` runs `select id,album,songer,fileurl from songtable group by album COLLATE NOCASE` on `/mnt/data/database.db` through `toolsQueryDbTable` (`0x5c8044`: the lazily opened shared handle at `0xa3bf44`, `deque_clear`, `sqlite3_exec`, then `algo_sort_if` with the stock comparator). Its row callback (`0x4fc324`) skips empty albums and fills `+0` id, `+0x10/+0x14` album, `+0x18/+0x20` artist and `+0xc` the group's `fileurl`, which is the first-track path the art build uses. When any album exists it then appends an "Unknown Album" row (id -1, no path). It returns the query count: -1 without a database, 0 when empty. That grouping is by name alone (stock's Albums list shares it), so Coverflow then runs `ALBUM_SQL`, the same columns plus `albumsonger`, grouped by album, then album artist, or for songs without one the folder (`rtrim(fileurl, replace(fileurl, '/', ''))`), through `split_row`: `album_row` on the first four columns, then the album artist into `+0x24` (`REC_ALBUM_ARTIST`), which the `stSongInfo` copy (`0x5b3b1c`) duplicates. An album's path, which its art comes from, is the song `ALBUM_TRACK` picks: the lowest disc, then track, then path, each number padded to four digits, a missing one taken as 0 and one outside 0-9999 (a date as the track, or -1) as its bound, so the prefixes stay four digits and the path whole. A bare `fileurl` was whichever row SQLite read last, so an album whose songs carry different covers showed a random one. Covers already made from another song stay until **Refresh Coverflow** (`coverflow_refresh_art`), which empties Coverflow's cache and stock's `.sldp` (Library's album covers, made once per name, which otherwise only a settings reset, `init_usersetting`, clears) and drops AWTK's cached bitmaps, as the cover thread writes the same names again. Each stock row is replaced in place by its name's rows (`split`), one per `album_cmp` album, so same-named albums by different artists are separate covers with separate art keys; `album_cmp` tells albums apart the same way everywhere else (tracks, Sort ranks, play counts, playback's album groups). Its folder fallback takes a trailing disc folder (`CD1`, `Disc 2`, `Disk_03`: cd/disc/disk, an optional space, `_` or `-`, digits) as its parent, so an untagged multi-disc album is one; the SQL keeps disc folders apart and `split` merges them. Shuffle Folders (`folder_cmp`) keeps each folder apart. The stock Albums list gets the same split (`ringnav_localclass`), and a row's album page (`load_album_detaillist`, hooked to keep the row) and resume (`memeory_startplayer`'s 0xff10 rebuild) keep only that album's songs from `getMusicByAlbum`. Its art cache, `/mnt/mmc/.sldp/<md5 of the name>0.jpg`, adds the album artist, else the album folder, to a name that adjacent rows share (`art_key`): the row renderer (`0x4a388c`), the page's `albumcoverinfo_init` cover tasks the art thread hashes, and the album page's big cover (`0x4a72a4`). An artist's albums tab (`load_localartist_list(1)`, hooked) groups by name too, and has one row of a name another artist shares, so adjacency can't tell: `ringnav_artist_list` splits a copy of the tab as Albums is and gives each row its album's `art_key`, the same-named album in its folder, else with the row's artist as album artist. Its renderer (`0x4ab804`, the row in `$s2`) and four `albumcoverinfo_init` calls (Album tab entry `0x4ac840`, page return `0x4ae860`/`0x4ae968`, init `0x4aebc0`) use those keys, and the album page's big cover follows its cover task as before. Local art completion (`0x4edda0`) passes the worker's copied key (`sp + 0x1688`) to `ringnav_cover_done`; the folder-cover path, which branched to the call itself (`0x4eeddc`), now enters one word earlier so it passes the key too: under `aclist_mutex`, it sets the small/big ready flag only if that row still has the key. A job finishing after navigation rebuilt the list therefore cannot mark a different album ready and make later requests skip its missing cover. When `rename_covers` changes a row's key, it clears both ready flags under the same lock, including a completion arriving between stock initialization and renaming. The grids' scroll start (`0x14e`: `0x4aab40` an artist's albums, `0x4a22b0` Library Albums) set the worker's shared `albumcover_taskstate` to 2, which it sleeps through, and their scroll end resumed it (`local_albumcover_task`) only when the first row in view changed; a wheel tick within the same rows, or the return to a scrolled grid (its `scroll_view_set_offset` sends `0x14e` alone), left every grid without art until reboot. Library Songs (`music_table_client`, scroll start `0x4a7b90`) did the same, so returning to a scrolled Songs list showed every row's placeholder until the wheel moved. All three stores are `nop` (`COVER_PAUSE`): the worker keeps going, and each scroll end replaces its queue as before. An artist's albums scroll end (`0x4ad878`) took its first row as `yoffset / 256 * 3`, though its rows are 160 px as Library Albums divides; it divides by 160 (`ARTIST_COVER_ROW`), so the nine covers it requests, and the renderer (`0x4ab798`) draws, are the ones in view. Coverflow keeps this order and shows the Unknown row with the placeholder, only when its card's query, `getMusicByAlbum(NULL)`, finds a song (`drop_unknown`, as [Unknown rows](#unknown-rows)): before V8.6 it showed even with every song tagged.

The copy outlives the page, because the query is the slow part of an open: `toolsQueryDbTable` sorts with a stock comparator (`0x5ba658`) that runs `utf8_to_pinyin` on both names, four `snprintf`s and several `memset`s per comparison. Only `scanAllMusicFile`, `scanSpecFolder` and `deleteMusicFromMusicDb` write `songtable` (every other `toolsInsert/Delete/UpdateDbTable` caller writes the favourites, playlist and play-record tables); their hooks step a generation before and after stock runs, and an open queries again only when the generation moved since the list was taken, or on Refresh. A list taken while a writer runs is never kept, and an empty or refused result is not kept at all.

**Folder tag refresh.** Scan specified folders keeps stock's recursive scan and cancellation, but its `toolsCheckItemExist` call (`0x5c981c`) goes through `ringnav_scan_refresh`: existing `songtable` rows re-read tags and update in place, matching filename, full path and CUE start/end. IDs, creation time and song count stay intact; a full library still refreshes existing files, while new files wait for space. CUE entries use the freshly parsed CUE metadata; ordinary files use `toolsGetMusicInfo`, including the FLAC album-artist fallback, after checking TagLib can read them. Tag validation and refresh disable TagLib audio-property reads, so rescanning existing tags does not probe the audio duration. The title’s stock pinyin initial is refreshed too. SQL text is bounded and quotes apostrophes; an unreadable tag leaves the existing row intact. The existing scan writer hooks invalidate cached album groups. New songs keep stock's insert path. Update Local Music drops `songtable` (`scanAllMusicFile`'s second argument is `toolsInitDatabase`'s drop flag) and dates every song again from `toolsGetFileCreateTime` (`0x5bddc4`): `st_ctime` on FAT32, the directory entry's creation time, but the file's `st_atime` when `getDiskMemoryInfoAndSetFormat`'s format byte says exFAT or NTFS, where stat has no creation time and a fresh copy's access time stood in for it. The update's own tag reads move it (relatime, once a day), so each full update dated the whole library to its last update or play, and `getAllRecentAddRecord`'s `order by time_create desc limit 100` (Library → Recently Added; Coverflow's Recently Added sort reads the same column) filled with those songs, the album added that day lost among them (#170, #188). `ringnav_file_created` hooks the function: `statx` with `STATX_BTIME` (`ringnav_statx`, the o32 syscall; demo imports none) reports the creation stamp the exFAT directory entry keeps, which reads never move (stock's 5.10.21 kernel has the mainline driver, which fills it), so every full update dates the library the same way; the first one on this version replaces the dates 1.6.1–1.6.3 kept and repairs those lost before them. Windows and Linux stamp a copy with the time of copying; a Mac keeps the file's own creation date. A file whose filesystem reports no stamp, or a failed call, takes stock's date (the stock kernel mounts no NTFS, so only exFAT takes the access-time path). 1.6.1's keyed `ringnav_added` copy of `time_create` across the drop is gone; one left by an update interrupted on 1.6.1–1.6.3 stays in the database, unused. The query hook keeps that `ORDER BY` outside its `songtable` subquery.

**Scan Folders' rows.** Stock's row click (`0x52e0ac`) ticks the row for a tap left of x 81 and opens the folder for one past it. The list holds folders only, so `specfolder_enter` refuses a folder with none inside, and stock answered "Folder not found" (`msg_notfolder`, #254), which left no way to reach an album's folder without knowing the column is there. `ringnav_specfolder_click` (`patch/scan_folders.c`) asks stock's `toolsLoadDirectory` and `toolsCheckTypeDeque` first, the same check, so a tap on such a folder moves to x 0 and stock ticks it; Centre's click already arrives at x 0. Limits: a folder that does open is listed twice on the tap, and a folder holding only folders stock hides (`.Trashes`, `System Volume Information` and the like) ticks rather than failing. Under Classic stock's `unselect` ring is emptied (`ringnav_image_add`), so the tick column shows nothing on a row until it is ticked.

**Indexes.** Stock's `songtable` has only its primary key, so `getMusicByAlbum` (`album like '%s'`: an album's page, Go to Album, an artist's All albums card, and twice for each album of Era's Play All), `toolsCheckItemExist` and `toolsDeleteDbTable` (`filename='%s' and fileurl='%s' and time=%d and time_end=%d`: the folder scan's check and `ringnav_scan_refresh`'s update for each file, and each song a delete removes) and `getAllRecentAddRecord` (`order by time_create desc limit 100`) each read the whole table. Both scans' hooks end (`library_scan`) with `create index if not exists` of `songtable_album` (`album collate nocase`), `songtable_filename` and `songtable_time_create` (`time_create desc`), through `toolsUpdateDbTable` on the scan thread and stock's shared handle: after stock's writes, as Update Local Music drops the table and its indexes with it (so a full scan inserts into a bare table and builds each index once), and before `wait_scan` sets the done flag, so the library reads as scanning meanwhile. `like` takes a `NOCASE` index on a text column (stock's `VARCHAR`) while `case_sensitive_like` is off, which nothing sets. The query hook's `MUSIC_WHERE` subquery is flattened into the query, so the scoped queries use it too; a pattern that starts with `%` or `_` still reads the table. With stock's libsqlite3 3.19.3 under QEMU (instructions on a cold connection, the row callback's fill included), an album's songs take 1.2 M instead of 257 M at 65,000 songs (9 tracks), and 1.4 M instead of 1.56 G at 400,000 (16), so Era's Play All on the 65,000-song library's largest decade (760 albums, 1,520 queries) is about 1.8 G of queries rather than 0.39 T; a file's check, delete or refresh 0.6 M instead of 50 M, and 0.6–0.7 M instead of 306 M; Recently Added 4.2 M instead of 780 M at 65,000 and 4.77 G at 400,000. The build costs 1.04 G at 65,000 songs and 7.30 G at 400,000, adds 5.4 MB and 33.6 MB to `database.db` (21.4 MB to 26.8 MB, 106 MB to 140 MB) and sorts through up to 2.6 MB and 15.9 MB of temporary file in `/tmp` (RAM) an index; a folder scan's new song then costs 40 k (65,000) to 64 k (400,000) more to insert. A library no scan has run on since has none and reads as before: a build anywhere else would hold the database's write lock for seconds while stock's library pages query it. A connection still reading when the build writes (a count started before the scan) fails the create with `SQLITE_BUSY`, as stock sets no busy timeout, and the indexes wait for the next scan.

They change other plans, for the same rows. An index keeps equal keys in rowid order, as stock's sort did, so with `desc` songs of one second (a CUE image's tracks, files copied together) keep their order newest first (Recently Added, `getAllMusic(3)`: Time added (descending), and the payload's unsorted reads). Oldest first (`getAllMusic(4)`, Time added (ascending)) would walk the index backwards and reverse them, so `library_query` adds `, id` to its `order by`; an ascending index would reverse the newest-first lists instead, and Recently Added would keep the other end of an album its `limit 100` cuts. `count(*)` counts `songtable_time_create` (5.2 MB at 400,000 songs) instead of the table (99 MB): 1.9 M instead of 22.6 M, so the count Update Local Music's message takes before the scan holds its lock for less of the scan's start. `getAllMusic(3)` walks `songtable_time_create`: 1.51 G instead of 2.02 G at 65,000 songs, and no sort spilled to `/tmp` (15 MB). `getAllMusic(4)` walks it too and sorts only each second's songs. The album groupings walk `songtable_album`, a row lookup each in album order: `getAllAlbum` 386 M instead of 889 M at 65,000 (2.32 G instead of 6.26 G at 400,000) with no 10 MB spill, `ALBUM_SQL`, Era's and Home's album count 5–14% fewer, their three-term `group by` still sorted through `/tmp`, an artist's albums tab 13% more (328 M instead of 289 M), and the other `like … group by album` queries (album artist, composer and genre tabs, album search) walk it the same way. Each reads its pages out of order, over twice a scan's (227 MB rather than 99 MB at 400,000). ponytail: those reads are unmeasured on a Q2; `not indexed` puts a grouping back on the table if they prove slow.
