**Empty or scanning.** Only the modal Update Local Music dialog (`dialog_updatemusic_dialog_init`) starts the scan: `tk_thread` `wait_scan` → `scanAllMusicFile` (`toolsInitDatabase`, `toolsLoadAllFile`, commit), storing the handle at `0xa271d8` and clearing `0xa271d4`, which `wait_scan` sets when it ends. The same init sets the byte at `0xa271d0` (`SCAN_WHOLE`): 1 for the whole library's scan, 0 for a folder's, as we read stock's code; no device run has checked it. Its Back button stops the scan and quits the dialog before the thread ends. Coverflow shows "Update Local Music first" while a scan runs (handle set, flag clear) or when `getAllAlbum` returns ≤ 0, as PictureFlow's `check_database()` does. Before each folder entry `toolsLoadAllFile` (`0x5c9660`) stops the scan, as Back does, once the song count (`toolsGetMusicNum`) reaches 20,000 (`slti` at `0x5c9780`). 65,000 does not fit `slti`'s signed immediate, so its count load becomes `jal ringnav_scan_room` with the count loaded into `a0` in the delay slot (`SCAN_LIMIT`): a leaf, as the scanner keeps using `gp` after it, true below 65,000. The queue's `QUEUE_LIMIT` (65,536) already holds that many. A larger queue (a folder's, a playlist's) gets no advanced play: `rebuild` refuses it before it takes its tables, 36 B a song. Play Next or Add to Queue past it (`playback_insert`), or a stock edit that grows the queue past it (`follow_queue`), still takes a 4 B a song `seen` first.

**Update during a count.** `toolsInitDatabase` drops `songtable` and creates it again on the connection every `tools*` function shares (`0xa3bf44`), which has no busy handler. A count reading `songtable` on its own connection, the dialog's `msg_count` or Home's `count_worker`, holds a read lock on the database until it ends, and a count reads every page of the table. So the drop could not commit: it failed with `SQLITE_BUSY` (stock ignores the result, `0x5c710c`), the create then failed as the table existed, `toolsInitDatabase` closed the handle and returned -1, and `scanAllMusicFile` skipped the update: the old library stayed and nothing said so. `coverflow_scan_all` now waits on the scan's thread, before the marker walk and stock's body, while either count runs (`library_counting`: `home.counting` or `st.msg_counting` 1), looking every 100 ms. Each flag is set on the UI thread before its worker starts: `msg_open` sets the dialog's as the dialog loads, before stock's init starts the scan's thread, and `home_counts` starts no count while a scan runs (`library_scanning`). The wait is outside SQLite and holds nothing, so no statement on the shared connection waits with it. The UI thread waits on it only when the dialog opens again over a running scan: stock's init stops the old thread and joins it, and the stop ends the wait within 100 ms. It can't deadlock: the counts' connections are their own, and a count takes no lock the scan's thread holds. Back ends the wait (`toolsStopUpdateMusic`'s flag, `toolsGetUserStop`) and skips stock's body, returning its 1, as `wait_scan` skips a scan stopped before it starts (`0x4a0ef4`): the drop would otherwise commit in a count's last 100 ms and `toolsClearDbCount` erase the Back, leaving a scan running behind a closed dialog, or fail and close the shared handle with a count still running. A folder scan never drops the table (`wait_specfolder_scan` passes a 0 drop flag) and does not wait. `test/patch.py` runs stock's `scanAllMusicFile` and `toolsInitDatabase` on a real SQLite file while the dialog's count holds a read lock on it. ponytail: the dialog stays on its first line for as long as a count runs: Home's album count runs about 14 billion instructions on a 400,000-song library.

Coverflow's main, artist and rated views keep only albums with a track outside `/mnt/mmc/Audiobooks/` and without an `.m4b` extension, case aside (`music_albums`, `MUSIC_ALBUM_SQL`). Filtering precedes the SQL grouping so a mixed album uses a music track for its artwork. The stock library lists keep their audiobook entries.

**Tracks.** A cover opens `getMusicByAlbum(id == -1 ? NULL : album)`, which is what a stock Albums row opens (`load_localclass_list(0xff10)` with `g_local_classinfo_save+0xd`, `+9` flagging Unknown); it returns stock-ordered songs (`algo_sort_if`). The Albums list has no -2 row; that one only occurs in an artist's albums (queue menu). The result comes through `staged` the same way, keeping only the cover's own album (`album_cmp`: the query matches the name alone). Coverflow then re-orders the copy into album order, disc then track then path then CUE start, so an untagged album keeps its file-name order. Its row callback (`0x4fb8b8`, `select *` from `songtable`) fills `+0x08` the file name, `+0x10` the tag's title, `+0x3c` `track` and `+0x40` `cd_num` (`REC_NAME`, `REC_TITLE`, `REC_TRACK`, `REC_DISC`). Each row shows the title tag when present, otherwise the file name less its file's extension; a CUE track's name, which does not end in it, stays whole. Folder play gets the re-ordered deque, so playback follows the list.

**Play class.** `0xff10` would resume the wrong album: memory-play re-runs `getMusicByAlbum` from `g_memory_info+0x611` (`memeory_startplayer` `0x5164e0`), which only `player_load_songlist` (`0x513634`) fills, from the live `g_local_classinfo_save`. Coverflow therefore uses folder play, `navigator_to_with_context("playing_page", {tracks, index, 1, 2})` as `startPlayFolderSong` does. `mclLoadPlayList` copies the deque synchronously, and memory-play reloads the last track's folder.

**Art.** Every stock caller holds a lock around the art calls, and none nests the two: `parse_albumcovertask_thd` (`toolsGetAlbumCover` `0x4edf18`/`0x4ef008`/`0x4ef0d0`, `toolsThumbSpecCover` `0x4ee960`/`0x4ef2d0`) and the UI-thread online thumbnails (`0x525244`/`0x52525c`) hold `parse_cover_mutex`; `player_parsecover_thd` holds `g_playcover_mutex` around `toolsGetAlbumCover` (`0x512fe0`) and `parse_cover_mutex` alone around its Tidal/Baidu downloads. `toolsGetAlbumCover` writes and removes the shared `/tmp/.tmp_picture`, so both stock threads can already race on it; Coverflow takes both locks, parse then play. `taglib_album_picture` (`libtag_c` `0x12508`) dispatches by extension to the C++ `get*_file_albumpic` helpers, none of which touches `libtag_c`'s unlocked string list (`taglib_tag_free_strings`), which the UI thread's `toolsGetMusicInfo` uses. That list is what failed the first design; embedded art is only as concurrent as stock's own player thread already is. `toolsThumbSpecCover(src, dst, w, h)` decodes JPEG (≤ 6 MB; libjpeg scales by the longest side, 1/1 to 1023 px, 1/2 to 2048, 1/4 to 4096, 1/8 beyond, `0x5b86d4`, so a baseline 3000×3000 cover is cheap, while a progressive one still holds its full coefficient planes, about 27 MB at 4:2:0, in demo) or PNG (≤ 1 MB and at most 1024 px on either side, `slti 0x401` at `0x5b6894`, but only after `png_read_png` at `0x5b6844` has expanded the whole image in demo, about 64 MB at 4000×4000 RGBA), by their magic bytes (`toolsGetPictureInfo`), and encodes, or `cp`s a source already small enough, straight to `dst` with no extension check, so `<key>.jpg.tmp` is safe. Both return 1 on success. Stock's own cover cache (`/mnt/mmc/.sldp/<md5>{0,1}.jpg`) and folder-image finder (`toolsGetExternCover`, `0x5c49e8`) are not used: the first fills lazily as the album grid scrolls, and the spec fixes cover.jpg → folder.jpg → embedded. Coverflow tries embedded art, then `cover`/`folder` `.jpg`, `.png`, `.webp` (none from a folder so deep that the image's path would not fit 1024 bytes: the cut name is a prefix, another file's at best), then ffmpeg on the embedded art (last: a process for every album without art). Whatever stock refuses (WebP, a PNG over 1 MB or 1024 px, a JPEG over 6 MB) goes to stock `/usr/bin/ffmpeg` (`ff_thumb`: fork and exec, its first picture or embedded `attached_pic` fitted into the box, written as a baseline 4:2:0 JPEG). `ff_thumb` has ffmpeg write `<dst>.ff` and renames it onto `dst` only on exit 0 with a non-empty file; otherwise it removes `<dst>.ff` and leaves `dst` alone, as stock's grid cache takes any file at its path for art. ffmpeg creates its output before decoding, so a picture it can't decode leaves an empty file even on exit 0, and a killed run a partial one. `-xerror` ends the run at a picture's first decode error instead of demuxing the rest of the audio file, and `waitpid` is retried on `EINTR` so the child is always reaped. That covers the embedded picture in `build_art` and every `thumb` caller with a readable source (photos, Spotify). It writes no shared file and takes no lock. `thumb` reads the source's header first (`big_picture`: a PNG's IHDR, or a JPEG's frame header within its first 64 segments) and gives a PNG over 1024 px either way, or a progressive JPEG (SOF2) over 1024 px, to `ff_thumb` alone: stock would hold the whole picture in demo, which the OOM killer then takes ([#216](https://github.com/DiamondBond/q2-pod/issues/216)). A baseline JPEG, a smaller picture and one it can't size stay with stock, and so do the arithmetic and hierarchical progressive frames (SOF6, 10, 14), which ffmpeg 4.2 can't decode. ponytail: a progressive cover cut short, which libjpeg shows with a warning, fails ffmpeg's `-xerror` and has no art now; fall back to stock when ffmpeg exits by itself if such covers turn up. Photos leaves no `.bad` marker when `ff_kills` moved during its decode, as `build_art` leaves no empty marker. Embedded art gets no such check, as its picture reaches a file only inside `toolsGetAlbumCover` (`/tmp/.tmp_picture`, between `taglib_album_picture` and the decoder's `jalr` at `0x5c44e8`), so a large embedded PNG or progressive JPEG still decodes in demo. Neither do stock's own callers of a folder image: Now Playing's `toolsGetExternCover`, tried before Coverflow's fallback, and the album grid's `parse_albumcovertask_thd`, so a big `cover.png` still decodes in demo when its album plays or its grid row shows; trampolining `toolsThumbSpecCover` itself would cover both. Stock's decoder also refuses palette and greyscale PNGs (its PNG reader, `0x5b66f8`, takes only colour types 2 and 6). Now Playing gets the same fallback: `player_parsecover_thd` tries `toolsGetAlbumCover` (embedded) and then `toolsGetExternCover` (folder images) under `g_playcover_mutex`, and that second call (`PLAYER_COVER`, `0x51301c`) becomes `jal coverflow_player_cover`. That runs stock's call first, then `ff_thumb` on the same `cover`/`folder` names and then on the embedded picture. It never calls `thumb`, whose `parse_cover_mutex` would nest under the player's lock in the opposite order from `build_art`'s. Folder images cost a process only when one exists. The embedded probe costs one for every track without art, so it is skipped when `g_play_id3_info` holds the track's path (`player_get_id3info` parses it before the cover thread runs) and Coverflow's cache file for those parsed tags, keyed as Home's art is, is an empty marker. ponytail: the marker records one track's result, so another disc's tracks, or tracks of a same-named album, whose only art is embedded WebP or a large PNG show none on Now Playing; tracks on albums Coverflow hasn't built, or by another artist than the album's key (compilations), still pay a probe per play. Albums already marked empty need **Refresh Coverflow** (Display → Coverflow). ponytail: one process per refused cover; a resident decoder if first builds drag. Library Albums grids and Folder view get the Ogg case: `parse_albumcovertask_thd`'s three `toolsGetAlbumCover` calls (`GRID_COVER`, `0x4edf1c`/`0x4ef018`/`0x4ef0d4`: an album's cover, a folder's first track with art, a track row) become `jal coverflow_album_cover` (the first), `jal coverflow_folder_cover` (the second) and `jal coverflow_grid_cover`, each stock's call and then, for an `.opus` or `.ogg` track only (stock's libtag reads no Ogg picture), `ff_thumb`. All three run on stock's one cover thread (`platform_init` starts it) under `parse_cover_mutex`, which the UI thread also takes for Library's online thumbnails (`localmusic_page_init`, `0x525244`/`0x52525c`) and Spotify's art (`thumb`), so the lock is dropped for the ffmpeg run and taken again before returning. That is safe: each call's source and destination are the thread's stack paths (`.sldp/<md5>{0,1}.jpg`, `.downpic/fd_<md5>.jpg`), which no other lock holder writes (the UI thread's are `.downpic/s_<md5>.jpg`), and what stock does after it (`toolsGetExternCover` on the album call) runs with the lock held again. The album call's stock fallback is `toolsGetExternCover`, which scans the folder for any `.jpg`, `.png` or `.jpeg`, `folder`/`cover` names first; `coverflow_album_cover` calls it before ffmpeg, so an Ogg album whose art is a folder image costs no process, and its Ogg art is ordered as Coverflow's. The folder-row call already runs it first, and the track-row call has no folder fallback, so neither does. Stock caches only a hit (`.sldp`, `.downpic`), so a track ffmpeg found no picture in is remembered in memory (an FNV hash of its path; not when the track itself is gone, the fork was refused or ffmpeg was killed) and its rows ask no further process. ponytail: 1024 misses remembered until reboot, the oldest overwritten; a picture added to such a track since shows after a reboot. Stock's folder row reads the folder in directory order, the order its files were copied to the card, and keeps the art of the first audio file that has any (folder images, then that file's own), cached as `.downpic/fd_<md5>.jpg`, which stock deletes at every Return in Folder view. So an album copied out of order showed a later song's cover. `coverflow_folder_cover` lists the folder itself and tries its first song by name, as Folder view lists it, before the song stock passed: regular files with the extension of stock's song, no dot files (a Mac's `._` copies). The folder, its first song and whether that song had art are kept for stock's next call; a hit ends stock's walk and clears them, so the next walk lists the folder again. ponytail: names compared by `strcasecmp`, not the list's own comparator; a folder with no art at all stays kept until another folder is shown.

**Build.** On open, the UI thread copies each uncached album's track path (`strdup`) and FNV key (artist, album) into a job array. One pthread then renders 160×160 art to `/mnt/mmc/.coverflow/<key>.jpg.tmp` and renames it into place. Missing art leaves an empty `<key>.jpg` marker; a track that is gone, or an ffmpeg run that never reached its own exit (a refused fork, a kill), leaves nothing, so the next open retries it. Before each album the thread writes the album's key and its track's path to `/mnt/mmc/.coverflow/building`, synced in place (a sync per album; not through a rename, whose directory write a reset could lose; a torn note reads short), and removes it after the last. One still there at the next open names an album whose build never ended: demo died on it, or hung on it until the watchdog restarted the Q2, and as nothing marked it every open would try it again and end the same way (two testers' "Preparing artwork… 0/2" restarts, 2026-11-09). `strike` gives that album the empty marker, unless its cover is there (a death between the rename and the next note), so it shows the placeholder until **Refresh Cover Flow**, and appends the track to the card's `q2-crash.log` under `== cover flow skipped`: the boot hook's `== last art` names it only when demo itself died ([boot.md](boot.md)), and the tester's log was the watchdog's "demo did not end" with no stall record, demo alive and the Q2 frozen, which fits a swapless kernel thrashing on a large embedded picture decoded in demo (the unguarded path above). One strike per restart, so a card with several such albums restarts once for each. A clean end mid-build (`coverflow_clean_end`, from `ringnav_clean_end`) removes the note and sets the cancel flag, so the thread stops after the album it is on, with no note for the next, which the kill that follows would otherwise have left for `strike`: a power-off is no album's fault. ponytail: the battery running out mid-build strikes an innocent album, which Refresh Cover Flow puts back. A 3000×3000 cover costs ffmpeg tens of MB (a PNG frame 27-36 MB, a progressive JPEG's coefficients up to 54 MB) on a device with no swap, and the kernel's OOM killer takes the process with the most memory, which was demo, so the Q2 froze and the watchdog rebooted it mid-build ([#169](https://github.com/DiamondBond/q2-pod/issues/169); only a dead demo reboots, see [boot.md](boot.md), a hung one never does). every child demo forks (`child_oom_first` in `patch/peq.h`: `ff_run`'s and the boot logo's ffmpeg, Audiobooks' chapter read, Tidal's tagger, q2video) writes 1000 to its `/proc/self/oom_score_adj` between fork and exec, so the kernel takes the child first; the album is then retried at the next open rather than marked artless, and Now Playing's `marked_empty` skip does not apply to it. Demo's own baseline matters too: `staged` keeps the staging deque's contents across a query by copying them out and back (`deque_init_copy`, `deque_assign`), so after Library → Songs on a 65,000-song library every `staged` call briefly holds two copies of that list (a `stSongInfo` is 0x58 bytes plus its strings), the likeliest link to Songs' deferred crash; a move would need the stock deque's layout. There is no build below `MIN_FREE_MB` (16) free on the card (`statfs`, o32 `f_bsize` word 1, `f_bavail` word 7). Progress and the cancel flag are volatile `.scratch` ints; a 250 ms timer polls until every job is done. Cancel and window destroy join the thread, a wait bounded by one album. A folder holding tracks of two albums gives neither its `cover`/`folder` image, which may be the other's: `folders_shared` finds those folders in one pass before the jobs, sorting the albums by folder (FNV hash, then the folder itself and the album key), replacing a check of every album against every other on the UI thread, albums squared: the likely cause (inferred from a tester's watchdog restarts, not reproduced) of a big library's first open stalling until the watchdog restarted the Q2. A cold open, one that queries the albums or finds albums with no art, also writes its phases (`start`, `staged`, `split`, `music_albums`, `drop_unknown`, `sorted`, every 1000th job and `done`, each with the milliseconds since it began) to `/mnt/data/q2-cf-trace`, synced, a few lines per open (a warm one writes nothing), and removes it when `load` ends, so after a forced restart `S90play` puts the phase it reached in `q2-crash.log` ([Watchdog](#watchdog)). Before each album the thread's `crash_note` names its track in `/tmp/q2-art-file` and, every 50th album, appends the count, demo's VmRSS and the track to `/tmp/q2-art.log`, for the crash log ([boot.md](boot.md)). **System settings → Display → Coverflow → Refresh Coverflow** (`coverflow_refresh_art`) unlinks the cache directory and steps `library_gen`, so the next open queries the albums again and rebuilds their art.

**Page.** The Home card clones `btn_localmusic` at index 2 in the carousel, reusing its images, and is the third row of Home's list; `home_page_init`'s `widget_foreach` visitor (`0x5239b4`) binds `img_*` clicks and translates `label_*` by name and ignores unknown names. The Local Music card's label is renamed `label_library`, so it skips it too and its literal "Library" shows in every language. The hook runs the stock init through `stock_home_trampoline` (`0x523c90`), then binds `widget_lookup(win, "img_coverflow", 1)`, whatever stock returned. In iPod it also sets up Home's art ([ipod.md](ipod.md#home)). The card opens a runtime `window_create(NULL, 0, 0, 0, 0)` named `coverflow_page`; `window_base_create` opens it through `window_manager_open_window`, and the window-manager key handlers that `home_page_init` registers route it like any page (`CTX_DYNAMIC`, exempt from the rootfs window-name check). Covers are a factory `slide_menu` of `image_create` children, each item `h` square (`0x5f3040`); with depth (below) the children stay empty and the payload draws the covers. In the flat fallback they are stock images (`image_set_draw_type` 4, as stock cover rows). `slide_menu` dispatches `EVT_VALUE_CHANGED` (`0xe`, after `0xd` will-change, `0x5f320c`) with the new index already at `+0x78`, which updates the captions (the album over its artist; with Artists set to Album Artist, `artist_type` 1, the album artist tag when it has one, `cover_artist`, so a compilation reads "Various Artists" or its compiler, and the Artist sort and its letter jump use the same name, sorting again when Artists changes); in the flat fallback only covers within ±3 then load their file, the stock way: `widget_load_image` → `image_base_set_image` → `widget_unload_image`. A failed load (the empty marker) shows `default_album_flow`. Clicks only schedule screen changes on a 0 ms timer, so no screen change destroys the widget whose click is running. Tracks hide the covers rather than destroy them, so Return restores the album.

Coverflow’s Album and Artist sorts, on ticks (a finger on the screen, the page sliding in, Classic's flip panel or no depth frame; otherwise the thumb's letters apply, [Letters](#coverflow-thumb)), once a spin passes `CF_FIRST_MS` (700 ms, longer than the lists' `LIST_FIRST_MS`), jump to the first album of the next or previous populated initial group. The active sort key skips leading articles; numeric, punctuation, separator and missing-name initials share `#`, while other Unicode initials are retained. The same carousel animator is retargeted; utility cards and wrap edges remain individual stops. The existing letter style expires after 400 ms. Recently Added, Most Played and Random keep single-album stepping.

## Tag text

Stock reads a song's tags with its own `libtag_c` (`toolsGetMusicInfo`, `0x5c5f40`, for the library scan and the player): for each of six fields (title, artist, album, genre, composer, album artist) it asks for the field twice, `taglib_tag_<field>(tag, 0)` (TagLib's `String::to8Bit(false)`: one byte per character, Latin-1) and `(tag, 1)` (UTF-8), and whether TagLib found every character below U+0100 (`taglib_tag_islatin1`, `String::isLatin1`). `toolsFixId3ErrorCode` (`0x5c5cf8`) then took a field for GBK whenever it was "Latin-1" that way: it converted the Latin-1 bytes from GBK to UTF-8 (`toolsIconvConvert(s, "utf-8", "gbk")`, with an `iconv_open("gbk", "utf-8")` round trip as its check) and kept the result, so any Western title with an accented letter came out Chinese: "Lo que la música me enseñó" pairs `ú` and `s` (0xFA 0x73), `ñ` and `ó` (0xF1 0xF3) into GBK characters. That covers FLAC and Vorbis comments (always UTF-8) and UTF-8 ID3v2 frames as much as Latin-1 ID3 frames: the test is on the decoded text, not the tag's encoding. `TAG_TEXT` in `tools/build.py` turns that round trip's `strcmp` (`0x5c5e7c`) into `jal ringnav_gbk_tag`, which also asks that the Latin-1 bytes read as GBK (`gbk_text`): not UTF-8, and every byte from 0x80 one of a pair, lead 0x81–0xFE then trail 0x80–0xFE, with no pair between two ASCII letters. Western text puts an accented letter next to an ASCII one ("mú-s" is 0xFA 0x73) or a pair inside a word ("Can-çã-o", "Beyonc-é’-s"), so it fails and keeps TagLib's UTF-8 copy; a GBK-tagged MP3 (Chinese rips' Latin-1 frames holding GBK bytes) is all pairs and still converts. Limits: a GBK character with an ASCII-range trail byte (0x40–0x7E, the rarer GBK/3 and GBK/4 ones) or a single one inside a Latin word ("a周b"), makes its field read as Latin-1, and Western text whose accented letters only come in pairs at a word's edge ("ñó" alone) reads as GBK. Stock's conversion writes back over a CUE field unbounded, and GBK grows by half as UTF-8, so a CUE field over 340 bytes is read as Windows-1252 instead. The library keeps what the scan stored: **Library → Update Local Music** reads the tags again.

TagLib 2.0.1 joins multiple genre values with `" / "`. Stock copied the result unchecked into the 128-byte style field at `info + 0x200`, immediately before the title at `+0x280`, so a joined genre of 128 bytes or more replaced the title in the library and player (`toolsFixId3ErrorCode`'s `snprintf` stops the value at 255 bytes, so nothing goes past the title). `GENRE_COPY` changes that one copy to `ringnav_genre_copy`, which keeps the whole entries that fit 127 bytes, or as many whole characters of an oversized first entry as fit. Tag files are unchanged. Limits: only `" / "` is an entry boundary, so a single value joined with `;` or `//` over 127 bytes is cut at 127 (at a whole character), and an empty first entry followed by an oversized one stores an empty genre. The library keeps what the scan stored. Update Local Music drops `songtable` and inserts every row through `toolsGetMusicInfo`, and Scan specified folders refreshes existing rows (**Folder tag refresh**), so either repairs the title. Favourites, playlists and play records keep their own copy of a row, which neither touches: a bad title saved there stays until the song is added again.

Every scrolling text is a stock `hscroll_label`, and its holds and pace are set where every one of them passes. Stock's start (`hscroll_label_start`, `0x5e867c`, which a label's focus, its paint on a new text, its lull and the payload all call, PC-relative or through the GOT) waits the label's `delay` before its first step; nothing sets one but a page's XML, so most labels moved at once. A looping label then ends a pass by adding its `lull` timer (2000 ms from `set_hscroll_label_attribute`, 3000 by default), whose callback (`0x5e8828`) restarts it: the start's step put the text back at its start, so the lull held the end and the start never held. Its pace is `speed` in pixels per ms (`set_hscroll_label_attribute` sets 0.1, some pages 0.2, Now Playing's title and artist 0.05), or, with no speed, the whole overflow in its `duration`. The `hscroll_label_start` hook (`ringnav_hscroll_start`, `HOOKS` in `tools/build.py`) sets every label's `delay` to `MARQUEE_HOLD_MS` (1500) and caps its speed at `HSCROLL_PACE` (0.05, Now Playing's; a label with none takes it too) before stock's start runs. `HSCROLL_LULL` turns the end-of-pass `widget_add_timer` into `jal ringnav_hscroll_lull`, which adds its own timer, `MARQUEE_HOLD_MS` whatever the label's lull, through `hscroll_lull_end`: it sets `xoffset` to 0, then runs stock's callback, so the end holds `MARQUEE_HOLD_MS`, the text goes back to its start, and the start holds the same before the next pass. A looping `yoyo` label scrolls back instead: its lull is 0, since the delay holds whichever end it is at. A label that doesn't loop (`set_hscroll_label_attribute` leaves `loop` off, so most stock rows that scroll by themselves scroll once as their page opens) ended its pass at once, its timer removed, and stock painted its start with the ellipsis the moment it reached its end, or a `yoyo` one stayed on its end: `HSCROLL_ONCE` sends that pass to the lull too, whose end clears the label's timer id and stops it on its start, as stock's own lull callback clears it, so the timer ends by its `RET_REMOVE`. While a label holds its start it is running, so stock paints it without its ellipsis. An `only_focus` label stock won't restart (unfocused) rests on its start rather than its end, and one started while unfocused holds its start before its first tick stops it, as stock's does. Stock counts a pass's time in its 16 ms ticks, so on the UI loop's idle passes (25 ms) every label runs about a third slower, as it always has.

The info page's Genre line (`genre_fill`) is a private `hscroll_label` at stock's label's place, in the style `s_scrlabel_white16c`, for every value: stock's plain 225-pixel centred label clips a long value at both ends, and stock rewrites it every 250 ms, which would reset an animation on it, so it stays hidden as the value source. The private label's text is set only when the value changes; a value that fits is centred and never moves. A wider one scrolls on `genre_step`, the album marquee's chained timer and pace (`np_marquee`: `MARQUEE_HOLD_MS`, then `MARQUEE_PX` every `MARQUEE_MS` to the end, `MARQUEE_HOLD_MS` there, back to the start), looping while Now Playing is the top window, lit, on its third slide (`view_info`, value 2). Otherwise the chain ends where it is, costing no timer and no repaint, and the next fill restarts it. AWTK's own scroller stays off (`only_focus`, with no focus); `hscroll_label_set_xoffset` moves the text. Stock measures `text_w` when it paints, so the first fill of a new label sees 0 and a later one starts the hold. A new page's `ringnav_playing` ends the last page's chain, since `np_gone` ignores a page already replaced. ponytail: `np_animating` doesn't count this chain, so after 2 s without input the loop's idle passes stretch a step past `MARQUEE_MS` and the line runs a little slower than the album's; counting it would hold the loop at its fast pace for as long as the line moves.

CUE sheets went through the same `toolsIconvConvert(field, "utf-8", "gbk")` in their parser (the unnamed function after `toolsFreeStSongInfo`, seven fields, `CUE_TEXT`): each call is `jal ringnav_cue_text`, which keeps UTF-8 (lead and continuation bytes, nothing overlong, no surrogate), passes GBK (`gbk_text`, as for tags) to stock's conversion, and reads anything else as Windows-1252, the code page Western rippers write, re-encoded in place and cut at a whole character to 511 bytes (the fields' buffers hold at least 512, and their lines 500).

`test/patch.py` runs the patched `toolsFixId3ErrorCode` on accented Latin-1 fields and on a Chinese one, and `ringnav_cue_text` on UTF-8, Windows-1252, overlong and surrogate input.

## English

Q2 Pod runs in English with nothing Chinese left in stock's interface:

- **Strings:** `en_US.bin`'s About row `system_prove` reads Certification (stock: 技术证明), with the other `LABELS`.
- **UI assets:** stock's `.bin` layouts carry Chinese `text` in about 40 of them, designers' placeholders that stock's code mostly replaces, but some shows in every language: Search's tabs (单曲, 歌手, 专辑, 风格), Now Playing's info labels, Folder's and Streaming's titles, Quick Settings' rows, the factory test pages. The build finds every asset with Chinese text (`CJK`) and `english_ui` gives each such `text` in English (`ENGLISH`, with per-asset choices in `ENGLISH_IN`), checking the round trip and that nothing Chinese is left. Layouts the build already rewrote (`patch_asset`) are given English after that.
- **Keyboard:** the T9 keyboard's EN key opened its Chinese pinyin page (`page:chinese`); it is a case key now (ABC on the lowercase page, abc on the capitals), so that page is never reached, and its 《》 key types «».
- **Streaming:** stock's Baidu Netdisk row (a Chinese cloud drive, signed in to from Baidu's app) is removed (`ringnav_stream` destroys the row whose image is `list_baidu`).
- **Tags:** [Tag text](#tag-text).

The language picker still lists stock's languages; Q2 Pod's own pages are English in all of them.

## Sorting

Every name-sorted library list (artists, album artists, composers, genres, albums and songs: the `group by … COLLATE NOCASE` queries and `sort_first_alphabet`) is sorted in C, not SQL: `toolsQueryDbTable(…, 1)` and `toolsQueryDbTableAndSort` mode 0 pass `algo_sort_if` one of two comparators, `0x5ba658` or, with the flag at `0xa3bf4d` clear, the pinyin one `0x5b9d40`. The `ORDER BY` queries sort by time, sample rate or id, and folders sort by file name in `toolsLoadDirectory`'s own comparators. Both name comparators `snprintf` the row's `+0x10` name into a local buffer and `bal toolsTrimLeft` on it, falling back to the file name when it is empty. Those four calls (`SORT_TRIMS`) become `jal ringnav_sort_key`, which trims and then drops a leading "The ", "A " or "An " (any case) when a name follows, as Apple does; the record is untouched. "Them", "Anthrax" and a bare "The" keep their place, and equal keys ("The Cure", "Cure") stay as stock's `algo_sort_if` leaves them. Coverflow follows this order. Stock has no A–Z jump index; on a `CTX_LOCAL` page iPod's fast-scroll letter skips the same article.

## Unknown rows

`load_localclass_list` (`0x5088cc`) runs the class's query into `tools_pdeq_directory`, `deque_assign`s it to `*p_deque_showlist`, clears the staging deque and returns `deque_size(*p_deque_showlist)`, which its callers take as the row count (Playlists, `0xf00b`, and Baidu's `0xff26` fill their own deque and return its size). For `0xf003`–`0xf006` the query is `getAllAlbum`, `getAllArtist` (`GetAllAlbumArtist` when `g_artist_type == 1`), `getAllComposer` or `getAllGenre`, and each appends an Unknown row (id -1) whenever the list has any rows (`getAllArtist` `0x4fd4c4`: type 4, the "Unknown Artist" string), even when every song carries the tag. `ringnav_localclass` runs stock, and when that class's last row has id -1 it runs the query the row's press would run (`class_query`, shared with the [Queue menu](#queue-menu): `getMusicBySonger(NULL)` and its kin, `where songer=""`). With no song it `deque_pop_back`s the row and returns one less. Whether a song exists is all it asks, so `ringnav_localclass` sets `library_first` around that call and `library_query` ends the scoped query with `limit 1`: stock puts one record in the staging deque, not every song without the tag, and SQLite stops at that song. ponytail: `songtable` has no index on the tags, so SQLite still scans it up to the first such song, and all of it when every song has the tag. The Queue menu's query on the same row (`qm_query`) still takes every song. The staging deque is cleared again, as stock leaves it. Coverflow builds its own album list and drops an empty Unknown card the same way (`drop_unknown`), but its query has no limit. A list with no rows at all (no card, an empty library): `empty_toast`, from a 0 ms timer so the page that loads it is up, closes that page (`album_page` or `localclass_page` on top with `g_class_type` the list's class) once the window manager has marked it opened (its `stage` property `WINDOW_STAGE_OPENED`, set after the open animation; the close path reads the same at `0x689c68`) with `window_manager_close_window_force`, polling every `EMPTY_POLL_MS` until then or until it stops being on top, then toasts "No albums", "No artists", "No composers" or "No genres" (`msginfo_dialog`, 2 s) over the Library list. Stock opened the black page, and an empty Albums grid's Return then did nothing until a restart, though Hold Return: Home's `back_to_home` left it; where its `window_close` went was not found, so no Return reaches that page now. A page that is no longer on top, or is another, keeps the toast alone.

## Shuffle

`localmusic_page_init` (`0x524424`) destroys `scroll_view_localmusic`'s children and builds 11 rows (`0x5247ec`), each button named by its index; the shared row click (`0x5241fc`) `atoi`s that name into a switch. Stock's rows, by index (`get_localmusic_showinfo`, `0x5012d0`), are Update Local Music, All Songs, Album, Artist, Genre, Hi-Res, My Fav, Frequent, Recent, Recently Added and Playlist. The hook runs stock through `stock_localmusic_trampoline`, appends its own rows in the same widgets and styles (`list_row`, also Display settings'; `library_into` adds stock's `list_into` chevron), then `widget_restack`s every row into one order: Shuffle; Artist, Album, All Songs, Genre, Era (see [Era](#era)), Playlist, Ratings (My Fav hidden after it, see [Ratings](#ratings)); Recently Added, Recent, Most Played, Frequent (hidden: Most Played lists the same from every listen), Hi-Res, Long Songs; the media rows; Upload Scrobbles; Update Local Music last. A page that doesn't hold exactly 11 rows is left as stock built it. The added buttons have no name, so stock's click never sees them. The Shuffle row carries the `local_shuffle` icon, a build-added copy of stock's 52 px `playset_playmode`, which iPod pre-sizes to 40 px for Settings, and opens `shuffle_page`, a runtime page (`page_list` and `page_row_detail`, as Most Played's). Its Shuffle Songs row runs `getAllMusic(3)` (`0x4fce5c`, no title sort) through `staged`, scoped to music as the Library's Artists and Albums are (`MUSIC_WHERE`, see [Podcasts and Audiobooks](#podcasts-and-audiobooks)), saves shuffle with `config_playmode(2, 1)` (`0x4f3fe8`, as the play-mode setting does), clears advanced play (`playback_clear`, so stock Shuffle's keep-the-groups rule below doesn't rebuild group shuffle), and folder-plays the copy from `rand_below(n)` as Coverflow does with `toolsRandnum` (`0x5b9bec`). Shuffle Songs is one-shot: `playback_unshuffle` takes the stock mode it replaces (or the one an earlier Shuffle Songs still holds), and when the next other queue loads, `ringnav_load` applies it with `config_playmode` before stock does, ending the advanced shuffle; a mode choice in between (`ringnav_mode`) is kept instead, and a reboot mid-shuffle keeps Shuffle, as the mode is held in RAM only. Its Shuffle Albums and Shuffle Folders rows run `playback_groups(all, 0|1)`. Picks leave the page open for another, and a note under the rows says albums and folders take a moment to shuffle. A resume after reboot therefore reloads the last track's folder, not the library. An empty library shows "Update Local Music first". **Most Played** uses `local_frequentplay`, the icon of stock's Frequent; see [Play counts](#play-counts). **Upload Scrobbles** shows with an account set up; see [Scrobbling](#scrobbling). **Podcasts**, **Audiobooks**, **Photos**, **Books** and **Videos** show with their folders; see [Podcasts and Audiobooks](#podcasts-and-audiobooks), [Photos](#photos), [Books](#books) and [Videos](#videos).

## Coverflow depth

The shelf and caption values below are the theme's `cfs` (see Themes); both built-ins use these macros. Coverflow draws its covers in software, after Rockbox PictureFlow's renderer: the selected cover faces the viewer at its native 160 pixels, three neighbours a side are turned 60 degrees inward, pushed back and dimmed, and each has a reflection. `patch/coverflow.c` owns the drawing; the stock `slide_menu` stays the navigation and animation controller, so the wheel (one retargeted animator, 200 ms or 120 ms fast, reversal), wrapping, centre, the saved album and track positions all work as before. Touch is the payload's own (below): the `slide_menu` stays insensitive, so stock's drag and throw never run. Tunables are `CF_*` in `patch/offsets.inc`; adjust them together.

**Layout.** With depth the `slide_menu` spans the frame, 375 × `view_h` (`CF_VIEW_W`; `coverflow.shelf.view_h`, 220 by default as `CF_VIEW_H`, `CF_TOP` + 160 + `CF_REFLECT` and a row, 242 under Classic; in both the reflections run under the caption) at the top of the page, so every animator step invalidates all of it. Its items stay `h` square (`view_h`), and `slide_menu_set_spacer` (a store at `+0x84` and an invalidate) sets the spacer to `CF_STRIDE - view_h`, so the stride that stock scroll, completion and ringnav's wheel steps use (item width plus spacer) is `CF_STRIDE` (110 px). The children are `image` widgets with no image, so stock paints nothing under the frame. The album and artist captions sit under the covers at `CF_TEXT_Y`, over the reflections' faint tail, and follow the centre cover: when the album `coverflow_paint` drew in the middle changes, a zero-delay timer (`retitle`, never text set inside the paint) names it, once per change; `EVT_VALUE_CHANGED`, when a move commits, writes them again.

**Position.** `ringnav_paint`, the border hook, calls `coverflow_paint` for every widget after stock has painted it; only Coverflow's `slide_menu` on the covers screen draws. The visual position is the index less the live offset over the stride (stock completion commits `index - offset / stride`): centre album `c` and `frac`, 16.16, in `[-1/2, 1/2)`. Every cover's rotation, place, depth, brightness and reflection come from that one continuous value, so an incoming side cover unfolds into the centre as the outgoing one turns away.

**Projection.** All integer (the stock build is `-mfp64`, and integer arithmetic is also cheaper here). A cover `t` albums from the position gets a rotation that grows linearly to `CF_ANGLE` (60 degrees, a 17-entry sine table) at one album, a centre `x` that reaches `CF_X1` then steps `CF_XS` per album (quadratic up to one album, so the two covers turning mid-step keep clear of each other), a depth that reaches `CF_Z1` then `CF_ZS` per album, and a brightness of 256 at the centre, `CF_BRIGHT1` (75%) one out, `CF_BRIGHT2` (50%) two out, and 0 three out unless `third`, so covers fade in and out at the edge of reach. A theme can turn its covers another way: `coverflow.shelf.tilt` other than 60 reads a second table, 0 to 90 degrees, stepped by the same interpolation. `horizon` puts the shared horizon that percentage down the cover (50 by default), and `third` (`CF_THIRD`, on in every built-in theme) keeps the third cover out at `bright2`, gone by half a step past it, so the ring's reach still ends it without a pop and the thumb's row, which draws it at `bright2`, settles onto it (with `third` 0 it vanished as the covers came to rest). `swing` changes only the path between centre and first slot, for a near edge that would otherwise balloon on a short eye: depth follows the sine of the turn, less `lift` px at most mid-way, and the near edge's screen x is two parabolas meeting flat at 45% of the way, 3 px past its rest place (`cf_rest16`, with the sine at one album `cf_s1`, cached by `cf_restyle`), with `xc` derived from it, so the cover swings on its outer edge with no corner in the path. Every other reader (`unproject`, `coverflow_hit`, the reflection) reads the pose, and at whole albums the pose is the one without it. Left covers mirror right ones about the centre line (between columns 186 and 187). For each screen column the renderer inverts the perspective (eye `CF_EYE` px away) to the cover column under it and the cover's projected height there, then steps down the texture column, top and bottom rows weighted by coverage. At rest the selected cover is its texture pixel for pixel. Below each cover its reflection is the column mirrored, `CF_REFLECT` rows at native size scaled by perspective, from `CF_REFLECT_TOP` of its brightness down to nothing: no blur or glow. Every theme has Classic's, 46 rows from 90/256 (35%).

**Compositing.** The frame is drawn nearest cover first: each pixel's alpha byte holds the coverage so far and every layer fills only what is left, which composites as drawing back to front would, and per column a span of rows known to be opaque is skipped outright, so hidden parts of side covers cost nothing. A last pass makes every pixel opaque over black. The hit test walks the same order, nearest first, over cover bodies only.

**Frame and textures.** One `bitmap_create_ex(375, view_h, 1500, 1)` (`coverflow.shelf.view_h`, 220 by default; RGBA8888; `bitmap_t` is 0x48 bytes, flags `u16 @0xc`, format `u16 @0xe`, buffer `@0x14`) is created with the covers and marked `BITMAP_FLAG_OPAQUE` (bit 0, as `bitmap_init_from_rgba` sets it for opaque images); `bitmap_lock_buffer_for_write`/`bitmap_unlock_buffer` bracket each render and `canvas_draw_image(canvas, bitmap, src, dst)` (`0x635254`, `dst` relative to the canvas origin, which is the `slide_menu`'s) draws it 1:1. It is re-rendered only when the position or a texture changes; a repaint in place only draws. Textures come from the existing JPEG cache: `widget_load_image`, `bitmap_lock_buffer_for_read` and `bitmap_unlock_buffer` read the decoded image (the stock loader, `stb_load_image` called with RGBA8888 for both formats at `0x6810cc`, never premultiplies), fitted whole and centred (a non-square cover between black bars), sampled to 160 × 160 over black into the payload's own buffer, and the load is dropped at once with `widget_unload_image`, as stock cover rows do; the image manager's copy is only read. Seven slots (`CF_RING`) hold the covers within three albums of the position; as movement crosses an album the one leaving is dropped and the one entering is decoded. A small library repeats round the ring as the `slide_menu` wraps, each album decoded once. Missing art and a card (an artist's All albums, the rated albums' Sort) use `default_album_flow`, a 160px copy of Home's disc resized with Lanczos at build time and decoded 1:1. Both are decoded once and left in the manager's cache as theme images. iPod clips each cover column to rounded corners at the Now Playing radius ratio (12/166), including the mirrored bottom in its reflection; the insets are computed once, with no alpha processing in the pixel loop. The frame and the textures (the placeholder and `FX_SLOTS`, 15 under a thumb theme, every built-in's: about 2 MB) are allocated together when the covers are built; if either fails, Coverflow keeps the flat stock covers exactly as before. Closing the page destroys the frame and frees the textures.

**iPod artwork.** Home's 290px disc is the master placeholder for album rows, Now Playing, the full-screen art, the lock screen and AirPlay, pre-sized at build time to each stock asset's dimensions, keeping the disc's transparent corners so a selected row's bar shows through them (Now Playing's to the 166px art it fills; the full-screen `default_bigcover` to its `BC_ART` square, the centred art clear of the glass). The artist-person icon keeps its stock image. Library thumbnails (`file:///mnt/mmc/.sldp/`) get rounded alpha at radius min(width, height) × 12/166 in `ringnav_image_add`, before the image manager caches them. The hook clears the cached opacity flag and keeps straight RGB, so stock can blend the corners over wallpaper or selection panels. Alpha is clamped to arc coverage, making reuse idempotent. Stock's temporary playing covers (`coverpic.jpg`, `externpic.jpg`, `album_tidal.jpg` in `/tmp`) are not: Now Playing, Spotify and the full-screen art round them after scaling, in `paint_cover`; Home's Split art is cropped past its corners.

**Touch.** The covers' view takes `EVT_POINTER_DOWN`, and the page (`open_page`) the move, the release and `EVT_POINTER_ABORT`, so a release off the covers still ends the touch (`touch`); the payload moves `SLIDE_OFFSET` itself and finishes through `carousel_glide` (`patch/navigation.c`), the wheel's one retargeted animator split out of `home_step`, with the run's easing set on the animator (`ANIM_EASING`, `+0x38`: `widget_animator_init` stores `easing_get(type)` there, so a reused animator would keep its first). Every finish commits through stock's completion as a wheel step does. On an album's tracks the view is hidden and made insensitive: stock's hit test (`widget_find_target_default`, `0x669c6c`) checks only the enable and sensitive bits (`+0x24` mask 9), not visibility, so a hidden covers' view over the list would take its taps and swipes.

- **Drag.** A move past `CF_SLOP` (8 px) more sideways than up or down is the covers'; one more up or down leaves the touch to stock (the Quick Settings pull-down). From there the offset follows the finger, one album per `CF_DRAG_PX` (90 px, about the centre cover's own travel on screen), starting where it passed the slop so nothing jumps. An artist's albums give past an end as iOS's rubber band, `x · 0.55 / (1 + x · 0.55 / D)` with `D` two fifths of a stride, so the centre never passes half a cover, where `visual` would wrap to the other end. A touch catching the band (or a wheel bounce) starts from its inverse (`unbound`), so the shelf doesn't jump.
- **Release.** The finger's speed is taken over its last 100 ms of moves, by each event's post time (`EVENT_TIME`; moves queued behind a frame are dispatched together) (0 if it rested `CF_STILL_MS`, 60 ms, before lifting; at most `CF_VMAX`, 6000 px/s, so a burst of queued moves can't throw the shelf). The covers glide to the whole cover that speed reaches in `CF_PROJECT_MS` (400 ms), and a flick of at least `CF_FLICK` (250 px/s) never settles back against its direction. The glide is `cubic_out` (`EASE_CUBIC_OUT`, AWTK's `easing_type_t` 5) for `3 d / v` whenever the goal lies ahead of the finger's motion, so it starts at the finger's speed and decelerates, between `CF_SETTLE_MS` (280 ms, also a still release or one that settles back) and `CF_GLIDE_MAX` (1.4 s); an artist's ends clamp it.
- **Tap.** A touch that never passed the slop hit-tests the drawn frame (`coverflow_hit`): the frontmost cover body under the finger, nearest first as drawn, rounded corners (iPod's `cf_inset`, as `column`) and reflections left out, and no cover under a quarter bright. The centre cover at rest opens, as Centre's click; a side cover glides to the centre with the wheel's easing in `CF_TAP_MS` (320 ms). The flat fallback opens its centre cover only.
- **Catch.** A touch while the covers move pauses the animator where they are. Lifting without a drag settles on the nearest cover and opens nothing; a drag carries on from there. A down while a touch is still open (a release the input queue dropped), or any key with the window manager no longer pressed (`coverflow_lost_touch`, in `ringnav`), ends the old one first, so Centre opens the cover the shelf settles on; a touch ending after Centre changed the screen still settles the covers on a whole one, and covers built anew drop it.

ponytail: no MIPS run of the gesture: `test/coverflow.py` drives the C on the host (`-m32`), and `test/patch.py` checks the MIPS renderer byte for byte. The drag scale, reach and glide lengths are `CF_*` in `patch/offsets.inc`, set by eye.

**Checks.** `test/coverflow.py` builds the renderer on the host and checks the selected cover pixel for pixel, that nothing is drawn outside the frame, mirror symmetry at rest and mid-turn, brightness one and two out, the reflection's rows and fade, that a small step changes little (including where the centre album changes hands), the texture window's loads and releases, the centre click, touch (the hit test against every drawn pixel at eight positions, taps, drags, flicks, a flick dispatched in one batch, a resting release, a vertical move, a catch and a dropped release), one- and two-album libraries, missing art, Refresh, return from tracks and the allocation-failure fallback; `--captures DIR` writes the renderer's frames at rest and a quarter, half and three-quarter turn as PNGs. `test/patch.py` runs the MIPS payload's renderer against the host build byte for byte, and the page's paint, centre click and fallback, and prints the payload's instructions per frame (about 3.5 million for a frame mid-turn under the mocks): a relative measure, not a frame time.

**The flip** (Classic, [ipod.md](ipod.md#cover-flow)). `to_tracks` builds the panel, then `flip_capture` paints it once by stock's `widget_paint` on `canvas_offline_create(375, 320, 3)`, the screen's size and format, translated to the page's origin so the paint hooks' coordinates are the screen's, as `window_manager_default_snap_prev_window` (`0x6878b8`) does for each window animation. The offline lcd's `LCD_OFFLINE_FB` is the bitmap's buffer (`sw $5, 0x110` in its set-buffer helper), checked for 375 × 320, line length 1500 and `LCD_BGRA8888` before it's read; the panel's 294 × 285 at (40, 35) go to `fl.back`, R and B swapped into the frame's RGBA. Stock's `canvas_offline_set_canvas_orientation` only records the 270 here (fast portrait is off), so the buffer isn't turned. The shelf's frame is rendered once without the centre cover (`flip_sides`) and holds the side covers; `fl.frame`, 375 × 294, gets each turn's frame from `coverflow_flip_render`: the sides copied ±24 by the eased time, paper between and under them, then one column of the cover or, past 90 degrees, of the back per screen column, nearest, with row coverage at the slanted ends. The projection is a flat 294 wide face seen from 700 px: size scales x and y, not depth, so the face's edge reaches 147 px towards the eye at any size. The shelf's `eye` (94) would put that edge behind the eye. New RAM is 441 KB for the frame and 335 KB for the back, held from Centre until the way back ends or the page closes; the offline canvas's 480 KB only during a capture. `flip_tick` invalidates the covers' view every 16 ms, `coverflow_turning` holds the idle sleep off, and Centre is dropped at `ringnav`, the page's `keyup` drops Return, and the wheel, with no surface, goes to `hold_wheel` as it does under a window animation. The panel's paint hooks are in `flip_paint`: its first child (`fl.sheet`) paints the paper and the header's fill, and the panel, after its children, the shadow and the border, as `ringnav_paint` runs after a widget's children. The border is flat and opaque (`#B4B9C0` light, `#333333` dark), a pixel outside the panel on every side: the earlier `#00000022` ring took the colour of whatever lay under it, the slanted covers on one side and the floor on the other, and the paper ran a column past the header and rows on the right, so the left and right edges differed.

## Coverflow step

Classic's two Cover Flow steps name `easing="follow"` (`EASE_FOLLOW`, one past AWTK's 24 names), and `tools/theme.py` refuses it on any other kind: `easing_get` has no such id and a window's `anim_hint` no such name. Under it the row's `ms` is a time constant, 81 ms, and the run's length comes from the distance. Other themes, the carousel, Photos and touch's release and tap keep their easings.

**The curve.** A critically damped follow of the goal: a tick only moves the goal, so the speed carries on through it, and nothing overshoots. Over `s` time constants a run `d` px long leaves `(d + (d - v tau) s) e^-s` to go, `v` being the speed it started with. The share is `1 - (1 + a s) e^-s`, with `a = 1 - v tau / d`, floored at 0 for a goal right ahead of a cover too fast to stop for it. `coverflow_follow_aim` takes the speed from the last run at `ANIM_ELAPSED`, only when the animator's easing is still the follow (a touch's glide gives a start from rest), and returns the run's ms, until within half a px of the goal.

**The animator stays.** `carousel_glide` sets `ANIM_EASING` to `coverflow_follow` after `scroll_create`, which gets `linear`, so `ANIM_X_TO`, `EVT_ANIM_END` and `home_done` work as for any step. `widget_animator_time_elapse` (`0x6660d4`) calls the easing with `$25` set, `k` in `$f12` clamped to 1, and passes `$f0` to the update (`+0x3c`, `0x681f30`), which writes `from + (to - from) p` truncated to whole px. That's AWTK's `float_t` as a plain single in this `-mfp64` build, so the easing is a float C function. It returns exactly 1 at `k` 1, so the truncated offset's last move is one whole px at most.

**The pose.** `coverflow.shelf.eye_turn` draws the eye back mid-turn by `eye_turn (1 - t^4)` px, in `pose()`, kept per cover in `pose_t.eye` for `project()`, `unproject()` and so `coverflow_hit`. Depth moves with it, which holds the near edge's place and height and lengthens the far edge. At `t` 0 and 1 no pixel moves, so rest poses are the same bytes.

**Frame gaps (`--dev`).** Each painted frame of a run goes to a ring in RAM, and at the first paint after the run to `/tmp/q2-cf-gaps.log`, if `/tmp/q2-cf-gaps.on` exists (off by default). `python3 tools/q2remote.py sh 'touch /tmp/q2-cf-gaps.on'`, turn the wheel, then `sh 'cat /tmp/q2-cf-gaps.log'`. Each run is a `== run` line, then a row a frame, all in ms: `gap`, since the frame before (a run's first row counts from the last run); `decode`, spent in `fx_ring` loading textures; `render`, spent in `coverflow_render`; then `offset`, the px when it was painted. The last row is the frame after commit. The crossing is where `offset` passes half a stride: a `decode` near that row's `gap`, with `gap` well over its neighbours, says the decode stalls the step. A big `gap` on the last row alone points at the commit.

## Coverflow thumb

Every theme's covers are held by the thumb: `coverflow.shelf.thumb` is 1 and every number below is an attribute of that element, so retuning on the wheel needs no C change. Minimal's `theme.xml` states them and Classic and Modern take them by `extends`, so a spin feels the same in all three; each keeps its own shelf. The carousel and Photos aren't the covers' page, so `coverflow_wheel_poll` never takes the wheel there and it stays on ticks and the animator, as it does on the covers' page whenever the thumb isn't shown (a finger on the screen, the page sliding, Classic's flip panel). A theme with `thumb` 0 is on ticks throughout, letters after `CF_FIRST_MS`.

**The wheel.** `thumb_poll` (`ringnav_sleep`, before `lyric_poll`) calls `coverflow_wheel_poll`. The wheel is raw only with the covers' page on top, not animating, `usable()` and no finger on the screen, and that's decided afresh each pass, so no way off the page can leave `wheel_raw` set. It's `WHEEL_THUMB` (2), not the `WHEEL_RAW` (1) of the lyrics and Vortex, for one reason below; each of the two pollers gives the wheel back only if the value is still its own, so neither clobbers the other's in the pass the wheel changes hands. A still thumb sends no frames, for seconds, so a gap in frames is never a lift: the thumb is `wheel_held`. `contact_ms` debounces it each way: a shorter contact is ignored with its travel, and a shorter lift changes nothing. A press only anchors (`wheel_sample`). Shake of `dead` units moves nothing. If a lift is lost (a known case: a thumb on the ring and a screen tap), `hold_ms` covers it.

**The goal.** A tick is `200 / ticks_turn` units at 50% Wheel Sensitivity, and `th_turn` scales the travel by `wheel_pct()`, so 100% has twice the ticks a turn. Every tick count and rate below (`ticks_album`, `fast_at`, `fast_full`, `jump_ticks`) is in those sensitivity-scaled ticks. `r` is the thumb's rate in ticks a second, filtered `rate_up` ms rising, `rate_down` falling. `speed = slow r + max(0, top_speed - slow r) smoothstep((r - fast_at) / (fast_full - fast_at))`, `slow = 1 / ticks_album`, and the goal moves `min(max(slow, speed / r), top_speed / max(r, r_pass))` a tick, `r_pass` being this pass's own rate, over a frame (`TH_FRAME_MS`, 16 ms) at least, so a pass a millisecond or two after the last doesn't read its ticks as a huge rate and throw them away. Classic's 36, 7, 23 and 90 make a slow album 39 units at 50% (70 degrees) and 19 at 100%, and the speed reaches `top_speed`, 20 albums a second, at 90 ticks a second: 2.5 turns a second at 50%, 1.25 at 100%. The cap holds at every sensitivity: without it, past `top_speed ticks_album` ticks a second the slow share alone would outrun it (31 to 62 albums a second at 100-200%), and while `r` is still rising to a quick spin's, `r_pass` keeps the share from overshooting. Turning back drops the goal to within `back` of the position, and `r` to 0.

**Letters.** With `r` at `jump_at` (32 ticks a second, so a steady turn a second at 50% clears it, the run ticks' `WHEEL_RUN_MS` asked for) for `jump_ms` (700, the 0.7 s the wheel's ticks had before 1.6.9: at 400 and then 500 a brisk turn through a few albums set letters off. From a standing start that is 0.95 s at a turn a second and nearer 0.75 for a hard spin, as `r` takes its `rate_up` to get there), the spin goes by letters. The count stops at `jump_ms` and any pass under `jump_at` takes its ms off it, so a dip costs only its own time and speeding back up refills only that. A letter has no motion of its own: travel moves the goal and the covers follow exactly as on any fast turn, so the speed, the lead and the row's shape all go on. Each `jump_ticks` of travel (5: one tick, about 28 units at 50%), the first at once, none sooner than `jump_pace` (140 ms) after the last and only while the covers move over 4 albums a second (`th_moving`), `th_letter` asks `coverflow_jump` for the first album of the letter after the last one named, `jump_to` (the previous letter's first, turning back), and `th_name` moves `SLIDE_INDEX` so that album is the one named. It goes from `jump_to` and not from where the flow has got to: the covers travel about three albums between letters at `top_speed`, which would pass over a letter of fewer albums, and turning back, where the flow leaves a letter's first album at once, every other letter. Such a short letter steps the pictures back by that much instead, on a row still moving. Only the pictures change, and never on a still row. Past the last letter they go round to the first (`coverflow_round`), as the covers do, or turning back from the first to the last letter's first album; with one letter in the library, the flow goes round by itself. `jump_pace` is the beat of a turn a second, so spinning harder doesn't race the alphabet. `coverflow_letter` (`navigation.c`, `home_step`'s too) shows the big letter. Only where `coverflow_jump` finds letters (`lettered`: the whole library sorted by album or artist); elsewhere the cap alone. Once in, letters stay while the thumb turns on the same way at `fast_at` (23 ticks a second) or more, and a lifted thumb leaves `r` alone, so a lift for as long as a letter shows (`LETTER_MS`, 400) goes on from there with no second dwell. Only a pass that turned starts letters or moves them on. A thumb turning under `fast_at` or that long with no tick ends them, and since the covers never stopped, the album named simply carries on under the thumb; turning back (`th_turn`) ends them too, dropping the goal as on any fast turn. A lift or a stop from speed with the covers still moving lands on the letter: as the goal goes to the album named, `th_name` makes that `jump_to`, so the row runs down onto the letter's first album. With the covers already at rest it lands where they are. Nothing commits while letters last. `jump_ticks` 0 turns letters off. Short arcs with a lift each don't reach `jump_ms`, as they didn't on ticks: `r` falls in the lift and has to rise again, so the count drains about as fast as it fills.

**The position** follows the goal, critically damped and exact over the real elapsed ms of the pass, so a slow frame costs no accuracy and speed stays continuous. Its time constant is `max(base, min(tau, rest))`: `base` is `track` blending to `spin` with that smoothstep (`snap` with no thumb), `rest` is `snap` to `spin` by `smoothstep(left / far_left)`. Only the thumb raises it, which removes a snap: lag raising it would add lag, and the row would jump out at one speed. A detent of `detent (1 - smoothstep)` draws the target towards albums, thumb down only.

**Settling.** A lift, the hold's timeout, or no tick for `stop_ms` with more than `stop_left` left sends the goal to the caption's album, so a stop from speed lands on what's named, with speed only falling. Otherwise a slow cover stays part-way while it's held. `th_commit` ends it through `carousel_glide`'s immediate move, so `EVT_VALUE_CHANGED` and `changed()` run as for any step. The position and goal are albums from `SLIDE_INDEX`, so once the position is `TH_REBASE` (64) albums out, `th_rebase` moves `SLIDE_INDEX` by the whole albums (`slide_menu_set_value`) and takes them off the position, goal and click count: nothing moves, and a float keeps the 1e-4 its settling needs however long a spin goes (past about 1024 albums it didn't, and the covers settled a step short and stayed held). The pass names the album in the caption and keeps it as the one to reopen on whenever the caption changes, so `closed()`, which can come while the page is being destroyed, only lets go.

**The row.** `lead` is `l smoothstep((|l| - lead_from) / (lead_full - lead_from))`, `l = goal - pos`, through a `lead_ms` follow. `draw_row` measures each cover's `t` from `pos + lead`, on that side, and slides everything `lead row_shift` px. `b` is `smoothstep` from `row_from` to `row_full`: `row_shrink`% smaller, no receding past `row_flat` from the focus (`row_out` px an album instead), and `e = b smoothstep(t / row_edge)` narrows a cover by `row_width e` and flattens its keystone by `row_key e`. At lead 0 none of this runs and `coverflow_render` takes its own path, byte for byte. A cover's columns take the art through `cf_texel`, as `draw_cover`'s do, so the two paths meet with no jump: the near half `r` times as wide as the far half, `r` going from 1 (even) at `coverflow.shelf.art_persp` 0 to the plane's own near over far height at 100. Minimal and Modern are at 100, `unproject`'s own texels to the byte; Classic is at 13, cover 1's near half 1.23 times its far half against the plane's 2.76.

**Textures.** The row shows about 11 covers, so a thumb theme decodes `CF_WIDE`, 15, around the facing cover (`fx_reach`): 1.6 MB, against 0.8. The window is centred on the album `round(lead)` on from the position (`coverflow_paint`, `draw_row`), so at any lead the covers the row draws are the ones decoded. A decode takes 6 to 8 ms on the UI thread beside a render of 6 to 9 ms, so `fx_window` decodes nearest the centre first and, while the thumb holds the covers, at most `FX_DECODES` (2) a frame; at rest the seven the shelf shows decode at once, as before, and only the outer eight wait. A cover not yet decoded shows the placeholder, and `fx.todo` has the thumb's pass ask for frames until none is left: after a letter the facing cover is there in the first frame and the rest within eight. ponytail: decodes stay on the UI thread, two a frame; a decoder thread if a re-centring's fill-in shows.

**What ticks gave.** Each is rebuilt, and `test/patch.py` holds a case for it:

- `KEY_LOCKOUT`: its first `LOCKOUT_KEEP` tenths drop travel, as `ringnav` keeps it for ticks.
- `tick_gate`: no frame in `WHEEL_FRESH_MS` drops travel read after a stall.
- Two Returns: `wheel_sample` counts `WHEEL_THUMB`'s travel into `wh_since` a tick's worth at a time, so the second Return stops it, and the poll honours `wheel_mute`. The lyrics and Vortex stay out of it, as before.
- `chord_gate` and Volume Guard work as they did: the first is on key events, and dark isn't `usable()`, so the wheel is ticks and the guard's.
- `hold_wheel`: a turn begun during the page's slide is ticks, raw being off. They play in as a step, and the next travel takes that animator over, goal kept (`th_grab`). A tick still queued as the wheel goes raw is dropped.
- Letter jumps: after `jump_ms` at `jump_at`, not `CF_FIRST_MS` of any turn, so a slower spin never jumps and lands where the caption says ([Letters](#coverflow-thumb)).
- The end bounce: an artist's albums clamp the goal, and the first push past an end in a contact kicks the covers out `1 / CF_BOUNCE` of a stride.
- The click: once an album of the goal (`buzzeer_switch`). A turn also resets the screen's timeout and the idle clock.

Wheel Sensitivity scales the travel (`wheel_pct`), as it does other lists' ticks. Centre lands at once on the album named, then opens it. A touch during a spin settles first: its press does nothing, and the covers run down to the album named (`coverflow_hit` only ever runs at rest).

The `--dev` frame gaps ([above](#coverflow-step)) go on working: `th.on` is a run, and its frames go to the log at the first paint after it.

## Slanted art

Classic's Now Playing is an iPod classic's: the art turned in perspective with its reflection under it, and a glossy progress bar with the times beside it. The numbers are fitted to Apple's own 320 x 240 screen capture of a 7th-generation Now Playing: its far (right) edge is 118 of the near edge's 135 px (`SLANT_FAR` 224/256), the width the near edge's height, centred; texture across follows 1 / height (a true perspective, which matched the flat cover better than a linear stretch); the reflection starts at a third (`SLANT_REFLECT_A` 84/256) and fades out over 22% of each column's height (`SLANT_REFLECT_PCT`).

- **Layout.** The `classic-light` sets' `np_ipod` makes the art `NP_IPOD_ART` (178 px) and moves it up into the top row beside More, its top on the first row where its square top-left corner clears the glass by `CORNER_SLACK`, as the full-screen art's corners do (screen y 38), then `NP_IPOD_ART_DROP` (10) lower (screen y 48): the slide_view_view starts `NP_IPOD_LIFT` (30) px higher, and the lyrics (`view_lrc`, "No lyrics"), the track's details and the dots move down it as far, so they stay where the other themes have them on screen; `visualizer_attach` starts its page at `view_lrc`'s y for the same reason. "3 of 12" (`label_ipod_pos`) leaves the top row for the art's page, under the album, and the title starts under More. More (`img_more`) is restacked as the window's last child, since the pages now reach into its row and AWTK hands a touch to the topmost child under it. `img_cover` is `NP_REFLECT` (40) px taller than wide, room for the reflection inside the widget, so the slider's repaint every second, whose rect reaches into it, repaints the reflection too; the slide_view grows with it (230 px) and the page dots sit over the reflection as it fades, which ends at the bar. The frame is opaque and now reaches the times' row, so the two times are restacked after the slide_view_view, over it. `paint_cover` treats a cover box taller than wide as Classic's.
- **Rendering.** `coverflow_slant`, from `img_cover`'s border paint, after stock's own scale_auto draw: the cover stock loaded (`widget_load_image`, kept loaded as stock keeps it) is box filtered (`box_filter`, shared with Home's pan) to a texture twice the art's side, fitted whole on the paper, then `slant_render` draws each pixel from two half columns of two bilinear taps, the slanted edges by their coverage, the cover, reflection and paper weighted by their shares of the pixel. The frame (178 x 218, 155 KB) is redrawn only when its key changes: the image's url, the cover's fingerprint (its size and 8 x 8 of its pixels) and the paper. The url in it lets the one frame serve local Now Playing and Classic's Spotify and Internet Radio pages (`stream_ipod`; `paint_cover` slants `st.stream_art` as it does `st.np_cover`): local, Spotify, local each redraws it once, from whatever the page's widget shows at that paint, so no path is kept. It is also redrawn when `np_art_poll` loads a new cover from the same file (the player writes every track's there, so it closes the frame). It is drawn 1:1 on every paint; the texture (627 KB) is freed at once. The page's close frees the frame. `test/coverflow.py` checks the renderer's edges, orientation and fade on the host.
- **Progress bar.** `paint_scrubber` paints over stock's plain bar (the theme's `np_bar`, `NP_BAR_IPOD` 20 px, as `bar_size`): `playing.scrubber.stops`' track rows (one list per half, 13 stops over the 20 rows: faint 1 px lines round a near-white inside, the dark half Apple's capture darkened; `stops_row` mixes the two stops round each row, so the inner stops run from 5% to 95% and no row but the first and last takes an edge's colour), the accent's selection gloss (`scheme()->sel`) over the played part, or under the theme's own scheme (Classic Blue) `playing.scrubber.fill` (`np_fill`, the bar's rows in Apple's native 320x240 capture in the iPod classic User Guide), square ends with a 1 px column at each in the track's last stop's colour, over the fill at the left whatever it reaches, and the bar mirrored under it from `SCRUB_REFLECT_A` (38/256) over `SCRUB_REFLECT_ROWS` (6). A scrub (`st.scrub`) does not turn Classic's fill white (it is painted over); `ipod_bar`'s `mark` draws its cue instead, #204's: a diamond in the half's ink (`scheme()->ink`), `NP_SCRUB_MARK` (8) each side of its middle row and column, so its 17 rows sit on the gloss inside the 1 px edge lines, one row in at the top and two at the foot, centred on the fill's end and held inside the bar at either end. The volume's bar over the page has none. The times are 21 px in Classic's face (`NP_IPOD_TIME_PX`, the largest whose line fits its `NP_IPOD_TIME_H` (21) box, half a row above centre on the 20 rows of the bar), `NP_IPOD_TIME_GAP` (8) either side of the bar, in boxes sized to the track's length. The build lays out the under-10:00 class's, `NP_IPOD_TIME_W1` (35, "-9:59"), so the bar opens at its widest and only a long track narrows it; their outer edges clear the glass over their own rows (`corner_x`, 23 px at Classic's 26 px bar), and the bar takes the rest: 66 to 309, 243 px. At run time `ipod_times` widens both boxes to the track's class: under an hour `NP_IPOD_TIME_W2` (43, "-59:59"), else `NP_IPOD_TIME_W` (56, "-9:59:59", "-1:02:03" fitting with no smaller size for the hours: 55.4 px, stb scales a font's ascent to descent to its size). The outer edges and the gap stay, so the bar is 66 to 309 (243 px), 74 to 301 (227 px) or 87 to 288 (201 px), still centred. `tools/ipod.py` `time_widths` measures each class's longest remaining time in `status_metrics`' model with the face's widest digit (its digits are tabular, 556 units), and the build fails if `offsets.inc` disagrees; the elapsed time, one character shorter, never needs more. The class comes from the length, never the ticking value, so the layout holds for the whole track: `np_sync`, every paint, compares the slider's x with `ipod_bar_x` of its `max` (stock's length, seconds) and arms `np_fit_due`, which moves the two labels and the slider outside the paint, invalidating each where it was and where it goes (a paint drops its invalidates, #116); `ringnav_playing` fits the page before its first paint when the length is known; a track just picked reads 0 there (stock's status poll, `mclSyncPlayerStatus`, has not fetched it yet), so the page opens on the build's bar and narrows within the first ticks only if the track is 10:00 or longer. A seek, pause or rate change keeps `max`, so the class. A `max` of 0 (stopped, or a length stock doesn't know) leaves the layout as it is: the build's, or the last track's, whose boxes hold the 0:00 stock then shows. Everything else reads the widgets: `paint_scrubber` and the scrub's diamond clamp to the slider's width, the reflection is drawn inside it, `np_beside` and `remain_due` go by the labels, and `vol_paint` papers the labels' boxes and draws the volume across the slider's width with a speaker at each box's inner edge. Stock places the A-B markers with the boot set's `np_bar_x` and `np_bar_w` immediates (`0x52a268`, from the placement `0x52a334`); its three calls are `jal ringnav_ab_x` (`AB_X_CALLS`), which returns the same share of the fitted bar. Spotify fits on each refresh from its track's `duration`, the first as the page opens; Internet Radio's running time has no end, so it takes the "-59:59" boxes, from the refresh that builds the page, until it has played an hour, then the hours': the time only grows, so they never shrink while a stream plays. AWTK centres the font's line (ascent to descent) in the box, which puts this face's digits within a pixel of the bar's middle. `np_sync` writes the remaining time mid-paint, where its invalidate is lost with the frame's dirty rects (#116); Minimal's and Modern's sit inside the slider's and elapsed time's repaint each second, Classic's beside the bar does not, so `remain_due` repaints it once after the frame.

ponytail: stock still draws its flat cover under the frame on each paint, as it always did. Under the volume overlay its band, from the slider's top, cuts the reflection's faint end (about 16%) with a straight edge; start the band lower if that shows. A 16-bit decode, or no memory for the frame or texture, leaves stock's flat cover, scale_auto in the taller box.

## Position memory

Position memory is a bounded 64-entry table keyed by the audited top-window context in `patch/contexts.inc` and a content fingerprint. Selecting a row updates its entry and moves it to the front; a full table evicts the least recently selected entry. Restoration alone does not promote an entry.

Local folders use the full bounded `g_folder_path`; local music lists use the stock browsing class, saved query and artist/album modes (`g_class_type`, `g_local_classinfo_save`, `g_artist_type`, `album_modetype`). Root Albums, Artists, Composers and Genres lists ignore the saved detail query: opening an artist overwrites it without changing the parent collection. `playlist_page` keys on none of these: opening a playlist writes the browsing state, so `context_now` scopes it by the page's add mode alone (`PL_MODE` + 1), and back from a playlist keeps its row while the Add to Playlist picker keeps its own. These are the inputs used by the stock folder and local-list loaders, not playback metadata. The builder verifies each object's size against the stock ELF. A changed fingerprint resets selection even if a surviving surface has the same row count, then restores the matching recent position and reveals it, or keeps the stock viewport if none is remembered. Fixed settings menus can restore by window name alone; multi-pane and other dynamic pages without an audited content identity keep their live widget selection but do not restore across recreation.

Memory lasts until power-off, with no filesystem writes. The table occupies 1,280 bytes. Ordinary paints do not search it; repeated selections in the current scope match its first entry and shift nothing. Lookup and promotion are bounded by `POS_MEM` (64).

Non-virtual lists also remember hashes of the selected row's first two text values, read through the stock `widget_get_text` UTF-32 accessor. A matching second text outranks proximity; equal matches choose the occurrence nearest the remembered index. Rows without text fall back to the index within the same content scope. The stock rows expose no stable item id. Both scroll views and virtual music tables protect an interrupted recall glide from silently replacing the remembered selection with a currently visible row. A real tap cancels the glide and selects its target; a wheel step advances from the remembered logical row. Pointer-down and synchronous click delivery load rows without recall, so native touch sees the widget it pressed and a rebuilt list cannot move or rebind that row first. Synchronous table rebinds discard the old row-pool snapshot before resolving the selected entry.

## Touch mode

A session-wide touch-mode flag suppresses only custom drawing, independently of logical selection and gesture state. Touch-down and native clicks (including clicks without touch-down) set it and invalidate the visible window. It persists through settling and page changes. Accepted wheel input, including a turn at a boundary, centre selection or an accepted Return release restores drawing, so the page Return goes back to shows its row; iPod's Home list ignores the flag and always draws its bar; synchronous centre-generated clicks bypass the native-click observer.

## Drawing

The canvas hook intersects the existing clip with the viewport and restores the clip, the LCD fill color and the LCD stroke color; this firmware's `canvas_save/restore` do not save those properties. The outline is one translucent fill (`0x402b2b2b`, a per-color alpha) plus two concentric one-pixel `canvas_stroke_rounded_rect` calls, a dark separator at radius 9 and the 70%-opaque white line at radius 8, with `bg_r = NULL`. The separator is the stock dark surface at `0xb32b2b2b`, so it disappears on the dark theme and only shows over bright artwork. It never calls `canvas_set_global_alpha`, so the shared alpha is untouched. A rounded call that reports failure (either stroke) or a row too small for the corner radius, keeps the square separator-plus-white outline instead.

iPod draws a selection bar instead of the outline. `widget_paint` (`0x65f28c`) calls `widget_on_paint_background`, then the widget's own paint, its children and `widget_on_paint_border`, so the background hook paints the bar behind the rows.
