The border hook then draws nothing, and the selection is loaded and settled once per frame, in the background hook. The bar spans the full surface width at the selected row's height and position, including the end bump; an entry narrower than half the surface, a grid tile, gets the bar in its own rectangle. The theme's selection painter draws it (`painter[PAINT_SELECTION]`, [Themes](#themes)). Minimal's `SEL_CARD` is a card (`fill_box`): `#EEEEEC` under Ivory, whose selected title and metadata colors are resolved from paint ancestry by `ringnav_style_color`, without mutating recycled widget styles, else the accent's selection tone under the rows' own ink; Display's Corners (`st.rounded`) picks the inset pill and rounded tiles, or the full-width bar and square tiles; Home uses a white dot instead. Classic's `SEL_STOPS` is its scheme's gloss band in final colours (`paint_stops`, one-pixel `canvas_fill_rect` (`0x634ad0`) bands, which avoid the stock `gradient_t` ABI), square, on list rows, Home's rows and a pop-up's buttons, under white ink ([ipod.md](ipod.md#theme)); a grid tile gets the inset card, square, in the accent's selection tone. Both themes inset a grid tile's cover, so the card frames it. The bar uses the same clip, color restore and touch-mode rules as the outline. Rows only show it where they are transparent: after the iPod style edits, list rows, table rows, grid tiles (`s_btn_listblack`) and the settings rows (`s_btn_listitem`) are.

The iPod border hook draws the chevrons. Windows flagged `DRILL` in `patch/contexts.inc` get the stock `list_into` image on every row, over the bar as well: `widget_load_image` (`0x65c1ec`, served from the image manager's cache) once per paint, then `canvas_draw_icon` (`0x638c9c`: canvas, bitmap, centre x, centre y, relative to the canvas origin like the fills) per row, placed as stock rows place their `img_into`: left edge `CHEVRON_W` (58) from the row's right edge, inside the same viewport clip, which hides rows outside it. A playlist button spans the same 20 to 355 pixels as a category or album button, so the glyphs cover the same screen columns (317 to 327); Local Music's own images sit 5 pixels further right at a fixed x. On a drill window the row layouter lays out as if that image were the last child, so the title and a playlist's "more" button end before it. The widget's own window, found through its parents, decides, so a window painted during a transition keeps its own rows. Rows narrower than half the surface (tiles) get none, and none are drawn while `g_navbar_status` is set, as stock hides its own chevrons in multi-select.

The iPod border hook also draws the fast-scroll letter over the list. A step of more than one row (so the list has more than `SHORT_LIST_MAX` rows and the run has reached `LIST_FIRST_MS`) restarts a one-shot `LETTER_MS` (400 ms) timer. While it runs and the spin run is still past `LIST_FIRST_MS`, the surface draws the selected row's first non-space character, past an article on a library page ([Sorting](#sorting)). `paint_letter` draws none on three kinds of list that are not in name order: a `CTX_FIXED` menu, Library's categories beside Home's pane (`scroll_view_localmusic`) and an album's tracks in Cover Flow (`coverflow_tracks` of the widget's own window), which `in_order` puts in disc and track order. An album's songs in the Library never arm the timer: stock shows them only in `albuminfo_page` (its init, `0x4a79f8`, is `load_album_detaillist`'s one caller: classes `0xff10`, `0xff11`, `0xff12` and `0xff14`) and `playerjumpinfo_page` (Album info and Go to Album, `0xff10`), and `getMusicByAlbum` and its kin sort them by disc, then track (comparator `0x4fb880`). The wheel handler tests the top window's name where it arms the letter, not at paint, where the top may already be the page a Return brings back; a class would not do, since `g_class_type` keeps its last value on pages that do not set it, and an artist's page reached with Go to Artist from an album keeps classinfo `+0` at `0xff11` after one of its albums is opened and closed (its return handler `0x4ae6ec` reloads nothing when the class matches the one it opened with). Not covered: other lists in an order of their own still show it, such as View Queue while a shuffle is on (`upnext_page`), `chapters_page` and Recently Added. The text is the row's first non-empty `widget_get_text`, taken from the same `row_id` walk as the position-memory hashes, and a virtual table finds its logical row in the recycled pool; an offscreen or textless row draws nothing. The box is `LETTER_BOX` (80) square, `canvas_fill_rounded_rect` at radius `LETTER_RADIUS` (12) in the stock dark surface at `LETTER_ALPHA` (85%), or a square fill without a vgcanvas. The glyph is `LETTER_PX` (60) in the system default font (`canvas_set_font` `0x633e6c` with a null name), white (`canvas_set_text_color` `0x633d9c`), centred by `canvas_draw_text_in_rect` (`0x639524`) with the canvas alignment fields (`+0x34` horizontal, `+0x30` vertical) set to centre and middle. The clip, fill and text colors and alignment are restored afterwards; the font is not, since stock sets it before drawing its own text. The expiry repaints the live surface, which then draws nothing.

## Accent

iPod's accent ([ipod.md](ipod.md#display-settings); each theme keeps its own; Minimal's own accent uses Graphite's) replaces Shanling red wherever it is drawn:
the theme's style colors, inline `style:*` colors (a mutable style answers through the same
vtable), decoded images, and the payload's own selection bar and progress fill, which read
the active scheme's tones (`tones()`, [Themes](#themes)). `accent_map` (`patch/theme_engine.c`) is
a pure function on one `color_t` (bytes r, g, b, a). Stock uses `#FF1448`, `#7F0A24`, the
pressed tint `#3D1920` and `#FF144840`, all stock red blended with a neutral: each channel is
`t * red + k`. Least squares against red with
the mean removed gives `t` (in 1/4096), then `k`. A color within `RED_TOLERANCE` (8) of that
blend, with `t` between 1/16 and 17/16 and `k` not below the tolerance, becomes `t * tone + k`
for one of the preset's tones (`ACCENTS`): the red tone for text colors (a property name ending
`text_color`, `tk_str_end_with`) and image pixels, the light tone for every other color property
and gradient stop; alpha is kept, and anything else, greys and other hues included,
is returned as it was. Anti-aliased edges onto black, white or a transparent background are such
blends, so they follow. No preset's colors are red blends, so mapping twice changes nothing.
Crimson returns every color unchanged, so its theme is stock.

- `style_get_color(color_t *ret, style, name, default)` returns its color through the hidden
  first argument. The hook runs stock through a trampoline and maps `*ret`, comparing the
  property name only when the color maps. Stock first asks `style_get_gradient` for every color
  (`bal` at `0x649fe8`; `style_data_get_gradient` turns a plain color into a one-stop gradient),
  so the gradient hook leaves the call that returns to `0x649ff0` unmapped, and the color hook
  alone picks the tone. The builder pins that `bal`.
- `style_get_gradient(style, name, gradient_t *out)` (`0x649f3c`) is a 12-instruction leaf with
  no GOT setup: null checks on the style and its vtable, then a tail call to the vtable's
  `get_gradient` (+0x18). The builder pins its first three words (`0x10800009, 0, 0x8c820000`)
  and replaces the first two with the jump; the payload repeats the checks, calls `get_gradient`
  through `$t9`, and maps the stops only when it filled the caller's `out`: `gradient_t` is 0x4c
  bytes (`gradient_init`), with the stop count at +8 and up to 8 `{color, offset}` stops from
  +0xc (`gradient_add_stop`, `gradient_get_first_color`). Widget backgrounds come this way.
- `image_manager_add(manager, name, bitmap_t *)` copies a freshly decoded bitmap into the cache.
  The hook maps its pixels first, only for plain theme image names (no `/` or `:`, so covers by
  path, `file://` or online URL are never touched), and only when the format
  is one of the 32-bit ones: `bitmap_t` has w at 0, h at 4, the format as a u16 at 0xe
  (1 RGBA, 2 ABGR, 3 BGRA, 4 ARGB in memory order, 4 bytes each by `bitmap_get_bpp_of_format`),
  its row stride from `bitmap_get_line_length`, and the pixels from
  `bitmap_lock_buffer_for_write`/`bitmap_unlock_buffer`. Premultiplied pixels are red blended
  with black, so they map the same way.

The confirm pop-up's discs (`confirm_ok`, `confirm_cancel` and their pressed images, names
starting `CONFIRM_IMAGE`) go through the same mapping with `CONFIRM_SURFACE` (`#2B2B2B`) as the
tone under every preset, Crimson included ([ipod.md](ipod.md#pop-ups)). The quick settings'
control discs (names starting `DROPDOWN_IMAGE`, `drop_`, except the brightness suns, `DROPDOWN_SUN`)
take the preset's red tone; when that tone is brighter than `GLYPH_LIGHT_MAX` (Graphite's silver),
an image that holds red also maps its neutral glyph pixels to `CONFIRM_SURFACE`, so the white glyph
turns dark ([quick settings](ipod.md#quick-settings)). Grey discs hold no red and are unchanged.
Any other image that holds red and an opaque pure-white pixel (a switch's knob, a disc's glyph; `#7F7F7F`
on a pressed disc) takes
the light tone instead of the red one, as do button backgrounds (names starting `BUTTON_IMAGE`,
`btn_`), whose white label is a widget's text; an image with red alone (a tick, the time page's
colon) keeps the red tone. Only Graphite's two tones differ, so only it scans for white.
The settings rows' category icons (`settings_icons` in `patch/ipod.json`, which the builder
writes to `SETTINGS_ICON_NAMES` in `stock.h`) are never mapped: several use stock red as their
category colour (Network, DLNA, Wi-Fi, Backlight), like the purple and orange ones.
`red_map` is the mapping with an explicit tone;
`accent_map` is it with the preset's tone, and the identity for Crimson.

Changing the accent saves it, sets the progress fill, calls `image_manager_unload_all(image_manager())`
(`0x645024`) and `widget_invalidate_force` on the window manager. Widgets ask for their style
colors, gradients and images on every paint, so the next frame decodes the images again through
the hook and shows every recoloured part; nothing needs a restart.

## Themes

Minimal (`THEME` 0), Classic (1) and Modern (2) are rows of `themes[]` (`patch/navigation.c`; the types are in
`patch/theme.h`); a stored `THEME` past the last row reads as 0 (`config_value`). Code asks the active theme for a field, never which theme it is. `theme()` and
`scheme()` read the settings first (`accent()`), then return `&themes[st.theme]` and its scheme for
the stored accent.

- `theme_t` holds the painters (`painter[PAINT_SELECTION]`: Minimal's `SEL_CARD`, Classic's `SEL_STOPS` or Modern's `SEL_BAR`), the
  surface flags (capitals, chevrons, monochrome icons, codec badges in the half's ink
  (`icons_badge_ink`), Classic Light's own art left final (`art_final`, which `tools/theme.py` refuses for another theme), bold labels, Now Playing
  opacities), the Home
  and settings geometry, the Now Playing bar heights, `art_square` (art takes Corners: Square,
  `ipod_square_art`), the accent's slot and config key
  (`accent_slot`/`accent_key`: Minimal's `MINIMAL_ACCENT`, Classic's `CLASSIC_LIGHT_ACCENT`, Modern's `ACCENT`), its schemes and its options.
- `scheme_t`: one per `ACCENTS` row and theme's own accent, indexed by the stored value; its tones are that row, read
  through `tones()` or `tones_of(v)`. `SCHEME_CARD` (Minimal's Ivory) draws the selection as a card
  in `fill`; `SCHEME_RAW_RED` (Crimson) keeps stock red. The roles `fill`, `chevron`, `muted` and
  `bar_light` are `0xRRGGBB`, 0 meaning unused, and `fill` is read only under `SCHEME_CARD`.
- `option_t`: the Appearance rows after Theme, in order. At the first read, a stored value outside
  `values` becomes `def`. Choosing a theme writes each option's `preset` unless the stored value is
  its `keep` (Light's Auto), and only values that change. The accent row cycles within `values`.

The table is generated. Each built-in theme is `assets/themes/<id>/theme.xml`. The format is a strict
XML subset: elements with double-quoted attributes, self-closing tags and comments, only whitespace
between tags, the entities `&amp; &lt; &gt; &quot;`, UTF-8, at most 32 KB. `tools/theme.py` fills
each theme in this order: the schema defaults, its `extends` base, its own elements, then
`ROLE_MACROS`, which takes the per-theme numbers (Home rows, the settings top, the progress bar, the
scheme colours) from `patch/offsets.inc`, so a theme file never names a macro. Any unknown name or
value stops the build. The tool writes `themes.h` (the enums and macros, which navigation.h includes) and `themes.inc`
(the tables, which theme_engine.c includes after the accent tables, where the hand-written table
stood) into the build directory, and `THEME_N` into `stock.h`, because `setting_counts[]` uses it. `edit_paint` stays Minimal's card in every theme.

**Now Playing layout.** `theme_t.np_layout` (`<view name="playing" layout="ipod|centred">`, ipod in
every built-in) picks the page `ringnav_ui_load` loads for `playing_page`: `np_pages[np_layout()]`,
where Centred Now Playing (`NPCENTRED`) forces `NP_CENTRED` (`playing_page_stock`).
`ringnav_playing` binds the lyric tap on the centred page only.

**Cover Flow.** `theme_t.cf` (`cf_style_t`: `coverflow.shelf` top, x1, xs, z1, zs, eye, bright1,
bright2, reflect, `reflect_top`, stride, radius, tilt, horizon, third, swing, lift, `view_h`; `coverflow.caption` `name_px`, `artist_px`, `text_y`) is
copied to coverflow.c's `cfs` by `cf_restyle` at every page open, so the stride is in place before
`slide_menu_set_spacer`; a radius change refills `cf_inset`, and a render before any page restyles
once. Built-ins take every value from offsets.inc's `CF_*` and `NP_ART_RADIUS` (`ROLE_MACROS`).
tools/theme.py checks each range, top + 160 + reflect ≤ `view_h` ≤ the client height, eye + z1 > 80 sin(tilt) (a first side cover's near edge in front of the eye), with swing eye - max(0, 80 - z1 / sin(tilt)) - lift / 4 > 0 (and all through a step), bright2 ≤ bright1, and
`text_y` + `CF_NAME_H` + `CF_ARTIST_H` ≤ the client height. A caption may overlap a taller frame: its labels draw
over it. `CF_EDGE`, `CF_VIEW_W`, `CF_CX` and `ART_SIZE` stay fixed. The frame's paper is the scheme's, as is a
placeholder's corners', so a theme's own white paper meets the page with no band.

**Bar and client height.** Each geometry set's bar height is in `geom_vals` (`themes.inc`, one
row per set, the `V_*` columns), 30 for SET0 and 26 for Classic's sets. `client_h()` (navigation.c)
is `320` less the bar of the set `theme_boot` wrote (`st.geom`), the window's height under the
bar. Cover Flow's views, Home's art panel, its pan frame, render loop and clip read it, and the
battery slot (`bar_sync`) takes the same bar. Before a set is written, after a failed `theme_boot`
and while a switch waits for its restart, both stay SET0's. The page fallbacks
(`widget_get_prop_int(page, "h", 290)`) stay sized for 290 and `DRIFT_TEXELS` for 294. On the
build side `Geometry` and `with_geometry(g)` (`tools/ipod.py`) drive `ipod_home`, `status_bar`,
`playing_page` and `patch_asset`; `patch_code` bakes `SET0`.

**Geometry sets.** `tools/theme.py` also emits the boot tables into `themes.inc`: `boot_values`
(one row of `ipod.json` values per set, SET0 first), `boot_sites` (each stock immediate's address
and value name), `geom_names` and `ui_redirects` (the windows a set has its own copy of).
`theme_t.geom` picks the row. `theme_boot` runs once a boot, at the first `system_bar` load in
`ringnav_ui_load`, after `accent()` has read the settings. It finds each `THEME_PAGE` (4 KiB, the
Q2's kernel page) page that holds a site whose low halfword differs from the set's value and
mprotects (4125) every one RWX, lowest first. Only if all of them succeed does it write the
halfwords, then per page call cacheflush (4147, BCACHE) and mprotect it back to R-X, and record the
set in `st.geom`. If one mprotect fails, it mprotects the pages it already opened back to R-X and
writes nothing: the code stays SET0's, `st.geom` stays 0 and `st.theme` becomes 0, so the boot
runs as Minimal, `THEME` untouched, until the next. SET0, Minimal's set, matches every baked
immediate, so it makes no syscall and writes nothing. Everything that follows the set (`geom()`, `geom_val()`, `client_h()`, `bar_sync` and
the redirects) reads `st.geom`, not `theme_t.geom`, so a Theme switch to another set, which takes
effect at the restart, changes nothing before it. A window in `ui_redirects` for that set loads as
`<set>_<name>.bin`, the path written into `ringnav_ui_load`'s own frame, in the same folder
(`dialog/volume_dialog` becomes `dialog/<set>_volume_dialog`).
The Library and Edit Playlist rows the payload builds follow the live geometry: `list_row_core`
reads the Library constructor's body and icon x words after `theme_boot`, and Edit Playlist's row
takes the written set's `pitch` and `body` (`geom()`).

**Classic.** THEME 1, `assets/themes/classic-light/theme.xml`. Its id stays `classic-light`, so its art (`classic_light_*`), geometry sets (`classic-light`, `classic-light-text`), face (`fonts/classic-light.ttf`) and accent key (`CLASSIC_LIGHT_ACCENT`) keep that name. It extends Minimal and states its own numbers in its theme.xml; what it leaves out is Minimal's or its `ROLE_MACROS` row's (`NP_BAR_IPOD`). A screen it does not theme yet (Tidal's pages, which keep their navbars, its dialogs, and the games and photo pages' own drawing) keeps Minimal's layout under this theme's colours and font. The values for Settings and dialogs, which have no measured reference, start from iPod.js (https://github.com/tvillarete/ipod-classic-js, MIT): values only, no files, code or marks.

**Modern.** THEME 2, `assets/themes/modern/theme.xml`: 1.6.5's Classic, kept on Minimal's set (SET0) with its own Home inset (`HOME_MODERN_*`), the accent's bar (`SEL_BAR`: `gradient()`, a pill under Rounded Corners) and its accent in `ACCENT`, 1.6.5's Classic's key.

**Geometry sets.** Classic's two sets are `classic-light` (Row Art on: 52 px art rows) and `classic-light-text` (all 32). A built-in theme declares its sets in its theme.xml (`<geometry name="classic-light" bar="26" …/>`, each omitted field the built-in set's or its `extends=` set's), and `tools/theme.py` checks each field's range (`GEOM_RANGES`) and builds the asset copies and boot tables from them.

- Values that follow the written set but are not immediates (the bar, the settings rows, the battery body, the text inset) are `geom_vals[st.geom][V_*]`, and `client_h()` is `320 - geom_val(V_BAR)`.
- `list_end`: list boxes take the whole window (294 under Classic), so rows draw down to the glass, while the selection's scroll limit (`sel_height`, `max_top`) stops at `list_end` so a selected row never lands where the glass hides its text. A last row selected leaves the glass's strip below it empty. A list ends after the last row whose text clears the glass at `text_x` (`fit_bottom`).
- `action_row`: Songs' action bar as a plain row. Stock rebuilds that view's children on each refresh (0x4a7e54: the label, Play All's image, Sort's image only on some pages), so the assets shape only the boxes around it and `action_row()` shapes its children as it paints, once per rebuild. They stay stock's widgets with stock's handlers.
- `home_lib`: Home's Library row, in Split and while Library on Home is on (`home_lib_on`), slides in a list of the Library's rows (`list_view_homelib`, filled by the same `library_rows` the page uses, `home_library`) instead of opening the page; each row opens its stock page and Return comes back to the list. The bar's title names the open list (`coverflow_home_title`). Full keeps stock's page. Its Streaming row does the same (`list_view_homestream`, `home_streaming`, `stream_rows`): stock's `stream_page_init` takes the list for its window, finds no `view_navbar` there and rebuilds the rows in the view of that name, its timer stopped by `STREAM_BG` and its handlers dropped, and the rows take Home's height (`home_fill`). Row Art doesn't reach this list; Full's page still follows it through `stream_row`. `svc_toggle` looks in every window, as Home's list comes first and is empty under Full.
- `np_ipod`: Now Playing as an iPod classic's ([Slanted art](#slanted-art)): the art `NP_IPOD_ART` up in the top row, `img_cover` `NP_REFLECT` taller than it, the slide*view tall enough to hold it, "3 of 12" under the album, and the times sit beside a bar narrowed for them (`with_geometry`'s `NP_BAR`, so the A-B markers' `np_bar*\*`immediates follow), the under-10:00 boxes, which`ipod_times` widens to the track at run time ([Slanted art](#slanted-art)).
- Songs' rows have their own immediates (`song_pitch`, `song_body`, `song_title24_y`, `song_meta_y`) and page kind `song`. Under a set without text art, a row taller than the pitch whose leading image has no width is two lines (`two_lines`): art and playing gif hide, the second line stays, and the artist is what stock's bind (0x4a90e4) writes, "Unknown Artist" when untagged. A playlist's songs are the same page and list; album tracks and an artist's songs are other constructors, title only.

**A theme's own options.** A theme declares them in theme.xml (`<option key="ROWART" label="Row Art" default="On">` over `<value label="Off" set="classic-light-text"/>` rows). The payload draws, cycles and stores them generically (`SETTING_OWN`, `theme_keys[]`, `st.own[]`) and reads their keys after the thirteen in declaration order, and `theme_geom(T)` boots the set that the stored value of its first option with sets names. Classic's Row Art is one such option; a change to it that names another set asks "Restart to apply?" (`restart_confirm`). Every value of an option names a set, or none does: one that names none is a plain choice the payload reads as `st.own[OWN_<KEY>]` through `theme_own` (0 while the active theme does not offer it), saved at once with no restart. `tools/theme.py` accepts only the plain keys in `PLAIN_KEYS`, the ones the payload reads: Classic's `HOMELIB` (`home_lib_on`) and `ROWPREVIEW` (`row_preview_on`). An own option takes `preset` and `keep` as a payload setting does (into its `option_t` line), so `theme_preset()` writes it on a switch to the theme, and the restart test applies the presets first. Undeclared options (Corners and Centred NP under Classic) take the theme's value, and their stored keys are untouched: `ipod_square_art()` reads Corners that way, so a theme with `playing.art.square` that leaves Corners out is always square.

**The picker.** The Theme row opens a picker. A pick that changes the set asks "Restart to apply?" (`theme_restart`): Classic boots its own sets, so any pick to or from it does. Cancel writes nothing, and OK writes `THEME` and the theme's preset (`theme_preset`) and reboots through `device_reboot`. A pick that keeps the set, Minimal to Modern and back, switches at once (`theme_select`).

**Painters.** `SEL_STOPS`, `BAR_GLOSS` and `BATT_CLASSIC_LIGHT` draw final colours (`paint_stops`), and `home_pane_paint` draws the Split placeholder in `home.pane.stops`, final in each half (Classic: a slate on paper, and on black the dark bar's `#1c1c1c`, so the two read as one surface). `list.separator.stops` are a two-line row's and Play All's last rows, one stop a row, final in each half (`paint_separators`). `list.row.tall_font`/`tall_px` (`ROLE_TALL`) are a two-line row's title, and `scheme.sel.tall_top` the band's row 1 on a tall row, whose stops span the whole row. A final scheme value of 0 in the dark half takes the light half's (`half()`), so a dark half that wants none states its own (Classic's band: its first stop).

**The bar's title.** Under Classic the bar's `label_title` names the top window by `bar_title_sync`'s rule, Home by the list it shows (`coverflow_home_title`: `coverflow_home_name`, "Q2 Pod", on its own list, else "Library" or "Settings"). It is written during a paint, where an invalidate is lost, so `title_due` forces it on to the panel just after. Time in Title decides the clock beside it: on, `label_clock` shows the time; off, `bar_title` empties it while the title has text and gives it back on a page with none, so the page is never named twice, and widens `label_title` meanwhile to EQ's box, where the right group's room starts, so a long title shows more before its ellipsis. Under Minimal the same switch names the page in `label_clock` itself ([ipod.md](ipod.md#status-bar-and-clock)). The title and clock are laid out at the set's `bar_px` (`status_metrics`, `tools/ipod.py`) and drawn at `bar.title.font_size`, so `tools/theme.py` rejects a `bar.title.font_size` other than the `bar_px` of each of the theme's sets that has one.

**Stock details the slider and Set Time pages lean on.** `playset_bootvol_page_init` makes `img_bootswitch` in `view_navbar` after the load, so `slider_paint` finds it at its first paint. Stock's slider fills its knob in `border_color` and draws none when it is clear (`0x61b3ec`). `text_selector`'s release adds the finger's speed times `yspeed_scale` to the drag (`0x5ff8fc`): at 0, Set Time steps a row, 14 px. Its index change arrives after the scroll's animator is cleared (`0x60087c`), so `time_changed` can put a dragged 12-hour hour back in its half.

**Message screens.** Under Classic (`classic_pages()`) `msg_open` takes Update Local Music's `updatemusic_dialog` and USB mode's `usbmode_page` as the loader hands them over. Stock's `img_animation`, `label_msg`, `label_scanfolder`, `img_usbmode` and `scrlabel_info` stay by name, hidden, and `btn_no` keeps its binding. `msg_paint` draws `paint_page`, the round icon (`classic_light_orb_note`, `classic_light_orb_usb`, `tools/art.py`) and the text's shadows under the window's children. The dialog covers the bar, so `paint_dark_bar` paints the bar there; USB's page sits under the real bar, which goes dark and reads Q2 Pod. Stock gives no total, so `msg_sync` reads the first number in `label_msg` against the songs a detached worker counts as the dialog opens (`msg_count`, `library_count`, its own connection, as Home's `count_worker`), clamped to 99%. Until it answers, or when the database is busy, the line is stock's text with no bar. A first scan, a scan past the total or a folder's scan (`SCAN_WHOLE` 0, by our reading of stock's code) shows stock's text and no bar. `msg_sync` also runs in the paint hook, so it writes `btn_no`'s "Cancel" only over a text that differs.

**USB Storage's eject.** In every theme, `ringnav_ui_load` adds `usb_poll` to USB Storage's `usbmode_page` (`g_usbmode` not 1) as a 500 ms widget timer, so it goes with the page. Stock's `storage_config.sh` exports the card as LUN 0 of the configfs gadget `storage_demo`. The computer's eject is a SCSI START STOP UNIT with LoEj set and Start clear. Linux 5.10's `f_mass_storage` answers it in `do_start_stop` with `fsg_lun_close`, unless the host holds PREVENT ALLOW MEDIUM REMOVAL ([f_mass_storage.c](https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v5.10/source/drivers/usb/gadget/function/f_mass_storage.c#L1298)). After that, `functions/mass_storage.msg/lun.0/file` reads empty (`fsg_show_file`, [storage_common.c](https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v5.10/source/drivers/usb/gadget/function/storage_common.c#L330)). Once that file has read a path, an empty read is the eject. A missing file (no gadget yet, or one torn down) is none. Under Classic the heading becomes "OK to Disconnect" and the line goes. Under Minimal and Modern, stock's timer (0x530abc, every 500 ms) rewrites `scrlabel_info` "USB Connected" through `usbmode_showface`, so `usb_poll` hides it and puts an `s_scrlabel_white24c` label in its place. The LUN stays closed until stock rebuilds the gadget, so the poll ends there and the page never goes back to Connected. Stock's teardown (`storage_config.sh stop`, from `usbmode_exit` at the page's window close, on the UI thread) unbinds the UDC, which closes nothing, then removes `lun.0`, which closes the LUN and its file together, so no tick reads it empty there. Limit: only a host that sends LoEj counts (macOS Finder, Linux `eject` and udisks, Windows Explorer's Eject). Windows' Safely Remove Hardware may only stop the device, which leaves the page on Connected. The vendor kernel (5.10.21 X2000) builds `f_mass_storage` in, and its xImage carries mainline's eject and close-backing-file messages; its only visible change there is the `pc_check` parameter (stock's PC-link status).

**USB DAC on a host that never reads the card.** Stock's `check_usb_status` (`0x4e91fc`, once a second from the idle loop `on_wm_idle_status`) starts the storage gadget as soon as a cable is in (`g_usbdet_value` 1, `g_usblink_status` 1) and waits there until `toolsGetpclinkStatus` (`0x5bf310`) reads `/sys/module/usb_f_mass_storage/parameters/pc_check` as 1, which the vendor kernel sets when the host sends the drive a SCSI command. Only then (`g_usblink_status` 2) does `on_wm_timer_dialog` open `usbmode_page`, which under Audio and DAC swaps the storage gadget for the UAC2 one (`uac_device_config.sh`). A Nintendo Switch 2 configures the gadget and never sends it a SCSI command, so stock stayed a drive nobody read (#290). `ringnav_usb_status` (`patch/usb_dac.c`) hooks `check_usb_status`: under Audio and DAC (`g_usbmode` 1), with the cable in and the gadget up, a UDC state (`/sys/class/udc/13500000.otg/state`) of `configured` has `toolsSetpclinkStatus(1)` (`0x5bf3d0`, `echo 1 > pc_check`) tell stock the link is there, and stock takes it from there on the same pass. A charger has no host, so the UDC never reads `configured` there; USB Storage keeps stock's gate. `suspended` doesn't count: a host that suspends the bus straight after enumerating waits as stock does.

**Motion.** A theme's `<view name="motion">` holds one `<element>` per kind, `ms` and `easing` (`linear`, `cubic_out` and the rest of AWTK's names, and `follow` on Cover Flow's two steps: see [its step](#coverflow-step)) on each, and defaults to 0 ms, so Minimal states none and moves as stock does. A window kind (`page`, `playing`, `coverflow`) also takes `anim` (`htranslate` unless `fade` or another AWTK animation) and becomes the window's `anim_hint`, e.g. `htranslate(duration=300,easing=sin_inout)`, as it loads or opens (`motion_load`, `motion_open`); a kind with no ms writes nothing and leaves the asset's own hint. `page` is a stock page that slides, `playing` Now Playing's close (its open stays immediate), and `coverflow` its page. No kind hints a page that has no slide of its own, nor any dialog: a dialog's background is clear, and an animated window is two snapshots, so a fade over a clear one shows black first (see [Queue menu](#queue-menu)). No builtin theme sets a window kind now; every dialog, popup and the hold menu opens and closes in one frame. Classic's pages open as Minimal's: its `page` is stock's own slide. A widget kind (`inplace`, `swap`, `step`, `step_fast`, `coverflow_step`, `coverflow_step_fast`) glides a widget's own property with an animator: a level inside a page, Home's lists to and from Settings, the carousel's and the Photos viewer's wheel step, slow and fast (`step_fast` within `HOME_FAST_WINDOW_MS` of the last), and Cover Flow's, the same. `recall ms` is how long a remembered row's glide takes (0 jumps) and `bump px` how far the outline nudges at the end of a list (0 stays put).

**Slider pages and the dark bar.** `slider_open` reshapes stock's Backlight, Max Volume, Balance and Boot Volume windows at load into one form, with stock's widgets kept by name: a band holding the slider, the value and the caption, painted from the page's background. `dark_bar` names the pages under the dark bar (those, Set Time and USB's page); `bar_dark_sync` puts the real bar's title across it and hides the clock on reaching one, and restores both on leaving. The bar is the scheme's dark half (`scheme.bar`'s stops and line) in either half, and its title, the message screens' and the lock screen's date take the bar's size and rows (`font_px[ROLE_BAR]`, `dark_title_y`), with no shadow.

**Set Time and Set Date.** `time_open` resizes stock's selectors at load, before its init places them; `time_paint` draws the clock face or calendar tile and the fields' trays; `ringnav_manualtime` follows the selectors after stock's init has clamped the day.

**Classic's own pages.** `painter[PAINT_PAGES]` (`<view name="pages"><element name="own" painter="classic-light"/>`, Classic alone) is the one switch for the pages below that a theme draws itself: Quick Settings, the slider pages, the rating picker, T9 and Search, Set Time and Set Date, the message screens, the QR page, the lock screen's info screen, Cover Flow's flip, Play/Pause's hold and the hold of Return from a list open on Home. Code reads it through `classic_pages()` (`theme.h`); `painter[PAINT_VOLUME]` is the volume panel's alone and `tools/art.py` draws the speakers for it, the pages' art for `PAINT_PAGES`.

**Dialogs.** `painter[PAINT_CONFIRM]` covers the confirm control, the hold menu, the toast and the small dialogs. `confirm_form` picks a confirm's form: the tray for a prompt `confirm_over` asked for, the page for any other, so a prompt stock opens over it stays a question.

**Hold menu panel.** Under Classic `hold_open` dresses the hold menu as Cover Flow's flip panel: `PANEL_X` (40) in, `BACK_W` (294) rows wide, a `QM_HEAD` (44) header holding stock's navbar with its title alone (31 px, the theme's bold face, white in either half), then the theme's 32 px page rows in the same face at 25 px, `QM_ROWS` (6) at most before the list scrolls. The panel is centred in the rows under the bar, so at its tallest (236 rows, 55 to 291) its square corners clear the glass by `corner_x`. The dialog keeps its clear background and no dim; `hold_paint` draws the panel behind the dialog's children and the header's shadow and border after them, with `coverflow_panel_paint`, which the flip panel uses too. Its rows take the theme's selection band, as the flip panel's do. The title is a looping `hscroll_label` in every theme, as Now Playing's; its holds at its start and end are every label's (`ringnav_hscroll_start`, `ringnav_hscroll_lull`). Stock's doesn't loop.

<a id="quick-settings-blue"></a>**Quick Settings' on tiles.** Under Classic a Quick Settings tile that is on takes the accent's red tone, flat, as Minimal's and Modern's lit discs do (stock's discs, which take it through the image hook, [ipod.md](ipod.md#quick-settings)); Classic Blue, the theme's own scheme (a `label`), keeps `qs_tile`'s `#207CDE`, not its Cobalt red tone. In the light half the border is the fill a quarter darker. On a tone brighter than `GLYPH_LIGHT_MAX` the glyph takes the `_l` ink: Graphite's silver, in the dark half, is a light tile with a dark glyph, and flips to a dark grey in the light half. Either way it stands apart from the off tiles. The brightness bar's fill (`quick_scrub`) follows the same rule: Classic Blue keeps its three blue stops, another accent fills flat in its light tone, as Minimal's slider does (`quick_fill`). The suns stay white, Classic's own marks, in every accent: tinting them would take a recolour path of its own.

**A page's switch.** A set with no toolbar (`V_TOOLBAR` 0) moves stock's toolbar switch to a row reading On or Off: `switch_row` on Lock Screen, `ringnav_sleeptimer` on Sleep Timer.

**Final text.** `text_ink` stores a label's final colour as what Light Mode's style hook turns into it, so the stored value depends on the half. `light_apply`'s `text_inks` walks the windows at Auto's turn and writes each again.

**The play state.** Classic's right group is EQ, Bluetooth, Wi-Fi, `img_state` and the battery (`STATUS_RIGHT_CLASSIC_LIGHT`); `bar_state_sync` hides EQ while something plays or is paused, as the group has room for one of the two. The play state stays there on every page, Home's own list included, so it never jumps sides.

**Home's pane.** It follows the highlighted row under a theme with `home.pane.icon_x` while Row Preview is on (`row_preview_on`; off, every row shows Now Playing's art and `home_counts` asks nothing): `coverflow_home_row()` from `paint_selection` and the wheel, `home_pane_paint()` for what it shows. Home's return to the front recomputes the row's pane (`home_foreground`), so a Row Preview change in Settings shows at once. Each row's image comes from `tools/art.py` (`classic_light_icon_*`, original drawings), captions only from real data read by `home_counts` while Home comes back to the top, at boot and after a scan, never on a wheel move: at most one query per library change, and a running scan leaves the song and album counts unknown, so those rows show their image alone. Cover Flow's album count is its own list's when it holds one, else `sum(e=0)+max(e)` over `ALBUM_GROUP`, which is the same number. The free space is in binary GB, as About's Remaining. Rockbox or Groovebox as the Shortcut row shows the game icon alone, and Folder has no caption. Streaming's count leaves out Configuration. On Streaming's list it shows the row's own icon, `home.pane.svc_px` square at `svc_x`, `svc_y`, read off the row (`pane_service`, its image and name widgets found once when the row is highlighted): a fixed service's `classic_light_stream_*` or `classic_light_list_tidal`, the build's Lanczos copy of its 52 px icon at that size, since the device scales with hard edges; a card app's `file://` image, scaled at paint, else `classic_light_stream_app`. Its name takes the count's place, and one too wide for `count_w` shrinks to three quarters of `count_px`, then ends in an ellipsis.

**Light and dark.** Every scheme has a dark and a light half (`theme_t.schemes`, `theme_t.light`). Light Mode (Off, On, Auto) picks one through `scheme()`, and a scheme's `mode` can keep it to one. theme.xml states a half's drawn-as-stated colours as `light.<key>`/`dark.<key>` (unprefixed: the dark half, stock's terms), and a half it leaves out is the flip of the other's greys. Minimal states none, so one array serves both of its halves.

**Other keys.** `tools/theme.py` also reads `alphas` keys, `list.art.*` (x, text_x, text_end), `bar.icons.*`, `bar.battery.{low,low_w,corner_a,foot_a}`, `list.chevron.x`, `home.chevron.x` and `list.letter.{raw,px}` (the fast-scroll letter: final colours in both halves, its size; `st.raw_ink` tells `ui_ink` to leave them alone) and `list.scrollbar.*` (`paint_scrollbar`'s thumb, from a list's border paint, with stock's bar drawn clear; `sb_w` 0 keeps stock's).

### Classic's Playlists

Under a set without toolbars (`toolbar` 0) `tools/ipod.py` hides `view_navbar` and takes its room for the list, whose `default_item_height` is the Songs pitch with no `item_height`: `children_layouter_list_view` (`0x5e9eb4`) then gives each row its own height, so 52 px playlists stack with 32 px action rows. `ringnav_playlist`, after every stock rebuild (`0x4b2dac`), dresses the rows (`pl_dress`): the tile row at index 0 hides and each playlist row loses its art, in `pl_fit`'s places on the page rows' boxes, under a full-width button so the chevron and band are the list's. Stock's count label is re-worded from its own number, so no second query runs. Rows it can't have are added: Select All, New Playlist… and Edit Playlists… restacked to indexes 1 to 3, above the playlists (the two modes never show Select All with the other two), then Import Playlists, Export, Delete and Done; the Add to Playlist picker, which has no tile row, gets New Playlist… alone, at index 0. They are shown by `pl_rows` for the mode, each list item tagged with its action (`PL_ACT`), which `pl_rows` reads in one walk, so no row is found by its place. Select All reads Deselect All while `g_allselect_flag` (`0xa38ac0`) is set: stock's Select All (`0x4b1b88`) toggles on it, and a row's mark sets it to whether every row is marked (`0x4b1e38`), so a hand-marked full list clears on the next tap. No stock function is replaced. Each row's click runs next pass (the latest click in a pass wins; the action is the timer's ctx) and finds its target by the handler bound to it, in the hidden toolbar or the hidden tile row (`pl_stock`), and dispatches a click there, so `g_navbar_status`, the marks in `p_vector_select_record`, every dialog and the reload stay stock's; `0x4b19b8` and `0x4b1ad0` rebuild only the toolbar, so `pl_rows` runs after them, and after each mark on a second click handler. A row is its action whatever scrolled: nothing keys on an index. The separators (`PAGE_TALL`) go on every shown row, playlist or action, but the list's last, in one walk from the end. With nothing remembered (a first open, or a count changed), `reconcile`'s fresh-page row, the first visible, moves past an action row to the first playlist (`pl_first`), as stock's and the iPod's lists hold playlists alone; an empty list stays on New Playlist…. A remembered row comes back by its text (position memory), so the playlists' shift by two rows lands on the same playlist. Between modes `pl_rows` maps the selection: into select mode on the first playlist, out of it a playlist keeps its own, Select All goes to the first playlist and an action row to Edit Playlists…. Export reads `g_navbar_status` after its confirm returns, which `dialog_modal` (`0x63f1ac`) does before an idle closes the dialog (`0x63ec6c`), so a marked Export stays marked; that close is what ends select mode (`0x4b3180`, `EVT_WINDOW_TO_FOREGROUND`), as stock's. Return in select mode runs Done: `0x4b1310`, which would close the page and leave the flag set, is rebound to `pl_keyup`, which falls through to it when no Done target exists; a page destroyed in the mode (held Return to Home, `PL_MARKING` set) clears the flag, and only then. Stock's hit from x 286 opens More outside select mode (`0x4b1d90` branches on `g_navbar_status`, `0x4b1e70`), so every row's click goes through `pl_pick` first: a picker's rows pick with a fresh click at x 0, and in the list a tap left of the dots' column (from x 333), the chevron's column (from x 317, `CHEVRON_W` from the edge) with it, opens the playlist the same way. The dots sit at the right edge, centred on the picker's tick (x 346), as Classic's other trailing marks and values do, since only the selected row has a chevron; on that row the chevron shows in their place, and a tap opens the playlist out to the edge (its More is the hold's). The picker draws no chevron, since a pick adds and closes. Stock's relayout of a playlist row keeps `pl_fit`'s places: `compact_row_layout` hands a `PL_ROW` item's button to `pl_fit`. The dots and marks are `paint_radio`'s; stock's `img_more` keeps its name for the hold menu, its image emptied as a ring. The bar reads the toolbar's first label, which stock writes with the last list opened (`g_navbar_name`); `pl_rows` writes what the mode is. New Playlist and Rename (`pl_box_open`) are stock's dialogs moved at load; the hint and bar title wait a pass for stock's init, which writes its own hint.

**Playlists' add mode and memory, every theme.** `PLAYLIST_ADD_MODE` is one global that only a mode 2 pick clears, and the page reads it as it comes back to the front, so a page below a picker rebuilt in the picker's form. `ringnav_playlist` keeps each window's own in a prop and writes it back before each rebuild. Opening a playlist writes `g_local_classinfo_save` (`0x4b20c0`), which `context_now`'s scope hashed, so the page lost its row on the way back; its scope is the add mode alone, so the picker keeps its own row.

**Classic's info screen, T9 and Search.** Stock's windows and widgets, moved at load. A search box alone on a page (`t9_box`: Spotify's, a card app's, the radio's, the playlist editor's; not the EQ's edits, over a title) takes the flat dark bar (`dark_bar_rows`) in the strip above it, its chevron, whose tap is Return, and the white field, with a hint of its own size when it would pass the field at the typed text's 26; the page stays under the bar, which reads Search, where the full-screen Search dialog covers it. The lock window's picture paints the page, the dark bar, the trays and the hint's soft shadow (`classic_light_hint_shadow`) in its border hook, under the clocks and labels; `clock_date`'s `digit_clock` format is `MMM D, Y`; `paint_clock` draws the face's hands, and a one-second timer repaints on a new minute, battery or play state. It stops while the screen is off, for the battery; the first loop pass with it lit starts it again (`info_poll`), the pass that repaints the whole screen, so no stale hands show. `info_face` gives the song's and date's labels the face their text has, as stock writes them after the window loads. The labels store the colour the style hook turns into white, and `ringnav_image_add` leaves `scrsaver_slide` white, so they stay white over a picture in either half. The T9 keyboard (`kb_default_t9`, shared with the other themes) stays stock's asset: `kb_paint_key` draws each key in place of stock's background, and the style hook clears stock's text. `search_dialog` is a dialog at 0, 0, which the window manager paints over the system bar, so `search_paint` paints the bar itself (`widget_paint`) over the dark bar's rows, which stand behind the field too; `dialog_search_dialog_init` (`0x49eb58`, hooked by `ringnav_search_init`) names the tabs from its translations in its `widget_foreach` visitor (`0x49aef8`: `search_track`, `songer`, `album`, `genre`, keys other pages share), so the hook writes the tabs' words once, after it; its rows are stock's builders with the `search_*` set values at their immediates, and `stock_set_row_layout` at their layout calls. The lit tab is `lit_rows` over its slot, rounded only where the slot meets the track's ends (`round_row_ends`), so no track shows beside it, and one row past the track, over the light row under it (`row_below`), which read as a gap under the blue. Stock shows the tabs (`tab_control`) only with results, else the history (`view`: its header, then its list) or, with none, its tip (`view_tip`) (`0x49bba4`, `0x49d9a8`). Those two sit in the tabs' place at `SEARCH_HISTORY_Y` (71), the list filling the screen under the header, and `search_strip_end` ends the strip there while the tabs are hidden, 7 rows under the field as it starts 7 above, so no empty band stands where the tabs would be; with them shown it ends at 108, under the tabs. Stock never moves either view, and neither has a `self_layout` to put it back. The history's delete buttons are stock's styles `s_btn_cancel_navbar` (each row's X, its button made at `0x49dc18`) and `s_btn_delete_navbar` (the header's trash, made at `0x49b244`): both carry an opaque black `border_color` with `round_radius` 4, unseen on stock's black and turned white by Light Mode, a frame broken at its corners over the selection. `search_del` names them inside the Search dialog; their border hook skips stock's, and the X's background is `search_x`, a hard-edged mark like `paint_tick`'s, in the selection's text on the selected row and the ink elsewhere, so stock's flipped image never shows. The trash keeps stock's image. ponytail: the X's press shows no pressed image (stock's `navbar_canceldown`); the row's own press shows.

## Page transitions (iPod)

Stock V1.32 carries AWTK's window animators and uses one: `saverscreen_page.bin` has `close_anim_hint` `slide_left(duration=200,easing=cubic_out)`, the only hint in the stock assets. The window manager builds the animator named by a window's `anim_hint` (`window_animator_htranslate_create`, `0x683a08`, for `htranslate`) when it opens or closes. `navigator_to_with_context` (`0x4ec758`) opens a page above the rest and `navigator_back` (`0x4ec8e0`) is `window_manager_back`, so the page below stays alive and Return reverses the slide.

iPod sets the hint in its assets ([ipod.md](ipod.md#transitions)). `htranslate` animates two snapshots. `window_animator_prepare` (`0x682ee8`) paints both pages into offline canvases, through the payload's paint hooks, before `window_manager_is_animating` (`0x66daf4`) becomes true and while the sliding page is the top window; a hinted page is not painted before that, so its snapshot is its first paint. Each frame then blits the snapshots and paints only the status bar. The payload follows the snapshots in four places (`slides()`, `patch/navigation.c`). A surface whose own window is under a sliding top window draws the row it holds and nothing else: loading, recall and settling read the top page's state. A sliding page reveals its remembered row at once instead of by the glide, so it does not arrive mid-glide. Home's art is checked when Home itself is painted, not only when it is the top window. After a touch, a Return by button ends [touch mode](#touch-mode) on a 0 ms timer instead of at once: the closing page's snapshot, painted inside stock's handling of the release, keeps its row hidden, while the page behind shows its own.

## Status bar (iPod)

The background hook also handles top-level widgets, whose parent is the window manager. The status bar widget, which `system_bar_init` stores in the `system_bar` global (`0xa3a6d0`, a size-checked `CONTEXT_DATA` entry), gets one `canvas_fill_rect` in black, `BAR_COLOR` (`#000000`, Minimal's `BAR_FLAT`), without a highlight, or under Classic's `BAR_GLOSS` its scheme's gloss rows over a one-pixel line ([Themes](#themes)), with the LCD fill color restored.

`label_clock` in the bar shows the device's local time, read once a minute rather than on paint: `clock_show` calls `time` and `localtime_r` (stock libc, with a caller-owned time buffer) for the hour and minute (`struct tm` `+8`, `+4`), applies Auto Light Mode's hour, and arms a timer due at the next minute (`60 - tm_sec` seconds; a minute after a failed read). The label follows stock's 24-hour setting (`g_time24h_flag`): `%02d:%02d` (`18:14`), or, on a bar with its own title (Classic's), `%d:%02d` so it clears the title, or `%d:%02d %s`, a 12-hour clock without seconds or a leading zero (`12:05 AM`, `6:14 PM`), through `tk_snprintf`, then `widget_set_text_utf8`. A failed `time` (`-1`) or `localtime_r` (null), or an out-of-range field, shows `--:--`. The minute and the 24-hour setting are kept as a key, so the label is looked up and written only when what it shows changes. Besides the timer, `clock_show` runs at once for a new time zone (`ringnav_timezone`), a time set by hand, and a wake, and the pass after the bar first paints or paints with a new 24-hour setting rearms it at 0 ms; a network time sync while lit shows at the next minute. Nothing else reads the time, so stock threads' `localtime` (one shared static buffer) has nothing to race. Every page and dialog shows it. There is no volume flash: stock `on_wm_keyup_fun` opens `dialog/volume_dialog` on every volume turn (`0x4e8bb0`).

The same paints run `bar_sync`. `systembar_showface` (`0x52f610`) sets `img_bt`'s image every tick: a codec badge (`bar_sbc`, `bar_aac`, `bar_aptx`, `bar_aptxhd`, `bar_ldac`, by `bt_showcoding`) while Bluetooth is linked, otherwise `bar_bt` or `bar_btcon`. It also sets `img_battery`'s image (`bar_charge`, `bar_lowcharge` under 15%, else `bar_battery`), `label_battery`'s `%d%%` text and `progress_battery`'s value, and never the battery widgets' visibility. A badge that differs from the last one arms a `CODEC_MS` UI timer, whose steps set `widget_set_opacity` (`0x659f24`, the widget's `+0x34` byte, which `widget_paint` applies as the canvas global alpha) down and back up, switching the image to `bar_btcon` halfway. After that the bar's background paint, which runs before its children, puts `bar_btcon` back over each badge stock sets again, so no badge frame is drawn. A hidden `img_bt` or a non-badge image other than `bar_btcon` ends the flash. `bar_sync` then hides `img_battery` and `label_battery` and shows `view_battery` with `widget_set_visible`, which returns early for an unchanged state and otherwise marks the parent for relayout (`widget_set_need_relayout_children`). The slot is sized to its ink: a percentage is its digit count times the `'0'` advance plus the `'%'` advance, both measured with `canvas_measure_text` on the first battery paint (`BATT_PCT_W` until then). `view_battery` is painted in the background hook from the level (`label_battery`'s text, since stock sets `progress_battery` to 0 while charging) and the charge state (`img_battery`'s image), and is invalidated when those or its displayed mode change. `BATTERY=3` uses a compact 28×15 solid rounded battery (radius 3; stock squares any radius under 3) with dark 13 px digits inside, omitting the percent sign; its body follows the charge-state colour. Resizing the slot immediately relayouts its parent row.

## Now Playing (iPod)

`playing_page_init` (`0x52ca88`, standard PIC prologue, resumed at `0x52ca94`) registers the page's handlers, runs the page update (`0x52b6f4`) once and starts a 250 ms widget timer (`playing_timer_start`, `0x52c114`) that runs it again. The update finds each widget with a recursive `widget_lookup`, skips any it does not find, and gets the times from `player_playtime_and_length` (`0x514128`): elapsed and total seconds from `mclGetPlayTime` (`0x5ac078`), less a CUE track's start, elapsed clamped to the total, both 0 while stopped. It sets `slider_play` to value = elapsed, max = total, and formats both labels with `toolsTimeItoa` (`0x5bc410`): `%02d:%02d`, or `%02d:%02d:%02d` from an hour. `label_playlen` is its only reference to that name.

The hook runs the stock init, then caches `label_ipod_pos`, `label_ipod_album`, `label_ipod_remain` and the slider, and syncs them. It also caches `img_cover`, whose border paint (after the image) rounds its corners: for each of the `NP_ART_RADIUS` rows of a corner, one `canvas_fill_rect` outside the arc and one edge pixel at the alpha it leaves uncovered, in 1/16 pixel from an integer square root, both in the page's black. Painting the top window or the status bar syncs them again. The position is `MCL_POS + 1` of `deque_size(*mcl_pdeqplaylist)` ("3 of 12"), empty without a current record. The album is that record's `REC_ALBUM` (the record comes from `queue_now`, which Home's art shares), or, once `player_get_id3info` (`0x5146bc`) has parsed that record's file, the album it parsed: `g_play_id3_info` (`0xa4103c`, `0xa9c` bytes) holds the parsed path at `+0`, album at `+0x404`, artist at `+0x504` and title at `+0x684` (`now_tag`). For a CUE track (`mclGetCueFlag`: the playing record has a CUE start or end) stock copies the record's tags instead of reading the file: stock's sheet parser (`0x5c02f4`) gives each track the sheet's `TITLE` as album and the last `PERFORMER` seen (the track's, else the sheet's) as artist. Its copy of the parsed path (`ID3_PATH`, `0x5148e8`), which every fresh parse reaches before Now Playing's title and artist lines are written, becomes `jal ringnav_id3_path`: an empty album or artist there is taken from the file's own tags (`toolsGetMusicInfo`), so a sheet without `TITLE` or `PERFORMER` (common for audiobooks) shows the book and author instead of Unknown Artist. Album artist is filled the same way. ponytail: only the playing track's tags; Library lists and the database keep the sheet's empty fields (Library's Artists and Albums leave audiobooks out anyway), so Home's and Now Playing's art key (artist, album) can miss Coverflow's cache, keyed on the database's values; fill at scan time if that matters. When a folder play (class 1) reaches its last track with the folder-jump setting on (Folder Skip, the byte `0xa3be53`), `mclNextSong` calls `on_player_autochange` (`0x513fc0`), whose `toolsLoadNextDir` and `player_refresh_playqueue(1)` replace the queue with the next folder's untagged file records, and only the parsed tags name the next album. Every stock folder jump tests class 1, so Coverflow plays its albums as stock's album class `0xff10` and Most Played and Shuffle Songs as `0xf001`. `album_memory` also writes the album into `g_memory_info` (`+0x60d` Unknown flag, `+0x611` name), as `player_load_songlist` does for stock lists, so stock resume rebuilds that album with `getMusicByAlbum`. Home's art keys its Coverflow cache lookup the same way. The remaining time is the slider's max less its value, in stock's format with a leading minus, so it matches the elapsed label and follows a touch drag. Each label is written only when its source changes (position, queue length, album string pointer, or those seconds); `EVT_DESTROY` drops the cache. Stock's total label stays hidden.

The V8.6 folder-rollover regression in `test/patch.py` executes stock `player_mainscheduling` → `mclAutoChange` → `mclNextSong` → `on_player_autochange` → `toolsLoadNextDir` → `player_refresh_playqueue` → `mclLoadPlayList`, then stock tag parsing, cover-worker completion and the Now Playing timer (`0x52bffc`). Filesystem enumeration, tag decoding, image decoding and the audio decoder are mocked at their boundaries; the harness does not supply the resulting player metadata globals. Equal-sized and one-track sibling folders, reused positions, missing tags/art, consecutive transitions, and delayed scheduler/cover completion pass in Stock and iPod. iPod's paint hooks also verify album text and Home art before and after completion. This does not reproduce a V8.6 regression, so no additional metadata firmware change is made.

**Info page**. The page update fills the info page through `0x52a508` (its one call, a `bal` at `0x52baf4`, after the player status checks): `label_album`, `label_genre`, `label_encode`, `label_year`, `label_bits`, `label_kbps` and `label_sample` as `%s:%s` or `%s:%d…` (`Album:Kid A`, `Bit Depth:16bit`, `Sample Rate:44.1kHz`; `%s:%s` is also wpa_supplicant's literal, so stays), `label_size` as `%s:%.2fMB`, and `label_location` as the bare folder with `/mnt/mmc/` as `SD:/` (it looks up `player_location` and never uses it), a missing value as the first `player_unknown`, which en_US has twice ("Unknown Artist", then "Unknown"). The build makes that call `jal ringnav_np_info` (`NP_INFO_CALL`), which runs it, then rewrites each line: `: ` after the label, a space before `MB`, `kbps` and `kHz` and a hyphen before `bit` after a digit, "Unknown Artist" as "Unknown", and the folder after `player_location`'s label (`getFormatString`), only where the text changes. Its labels **Genre** and **Year** are `LABELS` renames of `genre` (which the search page's tab shares) and `player_era`.

Under Classic the page is a list (#204's): `playing_page`'s `np_ipod` lays each label out as a row's name, `NP_INFO_NAME_W` (100) wide, the nine rows sharing the page from the top row's end to the dots (20 px each, `NP_DOTS_Y` / 9), from the set's `text_x` or further in where the glass needs it, and adds a `<name>_value` `hscroll_label` beside it to the mirrored edge, right-aligned and ellipsized as a settings value. `ringnav_np_info` finds `label_album_value` and writes the name and the value apart, both in the info role's face and size (`theme_font`, `ROLE_INFO`, Classic's face at 17 px), the value in `playing.info.value`'s grey for the half (`#8e9196` light, `#8a8a8a` dark, final: written through `ui_ink`, which the style hook undoes); the genre is a row like the rest, so it ellipsizes rather than taking `genre_fill`'s marquee, which Minimal and Modern keep. The rows end above the dots, so the times, the bar and the controls stay where they are. `test/build.py` checks every name fits its box at that size and both columns clear the glass.

`set_repeatpoint`'s marker placement (`0x52a268`) puts the 3x10 A-B markers at x = 50 + t \* 290 / length, y 250. iPod keeps y and patches x and width to `NP_BAR`; Classic's bar, fitted to the track, takes them along (`ringnav_ab_x`, [Slanted art](#slanted-art)). The slider's 44px target is centred on the same bar (window y 255), with the page dots moved 6px up, outside the target. The dots' `slide_indicator` gets its own anchor (`anchor_x` `187px`, `anchor_y` `0px`, the point stock's took: the slide_view's centre, above the indicator's middle, so the dots on its top edge). With none, stock centres them on its `indicated_target` as `widget_to_screen` places it, and that subtracts the widget's own `xoffset` property, which `slide_view` sets to the drag during a swipe. The indicator lays its dots out again only after its own layout (any relayout of the window), but then from wherever the slide_view was dragged, so the dots slid with the page until the next layout. Native `slide_with_bar` accepts a press anywhere in that rectangle and grabs the pointer, so a drag stays on the slider outside its bounds. Its dragger is skipped when the `icon` style lookup is empty and `border_color` is transparent, so the asset clears `border_color` in every state and no thumb shows; the fill then ends exactly at the value. `np_fill` sets the fill in every state a press passes through, so a touch keeps the accent.

**Volume.** Outside a scrub the wheel stays on the volume, and stock opens `dialog/volume_dialog`, a full-screen transparent dialog holding `slider_vol` and `label_vol`. Its `highlight="default(alpha=200)"` made the window manager draw everything under it as a dimmed snapshot taken at open, so iPod's `volume_dialog.bin` drops that property (`volume_dialog` in `tools/ipod.py`) and nothing dims. When the paint hook paints that dialog, `vol_paint` draws the volume in the dialog's own paint. With Now Playing directly under it (the window manager's second-to-last child) it covers the band from `slider_play` to `label_playtime` in black, then draws the track (`TRACK_COLOR`, the slider's `bar_size`) and a white fill to `value / max`, both pills like the capsule, and "Volume N" centred in the times' row at `NP_TIMES_PX`; where the times sit beside the bar (Classic), as on an iPod classic, it covers the times' boxes alone, Classic's own bar (`ipod_bar`) shows the volume, and a speaker takes each time's place (`vol_end`: `classic_light_vol_lo` ending where the elapsed time ended, `classic_light_vol_hi`, with its waves, from where the remaining time began, `tools/art.py`, white, which Light Mode flips to the ink as Sort's bars), with no number. ponytail: the paper over the elapsed time's box also covers the art reflection's last rows there (a few percent at most), a faint notch while the volume shows; hiding the two labels under the dialog instead needs a restore when it closes. Over any other window, Quick Settings included, it draws a rounded `VOL_PANEL_*` panel in the fast-scroll letter's style (`FILL_RGB` at `LETTER_ALPHA`, `LETTER_RADIUS`) exactly over Quick Settings' brightness row (the build checks it), its rounded corners clear of the glass corners, with the bar as a pill on a `VOL_PANEL_TRACK` grey track (a non-zero fill at least a round dot) and just the number at its right, at `VOL_PANEL_PX`. Under Classic (`T->painter[PAINT_VOLUME]`) the panel is the theme's, `VOL_CL_X` in, on the bar's gloss inside its line's ring over its soft shadow (`paint_panel`), and the bar is 17 of the rows painter's gloss rows (`paint_rows_bar`, `playing.scrubber`'s row lists) between the quiet and loud speakers, with no number; a card app's progress row (`addon_paint`) draws the same rows. `slider_vol` and `label_vol` are hidden in the iPod asset before the first paint, so transition snapshots cannot expose the stock full-screen controls; `slider_vol` is moved onto the area drawn, so stock's partial repaints land on it. Those alone stopped after a tick or two, so while the dialog is up a `VOL_POLL_MS` (50 ms) timer compares `slider_vol`'s value with the one last drawn and repaints the whole dialog when it changed; it removes itself once the dialog is no longer on top. Away from Now Playing, a theme with `VOL_CLASSIC_LIGHT` (Classic) draws its bar between two speakers on a soft-shadowed panel instead, with no number.

A theme with `motion.inplace` moves a level inside a page, with no window animating (`page_push`, `coverflow.c`): after `page_list` rebuilds it, the list_view starts a window's width to the side and animates its `x` to rest. `addon.c` keeps the depth's change in `pg.moved` through its Loading level, so the push falls on the first rows or error; a refresh finds it 0. Photos moves its kept `albums` and `grid` views. A folder level moves stock's `tableview_folder` when `ringnav_folder_click` or `ringnav_folder_back` sees `g_folder_layer` change and stay above 0. A level still sliding goes to its rest first.

## Visualizer (iPod)

hciplayer's PEQ filter (`patch/peq_player.c`) taps what it plays, after `peq_process`, into `/tmp/q2vis` (tmpfs), a `vis_tap` (`patch/peq.h`): a ring of `VIS_RING` stereo float frames, the frames written (`seq`), the ring's rate and the `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` time of the last write. It keeps the first two channels, and above 48 kHz every `rate / 44100`th frame, so the ring holds about 1.5 s at any rate, room for hciplayer's 500 ms ALSA buffer (ao_alsa's `buffer_time` 500000 at `0x438a2c`, 16 periods). No lock: the writer stores the frames, then the rate and stamp, then `seq`, and the reader tolerates a torn frame. demo makes the file (`open`, `ftruncate`) under `/tmp/q2vis.tmp` and renames it, so hciplayer never maps a short file; hciplayer looks for it (`open`, `mmap64`) once a second of audio (frames counted, no clock call) until it appears. demo maps it read-write and bumps `want` each tick; after a second of audio with no bump the writer stops copying, and the next bump clears the ring, so a return never shows audio from before.

`ringnav_playing` adds the page (`visualizer_attach`, `patch/visualizer.c`): a `s_listitem_black` `list_item` appended to `slide_view` at run time, so a tap clicks it but a swipe still slides, and the page dots get `max` set to the new child count. The page only takes taps: the `slide_view` clips a page added at run time to a sliver when it paints it, so the visualizer draws in the paint hook of Now Playing's window instead, after all its children, clipped to the slide area and with the canvas origin moved there, and its Halo art is an `image` child of the window over the area's centre. That image takes `img_cover`'s image name and draw type, so stock's reload of the cover (load, set, unload) refreshes it too, and shows only while the Halo draws. Painting starts a `1000 / VIS_FPS` ms timer, which removes itself once Now Playing is not on top, the `slide_view`'s `value` is not the last page or the screen is off; each tick invalidates the slide_view. Nothing draws unless settled: no window animation, and the page's own paint, which runs just before the window's in the same frame and draws nothing, last found its canvas at the slide area's left edge (and hides the Halo art the moment it does not), so the page is not being dragged or sliding (`value` only changes once a slide ends); a tap leaves it there, and the timer skips repaints while a finger is down. 1.5 s after the audio stops (everything at rest, the style name faded) it repaints once more, with the track's title and artist (`scrlabel_title`, `scrlabel_artist`, joined by a middle dot) where the style name shows, then stops repainting. A window paint that finds the page on top of the slide_view but not settled (under a closing window such as the lock screen, mid-slide or dragged) arms a `VIS_RETRY_MS` (250 ms) one-shot that invalidates the slide_view once it is settled; paused, nothing else would draw it. Each tick takes `VIS_N` (1024) frames ending `VIS_LATENCY_MS` (480) behind the last write, moved on by the time since it: that buffer, which MPlayer refills a period at a time, less about a period. Bluetooth adds its own delay, so it stays the sync knob. It runs a Hann window and a radix-2 FFT over the mono mix and takes each of 64 log-spaced bands' (40 Hz to 16 kHz) loudest bin, 0 at `VIS_FLOOR_DB` below a full-scale sine and 1 at it (`vis_bands`). Bars rise fast and fall at `VIS_DECAY`, and caps hold `VIS_HOLD_MS`, then fall at `VIS_GRAVITY`. The VU needle is a one-pole follower of the window's RMS (tau 65 ms, 99% in 300 ms) on the meter's voltage scale, with 0 VU at `VIS_VU_REF_DB` dBFS; the LED lights at `VIS_LED_DB` dBFS peak. With nothing playing (`mclGetPlayStatus` not 2, or no write in a second) everything comes to rest. Drawing uses the stock vgcanvas entry points (`canvas_get_vgcanvas`, which clips to the canvas, then its path, fill and stroke calls; `float_t` is a float and `color_t` passes by value), translated to the slide area.

The tap sees only what the filter sees. Boot instantiates the filter only when an EQ was left on (`PLAYSET/EQFLAG`, see [Parametric EQ](#parametric-eq)). So with the EQ off the page's first timer tick (outside the paint) while something plays calls `peq_chain` (`patch/crossfade.c`), which calls `peq_attach` (`patch/peq_ui.c`) once per hciplayer connect, unless the EQ is on or any `peq_stock_eq` since has already put the filter in (`peq_chained`, cleared by `ringnav_mode`'s connect-time call when the EQ is off, since a new hciplayer starts without the filter). `peq_attach` does what the editors' Off does, `peq_stock_eq`, which instantiates the filter in bypass, after making sure the [EQ state](#3eq) is saved Off, so a mode saved on while the stock flag is clear stays silent. Bypass is bit-exact, so 16- and 24-bit streams reach the output unchanged through the float conversion. DSD passes through the filter untouched, so during DSD the page rests. Boot itself is left alone: a listener who never opens the page keeps the stock chain.

## Boot resume (iPod)

Stock `home_page_init` resumes when Memory Playback (`g_memory_play`) or car mode (`g_carmode`) is set. `memeory_startplayer` (`0x5164e0`) reads the saved state, rebuilds the queue into a new deque, sets the saved position with `mclSetStartSeekTime` when `g_memory_play` is 2 or in car mode, and returns the track index; it neither plays nor opens a page. Home then calls `navigator_to_with_context("playing_page", ctx)` (the `jalr` at `0x523de0`) with `ctx` `{queue, index, class, mode}`, mode 2 in car mode and 3 otherwise. `playing_page_init` passes `ctx` to `player_start` unless the class is `0xff`; `player_start` loads the queue, starts the player and pauses it (`mclSetPause`) unless the mode is 2.

iPod makes that `jalr` a checked `jal ringnav_boot`; the delay slot still stores the mode. In car mode `ringnav_boot` makes the stock call unchanged. Otherwise it makes the page's `player_start` call without the page, so the queue and track come back paused and Home stays; the saved time is restored in Location mode, while Track starts at the beginning. Home destroys its deque afterwards, as before, since `mclLoadPlayList` copies it. The page's two resets before `player_start` (key-seek mode and a countdown) are page state, set again when it opens. `hciplayer` touches `/tmp/notfile` or `/tmp/notsupport` for a missing or unsupported track; the window manager's `on_wm_timer_dialog` (`0x4ea784`) turns them into a `msginfo_dialog` toast on any page, and `playing_page_init` only deletes stale ones, so a bad restored track still shows its toast on Home.

The English stock labels are Memory Playback: Off / Track / Location and
System: In-Vehicle Mode. Home's Now Playing action (`on_home_playing_onclick`
at `0x5237d0`) calls `on_backplay_click` (`0x4e805c`), which opens the player with
class `0xff`; this skips `player_start` and preserves the paused state. Play/Pause
resumes audio.

## Scrub (iPod)

**Stock audit.** `playing_page` is not a navigation context, so a centre release (218, the power key) passed through to `on_wm_keyup_fun`, whose `screen_action` (`0x4f77d4`) turns the screen off, and a wheel release to the same handler, which changes the volume and opens `dialog/volume_dialog`. The page's own key-up handler (`0x52c248`) only knows Return and keys 222/223. Those drive stock's key seek: a long press sets `0xa3a6c0` (`0x529df0`), key-down (`0x52c754`) calls `playing_timer_clear` (`0x52c634`), sets the mode byte `0xa3a6c1` to 1, steps `slider_play` 5 seconds within `[0, max]` and writes `label_playtime`, and key-up calls `player_seek_time` (`0x51454c`) with the slider value, then `playing_timer_start` (`0x52c114`). A touch drag takes the slider's handlers from the page's `widget_foreach` visitor (`0x52ae6c`): value-changing (`0xf`, `0x52c950`) writes the label and, in mode 0, clears the timer and sets mode 2; value-changed (`0xe`, `0x52c5fc`) seeks the same way only in mode 2 (`0x52c364`). `player_seek_time` takes track seconds: it adds the current record's CUE start (`+0x48`), pauses DLNA, calls `mclSetSeek` (`0x5ab1ac`), then polls `player_playtime` every 10 ms, up to 2 s, until it reaches the target. `player_seek_start` (`0x5143a8`) is DLNA's and not used. `slider_set_value` emits will-change (`0xd`) and changed (`0xe`), never changing, so a set value in mode 0 neither seeks nor writes the label.

**Scrub.** On the top `playing_page`, with no window animation or pressed pointer, the key-up hook takes the centre button. A release arms a `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` timer, and a second release before it ends cancels it and starts scrub. While scrubbing, `center_press` arms no timer: a release ends the scrub at once, and a release within `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` after that one is swallowed, so neither a single nor a double press turns the screen off. Otherwise the timer, a single press, replays the centre release to stock `on_wm_keyup_fun` (`0x4e8934`, which reads only the event's key there), so the screen turns off as on any other page; a release after an overdue timer runs it first and then passes through to stock. **Display → Controls → Single Press Scrub** (`IPOD SCRUBSINGLE`, off unless chosen; `st.scrub_single`) swaps the two: the timer (`np_scrub`) starts the scrub, a second release before it ends replays the release to `on_wm_keyup_fun` as the single press otherwise does, and a release after an overdue timer, whose scrub just started, ends that scrub instead of reaching stock. Either way, outside a scrub, with `dialog/volume_dialog` on top and Now Playing directly under it (`vol_band`, where `vol_paint` draws its band), the keys are the page's: a centre release takes the same path rather than reaching stock, which turned the screen off at the first, so without the swap a single press there turns it off `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` later, as on the bare page, and a wheel tick during that wait still goes to stock's volume without cancelling the press. `scrub_start` closes that dialog with `window_close`, as stock's own tap beside the bar does (`on_volume_tsup_fun`; its close timer is a widget timer, gone with the dialog), so the wheel's ticks reach the scrub and the band no longer covers the bar; on the bare page this also closes a band a tick opened during the wait. The close sends Now Playing `EVT_WINDOW_TO_FOREGROUND`, whose stock handler (`0x52c1dc`) refreshes the page, setting the bar to the player's second, and restarts its timer, so `scrub_start` reads the target and clears the timer after closing. After a paused scrub that refresh drops the bar's held target, as any window closing over the page does, the dialog's own timeout included. Wake is unchanged either way: a press that wakes the screen never reaches the page, and one within `WAKE_MS` after it is dropped (`wake_gate`), so with the swap a press soon after waking starts a scrub rather than turning the screen off. Starting (`scrub_start`) calls `playing_timer_clear`, reads the slider value as the target and sets the slider's fill (`fg_color` in the normal, pressed, over and focused states) to white. While scrubbing, a wheel release moves the target by `SCRUB_STEP` (5) seconds times the 100 ms ramp's step (`ramp` keyed on the slider with `WHEEL_RAMP_MS`, so 5 to 40 seconds; the lists' gentler curve does not apply: every tick moves the target), clamped to `[0, max]`. It sets the slider value, writes `label_playtime` in stock's format and syncs the remaining label, marks the target moved if the step changed it (a tick against an end does not), rearms the `SCRUB_MS` (3 s) end and swallows the release; nothing seeks while ticks come in, however far apart. Ending seeks once, with `player_seek_time(target)`, the stock key path's commit, so CUE tracks stay track-relative, and only if the target moved; it then restores the accent's light-tone fill. Paused (`mclGetPlayStatus` 3), `player_seek_time` would resume the track (`config_outputchannel`, then `mclSetResume`) to reach the target, so the scrub sends the seek alone, `mclSetSeek(target + the record's CUE start)`, as `player_seek_time` adds it: hciplayer's `mcl-seek` handler (`0x42c5b4`) mutes the DAC and flags the seek, and its seek (`0x42b010`) leaves the DAC muted while paused, so the track stays paused and plays from the target at Play. Unlike `player_seek_time`, it does not pause DLNA first, so a paused seek is not reported to a DLNA controller. After a seek, `playing_timer_start` waits for `settle_poll` (every `SETTLE_MS`, 250 ms): paused, the player's second stands still until Play, and the page's timer would put the bar back on the old second. The bar keeps the target until `player_playtime_and_length` is within a second of it, the track changes, the player stops, or `SETTLE_PLAY_MS` (2 s) of playing pass without it; a new scrub, the lyrics' wheel or an exit drops the wait (an exit that ends a scrub keeps the one its seek starts). A paused seek is remembered with the player's second when it was sent: reopening Now Playing on the same track, still paused and at that second, puts the bar back on the target and waits again, so a new scrub starts from it; playing or another track forgets it. Without a seek the timer restarts at once. It ends on a centre press, the timeout, Return (swallowed on the page), a touch (before the slider sees it, so a drag seeks the stock way), another top window, or the page's `EVT_DESTROY`, which drops the target. After the playing track changed (queue position and path, recorded at the start) the target is dropped: the next tick ends the scrub without moving it, and any other end does not seek. `player_seek_time` can block the UI thread briefly, as stock's key seek does; it now runs once per scrub instead of after every pause of 150 ms between ticks. The mode byte stays 0 throughout. Outside scrub the wheel stays on the volume.

**Lyrics.** The page's 250 ms timer also keeps the lyrics in view: when `mclGetLyricSize()` is positive it finds the current line among `scroll_lrc`'s children (`0x52bcc8`), sums the rows' heights up to three lines before it (stock creates a row 20 px tall, or 25 px a line when its text is wider than 225 px and wraps, `0x52a0c8`), and calls `scroll_view_scroll_to(scroll_lrc, 0, y, 10)` whenever the view's y offset (`+0x84`, `SCROLL_Y`) differs, so a moved offset lasts one tick. That y is a row top, but `scroll_view_scroll_to` (`0x5f0178`) clamps it to the end, content height (`+0x7c`, `VIEW_CONTENT_H`) less the view's 178 px, which is no whole number of rows, so near the end the top row was cut through. The call (`jalr` at `0x52bed0`, `LYRIC_FOLLOW`) becomes `jal ringnav_lyric_follow`: `lyric_pad` first grows the content to the first row top at or past that end plus the view's height, so the end is a row top and under a row of blank space shows below the last line. A content no taller than the view is left as is.
