The page's refresh (`0x52b6f4`) rebuilds the rows (`0x529f0c`, which ends with `scroll_view_set_offset(scroll_lrc, 0, 0)`) whenever `g_forcerefresh_flag` is set: Play/Pause (`playpause_quick_click`), the player's state changes and the page's return to the front (`EVT_WINDOW_TO_FOREGROUND`, `0x12b`) all set it, so the lyrics jumped to the top until the next follow scrolled back. Its one call (`bal` at `0x52b8f8`, `LYRIC_ROWS`) becomes `jal ringnav_lyric_rows`, which puts back the offset the view had; the follow moves it if the line changed. Limit: the rebuilt content is its natural height until the next follow pads it, so near the end the relayout's clamp can cut the top row for up to 250 ms, as above. Limits: scroll_view's relayout (`0x5f17a8`) recomputes the height from the children and clamps the offset, which cuts the row again until the next follow, at most 250 ms on. `ringnav_playing` keeps `slide_view` and `scroll_lrc`.

**Lyrics wheel.** Every UI loop pass, `lyric_poll` (iPod, from `ringnav_sleep`) checks for the lyrics page in front: `slide_view`'s value is 1, `playing_page` is the top window, `usable()`, no window animation or pressed pointer, `scroll_lrc` has children and `mclGetLyricSize()` is positive. While it is, `wheel_raw` is set, as for [Vortex](#games), so the wheel posts no ticks and each pass takes the raw travel from `wheel_travel` (200 units a turn). Setting or clearing it zeroes the travel, so nothing from before carries over, and the wheel is back on ticks and the volume within a pass of leaving. The travel moves a target, in sixteenths of a pixel so that slow turns keep their fractions: `LYRIC_ROW_PX` (20, stock's row; a line, `LYRIC_SLOT`, on the [lyrics page](#word-timed-lyrics)) per tick's worth of travel at the Wheel Sensitivity (`WHEEL_TICK_UNITS` x 50 / `wheel_pct()` units), clockwise down, within `[0, content height - view height]` after `lyric_pad`. A turn moves the lyrics as far as its ticks did, but a pixel at a time instead of a row. Each pass then glides the offset towards the target (`lyric_step`, `scroll_view_set_offset`): a `dt / (dt + LYRIC_EASE_MS)` share of the way left (50 ms, `dt` capped at it), at least a pixel, so the motion eases in whatever the loop's pace. Starting a glide stops any stock scroll animator on the view, a drag's fling among them. While a finger stays on the wheel the lyrics stay where its travel put them; once it lifts (`wheel_held`, which `wheel_sample` sets at a finger's first sample and clears at its lift, raw or not) after travel moved them, the glide's target becomes the row top nearest it (`lyric_snap`), so no row is left cut at the top; the end is a row top already (`lyric_pad`). No key event reaches stock, so the travel itself sets `ringnav_input`'s idle stamp and calls `reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 1)`, which keeps the loop at full pace and the screen on; `np_animating` keeps the pace while a glide moves. A tick that reaches the page before a pass has taken the wheel raw goes to `np_lyrics`, ahead of scrub, which glides to the next or previous row's top from the glide's target, so quick ticks add up.

**Lyrics hold.** Moving the lyrics, by wheel travel, a tick, or a finger that dragged `scroll_lrc` (a press that came down on the lyrics page, with `playing_page` on top, and the offset different at its release; stock's scroll animator stops at the press, so only the finger moves them, and a swipe that brings the page in holds nothing), ends any scrub (`scrub_end`, committing a moved target) and holds them (`lyric_hold`, keeping the playing track's `np_track`). Stock's timer keeps running, so the bar, the times and the highlighted line go on, but `ringnav_lyric_follow` returns without scrolling while the lyrics are held on that track, and while a pointer is pressed. A swipe to the art, info or visualizer page and back keeps the hold; the page's destruction (`np_gone`, leaving Now Playing) ends it, and so does another Now Playing replacing the page before it goes (`ringnav_playing`, as `np_gone` leaves a replaced page alone), as does full screen. Another song (`np_track`: queue place and file, checked every pass while the page exists, on top or not) drops it too and puts the lyrics at the top at once, with no glide, stopping stock's animator (`lyric_top`). So does `ringnav_lyric_rows` when it rebuilds the rows for another song than the last rows' (`lyric_rows_song`): it leaves stock's offset 0 instead of putting back the old one, whatever moved the view since the song changed. Stock's `scroll_view_scroll_to` animates for 300 ms whatever its duration, ignores a new target while an animation whose goal is the top or the end runs, and an animation started with the screen off can still be moving when it comes back on: a follow sent with the last song's rows could otherwise carry the new song's lyrics back to the old place, or move them under a swipe onto the page. Off the page, not held, the follow is stock's `scroll_view_scroll_to`, so the lyrics are on the current line when you swipe back; on the page it glides there instead (`lyric_glide`). Another window on top, the screen off or a touch leave the hold alone. Any other page, or no lyrics, leaves the wheel on the volume. Before 1.4.6 the wheel jumped a row a tick and stopped stock's timer, bar and highlight included, until `SCRUB_MS` after the last tick or a touch, when the page jumped back to the current line. Limits: a drag on the screen stops anywhere, so it can leave the top row cut, and one that ends where it began holds nothing; a song repeated on its own (Repeat One) is the same song, so lyrics held on it stay held.

**Lyrics search.** Now Playing's ⋯ lyrics match (`onlinematching_page`) runs stock's `online_lyricthd` on its own thread: `online_setkeyword_info` has filled `keyinfo` (`0xa40424`: file name, title at +0x200, album at +0x400, artist at +0x500) from the playing track's tags; the thread copies it (`online_getkeyword_info`, under its lock) and searches `title/artist` with `kugou_obtainlyric` (`0x510744`: `mobilecdn.kugou.com` search, then `krc.php`), then `com163_obtainlyric` (NetEase `api/search/pc` and `song/media`, which now want a phone-bound account and return no lyric). Each writes the reply to `/tmp/oslyric_<thread>`, which the page shows. Accepting it (`0x524f20`) turns the file name into `<name>.lrc` (`modifystringtolrcsuffix`, `0x511df8`: its last `.` on made `.lrc`) and, with a card (`/tmp/mmc_add`), `snprintf`s `cp -rf /tmp/oslyric_N "/mnt/mmc/.downpic/<name>.lrc"` (format `0x772630`) for `system()`, then reloads the lyrics (`mclLoadExLyric`); it never saved beside the song. That call (`jalr` at `0x525484`, `LYRIC_SAVE_CALL`; the format's `addiu` before it is pinned) becomes `jal ringnav_lyric_save`: for the queue's local song (the playing one first) whose file name stock turns into that `<name>.lrc`, not a CUE track's, it copies the file to `<path without extension>.lrc` beside the song through `.part`, removes `.downpic/<name>.lrc`, which the loader would show first, and leaves the command `:`, so `system()` does nothing and the reload finds the new file; otherwise stock's command. Limits: a song whose file name another song shares is saved beside the first of them in the queue; stock's "no lyrics" choice still removes only `.downpic`'s copy. Kugou counts as found whenever the transfer completes, so NetEase ran only offline. The entry of `kugou_obtainlyric` jumps to `ringnav_lyric_search` (`patch/onlinematch.c`): it copies `keyinfo` the same way, asks `https://lrclib.net/api/search?track_name=&artist_name=` (percent-encoded, `lrclib_url`) through libcurl with the bundled CA file (stock's `curl_http_request` turns certificate checks off), parses the reply with demo's cJSON and writes the first result's `syncedLyrics`, less its opening credit lines (`lrc_body`: each line before the first sung one whose text holds `:`, as NetEase's `作词 : X` or `Mix Engineer : Y`, with any blank line among them; a tag line such as `[offset:...]` ends the search and stays, and a result with no sung line is skipped), UTF-8 with a BOM as Kugou's, to the same file; without one it writes nothing, and the page finds no lyrics. When the search finds no synced lyrics and taking the accents off the title or artist changes them (`lrc_fold`: each Latin letter of U+00C0-U+017F and U+1E00-U+1EFF, Vietnamese's among them, and `ơ`/`ư` as its plain letter, strokes and ligatures too, `ł` l, `ø` o, `đ` d, `æ` ae, `ß` ss, `þ` th, combining marks dropped), it searches those once more: LRCLIB's search already matches `é` to `e` but not `ł` or `ø`, and holds some songs only under the plain names ("Hataałii" finds none of its "Hataalii" entries). A reply that isn't a results array (LRCLIB's 503 `ServerOverloaded`, below) is asked again 2 s later, 3 requests in all, as Download Album Lyrics does (`LRC_BUSY`). LRCLIB is the only source: the hook never resumes Kugou's search, whose first result was often another song's (a song LRCLIB lacks got unrelated Chinese lyrics), and returns nonzero so that `online_lyricthd` skips NetEase too, whose `api/search/pc` now answers code -462 (bind a phone) and finds no song. Limits: no duration check, so a live or remastered version's timing may come back; plain-only results are skipped, since stock's loader drops lines without a stamp; the reply is capped at 512 KB (`LRCLIB_MAX`); a sung opening line with `:` in it is cut as a credit, while credits written `作词：X` (no spaces), after a tag line or after the first sung line stay.

**Cover search.** The same page's cover match runs `online_searchcover` (`0x511f40`): it clears stock's cover list (`p_deque_onlinecover` `0xa39bec`, under `ponline_mutex`), adds `scover_default` (the track's current cover) and, when `/mnt/mmc/.downpic/<file name>.jpg` exists, that path, then starts `online_coverthd` (`0x511974`). The thread copies `keyinfo`, builds the keyword `title&artist` (`artist&album` without a title), URL-encodes it and calls `kugou_obtaincover` (`0x5112c4`), `com163_obtaincover` (`0x5117bc`) and, while the list holds under 12, `baidu_obtaincover` (`0x510f2c`), then sets `onlinematch_cover_threadflag` (`0x99f621`). Each source adds image URLs with `online_addcoverlist` (`0x510af8`: up to 0x200 bytes, a duplicate string is dropped); Kugou's parser (`0x511070`) takes only a `.jpg` URL, only while the list holds under 12, and only if a HEAD (`getDownloadFileLength`) reports under 1 MB or fails. Once the flag is set the page makes one 110 px `onlinecover_%d` image per entry, three to a row, and queues them all (`online_coverlist_init`, `batchadd_onlinecover_task`): stock's cover thread downloads each URL with `curl_http_request` (redirects followed, at most 3, 5 s in all, no certificate check) to `/tmp/online/ori%d.jpg` and sizes it to 330 px `thumb%d.jpg` (`toolsTidalSpecCover`). Picking another than the first sizes that thumbnail into `.downpic/<file name>.jpg` and `.downpic/s_<md5>.jpg` (`toolsThumbSpecCover`, under `parse_cover_mutex`, `0x525244`); picking the first removes both. The entry of `kugou_obtaincover` jumps to `ringnav_cover_search` (`patch/onlinematch.c`), which adds its own candidates first, then runs stock's Kugou search. With the album (else the title) and artist from `keyinfo` it asks MusicBrainz `ws/2/release/?query=release:"ALBUM" AND artist:"ARTIST"&fmt=json&limit=10` (Lucene-escaped and percent-encoded, `mb_url`; no artist clause without one) and, for each new `release-group` `id`, at most 4, sends a HEAD to the Cover Art Archive's `release-group/<id>/front-500` (5 s each): a redirect (307 today) names the group's front as a 500 px JPEG thumbnail on archive.org (`REDIRECT_URL`), which is added; a 404, no art, adds nothing. Then iTunes Search `search?entity=album&limit=5&term=ARTIST ALBUM` (`itunes_url`): each `artworkUrl100` with its last `100x100` made `600x600` (`itunes_art`), a JPEG. Ours stop once the list holds 10 (`COVER_FILL`), so stock's sources still fill it to 12, and fill it alone when ours find nothing. Requests use libcurl with the bundled CA file (DigiCert Global Root G2 signs MusicBrainz and iTunes, ISRG the Cover Art Archive), `Q2POD_UA` (`Q2Pod/<version> (repository URL)`, as MusicBrainz asks), 10 s to connect and 20 s in all (the HEADs 5 s), replies capped at 256 KB (`COVER_MAX`), on stock's cover thread; the images themselves are fetched by stock's thread as Kugou's. One MusicBrainz request per search keeps under its 1 request a second. Limits: a failed MusicBrainz request skips iTunes too, as a likely sign of no internet, so with Wi-Fi up but none the page waits one 10 s connect timeout more than stock; MusicBrainz answers a busy server with an error, which adds none of its covers; archive.org can take more than stock's 5 s download, which leaves that tile on the default art; the same cover from two services shows twice; only release groups' chosen fronts are offered, not each release's.

**Album lyrics.** Now Playing's ⋯ page (`playermore_page_init`, `0x528c14`, hooked by `ringnav_playermore` in `patch/onlinematch.c`) builds 8 rows, each button named by its position; its row click (`0x528a70`) `atoi`s the name into a table of kinds (`PLAYMORE_ROWS`, `0x7728a0`: 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 0, 1), so Download Cover & Lyrics, kind 4 in `get_playmore_showinfo`, is third. For a playing song on the card with an album tag (not an online queue, `MCL_TYPE` `0xf1`/`0xf2`), the hook adds **Download Album Lyrics** under it in `list_row`'s widgets, with stock's `player_onlinesearch` icon and its rows' 20 px, 253 px label; the button has no name, so stock's click never sees it. A press needs Wi-Fi (`get_wifisignal`), then copies the album's tracks off stock's records (`coverflow_album_tracks`, as Go to Album: the album's songs in disc and track order; a CUE track is left out, as it shares its file's `.lrc`) and starts one worker thread (`album_lyrics`), which outlives the page. A 500 ms timer shows "Downloading N of M" on the row while the page is open, keeps standby and auto power-off waiting (`reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 0)`), then joins the worker and toasts the summary (`album_note`). A press while it runs cancels it ("Cancelling…"); USB storage (`ringnav_usbmode`) cancels and joins it first, quietly. Each track is on its own: a file gone from the card since the library's scan is passed over; one with a `.lrc` or `.LRC` beside it is left as it is; any other is searched exactly as the single search above searches LRCLIB (`lrclib`, shared: the record's title and artist, the first result with synced lyrics, less its opening credits), its lyrics written to `<name>.lrc.part` and renamed to `<name>.lrc` once whole, and then stock's `.downpic` copy for it (`lrc_name` of its file name), which would hide it, removed; a song LRCLIB lacks keeps that copy. LRCLIB answers some uncached searches with a 503 `ServerOverloaded` object (`Retry-After: 1`) instead of a results array, and the same search a moment later with results; the single search and the job both ask again 2 s later (`LJ_RETRY_US`), up to 3 requests a track (`LRC_BUSY`, `LJ_BUSY_TRIES`). Once LRCLIB has answered in the job, a failed request is retried once, 2 s later; two tracks in a row unanswered or still busy end it ("Can't reach LRCLIB", or "LRCLIB stopped answering" after some answered), as does a card that can't be written. A cancel stops a transfer at curl's next progress call (`XFERINFOFUNCTION`). However it ends, no `.part` stays and the lyrics saved before stay. Limits: one album at a time; a song without a title tag counts as not found; LRCLIB down while the internet is up reads as offline; as the single search, a live or remastered version's timing may come back.

**Lyrics from tags.** Stock's `mclLoadExLyric` (`0x5aac90`) clears the list and, unless the queue is online (`MCL_TYPE` `0xf1`/`0xf2`), loads the playing record's `<path without extension>.lrc` or `.LRC`; before them, for source types 3, 6, 12, 13, 15, 21 and 23 (the word at `0xa40000 - 0x413c`, mask `0xa0b048`), `.downpic/<name>.lrc` (`/mnt/mmc/`, or `/tmp/` without a card), and for play type `0xf0` `/tmp/hifi/`, so a stale `.downpic` copy hides the `.lrc` beside the song (`0x5a9718`). It keeps only lines whose `[` is followed by a digit. A file without a byte-order mark that it does not take for UTF-8 it converts from GB18030 first (`iconv` to `/tmp/templyric`). Its test, `mclIsUtf8WithoutBOM` (`0x5a959c`, also the lyrics match page's), read only the 6 bytes from the first non-ASCII one and knew no 4-byte character, so a UTF-8 `.lrc` with an emoji, or with a character starting 4 or 5 bytes after that first one (`é` and three letters, then `’`; Korean's one-syllable words), was converted and loaded garbled or cut short. Its entry jumps to `ringnav_lrc_utf8` (`patch/lyrics.c`), which reads the first `LYRIC_TAG_MAX` bytes and checks them with `utf8_text`, the tag check in `patch/xiph.c`; a file that fails it still goes through stock's GB18030 conversion. Limit: a multi-byte character cut by that 64 KB read fails it. `ringnav_lyric` runs it first; when `mclGetLyricSize()` is still 0 for a local song (`REC_TYPE` 8, an absolute path), it opens the file with `taglib_file_new` and reads `LYRICS`, else `UNSYNCEDLYRICS`, with libtag_c's `taglib_property_get`, which demo does not import: its address is the one that call bound in demo's GOT (`TAGLIB_NEW_GOT`) minus `TAGLIB_FILE_NEW`, plus the pinned library's offset, and an entry still holding demo's stub reads nothing. Each line (CR, LF or CRLF), without the spaces at its ends, goes into stock's `stLyricInfo` list (`MCL_LYRICS`, under `MCL_LYRIC_LOCK`) at time `0x7fffffff`, which no track reaches: the page's timer then keeps the first line current and the view at the top, the wheel scrolls it as above, and the line `0x4d1748` shows in `label_title` stays the default (both read from the disassembly, `0x52bd3c` and `0x4d1820`). No temporary file is written. Limits: unsynced text only, so an ID3 `SYLT` frame is not read and `[mm:ss]` stamps inside a tag show as text; lines starting past the first 64 KB (`LYRIC_TAG_MAX`) are dropped; the file's tags are parsed once more on every track start without a `.lrc`, on the thread that starts it.

## Word-timed lyrics

iPod's lyrics page (`patch/lyrics.c`; [ipod.md](ipod.md#now-playing)): large lines, the playing one centred and lit word by word.

**Times.** Stock's `.lrc` parser (`0x5a9718`, inside `mclLoadExLyric`) keeps each line's whole seconds (`toolsTimeConvert`, `0x5bc28c`) and the text after its last `]`, A2 word stamps and all. Its `jalr fopen` of the file it parses (`0x5a9c5c`, `LYRIC_OPEN`; after the encoding probe and any conversion, so the file is UTF-8) becomes `jal ringnav_lrc_open`, which opens it as stock asked and keeps a copy of its first `LYRIC_TAG_MAX` bytes. After stock's load `ringnav_lyric` parses that copy as it parses a LYRICS tag (`lrc_parse`): leading `[mm:ss.xx]` stamps to the millisecond (`.x`, `.xx`, `.xxx` or `:xx`), and A2 `<mm:ss.xx>` word stamps cut from the text, kept for a line with one stamp as each word's millisecond and byte offset; a UTF-8 byte-order mark is skipped. Every line loses the spaces at its ends (ASCII's space, tab, vertical tab and form feed, no-break, ideographic and zero-width spaces, a byte-order mark; `lrc_trim`, its word stamps' offsets moved with the text), and a blank one goes: an empty line between verses, a line of spaces, a stamp alone (stock's `" "`, an instrumental break) and the empty line CR CR LF line ends leave between every two. So both lyrics pages keep an even spacing between lines; lyrics that are all blank leave stock's result. Synced lines replace stock's list (`deque_clear`, then each line at its whole seconds, under `MCL_LYRIC_LOCK`), so the list never holds word stamps and is in millisecond order; unsynced ones leave it. Every load then sends its synced lines, or none, to the page (`lyrics_publish`): their UTF-32 text, starts and word stamps, and an FNV-1a sum of each line's whole seconds and characters as stock's rows will hold them.

**Rows.** The page's refresh still rebuilds `scroll_lrc`'s labels (`0x529f0c`, `LYRIC_ROWS`), and so does `playing_page_init` (`0x52cc2c`, before `ringnav_playing` knows the page), so `ringnav_playing` takes the rows its init built too (`lyric_init`). `lyrics_rows` takes the sent table when its sum matches the labels' (`lrc_time` and text), keeps the last one when that matches, and otherwise builds one from the labels (whole seconds, no word stamps), so the page never shows another song's. It lays every line out with `widget_get_canvas`'s canvas at 34 px (`LYRIC_PX`): rows of `375 - 2 * LYRIC_X`, breaking before a space or beside a CJK character, else mid-word, up to `LYRIC_ROWS` (4, which still fit the view) of them, the rest cut. Each label's text is then emptied and it becomes the view's width and its line's rows high, 38 px apart (`LY_PITCH`), plus `LYRIC_GAP` (`LYRIC_SLOT` less a pitch, 21 px, so a line of one row is `LYRIC_SLOT`, 59 px). `lyric_init` gives `scroll_lrc` the list layout `list_view(x=0,y=LYRIC_SLOT,s=0)` (`tools/ipod.py` gives `view_lrc` and `scroll_lrc` the full 375 px), which stacks the rows after a blank slot. A line's follow target centres its row in the view (`lyric_centre`); `lyric_pad` makes the content the last row's plus the view's height, so the last line can reach the middle. `ringnav_paint` draws each row's line (`lyrics_paint`, the row found among the children), its rows centred in it, `LYRIC_X` from the left, in the system font (`canvas_set_font(canvas, NULL, px)`) drawn `LYRIC_BOLD` (2) more times a pixel apart for weight (laid out that much narrower); a row the view's edge cuts takes an alpha of 255 times the square of its share in view, and none under 8 is drawn. The playing line is drawn `LYRIC_LIT` with the clip ending at its lit width and `LYRIC_DIM` with the clip starting there; other lines `LYRIC_REST`. With no memory for the table the rows stay stock's, and the [lyrics wheel](#scrub-ipod) moves them as before, a row a tick.

**Follow.** Stock's follow (`ringnav_lyric_follow`) does nothing on the page. Every UI pass with the page in front and lit, else every `LYRIC_FOLLOW_MS` (250 ms, stock's timer's pace), `lyric_follow` finds the playing line, the first of those starting when the last started line did (lines sharing a stamp light together), and targets the offset that centres its row (line 0's before the first starts): glided to on the page, set at once off it, never while held or while a pointer is down. Wheel travel moves `LYRIC_SLOT` per tick's worth instead of `LYRIC_ROW_PX`. `lyrics_now` reads the clock on those passes, so its bounds narrow on each second's change; off them (another page, another window on top, the screen off) the follow goes by the whole second (`lyrics_sec`, as stock's), which lets the bounds go, so the clock starts again, wide, when the page is back in front. While `playing_page` is on top and lit, the playing line's rows repaint when its lit width changes, at most every `LYRIC_PAINT_MS` (66 ms, about 15 a second; a word-timed line's width moves nearly every pass, and the idle loop passes every 25 ms), and the last line's when another line starts. Limit: the lit sweep moves in steps of up to `LYRIC_PAINT_MS` of the song, a few pixels at a fast word; the value is a guess.

**Full screen.** Stock's page binds nothing to its lyrics, so on either layout `ringnav_playing` binds `view_lyric`'s `EVT_POINTER_DOWN` (before children), `EVT_POINTER_UP_BEFORE` and `EVT_POINTER_ABORT` (`np_lyric_touch`). A release within `TAP_PX` (10, AWTK's drag threshold) of its press either way, so no swipe or drag of the lines, with lyrics on (`lyrics_on`) or already full screen, toggles `np_full`: on, the column (`slide_view_view`) is restacked last among the window's children with a black `bg_color`, moved to x 0 at the window's width, and its `slide_view` sized to the window's width and down to the dots' top (`slide_indicator1`, y 270); `widget_layout` lays the pages out, `view_lrc` ending 20 px above the page's foot (`h=-20`, 178 of 198 px in the column) and "No lyrics" centred (`tools/ipod.py` `playing_stock`), and `lyrics_fit` wraps the rows at the new width. The hold drops, so the follow glides to the playing line. Off, the column goes back to its index, place and `slide_view` height from before (`np_was`), transparent, in the layout it went full screen in (`np_full` 2 for the iPod page's): Centred Now Playing, switched from a window over the page, takes effect only at the next Now Playing. The iPod page's column (`tools/ipod.py` `playing_page`: full width, under the 40 px top row, down to just past the dots; the title is on the art page) takes the whole window instead: y 0 and the window's height, its dots `NP_FULL_FOOT` (20 px) above the foot as Centred's, and `view_lrc`, whose `self_layout` fixes it at the slide's `lift` and 178 px, from the top to 20 px above them (`widget_set_self_layout_params`, the old place written back off); the width stays 375, so nothing rewraps. Return ends it as a tap does, and stays on the page (`STOP`). Handlers return 0, as the heart's do: the `slide_view` behind takes a swipe and `scroll_lrc` a drag of the lines as stock does, and either's abort ends full screen, so the pages slide in their column; a touch taken that way is no tap at its lift (`np_tap_x` `1 << 20`). `lyric_poll` ends it off the lyrics page, while a window over the page leaves it as it is; the page's destruction drops it; a page without `slide_view` has no column, so `np_full` does nothing and full screen never starts. Limits: a tap brings back lyrics the wheel or a finger held; any window over the page, a toast or the volume included, ends it; the covered widgets still repaint under the column (the bar every second, a long title's scroll), redrawing the lyrics beneath them.

**Clock.** `player_playtime_and_length` reports whole seconds, which stock's status thread (`mclSyncPlayerStatus`, `0x5a91c4`) asks hciplayer for every 200 ms (`{media-info\get-music-info}`, its fourth field). `lyrics_now` keeps the offset from `time_now_ms` to the position as an interval: the first second seen, or one after a jump (a seek, another song), puts it anywhere in that second plus a poll (`LYRIC_POLL_MS`, 250); the next second's arrival while playing puts the position at most a poll past that second, and the two intersect, or restart from that change when they disagree. Paused (`mclGetPlayStatus()` not 2) it holds; on Play it goes on from where it stopped until the next second replaces that guess. It never runs more than a poll past its reported second.

**Lit width.** A line without word stamps is lit whole from its start (`ly_lit`). One with them is lit through them, from stamp to stamp, each at its character's distance along the line's rows laid end to end; before the first, from the line's start; past the last, at `LYRIC_UNIT_MS` a letter (90 ms; three for a CJK character; `LYRIC_MIN_MS`, 800, at least), done by the next line's start. Limits: the clock knows the position to about a poll, 0.1 s either way, and doesn't allow for the output's latency (Bluetooth's); a line longer than four rows is cut.

## Pull-to-search

Pull-to-search starts from the existing touch hook only on a real pointer-down (`0x100`; the stock window manager registers `on_wm_tsdown_before_fun` for that type), while the separate move-before callback (`0x102`) tail-calls the same entry point. It applies to Local Songs (`localmusic_page`), the same list where a set with `home_lib` shows it on Home's window (`scroll_view_localmusic` on `home_page`, in Split), and Internet Radio (`radio_page`): Folder view has no working stock search, so it is excluded. It checks the screen coordinates, list bounds and initial scroll offset, then uses page-owned move/up-before-children callbacks (`0x102` / `0x104`). A downward drag of at least `PULL_CLAIM_PX` (8), greater than its horizontal travel, claims the pointer. The native abort event (`0x107`) clears pressed descendants; a widget-owned suppression flag also rejects late row clicks until the next touch. A floating, non-interactive label overlays the page without changing the list or its indices. Release at `PULL_SEARCH_PX` (48) invokes the stock Local Songs search callback `0x5241c4`, which opens `dialog/search_dialog` without replacing the browsing page; on Internet Radio it calls `radio_search` (radio.c) instead. The stock search dialogs quit on Return even while their edit's keyboard is open, which destroys the edit under the keyboard and freezes demo; `search_key` (navigation.c's `ringnav`) reads the editing target from the input method (`IM_WIDGET`, `+0x04`, pinned against stock's `input_method_request`), since the keyboard is a separate top window and its focus chain does not contain the search edit. It closes the keyboard on the first Return (`widget_set_focused(search_edit, 0)` plus `input_method_request`, since the searchbox dialogs' edit does not close on blur), and the next Return runs stock's quit with the keyboard already shut. Moving back below the threshold cancels activation. Page/surface destruction, backgrounding, close and input interruption clear the gesture. The stock quick-settings start region (`y < 31`) is excluded. In a set without Library icons (Classic), `library_search` also puts a **Search** row first in the Library that runs the same callback, which ignores its arguments and opens the dialog with its edit focused (`edit_set_focus`), so the keyboard comes up as after the pull.

`patch/ipod.json` records the added private prologue and native event ABI instructions alongside the compact sites and assets. The builder checks them against the pinned stock executable and checks the byte sizes of all referenced global flags/values.

## Manual skip

Manual Next and Previous run `player_change_music` (`0x51503c`), not `mclNextSong`. At queue index `n-1` (or `0` for Previous) it loads the next (previous) folder itself (class 1 with Folder Skip on), or in List Play wraps to the other end with `mclSetPlayPos`. In List Play, `ringnav_next` and `ringnav_change` stop a library queue (`0xfxxx`) at its last track instead (`list_end`), so the end of an album stops on a manual Next as it does at a natural end; `ringnav_change` returns 0, since its caller toasts -1 (no storage) and -5. In Repeat All with Folder Skip off, every branch calls `mclNextSong(0)`/`mclPrevSong`. While advanced playback is active, `ringnav_change` runs stock with `MCL_MODE` 3 and Folder Skip 0, then restores both, so the hooked `ringnav_next`/`ringnav_prev` choose the track and stock's refresh tail (tags, notifications, Bluetooth headset info) still runs. It writes `MCL_MODE` directly, since `mclSetPlayMode` would end advanced playback. `ringnav_mode` (the hook on `mclSetPlayMode`) ends advanced play through `playback_clear` for a user's stock mode choice, except stock Shuffle (2) while Shuffle Albums/Folders is on, which keeps the groups. `ringnav_prev` clears the `seen` flag of the track it leaves, so the next Next returns to it instead of skipping it. Shuffles draw from `rand_below` (`playback.c`), an xorshift32 seeded once from `toolsRandnum` and `time_now_ms`, because stock's generator alone repeated itself between shuffles started close together (the hold menu lists Next/Previous Album, first, only while `playback_option(0) >= 3`). `player_initconfig` (`0x513374`) also calls it, re-applying the saved mode when hciplayer reconnects (`player_reconfig`, whose flag `mclParseCommand` sets after a disconnect; the first connection at boot never reaches it); that is no mode choice, so `ringnav_mode` passes it straight to stock, told apart by its return address (`INITCONFIG_MODE_RET`, checked by the build). Earlier at boot, `config_init` calls `config_playmode` (`0x4f999c`) before Home restores the queue. That call goes through `ringnav_bootmode`, which marks it as restoration so `ringnav_mode` applies the setting without deleting the snapshot; explicit mode choices still clear advanced playback.

Memory Playback's `ringnav_memory` restores the saved snapshot using `getAllMusic(3)`, which avoids the pinyin title sort. A temporary index (8 bytes per library row) is sorted by file path and CUE start/end, then binary-searched for each saved occurrence. Equal identities retain the query's first row. The snapshot still controls queue order, history and playback position; missing files are skipped and unindexed files retain the stock directory fallback. Index allocation failure frees the temporary state and falls back to stock resume without rewriting the snapshot. `test/playback.py` checks those cases and bounds path comparisons for a 256-track restore so a full-library search per track cannot return unnoticed.

## Bluetooth boot reconnect

Stock `platform_init` starts `btctl_create_daemon_thread` (`0x512598`) before `config_bt_init` calls `openBluetooth`. Registering callbacks at the radio's GPIO write can still miss connections discovered by that thread during startup. `headset_bt_init` initializes the stock Bluetooth lists and installs the stock prompt and A2DP callbacks and `headset_bt_status` (below) before starting the control thread, preserving its thread pointer and return value. The later `initBluetoothParam` call in `config_bt_init` is removed so it cannot erase links discovered during startup. Stock's later registration remains for Bluetooth toggles, with `BT_STATUS` registering `headset_bt_status` there too. `test/patch.py` checks the patched call site, callback order and successful/failed thread-start forwarding.

A headset that connects to the Q2 while stock's link thread waits for another device was lost until Bluetooth was toggled. At boot and at Bluetooth on, `autoLinkBluetooth` (`0x4f17a0`) appends the last device (`BTLINKMAC`) and starts the link thread (`0x4f2068`), which waits up to 15 s for it. Stock's device-status callback (`0x4f1a50`) ignores a Connected event (3) from a device not in the link list while `bt_connectstatus` is set, and BlueZ sends that event once. If the incoming event arrived first, auto-link appended its target after it, and `checkBluetoothStatus` judges only the last item: the absent target's failure ran `clearBluetoothStatus`, which wiped the incoming link too. `BT_AUTOLINK` turns `autoLinkBluetooth`'s `bal checkPairDev` into `jal headset_bt_autolink`, which also skips while a listed device is connected (status 3). `headset_bt_status` wraps the stock callback; `headset_bt_init` registers it, and `BT_STATUS` turns `openBluetooth`'s registration into `jal headset_status_register`. A Connected event from a paired, unlisted device is adopted only while the thread waits (`bt_connectstatus` 1, not 2 once it ended or 3 while `checkBluetoothStatus` judges) for the saved device (its target, the last item, equals `BTLINKMAC` at `0xa38168`) and the thread has taken that target and is connecting it (status 5: not 1, before the thread reads it, nor 3, already connected). The device is appended with status 3 and the target set to 4, so the wait ends and stock's success path keeps the incoming device and switches the output. Everything else goes to stock. While the thread waits, the Bluetooth page ignores taps (`0x4bf314`) and stock adds no items, so the wrapper is the only writer besides the thread. Limits: no global holds the thread's mode, so a connect or pairing the user starts to the saved device from the Bluetooth page is treated like auto-link (a pairing still switches only when its 15 s wait ends), while one to another device still loses an incoming link as stock does. The target's BlueZ Connect is still pending, so if it powers on within its page timeout both may link; that includes the target connecting between the wrapper's check and its update, which then marks it 4. An incoming link whose event arrives after the thread ended but before `checkBluetoothStatus` ran is dropped as in stock. `test/patch.py` checks both patch sites and the wrapper's adopt and pass-through cases.

**Bluetooth abnormal.** When the controller never answers `rtk_hciattach`, `openBluetooth` (`0x4efc58`) retries, then sets `bt_rtk_error`; `checkBluetoothStatus` (`0x4f2cfc`) turns that into `bt_initstatus` (`0xa26990`) -102; and `on_wm_idle_status`, on its next pass, shows **Bluetooth abnormal** (`msg_btabnormal`) and calls `device_reboot` (`0x4e9d0c` and `0x4e9f1c`, its only direct callers). A Q2 whose radio module has failed therefore reboots at every boot, and each reboot was a clean end (`ringnav_clean_end`, through `reboot_save`) that left nothing on the card to say why; Wi-Fi fails with it, as the RTL8733BS is one module (`module_driver/rtl8733bs.sh`). `ringnav_reboot` (`patch/playback.c`, the hooked `device_reboot`) now first calls `reboot_record` (`patch/system.c`): when `bt_initstatus` is -102 it writes `/mnt/data/q2-crash.log` through `system()`, whole then renamed, as `S90play`'s crash path does ([boot.md](boot.md#rockbox-from-home)): `== date`, `== reboot` with the reason, the status, the uptime and the caller's return address, `== dmesg`, its last 200 lines, and `== stderr`, the last 60 lines of `/tmp/q2-demo.err` when a build keeps demo's stderr there, which is where `rtk_hciattach` says what failed. The next boot moves it to the card as any crash log. Any other status (Restart, a factory reset, the power-off page's long press, the firmware update's countdown) writes nothing. `test/fw/test_playback.py` checks the gate and the command, and runs the command under `sh` with stand-ins. ponytail: the record names the symptom, not the cause; `dmesg` and `rtk_hciattach`'s words are what tell a dead module from a UART or firmware fault.

## Bluetooth LDAC quality

The Quality page's rows are LDAC HQ, LDAC Standard, LDAC Connection (auto), aptX HD, aptX, AAC, SBC HQ and SBC (`g_btcoding`, `[SYSSET] BTCODING`). `switchBtPriority` (`0x4f0314`) sends `btctl_set_ldac_quality` over D-Bus to `bluealsa` (`SetLDACQuality`). bluealsa maps 0x15, 0x16 and 0x17 to the encoder's eqmid HQ, SQ and MQ, and maps any other value to adaptive bit rate. Stock sends 0x16 (SQ, 660 kbps, or 606 at 44.1 kHz) for both HQ and Standard. `LDAC_HQ` rewrites the jump-table dispatch to keep `0x15 + row` in the frame's free `0x14($sp)` slot and loads it in place of the constant. HQ then sends 0x15 (990 / 909 kbps), Standard 0x16, and auto still 0x18. Turning Bluetooth on applies the saved row again, and Rockbox plays through the same daemon.

Auto already uses Sony's native ABR controller, including the intermediate 492 and 396 kbps rates (48/96 kHz), alongside 990, 660 and 330. These are discrete encoder frame sizes, not arbitrary 1 kbps increments. The shipped `libldacBT.so.2.0.19` supports them through `ldacBT_alter_eqmid_priority`; `libldacBT_ABR.so.2.0.0` selects them from queue congestion and raises quality after recovery. Bluealsa's `0x416388` maps Auto to an initial SQ encoder with ABR enabled; its encode loop calls `ldac_ABR_Proc` at `0x41ab00` with the queued packet count, using a 40 ms interval and thresholds 12/8/4. The HQ fix changes the fixed HQ row only; Auto could already recover to HQ. `test/ldac.py` executes the shipped encoder's five rates, the daemon's mode mapping and the ABR controller's congestion/recovery paths. No custom rate controller is added. This does not establish a cause or fix for sustained distortion requiring reconnect, or USB-dongle heat; those need device reproduction.

## Bluetooth AAC

When a headset opens the A2DP stream itself (AirPods taken out of the case), it picks AAC at 44.1 kHz. Stock `bluealsa` is still fed 48 kHz audio and drops about 8% of the AAC frames, so the audio is choppy until Bluetooth is toggled; a connection the Q2 opens picks 48 kHz. The build clears the 44.1 kHz bit in `bluealsa`'s AAC source capability, `c0 01 84 84 e2 00` at file offset `0x317b7` (object type, sampling frequencies, channels, VBR and bitrate). Octet 1 holds 8 to 44.1 kHz with 44.1 kHz in bit 0, so `0x317b8` goes from `01` to `00` and headsets pick 48 kHz, which every AAC sink must support. `ZIP_SHA` pins the stock binary, and `test/build.py` audits the allowed byte changes and checks the manifest's `bluealsa_sha256`.

Manual skips also call ALSA DROP. Stock acknowledges it after sending signal 6, but the encoder flushes PCM later (`0x4147ac`), potentially discarding the next track’s first samples. The patched DROP (`0x40cb50`) flushes the nonblocking FIFO under the PCM fd mutex before sending that signal and acknowledging the request. The encoder’s shared DROP handler discards each audited caller’s partial PCM input, restores full input capacity, and resets its pacing clock through the existing OPEN/RESUME path instead of flushing again. Natural gapless transitions do not issue DROP. `test/bluealsa.py` executes both MIPS paths with a delayed signal and empty, normal and oversized old queues; stock loses new audio and the patched binary preserves it.

A manual skip also left the A2DP stream without packets. On a Next mid-song, hciplayer either sends Drain (the PCM still buffered, about 440 ms, drains, then Drain's 100 ms poll times out) or, when demo pauses it first, PAUSE, which stock answers by polling again without a timeout. About 300 ms later it sends DROP, closes the PCM and opens the next one. Traced on a Q2 (stock 1.5.4, AAC), no packet left for 404 ms. The headset's buffer ran dry, and OPEN/RESUME restarted the encoder's pacing (asrsync) from zero, so the new song played on with the buffer near-empty and crackled. A phone keeps its stream running through a skip. `tools/bt_silence.S`, linked at `BT_SILENCE` in bluealsa's payload, patches the shared `io_poll_and_read_pcm` (`0x4145d0`), which every encoder loop calls (all six callers are A2DP encoders). The build points the signal jump table's PAUSE entry (`0x42d09c`) at Drain's case (`0x4147a0`, a 100 ms poll). When that timeout ends and real PCM was read within `BT_SILENCE_SAMPLES` (192000 interleaved samples: 2 s at 48 kHz stereo, 1 s at 96 kHz), the function still wakes Drain's waiter but returns the requested samples as zeros, and from then on it polls without waiting. The encoder's own pacing sets the rate and catches up the 100 ms in a burst that refills the headset's buffer. The next real PCM ends the fill and re-arms the budget; once the budget is spent it behaves as stock. Without a Drain, a poll still blocks as stock, so a stall mid-song is caught up as before. Traced again, a skip leaves 110–131 ms without packets, which is the 100 ms wait. The small click left at a skip is heard with stock as well; the PCM on either side of the skip had no step in it. ponytail: one budget and fill flag serve every encoder thread, which is fine for one headset at a time. A second stream, or a stream torn down while filling, shares or inherits up to the budget's silence. A pause or stop streams up to the budget's silence before the link goes quiet. Unchecked on LDAC Auto: whether the catch-up burst makes its ABR step down a rate at each skip. `test/bluealsa.py` runs the patched function: stock returning nothing at Drain's timeout and polling unbounded after PAUSE, PAUSE's 100 ms poll, the fill for S16 and S32, Drain's wake, unpaced polls and pacing init, a closed PCM, the budget running out and re-arming, and stock behaviour without a budget.

## Bluetooth keep-alive

`hciplayer` closes its ALSA PCM at a stop, a skip and a cold start (a gapless change keeps it open: [USB output buffer](#usb-output-buffer)), and stock starts `bluealsa` (`/usr/bin/bluealsa2`) without `--keep-alive`, so the A2DP transport is released and every song begins with a fresh AVDTP start. True-wireless buds start the primary bud (often the right) first and the other joins a moment later, so one ear began early on every track. The build adds `--keep-alive=5` to that launcher (`BT_KEEPALIVE`): the transport stays open 5 s after the PCM closes, which covers a track change. ponytail: inferred from bluealsa's option and the report, not traced on a Q2; the headset stays in streaming state up to 5 s after a stop, and a pause longer than that still restarts the stream.

## Clock

The timezone picker keeps stock's saved indices (`g_time_zone`, 0-26) and appends the missing offsets and a few populous zones. `TIMEZONES` in `tools/build.py` holds every index's label and `TZ`. `toolsSetTimeZone` (`0x5bc4f4`) is only `setenv("TZ", name, 1)`, and `ringnav_timezone` replaces stock's mapping (`select_timezone`, `0x4f93b0`) for every index, so glibc resolves an IANA name under `/usr/share/zoneinfo` or, failing that, reads a POSIX rule. The rootfs tzdata is older than 2022, so Mexico City (`CST6`), Tehran (`<+0330>-3:30`) and Fiji (`<+12>-12`) take POSIX rules without the daylight saving they have since dropped. Stock's GMT-12 row set Pacific/Kwajalein, which is +12, so it is `<-12>12` now. Its +6 Almaty row is Asia/Dhaka, since Almaty is +5 from 2024 and the rootfs still says +6. `tools/build.py` writes the labels as literal text (no `strings/*.bin` key, so every language shows them as written) into the existing label table at `0x76b5d0`, compacted to 32-byte slots, and extends the row counts and boot validation. The page's row builder (`0x4bcd1c`, `(item, i)`; row 0 is the switch) labels, names (`atoi` in the click handler, less one, is the saved index) and ticks a row by the index it is given. As row 1 is built, `ringnav_timezone_row` sets each zone's `TZ` in turn and reads its offset from UTC now (`localtime_r`'s `tm_gmtoff`), then sets the saved zone again; row i is handed the i-th zone west to east by that offset (ties in `TIMEZONE_ORDER`: standard offset, then name), plus one, and its label is rewritten as that offset and the city (`TIMEZONE_CITIES`), so New York reads GMT-4 in summer and GMT-5 in winter, as its clocks do. Labelled by standard offset, a New Yorker in summer took GMT-4 Halifax, an hour ahead. ponytail: another thread's `localtime` while row 1 builds may read one of the other zones, as `TZ` is process-wide. Stock's `set_hscroll_label_attribute` scrolls every too-long label once as the page opens; the hook makes the row's label focus-only, so one that does not fit keeps its ellipsis. The labels are sized to fit the 246 px label at 24 px. The table's labels state the standard offset; the rows show the one in effect. ponytail: the rules are fixed in the build and the rootfs tzdata stays as it is, so a zone that changes its rules later needs a new row or rule here.

Stock loses the time zone across a power-off. Setting the time, by hand or from the network (`toolsSetRtcTime`, `0x5bc760`), runs `date -s` and then `hwclock -w`. BusyBox 1.31.1's `hwclock` writes local time unless `/var/lib/hwclock/adjtime` says `UTC`, and the rootfs has no such file, so the RTC gets the wall time of the `TZ` the player exported (`toolsSetTimeZone`). At boot the kernel (`hctosys`) reads the RTC as UTC and the player adds the time zone again, so the clock comes back off by the zone's UTC offset until the next network sync. The build changes the literal at `0x777928`, `hwclock -w` and two NULs, to `hwclock -wu`, so the RTC holds UTC as the kernel expects. An RTC last written by stock still holds local time, so the time has to be set once after updating.

The status bar, Auto Light Mode and timezone picker use `localtime_r` with their own `struct tm` buffers. Stock's background time conversions can overwrite `localtime`'s shared buffer while the UI reads it, briefly displaying another time. The status bar's clock and Auto Light Mode read the time once a minute: `clock_show` writes the label, applies Auto's hour and sets a timer for the next minute, and runs at once for a new zone (`ringnav_timezone`), a time set by hand (`time_click`) and a wake, and on the pass after the bar first paints or paints with a new 24-hour setting. Before, every paint of the bar or the top window read the time, and Auto Light Mode's check read it on every UI pass (125 a second). ponytail: a network time sync while lit shows at the next minute. The payload resolves demo's imported `tzset` once (it writes no shared buffer), then calls the pinned libc's `localtime_r` at its checked offset from it. Timezone changes explicitly call `tzset`, since `localtime_r` does not reload `TZ` itself.

## Lock screen

Stock's lock window (`saverscreen_page.bin`, 375x320, fullscreen, so no status bar) shows `g_screensaver_picture` (`SYSSET SCREENSAVERPATH`): the asset `screensaver_default`, else the file if `access()` finds it, else the name as an asset again. The picker on **System settings → Display → Lock screen** (`screensaver_page`) lists `p_deque_screensaverlist`, a libcstl `char*` deque that `get_screensaver_image` fills: up to five card images from `lockscreenimage` (`toolsGetScreenSaverPic`, 375x320, 1 MB), then the default pushed in front. It also resets a picture that is neither the default nor a file. Its list builder (`0x4c9134`, rebuilt when the page's switch turns) makes Time show and About, then, with the switch on, the 3-column grid of 110x94 `image_%d` thumbnails: the default from its asset, the rest by `file://`.

**Wallpapers** (`patch/lockscreen.c`). Six built-ins are drawn by the payload, since the Stock rootfs has about 25 KB to spare: Aurora, Sunrise, Graphite, Tide, Ember and Black. `wall_render` evaluates each one's colour field (inverse-distance mesh gradients, waves, glows) every 5 pixels, interpolates bilinearly and adds a 4x4 ordered dither so gradients don't band; Graphite adds a brushed per-row grain. Their names (`q2pod:aurora` … `q2pod:black`) are no files. `lockscreen_images` lists them after the default and before the card images: demo has no `deque_pop_front`, so the card images come off the back and go on again, and a rebuilt list never doubles them. When stock reset a built-in, it is put back and saved. `lockscreen_rows` clears those thumbnails' `file://` names, and `lockscreen_init` clears the lock window's picture name, so nothing is looked up on each paint. `lockscreen_paint`, from `ringnav_paint` after stock painted the widget, draws the wallpaper over it: 375x320 rendered once per picture and size and kept between lock windows (Black needs none, the window is black), thumbnails on first sight. A thumbnail's bitmap goes with its widget (`EVT_DESTROY`), and both widget pointers are forgotten then, so a stale pointer never matches.

**Cover.** The lock window's 20 ms timer reloads `img_playcover` (`0x52d844`) once `g_playcover_finishflag` says a new track's cover is parsed, by the same `file://` name, and stock never invalidates it: the old art stayed until something else repainted there (a scrolling title's edge, a tap). `lockscreen_cover` invalidates it after stock, outside any paint.

**Wake (1.4.4 only; removed in 1.4.5).** With the lock screen on (`g_screensaver_mode`) and no lock window open (`g_lockscreen_pageflag`), stock's centre release from dark (`on_wm_keyup_fun`) and its Standby resume (`device_standby`) call `enable_fb` (`FBIOBLANK` unblank, which lights the panel) and then `navigator_to("saverscreen_page")`. The panel showed the page left at screen-off, and the lock window stayed unpainted: `ringnav_wm_paint` paints nothing while `g_backlight_status` is clear, and only the lock window's 20 ms timer sets it, on its second tick (a countdown of 2 from `saverscreen_page_init`), after a 50 ms `usleep` and before `screen_action(1)`. With the screen off a UI loop pass takes 100 or 250 ms (Battery), so those ticks came late. The build turns both `bal enable_fb` into `nop` (`LOCK_WAKE`), and the timer's `jalr usleep` (`0x52da54`) into `jal lockscreen_light` (`LOCK_LIGHT`), reached only when the countdown ends with the screen still dark. It sets `g_backlight_status` (stock sets it right after), paints the whole screen, unblanks with `enable_fb` and then sleeps stock's 50 ms. The panel stays blank until the lock screen is drawn, and stock's backlight follows. Without the lock screen, wake is unchanged. ponytail: the panel lights two dark loop passes after the press (about 0.2-0.5 s). If the lock window never opens, the panel stays blank until a stock `awake_screen` (a held centre press, for one) unblanks it.

**Clock.** The Lock screen page's **Clock: Standard / Large** row (`Q2POD LOCKCLOCK`, default Standard) follows stock's Time show, 78 high as stock's rows. Large re-lays out `view_timeinfo` when the lock window opens: the date (22 px) centred on a top line at y 14 with the battery percent at its right end when the Battery setting shows a percentage (the icon hidden; Battery: Icon hides both), and the time (116 px) centred across the screen under it, all above the now-playing card (y 190). Time show off still hides it all. `clock_time` is a `digit_clock`; in 12-hour mode stock sets its format to `HH:TT` (`0x52d840`), which `tk_date_time_format_impl` writes as the padded hour, a colon and AM/PM ("05:PM"). The build rewrites that literal, in its 8-byte slot after the 24-hour `hh:mm`, to `H:mm` (`LOCK_CLOCK_12H`): the hour 1-12 unpadded and the minutes ("5:07"), as the status bar's clock.

Checks in `test/patch.py`: the list's order and the kept built-in, the row's place, text and saved value, the thumbnails, and both clock layouts. `wall_render` is plain C, so it also builds on the host (`-DPEQ_HOST`).

## Night Shift

**System settings → Display → Night Shift**, after Smart Awake on the main page (`ringnav_display`, stock's `display_backlight` icon; `patch/nightshift.c`), warms the screen as f.lux does: red is kept and green and blue are scaled to a colour temperature, from Tanner Helland's fit to the black-body colours (`ns_green`, `ns_blue`, a pair each 100K from 2000K to 5000K). It reads `Night Shift: Off`, `Night Shift: 3400K` or `Night Shift: Auto` (`Q2POD NIGHTSHIFT`, 0 Off, 1 On, 2 Auto, and `NIGHTK`, a hundred in 2000-5000, else 3400, in `config.ini`). The row opens [Crossfade](#crossfade)'s re-dressed Boot volume page (`xfade_open` context 3): titled Night Shift, the switch and Centre cycling Off, On and Auto as Light Mode's row does, the slider 5000K at its left to 2000K at its right, +/- and the wheel 100K a step; a move while it is off turns it on, and one in Auto keeps Auto. **Auto** warms the screen from 19:00 to 06:59 by the device clock, the hours [Light Mode](ipod.md#light-mode)'s Auto is dark (`clock_night`, `patch/theme_engine.c`): roughly the year's average sunset and sunrise on the clock, as daylight saving and most time zones run the clock ahead of the sun. It is off while the clock cannot be read. `clock_show` hands the hour to `night_clock` each minute with the status bar's clock, and a crossing repaints as a change on the page does; at boot the hour is read with the setting. Each change shows at once (`night_set`), a drag's every step too, and is saved as the page saves the rest (`night_save` from `refresh`: at a drag's release, else at once); the row's label (`label_night`) follows.

**Tint.** AWTK draws each frame into the screen's `lcd_mem` buffer (`LCD_OFFLINE_FB`, BGRA8888, `LCD_FORMAT` 3) and `lcd_end_frame` (`0x647958`) then calls the lcd's `end_frame` (`LCD_END_FRAME`), which hands the frame to the flush thread that turns it onto `/dev/fb0`. While Night Shift is on, that pointer is `night_end`: before calling stock's, it warms the pixels drawn in the frame and sets their alpha to `0xfe` (`NS_DONE`), which the panel ignores. A pixel already at `0xfe` is skipped, so nothing is warmed twice. Stock's blends write alpha `0xff` over any pixel whose alpha is at least `0xf5` (`fill_bgra8888_rect`, `0x6cbf24`), as do its opaque fills, so a pixel drawn again in a later frame comes back at `0xff` and is warmed once more. A colour or image pixel whose own alpha is exactly `0xfe` keeps it (stock's clear path and `image_copy`) and stays unwarmed; no colour of Q2 Pod's has it. Only the frame's bounding rect (`LCD_DIRTY`, `dirty_rects->max`, which `lcd_begin_frame` sets, `0x6472a8`) is scanned: stock calls `tk_init` at 320 x 375 and turns the lcd to 270 without fast portrait, so `lcd_mem_resize` gives it a 375 x 320 buffer (`LCD_LINE_LENGTH` = 4 x `LCD_W`) that the flush thread rotates onto `/dev/fb0`; any other layout is left as drawn. Stock paints each dirty rect apart, so the pixels between them are already warm and cost a test each. A window's snapshot for its animation is drawn on `canvas_offline`'s own lcd, so it reaches the screen as an image and is warmed when shown. The scan costs about 9 instructions for a pixel already warm and 20 for a new one, so only the drawn rect matters: a full-screen redraw is about 2-3 ms, a progress bar's tick a few rows. Nothing paints with the screen off. Videos draw on `/dev/fb0` themselves (`q2video`), so they show untinted.

**On and off.** `night_paint`, from `ringnav_paint` after stock paints each widget, gives the lcd `night_end` once Night Shift is on, or in Auto's hours: the first paint after On whose canvas is the screen's (`LCD_DRAW_MODE` normal, BGRA8888); an offline canvas draws into a bitmap and is never taken. Off puts stock's `end_frame` back at once (`night_set`), so with Night Shift off nothing runs per frame, and that paint check is a flag test: the setting is read once at boot (`ringnav_bootmode`, `patch/playback.c`), so no paint reads `config.ini`. Every change repaints the whole screen (`widget_invalidate_force` on the window manager), so the screen goes warmer, cooler or back to stock's colours in one frame.

Checks in `test/patch.py`: the row from each saved value and its place at the list's end; the page's switch, wheel, +/- and slider, with saving and the repaint; and the tint run through stock's `lcd_end_frame`: the frame's rect warmed with red kept and alpha `0xfe`, a pixel outside it or already warm left alone, a pixel drawn again warmed again, rotated or padded rows left as drawn, and Off restoring stock's `end_frame`; boot reading the setting and a paint reading none; `night_paint` takes no offline canvas, no other format and nothing while off.

## Parametric EQ

**Stock presets.** Stock's preset slots are `reset_user_eq`'s (`0x4f91a4`) constant 12 × 10 float table, modes 1-3 the zeroed custom slots and 4-11 Pop, Rock, Dance, Blues, Metal, Vocal (the `eq_scene` image), Classical and Jazz in its `eq_*` image order, in dB over the same ten bands (`set_equalizer_value` stores them plus 6). `patch/peq_ui.c` keeps modes 4-11 as `stock_gains`; picking one loads `peq_default` with those gains, bands with a gain on, and Auto preamp into the editor as a saved preset does. Nothing is written until Apply.

The PEQ adds two demo hooks: `playset_equalizer_page_init` (`0x4b642c`) builds the editor from `patch/peq_ui.c` (ten band rows, plus a spare past the last used band, up to 30), and `set_equalizer_value` (`0x4f9230`) keeps the stock equalizer filter instantiated, then sets `g_equalizer_flag` to the PEQ on/off state so the status-bar EQ icon (`systembar_showface`) follows it. The editor's ON/OFF writes the stock switch's `write_int_config(flag, "PLAYSET", "EQFLAG")`, which boot reads before instantiating the filter, so the state survives a restart. Frequency and Q open a value menu with an AWTK `edit` created at run time over the title (page y 2-28, as the search fields'), keyed and styled as the stock playlist dialogs' (`kb_default_t9`, `text`, `#2B2B2B` rounded), but with action text `done`. T9's window starts at page y 30, so the keyboard neither covers the edit nor moves the page; as the list's first row (page y 52-92) it sat under T9, showed none of the typing and lost the keys (the cause of the loss inferred, not traced: stock `edit_on_event` drops `EVT_IM_COMMIT` on an edit without its focused flag). Row 0 names the field and, like Centre on it, focuses the edit and asks for the keyboard; on a Frequency or Q edit still focused after Done, which sends no `EVT_FOCUS`, it starts the number-page timer below itself. The rename edit is placed the same way. Return with the keyboard up cancels either edit (`search_key`, see [Internet Radio](#internet-radio)): an empty entry restores the value, or leaves the name for the preset list. The 123 page is set on the input method's own keyboard (`IM_KEYBOARD`, `+0x0c`, while `IM_WIDGET` is the edit), not a window looked up by name, which could be the previous keyboard still closing. The edit's action text is `done`: stock `edit_on_event` acts on the keyboard's OK (`EVT_IM_ACTION`) only for `done` (close the keyboard, then `EVT_VALUE_CHANGED`) or `next`, and the dialogs' `OK` relies on their own handlers, so with `OK` the value only reported on blur; its `EVT_VALUE_CHANGED` applies the text through the preset parser's number reader, clamped like Raise/Lower, and its `EVT_FOCUS` (`0x10e`) starts a 16 ms timer that switches that keyboard's `panel` pages to `symnum`, the number keys, trying up to 20 times since the keyboard can open a moment after the focus, and selects the edit's value (`edit_set_select`), so the first key replaces it. The edit uses text input so stock's numeric validation and auto-fix cannot rewrite unfinished decimals or zero digits; `peq_number` validates the completed text and the editor clamps it to Q 0.1–10 or Frequency 20–20,000 Hz. Auto preamp is minus the largest combined response peak of either channel at the rate actually playing (`peq_auto`, `patch/peq.c`): the player works it out from the coefficients it has just compiled for the negotiated rate (`prepare`, from `peq_reset` at each track's or rate's negotiation and `peq_update` for an applied edit), on the playback loop, never in the PCM callback, and plays it through the usual 20 ms ramp. The sweep is 1024 log-spaced points from 1 Hz to Nyquist, plus DC, Nyquist and the band centres; it never boosts (a flat or cut-only preset gets 0 dB) and is capped at −60 dB. It is one gain for every channel, applied with balance as a set preamp is, so the filters and the left/right balance are unchanged; loudness can differ between tracks at different rates (the reported 26-band preset: −12.35 dB at 32 kHz, −9.25 at 44.1, −8.81 at 48, −7.87 at 96). It is a sampled steady-state peak, not a limiter: an unsampled narrow peak (0.003 dB on that preset at 44.1 kHz) and transient or intersample overshoot can still need more. `peq_preset.automatic` records which preamp it is: 1 Auto (the preamp picker's Auto row, a stock preset), 2 a value picked from the preamp list or an import's own `Preamp:` (`peq_parse`; an import without one is 1, Auto, so its boosts can't clip), 0 never picked (a preset or state saved before 1.3.6, or imported before 1.4.4), which the editor and PEQ playback (`eq_effective`) take as Auto: a preamp picked before 1.3.6, or an older import's, must be picked again (or the file imported again). A pick on the Preamp picker is also written to the saved preset the draft was loaded from (`remember_preamp`: its `automatic` and `preamp` only, found by the loaded name in `/mnt/data/peq-presets`), so an import switched to Auto stays Auto when loaded again; the card's `.txt` is only ever read. Keyed on the name, a stock preset named like a saved one would write to that file. A picked preamp plays exactly as set at every rate, even when it equals an Auto figure. On Auto the saved `preamp` is the 48 kHz cut, a fallback the player does not use, so a rate change rewrites nothing and opening the page offers nothing to apply, whatever number an older version saved. The Preamp row and its picker's Auto row show the draft's cuts at two reference rates, `Auto (44.1: -9.2 / 48: -8.8)` (`autos`, cached until a band changes), one decimal; other rates play their own. The row's value is 16 px there, as two double-digit cuts at 20 px overrun the iPod's 280 px column beside the caption and chevron (16 px leaves 6 px). A band edit, including a gain picked from the wheel list, recomputes it only on Auto, so a picked preamp stays put. The editor handles Return (key 170) itself, since it replaces the page init that registered `on_common_keyup`. On the main and band screens a `PEQ_GRAPH_H` view heads the list, the scroll view's first child, and scrolls with the rows; it shows the draft's response (`peq_response`, the same `|H|^2` as Auto preamp, at 48 kHz, one point per column, 20 Hz-20 kHz, ±`PEQ_GRAPH_DB`), drawn by `peq_paint` from the paint hook, at the view's own canvas origin and inside the list's clip: a grey grid with 100, 1k and 10k labels, a 2-pixel vgcanvas line in the accent's light tone (white in Stock) with a fill that fades toward 0 dB, the other channel's line dimmer when left and right differ, a ring per band in use, the edited band's larger and filled; grey with no fill when PEQ is off. It takes no click, so ringnav's rows, row 0 first, are the ones under it. The list sets no `item_height`, which stock's layout (`0x5ea5f4`) would give the graph too; the rows are 48 px themselves, the graph two of them, so whole rows fill the list at either end. Arriving on row 0 shows the whole graph, since ringnav's `reveal` shows everything above the first row when it is reached ([Wheel movement](#wheel-movement)); `eq_key` scrolls nothing. A render on the same screen keeps the list's offset. Rows show the caption on the left and the value right-aligned in grey, left of stock's `list_into` chevron (x 302) on rows that open a screen or picker (`INTO`: Balance, Preamp, Presets, the bands, Frequency, Gain, Q and the Presets menu's five); **Enabled** and a band's **Band** row are switches (`list_toggle`, navigation.c), Channels and Type cycle in place; off bands and display-only rows are grey. Band captions use 16 px, leaving room for the 20 px gain and chevron even with a channel-specific shelf at 20000 Hz. The **Presets** row's value names the preset loaded from there, and keeps the applied one's name until the active preset changes. It is stock's right-aligned ellipsis `hscroll_label` (`long_value`), not the other rows' plain label, in a column fixed from x 130, past the caption in the widest row font (Classic's 27 px ends at x 118), so a long file name stops short of the caption; a theme with a wider row font has to move it. Stock draws a cut name from the label's left (`0x6397d0`), so it can end up to a letter short of the values above it; the selected row's marquee scrolls it uncut. **Rename a preset** lists the saved presets; a pick opens the name in an edit (letters, as the value menu's edit otherwise), focused at once, so the keyboard is up, with `select_none_when_focused` set: stock's focus selects the whole text (an idle, `0x60f560`), so the first key replaced the name. The cursor stays at the end, where stock's text setter leaves it (`0x614198`), so keys edit the name's end. Done (`EVT_IM_ACTION`, and the `EVT_VALUE_CHANGED` stock sends only for a changed text; `named` acts once) or the one row, **Rename**, renames the file (`rename_preset`): spaces and dots trimmed from the end, as FAT drops them; refused as "Name not allowed" when nothing is left, starting with a dot, holding `/ \ : * ? " < > |`, or too long to read (256 bytes or more: stock's `widget_get_text_utf8` writes nothing for a text that does not fit, `0x716bb0` measuring first), and as "Name taken" when naming another preset (`access`, a case-only change allowed); the editor's and the applied preset's names follow it. An empty name (Return's cancel, Done or **Rename** on a cleared edit) goes back to the Rename list with no message on every path. The **Reset to defaults** row puts `peq_default`'s ten bands, all off, and 0 dB preamp and balance into the draft, keeps Advanced's On/Off and clears the loaded preset's name; Apply changes activates it. In `/usr/bin/hciplayer` (modified MPlayer 1.3.0, pinned by `PLAYER_SHA` in `tools/peq.py`) the equalizer descriptor's open pointer at `0x893e0c` is redirected to `patch/peq_player.c`, loaded through the player's unused `PT_NULL` header at `0xe10000`. Stock skips the equalizer above 48 kHz (`slt` against 48001) in two places: the EQ-on command (`beqz` at `0x42ced0`, to the `===setequalizer off===` log) and the per-stream insert (`bnez` at `0x42fd84`, around the add-equalizer call `0x4291ac`). The build makes the first a nop and the second unconditional (`EQ_RATE_GATES` in `tools/peq.py`, pinned), so the filter runs at every rate; `peq_compile` accepts 8-384 kHz. DSD is the exception: it reaches the chain as integer PCM, possibly DoP, whose bits must arrive unchanged, so config accepts the format as it comes and `play` leaves every block alone when the playing codec (`mpctx`, a pointer at `0xaf9e30`, `+0x2c` `sh_audio`, `+4` `codec`) has driver `dsdiff` (`+0x3e0`; the vendor's decoder for dsf, dff and SACD iso) or a name starting `ffdsd` (`+0x3d0`); so do rates outside 8-384 kHz and unknown formats. It never detaches (2): the per-stream insert (`0x4291ac`) adds it with `af_add` (`0x44d098`), which returns the new filter even when its reinit (`0x44c1c4`) detached and freed it, and the insert then sends the gain set to the freed instance. The player re-adds the filter at each file load (`0x4224cc` sets the insert state back to pending), so the next PCM track is equalized again. The player reads what plays from the [EQ state](#3eq) in `/mnt/data/peq-active` on the playback loop, never in the PCM callback; `patch/peq.c` holds the shared parser, storage and biquad DSP.

**Float headroom.** `peq_process` preserves samples above ±1 for downstream software volume and mixing; a picked preamp and balance are applied exactly. Non-finite input is still zeroed while EQ runs, and steady bypass stays bit-exact. The stock float volume path multiplies before clamping (`0x45c104`); the signed PCM converters saturate at final conversion (16-bit `0x451c14`, 24-bit `0x4528b0`, 32-bit `0x452474`). With hardware volume or full software volume, a preset with insufficient headroom can still clip there: lowering the DAC's analog volume cannot recover those peaks. No limiter is added: Auto is a fixed gain for the stream's rate.

A band's `enabled` is 0 off, 1 both channels, 2 left only, 3 right only (stream channels 0 and 1); `balance` (dB) attenuates the left above 0 and the right below, so it never adds clipping. APO `Channel:` sections set the scope of the filters after them, and per-side preamps become the louder side's common preamp plus `balance`. Out-of-range OFF filters, the blank slots some exporters write as `Fc 0 Hz … Q 0`, are skipped. Presets are `Q2PEQ02`; ten-band `Q2PEQ01` files load with `balance` 0 and default bands 11-30, and `Q2PEQ02` files from before 1.3.6, 8 bytes shorter (`PEQ_PRESET_V2`, no `automatic`), with `automatic` 0. Firmware before 1.3.6 rejects the longer files, so a downgrade loses saved presets and the EQ state. The DSP runs bands only up to the last one in use, or still draining memory carried from the previous preset.

## 3EQ

3EQ is the **Simple** tab of stock's **Playback → EQ** page, which `peq_page_init` builds with two tabs (`ui.tab`), opening on the tab it was last on (Simple after a boot, as `.scratch` is zeroed); **Advanced** is the PEQ editor above. On Simple and on Advanced's main screen the 48 px title label is the tab bar (`ui.bar`, drawn by `tabs_paint`): the open tab white over an accent line, the other grey. A message takes the bar until the next action. A press on the bar picks a tab by its half (`tab_to`, rendered from a timer, never inside the press). Switching tabs changes nothing that plays. Simple is one view that `e3_paint` draws from `ringnav_paint`: the switch, **Presets** and Reset (**R**, a small box; `e3_button`) under the bar, then three sliders over a 0 dB line. `eq_key`, from `ringnav` after `xfade_key`, takes Simple's wheel and Centre: the wheel moves through the bar, switch, Presets, R, Low, Mid and High; Centre (`center_press`, so a double press still turns the screen off) switches tab, flips the switch, opens Presets, resets or takes a slider, whose gain the wheel then steps by 0.5 dB until Centre or Return; the page's key-up handles Return. On Advanced's main screen the list keeps the wheel, except a turn up from its first row (or with none selected), which `eq_key` takes to put the bar in focus (`ui.tabs`, the list's `_ringnav_index` -1); a turn down returns to row 0 and Centre opens Simple. A press on Simple's view (`EVT_POINTER_DOWN`) picks a control; the page's move and up handlers (`EVT_POINTER_MOVE_BEFORE`, `EVT_POINTER_UP_BEFORE`, as pull-to-search's) drag a slider in 0.5 dB steps.

**Presets.** Simple's Presets (`e3_presets`) opens Advanced's Stock presets screen (`STOCKS`) with `ui.from_simple` set; Return there goes straight back. When `action` lands back on Advanced's main screen, it returns to Simple with Presets focused: on Return, with nothing changed, or on a pick (`load_draft`), which `apply(1)` saves as the playing PEQ (3EQ off, `hold` cleared) under its name. Like a load in Advanced, a pick replaces unapplied Advanced edits. While PEQ plays (`ui.draft.bypass` clear) the switch shows it on, lit, under `ui.applied` ("Advanced" when unnamed), cut to 12 bytes on a character boundary (16 px past 9), and the sliders are dimmed; the switch then turns the EQ off with no `hold`, since hold plays 3EQ's preamp. Taking a slider while PEQ plays, by Centre or touch, turns 3EQ on first (`e3_switch(1)`); PEQ's preset stays saved. Leaving the page from that screen reopens it on Simple.

**Filters.** `eq3_bands` gives the shared engine three bands, Q 0.707: a 155 Hz low shelf, a peak at 900 Hz for a cut or 1,750 Hz for a boost (0 dB is an identity band), and a 5 kHz high shelf, each -6 to +6 dB. The filter types and rough frequencies follow [Listener's measurements of Apple's AirPods EQ](https://headphones.com/blogs/features/apples-airpods-eq-is-it-any-good); the Q, the 155 Hz within its reported range and the ±6 dB limits are Q2 Pod's own, not Apple's coefficients, and its volume-dependent limits are not copied. The preamp is saved with the gains and played as saved (`automatic` 0, never PEQ's per-rate Auto): `headroom` takes the loudest of PEQ's Auto cuts at the standard PCM rates (8, 11.025, 12, 16, 22.05, 24, 32, 44.1, 48, 64, 88.2, 96, 176.4, 192, 352.8 and 384 kHz); nonstandard rates are not covered. A change crosses over in the engine's 20 ms ramp, including Mid's frequency moving across 0 dB. A shelf above Nyquist is skipped at that rate, as any band is.

**State.** `/mnt/data/peq-active` holds one `eq_state` (`patch/peq.h`), written whole (`.tmp`, `fsync`, rename) with the magic `Q2PEQ03`: the mode (`EQ_OFF`, `EQ_3`, `EQ_PEQ`), `hold`, the last applied PEQ and the 3EQ. `eq_load` reads it, or upgrades a `Q2PEQ01`/`02` preset from before 3EQ (PEQ on unless it was bypassed, 3EQ flat); 1.3.5's record, two presets without `automatic`, loads with it 0; any other short, long or invalid record reads as none (Off, PEQ's defaults). `eq_effective` is what plays: the mode's preset, bypassed when Off; PEQ's never-picked preamp (0) becomes Auto (1) there, and only there, so 3EQ and the hold below keep their saved preamp. The player reads it through `peq_load_active` on the playback loop, as before. Saved presets in `/mnt/data/peq-presets` stay `Q2PEQ02`. Every mode change goes through `eq_commit`, which saves the record, writes `PLAYSET/EQFLAG` when on/off changes and calls `peq_stock_eq`, which sets `g_equalizer_flag`, the status-bar icon, to the mode not being Off. Turning either EQ on selects it and turns the other off; PEQ's Apply keeps the mode unless PEQ is on. Slider edits are saved no more than every 250 ms (`E3_SAVE_MS`) and at once when an adjustment ends, and the player is told only while 3EQ plays, so editing an Off 3EQ changes no sound. A failed save says so in the title and keeps the gains to save again. **Hold:** turning 3EQ off on its page saves Off with `hold` set, which plays the 3EQ's preamp alone (`count` 0), so comparing against bypass is not suddenly louder; closing the page clears it. `peq_boot`, called by `ringnav_bootmode` during `config_init` before queue restoration, also clears a saved hold left by a shutdown while that page was open; the selected EQ mode and both presets stay as saved. Live player updates retain the comparison until the page closes.

**Track changes.** hciplayer negotiates a filter for every file, a new instance or the same one again, and `peq_reset` starts its cascade with zero memory. At a gapless change that zeroed memory under a continuous signal is a step: an audible click on any band with gain, which on music can read as a gap (#137). The instance that played last (`last_id`, set by filtered and passed-through blocks alike) leaves its settled filter memory in `carry` when it is freed or renegotiates (`leave`), unless it is mid-ramp, bypassed, never played, or was last filtered more than `XF_LEAD_S` (2 s) before its length, by crossfade's position and length. The next negotiated instance takes that memory once, on its first block (`adopt`), when rate, channels and preset match and that last block was filtered under 3 s ago (`CARRY_STALE_NS`, as crossfade's `XF_STALE_NS`). A skip or stop mid-track, or a pause near the end, starts the next track from zero: it begins from silence, and the old memory would only ring into it. The position is read only for blocks the EQ filters and the clock only near a track's end, so with EQ off playback reads neither. ponytail: a skip in a track's last two seconds carries, and with Gapless off a track played to its end carries into the next if it starts within 3 s; both start after a cut or silence, where the carried memory is usually small. Gate on stock's Gapless flag if that is heard. A different preset or format starts from zero as before. ponytail: this relies on hciplayer freeing the old instance before the new one plays its first block, which is what the device showed; a new instance that plays first starts from zero. `test/peq.py` (`carry_check`) runs two instances and a renegotiation against one uninterrupted DSP, and checks that a skip mid-track, a pause or a passed-through track in between starts from zero.

## EQ per Output

**Playback → EQ → Advanced → EQ per Output** (`Q2POD EQOUTPUT`, off by default; below Enabled, whether the EQ is on or off; `ringnav_eq_output_toggle`) gives each output its own EQ: the whole `eq_state`, so 3EQ, PEQ, and whether the EQ is on at all. There is nothing to assign: changing the EQ while an output plays is what sets it. `PEQ_ACTIVE` stays what plays, so the player is untouched.

**Which output.** `eq_output_key` (`patch/headset.c`), from `headset_eq_poll` every 500 ms of `ringnav_sleep`: `mclGetOutputWay` 2 is `usb`; 1 is the Bluetooth link list's connected (3) item, its MAC; 0 is a jack, by `g_headset_output`. Stock's `config_bal_output` and `config_po_output` set that byte to 1 (4.4 mm, `PA08`) or 0 (3.5 mm, `PA07`) at each plug-in and `config_outputchannel` sets the way to 0, so the jack last plugged in plays, over Bluetooth too. Unplugging falls back through `check_outputdevice` to Bluetooth, USB, the other jack or 0, so the jack's own `g_bal_status` or `g_po_status` must be set too. No output in use names nothing and changes nothing: an unplugged Q2 keeps the EQ until something else takes over.

**The swap.** `eq_output` (`patch/peq_ui.c`) keeps files in `/mnt/data/peq-outputs/`: an `eq_state` per output (`Q2PEQ03`, named `3.5mm`, `4.4mm`, `usb` or the MAC) and `owner`, the output the EQ that plays was set on. When the named output differs from `owner`, what plays goes to `owner`'s file with `hold` cleared, the new output's comes back through `eq_commit` when it differs (only saved when both are Off, so the filter stays out of the chain for `peq_chain`), and `owner` changes.
