A new or unreadable slot keeps what plays. With `owner` on disk, nothing needs saving on each edit: an edit just before power-off is filed correctly at the next swap. If the save fails, nothing changes and the next pass tries again, every 500 ms: a card of settings that can't be written has bigger problems, so there's no backoff. A `PEQ_ACTIVE` that exists but can't be read is not filed over a good slot. An open EQ page follows the swap, whether on top or covered: Simple's unsaved gains and a slider in use end and go to the old output's file first, then `page_load` brings up the new output's EQ and caption. Unapplied Advanced edits stay in the draft, and Apply puts them on the output playing then. The swap waits only while a keyboard is up (Frequency, Q, a preset's name), as the render would close it; those screens edit only the draft or a file name, so nothing is filed wrongly, but left up under another page the old output's EQ plays until it closes. The stock preset name shown on Simple is not stored; `peq_page_init` falls back to "Advanced" when the preset differs from the one last applied, as after a restart. With the setting on, the EQ page opens with the output in its bar ("EQ for 4.4 mm") until the first action or turn of the wheel. It names `owner`, whose EQ the page edits, and changes with a swap. Turning the setting off files what plays to `owner`'s file, then removes `owner` and leaves what plays and the other files alone, so an edit made with the setting on is kept, and turning it on again brings back the current output's file, or adopts what plays if it has none. An EQ changed while the setting was off is replaced then, and filed nowhere: the alternative overwrites that output's remembered EQ.

**Limits.** `EQ_DEVICES` caps Bluetooth slots at 32, about 2 KB each: past it, a new headset plays and can edit the current EQ, but it is not remembered. A forgotten device's slot stays, so re-pairing brings its EQ back; removing slots would need a last-used date, and stock's pair list is refilled on another thread without a lock, so it can't safely say which are gone. USB DACs share one slot, since stock does not identify them. Power lost between the swap and `owner`'s save files the new output's EQ under the old output, once; a failed `owner` save does the same at the next boot, since moving back in RAM would do it at the next pass.

**Bluetooth connect.** The swap is inferred safe where stock stops and restarts the player around the same time; if it races, the filter still reads `PEQ_ACTIVE` when playback starts.

## Bluetooth resampler

Over Bluetooth hciplayer's ALSA output uses the negotiated transport rate (48 kHz for AAC/SBC; 96 kHz S32 on the reported LDAC link), so for a file at any other rate `af_init` appends MPlayer's `lavcresample` (`af_info` at `0x8a4540`: open `0x4ac2c0`, control `0x4ac5a8`, play `0x4ac35c`, uninit `0x4ac534`), libswresample at filter size 16 and cutoff 0.8. The player builds a new chain for every file, gapless changes included, and the `SwrContext` holds the last half filter of input back until more arrives. Freed with the instance (`swr_free`, `0x41e2d0`), that tail never plays: the next file starts about 10 output frames (0.2 ms) early, a click on a continuous tone at every change, at any source rate. Wired output plays the file's own rate without one. `RS_OPEN_SLOT` in `tools/peq.py` points the descriptor's open at `rs_open` (`patch/peq_player.c`), which runs stock's and wraps play and uninit. An instance freed when its last block was near its track's end (`rs_end`, the EQ's `carry` test: within `XF_LEAD_S` of the length, under `CARRY_STALE_NS` ago, read in `rs_play` itself since the PEQ filter is left out of the chain with the EQ off from boot) keeps its context; the next instance, on its first block, swaps it for its own fresh one when its `ctx_*` fields (rates, filter size, phase shift, linear, cutoff: what stock's reinit compares before rebuilding) and channels match and the context was kept under `CARRY_STALE_NS` ago, and frees the other. The output is then what one instance playing both files would put out. A skip or stop mid-track, a pause, and a rate or channel change start from a fresh resampler, as stock; wired playback has none, so it reads neither the position nor the clock for this. ponytail: as the EQ's carry, a skip in a track's last two seconds, or a track played to its end with Gapless off and the next started within 3 s, carries the old tail's 0.2 ms into the next track's start; gate on stock's Gapless flag if that is heard. `test/bt_resample.py` runs the built player's lavcresample and libswresample under Unicorn: a tone split across two instances matches one instance sample for sample at 96→48, 44.1→48, 44.1→96 and 48→96 kHz; stock loses 10, 10, 17 and 16 output frames respectively, and a skip, pause or input-rate change starts fresh. This verifies sample continuity in the resampler, not real-time scheduling, the following S32 conversion, or Bluetooth delivery.

## USB output buffer

hciplayer's ao_alsa init (`0x438988`) asks ALSA for a 500 ms buffer (`0x438a3c`) in 16 periods (`0x4389a4`), which is what it gets at low rates (22048 frames at 44.1 kHz) and not at high ones, where the driver's limit answers (16384 frames at 96 and 192 kHz, 171 and 85 ms), but on output way 2, a USB DAC, stock cuts it to 100 ms: the delay slots at `0x43977c` and `0x439a60` store 100000 µs over it. With 100 ms in hand, a track change that keeps the player busy longer than that underruns, a gap at a gapless change that the headphone jack's 500 ms rides out (#159). `USB_BUFFER` in `tools/peq.py` turns both stores into nops, so a USB DAC keeps the 500 ms buffer the other outputs get (`set_buffer_time_near`: at rates high enough to meet the DAC's own buffer limit, less, still above 100 ms), and the visualizer's `VIS_LATENCY_MS`, set for that buffer, fits USB too. ponytail: where hciplayer scales a USB DAC's volume itself (`usbaudio_softvol`), a volume change now takes up to 500 ms to be heard, not 100 ms.

At a gapless change that init keeps the PCM open only when the file's rate, channels and format equal what the previous call asked (the triple at `0xafa164`-`0xafa16c`, compared at `0x438cc0`, `0x439598` and `0x43972c`); otherwise it closes the PCM, on USB without draining it (`0x438cfc`), and opens it again. But what it saves as the previous rate and format is what ALSA negotiated (`0x4393b8`, `0x43934c`), and on a DAC whose highest rate is 48 kHz it asks for 48 kHz whatever the file (`0x43999c`), so a 44.1 kHz album's every change closed and reopened the PCM: up to the buffer's worth of the ending track lost, then the DAC's own start-up, which on USB headphones that mute until their stream runs (a Momentum 4) is up to a second of silence. Bluetooth had the same hole. `reinit_audio_chain` asks way 1 for 48 kHz whatever the file (`0x42efcc`, a constant, not what the last call got). hciplayer opens `plug:bluealsa` with resampling off (`0x439370`), and bluealsa's ioplug offers exactly the transport's rate and channels (`snd_pcm_ioplug_set_param_minmax(HW_RATE, sampling, sampling)`, at `0x7f60` in `libasound_module_pcm_bluealsa.so`), so `set_rate_near` answers the transport's rate. At AAC's and SBC's 48 kHz the compare matches and the PCM stays open, but LDAC at 96 kHz (or any 44.1 or 88.2 kHz transport) was asked 48000, answered 96000 and saved 96000, so every change at any file rate ran drain, close, open. On Bluetooth nothing is lost there, the PCM only drains and reopens: a gap, then the music carries on where it left off. The descriptor's init slot (`0x88f760`, `AO_INIT_SLOT` in `tools/peq.py`) points at `ao_init` in `patch/peq_player.c`: on output way 1 or 2 with the PCM open, a request equal to the last one the output changed on that way asks for what that one got (`ao_data`, `0xafa1a0`), so the compare matches, init returns early with `ao_data` at the negotiated values and the filter chain resamples as it did (`rs_open` carries the resampler's tail across the change). A file at another rate needs no reopen on Bluetooth: the request is 48 kHz for all of them and only the chain's resampler changes, which starts fresh, so the pitch stays right. Crossfade's tail is mixed in the filter at the file's rate before that resampler, so it behaves as at 48 kHz, with less of a wait against `XF_COLD_NS` than the reopen left it. The remembered pair moves as one and carries its way, so another output asks what the file asks and can't inherit Bluetooth's 96 kHz (LDAC ending leaves the PCM open: `mcl-orderstop` skips the close on way 1, `0x42ce78`, and `player_save_stop` around `config_outputchannel` stops only a playing player; ponytail: that holds for the callers read, the 22 other `config_outputchannel` callers aren't audited, so one reaching way 2 or 0 with a Bluetooth pair and an open PCM isn't ruled out here, only by the way test on the pair). It moves on a fresh open (another DAC or transport may have come), on another way, when the request was changed, or when the request it holds was taken as asked (a reopen found a new answer, so the old one isn't asked for ever); another request taken as asked leaves it, so a 44.1 and 48 kHz mix stays matched, and a 24-bit album is not asked at the 16 bits a track in between negotiated. A pair that changes nothing (AAC and SBC at 48 kHz, a DAC that takes the file's rate and format) isn't looked at, so those call exactly as stock. A transport gone under an open PCM leaves the ioplug `SND_PCM_STATE_DISCONNECTED` (the plugin's pointer callback sees the FIFO's fd gone, `0x5b28`), or `XRUN` if the plugin had already underrun (it stops writing, `0x70c4`, so it can't see the dead FIFO), and stock's play (`0x43b038`) and reset (`0x439c68`) only prepare either. So `ao_init` substitutes only for a PCM `RUNNING` or `PREPARED`, and anything else (with `PAUSED` and `SETUP`) negotiates anew, as stock's reopen did. The ioplug's state op only reads (`0x904d0` in libasound): the callback runs from `snd_pcm_status`, so `ao_init` calls stock's `get_space` (`0x43815c`) first. The normal natural change leaves the old buffer playing (stock's uninit returns at once), `RUNNING`, so ordinary gapless stays open; a next track later than the buffer underruns and reopens, as stock did at every change. A server that closed the FIFO while the PCM is still running stays the hole AAC has in stock: the state reads `RUNNING`, and the first write finds it. On a USB DAC and the jacks a next track later than the buffer doesn't underrun: `stop_threshold` and `silence_size` are the boundary (`0x4394dc`, `0x4394f0`, the same code on every way, USB's included), so the PCM stays `RUNNING` on silence with `hw_ptr` past `appl_ptr` and the new track's first frames would be written behind the hardware. After a natural end `ao_init` reads `snd_pcm_status_get_avail` (not stock's `get_space`, which caps an MQA file's at 6/7 of the buffer, 3/4 on USB, `0x438210`; on ways 0 and 2, two status reads a same-format change gains, and they write nothing), and a ring's worth or more resets the triple, so stock reopens; a status that fails does the same. The first write comes later, after the chain has decoded nearly a whole buffer (`fill_audio_out_buffers`, `0x42f3f4`), so `ao_write` (`0x43afa4`) looks again, on every write on the jacks and USB, and, past the ring, calls `snd_pcm_forward` by the deficit plus a period (hciplayer doesn't import it: `dlsym`); a status that fails forwards nothing. The ring is silence there, so a late track starts after a short gap and whole. The same happens mid-song: a network stream that stalls (Wi-Fi gone for 20 s) runs the PCM dry, and the music that came back was written behind `hw_ptr` and lost until `appl_ptr` caught up, heard as the song jumping ahead in chunks; forwarded, it resumes where it stopped. That costs one status read a write. ponytail: there's no threshold, so any underrun on the jacks or USB takes the forward: a short one costs one period of silence (1378 frames, about 31 ms at 44.1 kHz) and skips nothing. The kernel limits a forward to `avail` (`forward_appl_ptr`, read in the generic 5.10.186 tree), which exceeds this request. Bluetooth's plugin stops at its own underrun and stock's play prepares it, so it doesn't look. A `stop_threshold` of the buffer size (`0x4394e0`, one word) would instead turn each underrun into a stream restart on all three ways; dropping the check at init for the forward alone would spare a dry change its mute. A fresh open, another request and the headphone jack run as stock (stock's own init calls at `0x439b5c`, the hw_params retry, and `0x439d14`, reset's, bypass the slot). `test/bt_resample.py` runs `ao_init` under Unicorn against a stub init that negotiates like a DAC, then stock's own init with libasound answering as `plug:bluealsa` at 44.1, 48 and 96 kHz. libasound and the plugin are modelled: the PCM's states are set by hand in the test, from the disassembly above, and neither runs. A queue that changes bit depth or channels still reopens at each change, on Bluetooth as well: the rate is a constant there but the format follows the file (`0x42ed88`-`0x42efc0`), so 16 to 24 bits reopens (`test/bt_resample.py` runs S16 to `0x19`). Holding the format wouldn't be right: `plug:` would convert, but whichever came first would stay, so a 24-bit album after a 16-bit track goes to LDAC at 16. Not changed: a track started from a stop, and a seek (`reset`, `0x439c00`, drops, closes and reopens the PCM on USB), still start the USB stream afresh, so such headphones cut the first part of it; a silence pre-roll would delay every start and wants a setting. ponytail: one request is remembered, so a queue that changes rate reopens at each change, as the headphone jack does; a small table keyed by request would cover mixed queues on a 48 kHz DAC.

**The old track's end.** A natural change that reopens used to lose the end of the track. On the jacks init mutes (`0x4395dc`, `user_mute(1)`, immediate) before it drains (`0x438d04`), so the drain ran silently, and a USB DAC closes without draining (`0x438cfc`), so with `USB_BUFFER` up to the buffer's rest was lost, against stock's 100 ms. A file's end on stock's own code doesn't close the PCM: `0x43868c`, the uninit slot `0x88f764`, returns at once unless the stop flag `0xb10e70` is up, so the old track's buffer is still playing when the next init runs. `ao_uninit` notes a natural end there, and `ao_init` then calls `snd_pcm_drain` before stock's init if stock's init is about to reopen (Gapless off, or the request differs from the triple) on output way 0 or 2. Stock's mute, then its own drain (`snd_pcm_drain`'s state check at `0x3ff94` gets a PCM in SETUP and returns `-EBADFD` at once, unread), close and open follow.

What says nobody pressed: the eof word `mpctx+0x14` decides the slot's `immed` at `0x42e218` (`(eof & ~4) == 0`). Each route:

| Route                                                                         | Commands                                                                                                                                                     | eof, immed | Triple                                  | Result                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------- | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Gapless, same format                                                          | `set-gapless` (`0xb10df8` = 1), preload                                                                                                                      | 1, 0       | kept, matches                           | one status read; no drain, mute or wait. Dry: as a new format                                                                                    |
| Gapless, new format                                                           | same                                                                                                                                                         | 1, 0       | kept, differs                           | drain, stock's mute, close, open, unmute, jacks wait                                                                                             |
| Stock Shuffle, Repeat One, under 3 s, a folder's end, Gapless on, same format | `set-songurl` after the end (demo not playing, `0x5ad18c`)                                                                                                   | 1, 0       | -1 at `0x4226a4`, restored by `ao_init` | stays open, no mute, no wait (jacks: with demo's mark; Bluetooth: with `BT_STAY_MS` left, else stock's reopen and its Drain)                     |
| The same with Gapless off, or another format                                  | same                                                                                                                                                         | 1, 0       | -1, or compare skipped                  | drain, stock's mute, close, open, unmute, jacks wait                                                                                             |
| The same from a DLNA or online queue                                          | same, demo muted                                                                                                                                             | 1, 0       | -1                                      | stock's timing after the drain                                                                                                                   |
| Skip while playing, or a CUE track's end                                      | `set-nexturl` (next flag `0xb10e6c`, demo playing)                                                                                                           | 4, 1       | -1                                      | stock: mute first                                                                                                                                |
| Stop                                                                          | `mcl-stop` (stop flag)                                                                                                                                       | 4, 1       | -1 at `0x422450`                        | stock: mute, close                                                                                                                               |
| Stop or skip landing after eof 1                                              | next flag up at the slot, stop flag up there (its close leaves nothing to drain), or either set before the drain (`hci_command`, then `ao_init` looks again) | 1, 0       | either                                  | stock                                                                                                                                            |
| Skip, stop or `set-songurl` during the drain                                  | `hci_command` sees the flag or the play flag's rise                                                                                                          | 1, 0       | either                                  | jack: stock's mute (`0x43b280`) at once, its flag set so stock's unmute runs once; USB: `snd_pcm_drop` from the command thread, ending the drain |
| Seek                                                                          | `mcl-seek`, never leaves the file                                                                                                                            | none       | kept                                    | stock                                                                                                                                            |
| Queue's natural end                                                           | none: `mclStop` with demo not playing only sets `-0x4154` (`0x5aaf1c`)                                                                                       | 1, 0       | -1                                      | the PCM plays out and stays open                                                                                                                 |
| An unplayable file's dialog                                                   | `mcl-orderstop`: the jalr at `0x4ea840` has `mclOrderStop` when `s0` != 0 or `v0` == 11, its only caller, else `player_next_music`                           | as the end | -1                                      | stock (`0x43b1d0`): the jacks drain unmuted on the command thread, USB closes undrained                                                          |

The next file's failed open (`0x422664`) reaches `uninit_player` with the AO's bit already cleared (and eof 1, set at `0x42a1dc` if it was 0), so it doesn't call the slot. A stop flag with `immed` 0 (a stop within one loop of a natural end) stays stock's muted drain. DSD (`0xb10e5c`, read at both ends) stays muted.

Demo's mute on the cold route (`mclStartPlayer`, `0x5ad1bc`, then 100 ms asleep at `0x5ad1d4`) stood before all of this: the mute line rose about 100 ms before the PCM left RUNNING, then 310-420 ms drained under it (a same-rate folder rollover). What it tests is the hardware word at `-0x413c`, set once in `initMclModule` (`0x5aa4ac`), and the output way at `-0x4150`, so only the jacks ever mute there. Both calls now go through `start_mute` and `start_sleep` (`patch/crossfade.c`, `START_MUTE_CALL` in `tools/build.py`). They skip only when `press_natural` is 2, which only `ringnav_next(1)` sets (`mclAutoChange`'s, on the route where demo isn't playing, `0x5ae8a8`), on a local queue, with the press word mapped; they then leave the word even (a press's is odd), and `ao_init` takes a fresh, unused even word as "demo didn't mute". Everything else keeps stock's mute and sleep: a press route, Memory Playback's boot, a resume (`player_save_start`, `player_reconfig`), an app's Stream start and an unplayable file's skip (`press_natural` 1), DLNA and online queues, Tidal, and the other sender branches (`0x5acfe8` on a CUE track's end, whose set-nexturl is a skip to hciplayer; `0x5ad228` at the other hardware word). `mclStartPlayer`'s callers: `player_reconfig`, `player_change_music` (four sites), `player_play_pause`, `player_save_start`, `player_start`, `player_start_queue`, `mclPrevSong`, and in `mclNextSong` the rollover (`0x5adcdc`) and the next (`0x5ade00`), both under `ringnav_next`. A queue's natural end sends nothing here: `mclStop` with demo not playing only sets `-0x4154` (`0x5aaf1c`). `mcl-orderstop`, from the dialog above, closes the PCM, so `ao_init` finds nothing open and neither reads the mark nor drains.

With the mark, the same format as the open PCM (`ao_uninit` saves `ao_data`, which `reinit_audio_chain` overwrites with the request before init, `0x42e9e4`, `0x42eaa0`), the PCM running and Gapless on, `ao_init` puts the triple back, and stock's compare keeps the PCM open, on the path a same-format preload takes: stock Shuffle, Repeat One and a folder's end run on with no mute and no wait, gapless when the next track's first block comes inside the buffer. With Gapless off, another format, or a dry buffer, the reopen is: the old track to its end (`snd_pcm_drain`, unmuted, so nothing is lost), stock's mute (`0x4395dc`), close, open, unmute, `DAC_RELEASE_MS` (`dac_ioctl`, `ao_wait`), frames. So the mute only covers a moment with no music, and the line opens before the first frame. A preloaded change at another format waits too. Crossfade is the same route: its tail is held in the filter, so an open PCM runs the fade through, and at a rate change the mix starts after the wait with nothing lost. Bluetooth and a USB DAC keep it too, with no mark to look for, since demo's mute is the jacks' only (`0x5ad1b0`); on Bluetooth the request is a constant, so a change of file rate stays open as well and only the chain's resampler changes. Bluetooth keeps it only with `BT_STAY_MS` (200 ms, derived) of the ring still to play, read from stock's get_space: a kept PCM gets no Drain, so bluealsa's silence fill doesn't start, and a first write later than the ring would send no packet for the whole stall. With less left, or under MQA, whose get_space counts in other units, stock's reopen runs with its Drain and fill. ponytail: a stall starting after `ao_init` on Bluetooth has no fill, as a late preload already hasn't. ponytail: there a not-playing press any time before the buffer runs dry can't be told from the cold route's own `set-songurl`, and hears the buffer's rest first; the word's odd stamp would close that. A mark left unspent lasts 5 s or until the next start that mutes at the same site clears it; a press's stamp overwrites it. Bluetooth drains unmuted in stock's code and doesn't take a second one.

The drain blocks the player's main thread, as stock's muted one did, for the same time on the jacks; commands still arrive on their own thread and, with `hci_command`'s cut, a press during it silences at once, where its handler alone only notes a flag. Demo's own mute comes 100-150 ms before its command (`0x5acfe8`, `0x5ad170`, `0x5ad1bc`; on the jacks, the mute line 150-178 ms ahead of the PCM's PAUSED or DRAINING, then Next and Previous drain about 470 ms under it), so on the jacks this ioctl only adds to it where demo's word or `start_mute` skipped its own; a USB DAC doesn't hear either. The drop is safe against the blocked drain: `snd_pcm_drain` takes no lock (`0x3ff44`), the hw plugin's is the bare ioctl (`0x4f784`), plug's fast ops are its slave's, resampling is off (`0x439370`), and the kernel's `snd_pcm_drain` sleeps unlocked and leaves its wait when `DROP` moves the state (`sound/core/pcm_native.c`, read in the generic 5.10.186 tree, not the Q2's 5.10.21). `ao_draining` keeps the main thread out of the close while the command thread is on the PCM. The watchdog's six seconds aren't near, unless a USB stream stalls without being unplugged. ponytail: then the kernel's wait runs to its own timeout (at least 10 s, past the heartbeat's 6 ticks, `0x42d95c`-`0x42d98c`) and hciplayer exits; a press drops it first. Stock's `0x438768` has the same exposure. A reopen now takes the buffer's rest longer, the time that audio plays; Crossfade's `XF_COLD_NS` of 30 s still allows for it. A rate change under Crossfade plays on to the end of what was buffered, with no jump, before the fade. ponytail: a press from demo's not-playing state before the drain starts, within a UI pass of the track's end, can't be told from the cold route's own `set-songurl`, so it hears up to the buffer's rest play out (it came to silence before); demo's press word would close that. `mcl-orderstop`'s close on the command thread during the drain races it as it races stock's own drain, now on USB too; demo sends it only from an unplayable file's dialog. A PCM that drains unmuted stops with nothing left to play, and every close stays under stock's mute. `test/hci_ao.py` runs the real player and its decoders (`hci_emu.py`) against a modelled PCM with the kernel's pointer sums: cold changes of rate, depth and channels reopen at what the file is, the same format stays open, a kept PCM dry by the first write, and a stream stalled mid-song, lose no frame, on ways 0 and 2, and a Crossfade mixes its whole tail. libasound's and the kernel's `snd_pcm_forward`, the ring's silence and all timing are modelled there. `test/bt_resample.py` runs each route through stock's `uninit_player`, slots and init in order of calls, with libasound modelled (`drain` takes 500 ms, then `-EBADFD`; states set by hand) and `hci_command`'s handler at `0x42be20` stubbed to set the flag the disassembly says it sets: that proves the order, not the sound or the real commands' timing.

## Wired start

On output way 0 (3.5 mm and balanced), every manual action cut the first fraction of a second, heard as a swift fade-up; stock has it too. `/dev/shanling_dac`'s ioctl `0xc0044d1f` is `user_mute(value, 1)` in `codec_cs43131.ko`. `user_mute(0)` restores the volume at once but queues `pop_delayed_work` for 10 jiffies, so the mute line lets go 90-100 ms after the ioctl, plus the worker's latency. After demo's `user_mute(2)` (`mclSetMute`, before a pause) the volume also comes back from `0xff` on the DAC's soft ramp (`0x90003` = `0xa0` at init; about (254 - code) / 2 ms, from the CS43130 datasheet's rate, not measured), which ends about when the hold does. Stock lets frames flow during all of that.

`0xb11584` is 1 after hciplayer mutes: at pause (`0x438668`), at uninit (`0x438788`), in init on a change of rate, channels or format (`0x4395dc`, with or without a PCM open: first play, and a manual skip at the same rate too, since `set-nexturl` clears `0xb10df8` and `0x4226a4` then sets the triple to -1), and through the helper `0x43b280` (`mcl-seek`, `0x42c5e0`). Each unmute site tests it for 1 and clears it, so stock unmutes once per mute. Three of them now release, wait `DAC_RELEASE_MS` (`patch/peq_player.c`, 130 ms, derived and not measured: the hold plus the ramp at the top volume codes) and then let audio flow (`DAC_RELEASE` in `tools/peq.py`), the first two only when a [stamp](#a-press-not-a-natural-change) below asks:

- The end of init (`0x4391b0`, `dac_ioctl`), on a fresh press only. It passes when paused (`0x42b710`) or with a start offset pending (`0x42b738`), which leaves the flag for the next two.
- `seek()` (`0x42b164`, `dac_unmute`, around the helper), after `reset`'s drop and prepare. It passes when paused. It waits on a fresh stamp too, for a start offset's seek (`0x422f58`) and a user's `mcl-seek` alike. Those two aren't all that leave the flag set here: the helper(1) sites `0x42368c`, `0x42fde0` and `0x4ccdb0`, behind `0xb10e5c`, set it too. Not the helper's own ioctl (`0x43b2c8`): that also carries its mute(1), and a DSD unmute mid-stream (`0x4cce04`).
- Resume (`0x43881c`, `dac_resume`): `snd_pcm_pause(0)` came before the unmute (`0x438978`), so the unmute now goes first and stock's check after the call (`0x438844`) finds the flag clear. It always waits: demo sends `mcl-play` only from `player_play_pause`, `player_seek_start`, `player_seek_time` (a user's, or Resume Songs' jump on a track paused in its first seconds) and for a phone (`dmr_mainscheduling`, `handlePlaySeekResponse`), never at a change of track. On way 0 it also drops, prepares and rewrites the PCM ([Unpause](#unpause)).

During the wait the PCM is prepared or paused, so nothing runs out and the position clock stands still, and no frame is written, so DSD stays bit-exact. Bluetooth and USB (way 1, 2) never wait. One action waits once, since the flag gates every site: a start with an offset passes init and waits at `seek()`, and the hw_params retry's inner init (`0x439b5c`) clears it for the outer one, which returns without looking. Resume runs on hciplayer's main thread (the pause loop, `0x4303bc`), past the loop's heartbeat lock (`0xb10db4`, released at `0x43004c`), and demo's `mclSetResume` and `mclSetSeek` send and return, without waiting on a reply. `mclStop`'s wait for a stopping player allows about 1.5 s. `test/bt_resample.py` runs the three sites under Unicorn; `test/playback.py` and `test/patch.py` hold demo's stamps.

Every natural end out of DSD waits here, a preloaded next track with no mark included: `ao_uninit` sets `ao_wait` from `0xb10e5c`, which also reads 1 at a PCM track's end into a preloaded DSD one, so that waits too, though demo's `set-gapless` sender refuses a DSD URL (`0x5a8a60`), so only a raw command preloads one. A natural start from a local queue also waits here, on demo's even mark, when `ao_init`'s natural branch didn't read it: DSD at either end, no PCM open, or the arm dropped. Demo skipped its mute and sleep for that start, so without the wait about 100 ms of it plays under the mute line. Each word answers once, so a mark `ao_init` read can't wait twice and a press that cut the drain gets no wait from it; a preloaded change at another format waits once on `ao_wait`, and a same-format stay-open reaches no unmute at all. `test/hci_ao.py` runs these in the real player.

Limits. ponytail: a natural start with a start offset that reopens, on any route, passes init's site, and `dac_unmute` reads only a press's stamp, so it keeps the cut. Two more, the same before any of this: the mute line rises about 2 s before a DSD track's end, and about 0.4 s of a FLAC's end is lost going into DSD.

### A press, not a natural change

A track change nobody pressed for adds no sleep unless it really reopens the PCM on a jack, which waits as a press does, after the old track has played to its end ([The old track's end](#usb-output-buffer)). The rest of its path adds `dac_told`'s open, read and close of a tmpfs file, on the wired outputs only, right after the unmute ioctl of a reopen, where the PCM isn't running, and one at `ao_init` on a cold change; a preloaded change at the same format reaches neither. hciplayer can't tell a press from a natural change, but demo can. It writes `/tmp/q2press` (`PRESS_FILE`, an `int` made whole under `.tmp`, renamed and mapped shared, as `/tmp/q2xfade` is; none until the first press or natural start, and no file reads as no press): `now_ms` (`CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, in `peq.c`, so both sides read the same clock; `time_now_ms` runs from demo's own origin, `stm_now`), odd, so never 0. `dac_told`, at the two gated sites only, reads the word: fresh means younger than `DAC_PRESS_MS` (5 s, for demo's mute, its sleep and a slow card's open), compared wrap-safely, and each word answers once. Stale, 0, from the future or no file: stock's unmute and on. `shared_int` writes the file's four bytes rather than `ftruncate` them, which allocates no page: on a full tmpfs the first store would SIGBUS, and now the file simply isn't made.

The stamps are opt-in, since a missed site only keeps the old cut, and all of them in `press_stamp` are for a local queue only (folder or library class; DLNA's load as 2 and 3, the online queues as `0xf1` and `0xf2`):

- `ringnav_change`, past its restart guard: every Next and Previous, Folder Skip's sibling included, so `ringnav_next(0)` and `ringnav_prev` need none, their only callers being under it.
- `ringnav_load` outside a restore: `player_start`'s list picks, so every Cover Flow, Home and Library play, through `play_folder` and `playing_page`.
- `ringnav_play_pause` when stopped (`mclStartPlayer` at `0x515918`), a headset's own play included, which waits only if the output has since become wired.
- `playback_group_skip`, and Up Next's row (`un_play`, not the `player_start_queue` hook, which DLNA and SyncLink's URL plays reach too).
- A user's seek: the hooks on `player_seek_time` (a scrub, a chapter; a paused scrub sends `mclSetSeek` alone, and Play waits in `dac_resume`) and `player_seek_start` (Previous restarting the track, past [its guard](#resume), and only when it will).

SyncLink's phone presses stamp like the Q2's own: `startPlayFolderSong` (class 1) and `startPlayClassSong` reach `player_start`, and its Next and Previous `player_next_music` and `player_prev_music`. `mclStartPlayer` itself would be one site but is the wrong one: the natural routes below all pass through it.

Natural routes, which stamp nothing:

- A preloaded change at the same format never reaches the sites (it neither mutes nor reinits); at another rate it's hciplayer alone (`0xb10df8`), and it reads the word, subject to the limit below. Demo's `ringnav_next(1)` zeroes it after.
- Everything demo drives comes from `player_mainscheduling` → `mclAutoChange` → `mclNextSong(1)`: Gapless off, stock Shuffle's cold start with Crossfade waiting (`XF_COLD_NS`), advanced play's `mclStartPlayer`, Repeat One, DSD to DSD and the queue's end. `ringnav_next(1)` forgets the press and sets `press_natural` to 2 around it, so Folder Skip's rollover inside it (`on_player_autochange` → `player_refresh_playqueue` → `ringnav_load`) stamps nothing.
- `player_reconfig` and `player_save_start` (an output's change, a headset, Standby's resume) call `mclStartPlayer` directly and stamp nothing. They keep the cut.
- Stock's skip past a file it can't play (`on_wm_timer_dialog`, `jalr` at `0x4ea840`): `player_next_music` tail-branches into `player_change_music` with `ra` restored, so `ringnav_change` sees `0x4ea848` and sets `press_natural`, for a Folder Skip load within it too.
- An app's Stream mode (`stream_start`, from its poll) stamps nothing, nor do DLNA and online queues.
- A natural next into a track with a saved place: its start is natural, and Resume Songs' jump a second on goes past the seek hook (`stock_seek_time_trampoline`), so it doesn't wait.
- A-B Repeat's loop (`check_abrepeatmode`, `open_repeatmode`, `mclSetSeek` from the idle callback) isn't a track change and doesn't wait; the point it loops to already goes mute plus 150 ms in stock, unchanged.

ponytail: every wired manual start comes about 130 ms later. A stamp left unspent under 5 s (a refused Next, a press that opened paused, a seek while paused, whose word stock's paused `player_seek_time` leaves, a press and a track under 5 s) gives a natural reopen one 130 ms gap, at most one per word; a per-file stamp would close that. A rapid double Next can spend its word on the first init, so the last track keeps the old cut. Stock's cut stays on A-B Repeat's loop, Resume Songs' jump (a list pick's too, though its start waits), a phone's seek, Next on an online queue, a Memory Playback boot and stock's View Queue row. Spotify's `aplay` and q2video (Videos, Internet Radio, Spotify's sink) don't go through hciplayer and keep the cut. The tail a rate change loses is as it was. A silence pre-roll would cover a pop with the mute line open before the first frame, and wants a setting.

## Unpause

**The fault.** `soc_aic.ko`'s `aic_dma_trigger` sends `PAUSE_RELEASE` to `audio_dma_start`, which starts the DMA at its first descriptor, the ring's base, and `PAUSE_PUSH` to `audio_dma_stop` (the `audio_dma_pause_push` and `_release` it exports are unused). So `snd_pcm_pause(0)` skipped what the ring still held: `hw_ptr` jumped to the next wrap. And demo's pause fades first: `player_play_pause` (`0x515728`) calls `mclSetMute(2)` (`0x5acdc0`), whose driver steps the volume down over about 180 ms and then raises the mute line, sleeps 150 ms and only then calls `mclSetPause`, so the DMA runs 330-365 ms under the fade and the closed line.

**What happens now.** Way 0's writes are also kept in a second ring, `ps_ring` (`ao_write` in `patch/peq_player.c`, on the one `snd_pcm_writei` of ao_alsa's play, `0x43afa4`, one `memcpy` of what was accepted): the ALSA ring plus `PS_HOLD_MS`, in a 2 MB bss array touched only as far as a format needs. `dac_resume` reads the status; on way 0 with the PCM `PAUSED`, it drops and prepares under the mute, releases the DAC, waits `DAC_RELEASE_MS`, and writes the frames again from the fade's second step, through the end of the ring. The DMA starts at the base from a prepared PCM, its normal start. What follows from the next `play` continues the stream. Nothing else on the write path differs, so no byte of a natural change, Gapless or Crossfade does.

**How far back.** `user_mute(2)` (`codec_cs43131.ko`, `0x220`) starts from the volume table's code and loops six times: code += 15 (0.5 dB a code, so 7.5 dB), `cs43131_volume`, `msleep(5)`; then it writes `0xff` and raises the line. The DAC's own code is 12 past the table's (77 and 65 at volume 30), so the first write takes it only 3 codes down. The six read -1.5, -9, -16.5, -24, -31.5 and -39 dB, at volumes 12, 30 and 48 alike. Read from the register each 0.6 ms, the first write is within about 1 ms of the stamp, and writes come 30 ms apart (24-40 ms; 8 of 234 over 35 ms), with the line about 4 ms after the last. Reads that fast don't disturb the writes: the stamp to the line's rise has a median of 181-187 ms with them, by rate and volume, and 185 ms with no I2C at all.

`mute_stamp` (`patch/crossfade.c`, from `ringnav_play_pause` when demo's status is 2) writes the monotonic clock's ms to `/tmp/q2mute` just before stock's `mclSetMute(2)`. Back is `(trigger_time - stamp + PS_TRIM_MS) * rate / 1000`, from the pause's own trigger time in `snd_pcm_status`, so the variable ramp and sleep are measured each time. `PS_TRIM_MS` is -29, the median from the stamp to the second write: 29.0 ms over 39 pauses at 44.1 and 192 kHz and volumes 12, 30 and 48, within 23.6-33.5 ms, with no trend by rate or volume. That is the join. Everything before it was heard at -1.5 dB or better, and everything after it was first heard at -9 dB, then falling 7.5 dB every 30 ms, with the pause in between. So nothing is lost, and what repeats was at least 9 dB quieter the first time, and at least 16.5 dB after its first 30 ms. The write's spread puts the join up to about 5 ms either side of it: a little of the first step repeats, or a little of the second isn't heard again.

An age no greater than the trim, or a stamp older than `PS_FRESH_MS` (1 s) at the pause, or none, means nothing goes back. The clamps are `hw_ptr` (a prepare zeroes it, so frames from before a seek, a reopen or an earlier unpause can't come back, with no hook on any of them) and what `ps_ring` holds. The first `PS_FADE_MS` (4 ms) rise linearly from silence, as the line is open and they'd otherwise step from it. That goes by the format's own bits (`ps_scale`: 1 little-endian, 2 unsigned, 4 float, bits 3-4 its bytes less one), so S16, S24, S32, float (AAC, Vorbis, Opus, WMA) and WAV's and AIFF's others all rise; DSD (`dsd()`, DoP included) goes back bit-exact, with no fade.

**The cases.**

- A seek pending (`0xb10c38`, set before the `mcl-play` demo sends after it): drop, prepare, wait, write nothing. `seek()`'s reset comes next and no old frame sounds.
- Next, Previous or a new file while paused: resume isn't called; stock's path drains and reopens under the closed line, and demo's Play then pauses or resumes the new track as ever. A rate or format change clears `ps_ring` at its first write; a resume on a format it doesn't hold is stock's.
- Not `PAUSED` (a pause that failed), Bluetooth, USB: stock's `snd_pcm_pause(0)`. Those ways don't go through `soc_aic`. A pause during a drain can't reach this: the pause runs on the main thread, which is in the drain, so it lands on the next track's fresh PCM, whose `hw_ptr` holds it to nothing going back. The tail that drain played under stock's closed line (about 350 ms before a change of rate) doesn't come back, as it doesn't on a change nobody paused.
- A way that turns to 0 while paused on a Bluetooth or USB PCM: every write off way 0 forgets what's kept, so this PCM is stock's and no wired frame goes into it. (Not by comparing handles: a reopen's `snd_pcm_t` can land on the old address.)
- Pause and Play both before the main thread acts on the pause, as a double tap or inside the rewrite: no PCM pause happens, so no resume would release demo's `mute(2)`. `hci_command` sees the state go from paused to playing with hciplayer's own flag clear, and sends `mute(0)`. hciplayer's mute sets that flag first, so if the main thread is just getting there, it mutes after this and its resume releases as ever. A DAC that isn't muted takes `mute(0)` as a no-op, the volume it already has.
- A pause after `ao_write` forwarded a dry PCM past its ring, at a kept PCM's first write or a mid-song stall: the ALSA timeline has a gap there that `ps_ring`'s bytes don't, so the forward empties `ps_ring`. What goes back is what was written after the gap, however old the fade; with the gap itself still to play, the ring isn't all held, so the resume is stock's.
- A pause in a track's first frames goes back only as far as `hw_ptr`. Earlier frames of the last track may be in the ring, but a reopen zeroed the pointer.
- A pause after a same-format Gapless change, or in Crossfade's hold or mix: what comes back is what was written, the mix included. The filter and decoder are upstream of the rewrite and don't see it. `get_delay` (`0x438154`) returns 0.0 and the clock counts writes through `play`, so the elapsed time doesn't move.
- The phone and DLNA (`dmr_mainscheduling`, `0x506880`) and the headset's routes and media keys (`patch/headset.c`) go through `player_play_pause`, so they fade and stamp like the Q2's own Pause. Demo's other `mclSetPause` callers (Baidu, Tidal, output changes, `player_save_start`, `player_start`) neither fade nor stamp, and find any stamp stale: back is 0, so only what the ring held is written.

**The other users of the PCM.** Spotify's `aplay` and q2video never pause it; Rockbox's `pcm-alsa` only drops.

**Other designs.** `audio_dma_pause_release` would keep the ring's place in the kernel, but it only fixes the skip, not the fade's lost frames, and it means a kernel module patch; with this it would be used by nothing, as way 0's only remaining `PAUSE_RELEASE` is the kernel's own, inside `snd_pcm_drop`, under the closed line. Seeking back was rejected: a decoder's seek has no frame precision.

ponytail: the rewrite blocks the main thread for what doesn't fit the ALSA ring, at most the back's length, while nothing else needs that thread. A format whose ring plus 500 ms passes `PS_BYTES` is capped, and so may go back less (none at today's rates; 192 kHz S32 takes 899 KB). A second pause reaching the same frames fades them again, since the fade is in place. `hw_ptr`'s boundary wrap, calculated at about 9.1 h of 44.1 kHz on a 22,048-frame ring and 3.1 h of 96 kHz on a 16,384-frame one at one open, makes one resume stock's. What repeats from the join on was heard quieter before the pause. Standby during a pause is traced, not run: `device_standby` calls `player_save_stop` first (`0x4f7a58`). `test/bt_resample.py` drives `dac_resume` and `ao_write` under Unicorn with a modelled PCM; it can't see the DMA itself. `test/playback.py` holds the stamp.

## Large MP3s

Stock `hciplayer` reads an MP3's Xing or VBRI header for its frame count, as MPlayer does, and then, when it found one, walks every frame of the file before playing (`0x4a2d5c`, called from `demux_audio_open` at `0x491de0`): it recounts the frames for the duration, stores the first `0x5000` frame offsets (about 8 minutes) in a table at `0xd30eb0` and sets MPlayer's `hr_mp3_seek` (`0xb152b8`). That reads the whole file off the card, 140 MB for a two-hour yt-dlp mix and 700 MB for a six-hour one, so the track starts late or seems not to start, and the gapless preload of the next track does it again. A seek past the table walks frames from its last entry, as slowly. An `Info` (CBR) header skips all of it.

The build makes the branch to that walk (`bnez` at `0x48fb3c`, `VBR_SCAN` in `tools/peq.py`, pinned) a nop, so a VBR file takes the path `Info` files already take: the duration is the header's frame count, `hr_mp3_seek` stays 0 and a seek goes to `movi_start + seconds * average bytes per second` at once. On its own that seek is as exact as the bitrate is even: on the card's files the worst case was 9 s in a 2.4-hour jazz mix and 6 minutes in a 5-hour game soundtrack whose tracks differ widely.

The Xing header's 100-point table fixes that. The audio demuxer's descriptor (`0x89fd90`) has its seek slot (`0x89fdbc`, `SEEK_SLOT` in `tools/peq.py`, pinned to stock `demux_audio_seek` at `0x48d1ac`) pointed at `mp3_seek` in `patch/peq_player.c`. For an MP3 (`priv->frmt` 1, `priv` at `demuxer+0xc70`) without `hr_mp3_seek` and not a factor seek, it reads the first frame once per file with `lseek64`/`read` on the stream's fd (`stream_t +0x14`) and restores the fd's offset, since stock's `stream_seek` can reuse its buffer without seeking. `mp3_toc` then parses the Xing header: MPEG 1, 2 or 2.5 Layer III, frame count and table both present; `Info` (CBR) and VBRI keep the stock seek. The target second (`priv->next_pts`, a double at `+8`, plus the offset for a relative seek) becomes a fraction of the track and is read off the table, interpolated between its points. Stock then seeks with `SEEK_ABSOLUTE | SEEK_FACTOR` to that fraction of `movi_start..movi_end`, and `next_pts` is set to the target second, so the shown time is exact. The landing is within the table's resolution, about 1% of the bytes between two points.

## Forced EOF

Stock's main loop (`0x4232a4`) set the eof flag (`mpctx+0x14`) once the whole-second position passed the header's length by more than a second, for a file on the card, samba or a CD (`0xb15df0` 1, 2 or 3, set by the file stream's open and 0 for anything else, so never a URL). On most lengths that only cut audio: a VBR MP3 with no Xing and a quiet start (header 112 s, true 120 s: 114 s played), a WMA whose positions start at its 3.1 s preroll (one synthetic 8 s file: 6.91 s played), a FLAC whose STREAMINFO understates.

An MP3's Xing or Info frame count is different. It says where the audio ends, and past it libmad finds false syncs in whatever follows, about 0.6 s of noise per MB, up to full scale, each moving the position on. But the count can be stale, or two tagged MP3s joined with `cat`, with music past it. So `forced_eof` (`jal` in place of stock's store, `0x4232bc`) stores only if the audio demuxer's MP3 has that count in its first frame (`forced_eof_c`, read off the file), the position is past it by more than a second, and no two frames chain at the file's cursor (`frames_follow`: the second header where the first's length says, both of the first frame's version, layer and rate; false syncs don't chain), with at least 4 KB of file left. It runs only once the position is past the player's length, so a track that isn't over never gets there. A Xing-tagged MP3 with 3 MB of random bytes after the audio: 0.34 s past its audio, as stock (2.22 s with the store removed outright). Removing the store entirely would play a 3 MB APEv2 cover as 2.18 s of full-scale noise, against 0.22 s. A count of any shortness plays out, and so does a joined file, with junk after it stopped the same way. Everything else ends at the file's own end.

M4A, Ogg, WavPack, FLAC, WAV and WMA with the same 3 MB appended play exactly what they do without it (`test/hci_natural.py`, `tail_noise`). CUE doesn't use this: the player's length is the whole image's and demo ends a track itself (`mclAutoChange`, `0x5ae6b0`).

`ponytail:` 20 s of unsyncable bytes after the count run the buffer dry about 2 s before the next song starts, silent and with no XRUN; 3 s of the same is clean.

`ponytail:` a joined file whose own big tag is being read at the count + 1 s, or whose rate changes, ends there, as stock. Junk after a joined file can end it a little before its audio does: the chain is read ahead of what's playing. The real ceiling is noise. An MP3 with no header followed by junk still plays it, at full scale, about 0.74 s per MB (3 MB: 2.22 s) with elapsed running past the total, exactly as stock does: its length estimate counts the junk. Nothing says where its audio ends. A WMA's elapsed still starts about 3 s on, as in stock, so Next's preload and Crossfade's hold come about 3 s early, which neither minds.

A DSF's header can't understate here: the same file with its sample count halved plays the same length, and a halved data chunk stops there. Stock's `0x4232cc` calls the mute helper `0x43b280(1)` at position >= length - 1 for formats `0xc`, `0xe`, `0x10`, `0x11` with `0xb10e5c` at 1, and relies on the store to end such a track, so `forced_eof_c` keeps stock's store whenever `0xb10e5c` is 1. `test/hci_natural.py` lowers the length under a playing DSF to reach it.

## Music info between songs

demo's status thread (`mclSyncPlayerStatus`) asks `{media-info\get-music-info}` about four times a second, on the player's command thread. That branch (`0x42c030`) checks `mpctx` (`0xaf9e30`), its demuxer (`+0x28`) and the length words, then reads `sh_audio` (`+0x2c`). When `sh_audio` is NULL stock's branch only skips the bitrate lookup and reads `sh_audio->0xd4` anyway (`0x42c094`): SIGSEGV, and demo restarts the player, so the song starts again from its top.

A natural change stores the demuxer about 7,200 instructions before `sh_audio`, each time. A request landing there is the crash. So the branch (`0x42c068`) now goes to the reply for no stream, `{media-info\no-playing-status}`, which demo already handles. The same branch covers the path through the length call (`0x42c588`, over a dead load), which otherwise reaches it past `0x42c064`'s read unchecked. That the pointer goes NULL there isn't established: the only store found is `0x73efbc`, whose base looks unrelated. The check stays, since it costs a dead load.

`mcl-seteq`'s enable read `mpctx->sh_audio->0x3c` with no check at all (`0x42cec0`), only for the 48 kHz gate the EQ already removes (`EQ_RATE_GATES`). demo sends enable when its status isn't stopped: a switch of the EQ or its output, or the filter's first attach. Those three loads are gone, so it can't fault; with or without a stream it stores what `pre_eqset` does.

`ponytail:` get-music-info's fix closes the NULL only. Its checks aren't under a lock, so a stream freed just after them is still read.

## A bad file next

With Gapless on, `set-gapless` starts the preload thread (`0x42b8d0`) about 6 s before the song ends. When the next file opens but no demuxer takes it (random bytes or a 0-byte file named `.flac`, an `.m4a` with no moov), the demuxer list reaches the DSF check (`0x47b8f0`), whose failure path stores the global stop, code 2, on purpose: pass 2's loop (`0x485934`) reads it and gives up before lavf, which is why such a file is "not supported" rather than handed to lavf. The DFF and SACD ISO checks (`0x47a1ac`, `0x478444`) do the same. On the main thread that's right. On the preload thread it stopped the song still playing, about 5 s early: the main loop read the flag as its own stop (`0x4234cc`), closed the PCM and touched `/tmp/notsupport`.

So those stores now jump to `probe_stop`. On the main thread (`gettid == getpid`) it stores as stock does. On any other thread it stores nothing, and the loop still gives up, as it reads the flag through `$18`, which `probe_stop` points at a word of our own for that one read. A `.dff`, `.dsf` or `.iso` goes to its check first, by name (the forced path, `0x485aec`), which reads no flag: it falls to the probing, which reaches the loop. Saving and restoring the flag around the thread's open wouldn't do: the main thread and the FLAC decoder's check (`patch/flac_sync.c`) can read it between the store and the restore, and a real stop landing during the open would be lost. A good file never reaches this store, so a successful preload runs the same instructions.

The song now plays to its last frame, except under Crossfade, below. The thread's -1 stays in `0xb10de8`; at the song's EOF the main loop opens the bad file itself, on the main thread, and that open reports as stock's does: `{player-status\error}` and `/tmp/notsupport`, which demo shows for 2 s before its Next. A missing file never got this far (`ENOENT` stops at the open). macOS `._x.flac` files don't get here from demo's scanners, which skip `._` names (`0x5c22a0`, `0x5c3fe0`); only a playlist or an app's list naming one does.

Three more stores have the same shape: `0x478b5c`, the ISO check's TOC with no tracks; `0x48018c`, the AAC scan finding no frame in a `.aac`; `0x4826bc`, the ASF open finding no streams after its check took the file. Each takes its base from a hook in place of its first `lui` (`stop_base`): stock's on the main thread; on any other, a word of our own, with the `$18` the probe loop's call saved pointed at `probe_stopped` so the loop gives up as stock's does. `0x46c184`, the ISO's fill running out of retries, runs on the thread once its decoder starts, and there's no loop to stop, so `stop_fill` just doesn't store. `0x42a47c` is `exit_sighandler`, the process's own crash: left.

`ponytail:` stores deep in the decoders (`0x4ee61c`, `0x508370`, `0x52f584`, `0x5b6260`) aren't covered or traced; a thread's first decode of a next file reaching one still stops the song. The fill's hook has run only in review, not on a real ISO.

Under Crossfade the hold runs the decoder ahead, so when the bad file reports, A's last seconds are held in the fade's buffer, not played: a gap and the prompt, then A's ending mixed under C when demo's Next starts it, about 2 s on (a 3 s fade: A at 0.97 of its level in C's first second). A's file ended by itself, so the hold waits up to `XF_COLD_NS`, 30 s, through a second bad file too (0.97 at 5 s); if C starts later than that, those last seconds are dropped (0.05 at 35 s). The limit stands until a fade can play its tail out at the end of A.

A next file that opens and has no audio, say a FLAC's STREAMINFO with a good rate and no frames, opens, selects its decoder and ends at once: hciplayer sends `{player-status\exit}` for the song and again for it, about 4 ms apart, with no `error` and no `/tmp/notsupport`. demo learns of an end only from the newest `player-status` it reads (`0x5a8688`, `0x5a7e88`): one value in a map, cleared by a read of `exit` and overwritten by any later reply. The read clears `0xa3be55`, and the next UI pass's `mclNextSong` (`0x5adbbc`) sees the preload and moves the position without a word to hciplayer. A second `exit` that reaches demo before that move is read with the first or overwritten by a `ready`, and is lost. hciplayer then idles answering `ready`, demo keeps asking, the file's length is 0 so no time check ends it, and the queue sits on the silent file until a key. Nearly every pass went that way; the odd one that moved on read its second `exit` after the move. Read from the code; the other `0x5a8688` callers (`0x61873c`, `0x618cd8`) aren't.

So `exit_hold` (`jal` in place of the send's first word, `0x4226d4`, the only code using that string) sends an `exit` no sooner than 300 ms after the last. Requests are answered on the command thread (`0x42c9f4`) and `exit` goes from the main one, so nothing queues behind it. A UI pass runs up to 222 ms with the screen dark, and a second `exit` read after the first just waits in the map a pass later. A track of 300 ms or more never reaches the wait, so an ordinary change runs as it did. It covers the swap and the cold path, and a real very short track, whose audio is all there and whose end comes up to 300 ms late with the ring still playing. Counting `exit`s in demo isn't an answer: a count left over after a manual skip would skip a good song. Nor is a watchdog: `ready` during a slow cold open looks the same as this.

A file with no rate at all (`Cannot find codec`) is a different path: `exit`, `error` and the prompt.

## A FLAC cut inside its metadata

`demux_audio_open`'s FLAC metadata walk reads each block's header and skips its length until a block flagged last. Past the file's end, `stream_read_char` gives -256, so the type byte has no last flag and the length is negative: the walk seeks back by it (`0x490ba0`, `0x4929a4`). A file under 256 bytes goes to position 0 (`Invalid seek to negative position`), reads `fLaC` as a block header, skips to the end and starts again, with no end. demo's process watch kills hciplayer and starts it on the same file, over and over, silent, until a key. A longer file lands 256 bytes before its end, and whether that ends depends on those bytes. `FLAC_META_EOF` sends a negative length to the walk's end, where a last block goes: such a file is then a FLAC with no frames, handled as in [a bad file next](#a-bad-file-next).

## A preload too late

A `set-gapless` that arrives less than one open before demux EOF loses the race: the main loop sees `0xb10de8` at 1, clears it (`0x421a3c`) and cold-starts the file itself. Stock's thread then stored 2 regardless (`0x42bb18`), so the next `set-gapless` found the word non-zero and started nothing, and at that song's EOF the main loop swapped in the stale context: the same song again, bit for bit, while the log and the screen named the next one. A thread that failed stored -1 the same way, which only cost the next preload (a gap, the right song).

The word has a fourth value now, 3: the thread lost. `cold_clear` turns 1 or 3 into 3 and anything else into 0; `preload_publish` stores 2 only over a 1; a thread that finds anything else frees its context with stock's `0x42a9ac` and moves 3 to 0 (`preload_stale`); the failure exits move 1 to -1 or 3 to 0 (`preload_failed`, the three stores of -1 moved there). Spawning wants 0 (`0x42358c`), so no second thread starts while the stale one runs, and two never share the one preload context: a `set-gapless` in that window waits, and its thread starts when the word reaches 0. All are ll/sc, since the main thread's store can land between a load and a store. Stock's handlers that clear the word test for 2 first, so they don't touch a 3.

Publishing costs 11 instructions in place of one store, and no call. A cold start's clear costs 11 too, in place of its one store.

A queue edit does the same from the other side. `mcl-closegapless` and the direct stop only clear a 2 (`0x42c268`, `0x42c998`), and demo's `close_preload()` has 14 call sites. So `set-gapless b` with a slow open, a close, then `set-gapless c` left the word at 1, the thread published 2, `c` never opened, and b played after a. `preload_close` and `preload_stop`, from the `li` before each read, turn 1 into 3 with ll/sc, and the thread is then stale as above. Its URL has gone, so nothing restarts it, and a `c` sent meanwhile starts once the word reaches 0.

The thread and the cold path do run `demux_open` at once; stock's mutex `0xd30b9c` is taken by the thread and `0x42a9ac`, not the cold path. Comparing static memory across each open, the only words both change are the preload's own state and `mp_msg`'s line buffer (`0xe0c288`, a log line at worst); read from the code, also `0xb15df0` and the stream cache size (`0xaf9b60`), whose values depend on the file. That comparison sees changed values, not a store of the same value.

`ponytail:` a thread that publishes just before the cold path's clear leaves its context unfreed until the next preload overwrites it, as `mcl-closegapless` does in stock (a file handle). That close is reachable: Gapless off in the 6 s before a song's end adds one open file each time, for the boot (8 to 26 fds over 20 closes), and so may demo's other `close_preload()` sites; Next and Previous don't send it. Under MQA decode the main thread has cleared `0xb10d70`, so a stale thread's decoder isn't uninitialised. `0x42a9ac` has no decoder uninit, so each lost preload may leak the decoder, as stock's close sites do: not measured. A preload racing a cold start can hit `avcodec_open2`'s "Insufficient thread locking" (read from the code, not seen). In a narrow window with no `mcl-stop` before it, `mp_msg("Failed to open %s.\n", -1)` at `0x4a53a0` faults on `strlen(-1)` (`pc=0x60580038`, in the emulator): stock's, and left.

## A FLAC seek near the end

`mcl-seek` on a FLAC goes to stock's demuxer seek (`0x48d1ac`), which writes the target seconds to `0xb152b0` (cleared when the file closes, `0x48b808`), estimates a byte position from the file's size, probes the first frame's time there (`0x48b81c`), steps 64 KB at a time (`0xffff`), then scans for a frame's sync (`0x48d578`). The decoder is what makes it exact: `0x5b6aac` fails any frame whose time is before the target. That time is frame number x this frame's block size / rate. Three faults.

**The file's end.** The last frame of a fixed-block file is short, and its time by its own size is early: 40 s, 430 frames, a last frame of 3497: 430 x 3497 / 44100 = 34.1 s. Any target past that lost the frame, with a step at the seam and the mute line open. 20 s of noise with a last frame of 2137: 215 x 2137 / 44100 = 10.4 s, clean to 10 and lost from 11. So it depends neither on the bytes nor the time remaining. `flac_gate` (at `0x5b7dc4`, the gate's failure) calls `flac_last`, which passes a frame shorter than STREAMINFO's maximum only if frame number x maximum / rate isn't before the target and, with a total in STREAMINFO, the total less this frame divides evenly into frames at least as long as it. So a stream whose fixed block is below its maximum, every frame of it short by that test, stays gated until its own time reaches the target; only its last frame passes early. A variable-block file's time has the sample number, so it stays gated.

**One block, then silence.** A gated frame returned -1. `ad_ffmpeg` stops on that (`0x4ad0c0`), and if the demuxer had already read to EOF, the main loop ended the track with the rest still queued in the parser: no audio or a 4096-frame block, about 2 s of silence, then the next track. `flac_gate` now consumes it: the packet's size back, `got_frame` still 0 (`0x5b7128`, stock's epilogue), and `ad_ffmpeg` carries on to the next frame.

**The 64 KB step.** Within 64 KB of the file's end the probe loop left at the unprobed next position (`0x48d418`), and a probe past the target stepped on, not back (`0x48d80c`). At 33 KB/s that's 2 s: 37 of 40 landed at 39. Or it held no sync at all, so the seek landed on the file's last bytes and the demuxer ended (38 of 40). Those exits now keep the position probed, and the gate plays from the target; `seek_nosync` covers a scan with no sync from an unprobed estimate, by going back 64 KB. A seek that starts before the target lands on the first frame at or past it, and from there it's bit-exact to the end.

`ponytail:` a seek whose estimate falls within 64 KB of the end doesn't probe it, so it starts where the estimate says, and on a file of very uneven bitrate that can be after the target, by however far the estimate is off. A STREAMINFO total that's wrong keeps a short last frame gated, as stock; one that's zero leaves only the maximum's test, so a stream of blocks below its maximum can land early. None of these loses anything after the landing.

## MP3 gapless playback

`patch/mp3_gapless.c` reads Xing and Info headers and the LAME-compatible extension. LAME's, Lavc's and Lavf's names read in any case, and any other printable name (`L3.99r`, `GOGO`) reads only if the tag's CRC-16 holds, whatever the tag revision. An exact `LAME`, `Lavc` or `Lavf`, as this read until then, is what left real files untrimmed: an encoder writes `Lame3.100`, and on those files the CRC is right for that spelling and wrong for `LAME3.100`. FFmpeg's list compares exactly too, so FFmpeg leaves them untrimmed and its agreement is no evidence for this class of file; [mpg123](https://github.com/madebr/mpg123/blob/master/src/libmpg123/parse.c), which has no list, trims them to frames x 1152 - delay - padding. Those three need no CRC, as stock's list never asked it. The name's first four bytes must be printable, so a tag with no extension (zeros, or a tool's binary) doesn't read as delays, and the 12 field bytes after the 9-byte name must not all be text, which would be a longer name. It follows the optional Xing flags to find the extension, bounds every field to the first MPEG frame, and accepts MPEG 1/2/2.5, mono/stereo and both protected-frame layouts. A Xing, Info or VBRI frame with no usable extension still has no audio, so its own samples go (`start` = one frame, no padding), as FFmpeg drops it, and mpg123 too for Xing and Info; the test is its side info, all zeros, which lame and FFmpeg both write, and a frame with anything there keeps playing. Missing or truncated tags keep stock playback. No silence detection is used: intentional silence stays in the recording.

The encoder records two packed 12-bit sample counts. The decoder adds its own 529-sample delay, so the leading trim is encoder delay + 529, and the trailing trim is max(encoder padding − 529, 0), per channel. Unlike [FFmpeg's MP3 demuxer](https://github.com/FFmpeg/FFmpeg/blob/master/libavformat/mp3dec.c), MPlayer's `demux_audio` sends the Xing/Info frame to the decoder as audio, so the initial trim also includes that frame's 1152 or 576 samples. The layout and delay correction agree with [LAME's tag writer](https://github.com/gypified/libmp3lame/blob/master/libmp3lame/VbrTag.c) and [minimp3's gapless reader](https://github.com/lieff/minimp3/blob/master/minimp3_ex.h).

The checked allocation call at `0x48fb90` becomes `mp3_private_alloc`: the original 16-byte audio-demuxer private prefix is preserved, with zeroed gapless state appended. The `af_play` call at `0x47f8e0` goes through `mp3_filter_entry`, which passes the caller's `sh_audio` from `$fp` to `mp3_filter`. This runs before every audio filter, independent of EQ, with both raw MP3 decoder paths. The first block reads up to 2048 bytes at `movi_start` through the stream fd and restores the fd offset. PCM trimming uses two fixed tail workspaces of at most 3565 frames each in one allocation (at most 57,040 bytes per track). It shifts PCM in place, so larger blocks need no further allocation and cannot lose held audio on allocation failure. Tracks with no trailing trim need no allocation or PCM copy. The tail is discarded at EOF. It does not estimate the end from Xing duration or a seek's approximate byte position. Preloaded tracks have independent state. `mp3_seek` clears held audio on every seek and reapplies leading trimming only at the start. Stock's resync flag (`0xb152b4`, set by the seek at `0x48d7f4`) makes the fill (`0x48c3b8`) read past the first frame to check the next one's header and go back to it (`0x48c3d0`), and then size that packet from the next frame (`0x48c4d0`): on VBR, or a padded tag frame ahead of a shorter one, the tag frame is cut short, libmad loses sync and the track starts two frames (2304 samples, 52 ms) late, in stock too. `mp3_gapless_seek` clears the flag on a seek to the start, where there is nothing to check, and the tag frame reaches the decoder whole. In demo's calls, that seek comes from Previous when it restarts the track rather than changing it (`player_prev_music`, `player_seek_start`), an A point at 0 (`check_abrepeatmode`, `open_repeatmode`) and Scrub, the slider or Resume landing on 0 (`player_seek_time`); no Repeat One path calls them. There is no second decode ahead of the change: a preloaded track is opened when the last one hits EOF (`0x42a1b8` frees it, then the normal open and `reinit_audio_chain`), and `mp3_filter`'s first block is the new track's first. The audio demuxer's close slot (`0x89fdb8`, stock `0x48b7e4`) frees the buffers before stock frees its private state. Allocation failure disables trimming for that track. libmad decodes a frame only with `MAD_BUFFER_GUARD` (8) bytes after it, and `demux_audio` hands `ad_libmad` whole frames, so at EOF stock's `read_frame` (`0x4b23a0`) dropped every MP3's last frame: 1152 samples of the track's end, which the trailing trim then shortened further, an audible tick into a continuous next track (#135). Its `demux_read_data` call (`0x4b248c`) goes through `mp3_read`, which answers the first EOF of an audio-demuxer MP3 with 8 zero bytes so libmad decodes that frame; a seek re-arms it. This applies to untagged MP3s too.

Stock's fill (`0x48c184`) scans on for frames past the audio. It only checks `movi_end` on a failed header (`0x48c91c`-`0x48c948`), so a frame sync in an APEv2 tag decodes as noise, and the trailing trim then cuts that and plays the real padding. Real JPEG bytes happen not to do it; a PNG does. mpg123 stops there. The first block's read also takes the file's last 160 bytes; `mp3_tail` finds ID3v1 and an APEv2 footer there, and `mp3_fill`, at the demuxer's fill slot (`0x89fdb0`), reports EOF once the stream reaches them. A wrong size would cut music, where FFmpeg, mpg123 and stock play on, so the footer must have version <= 2000 and size >= 32 and say it has a header, and one more read must find `APETAGEX` at that place. Without a header the tag is left alone, a noise risk taken over a false stop. On a file's first block only; the guard, seeks and the frames before the tag are as before.

iTunes writes no Xing frame or extension, only an iTunSMPB comment in ID3v2: reserved, priming, remainder, samples, in hex, usually `COM` in ID3v2.2. FFmpeg parses it since a1d17fdd28, and `mp3_smpb` keeps its refusals: priming over 16384, no samples, or, when a Xing or VBRI frame gives a count, priming + samples + remainder not equal to the count's samples. It adds remainder over 4095, as much as a LAME tag can ask for, since `mp3_trim`'s tail buffer is sized from it. A valid extension wins, and such a file reads nothing more; otherwise `mp3_smpb_text` walks the tags ahead of `movi_start`, reading frame headers and seeking past bodies, 64 frames at most, on a file's first block.

The reading differs from FFmpeg's. That commit says priming covers the decoder's delay, as the mov demuxer's AAC does, so it skips priming and cuts the remainder. The FATE file (priming 528, remainder 2154, samples 418950, exactly 9.5 s; 366 frames, no Xing) decodes with its loud audio stopping dead at sample 420006, 528 + 529 + 418950 - 1: past FFmpeg's cut at 419478 the level is still 9113, past 420007 it is 60. mpg123 and libavcodec agree on that to one count; through this chain the file comes out at exactly 418950 samples. No other iTunes file was measured. The same audio cut hard and put through `lame -t`, known delay 1105, reads its known end exactly (420054), so the measure is good. The sum can't tell the two readings apart. So here it is LAME's arithmetic: start = priming + 529, end trim = remainder - 529, clamped at zero, with one frame more at the start for a tag frame.

Limitations:

- A file with no gapless metadata (no Xing or Info frame, VBRI, a Xing or Info frame without the extension, `lame -t`, sox, FFmpeg's `-write_xing 0`) can't be exact: nothing says how much to cut. Measured at 320 kbps on a sweep, 1105 samples of delay at the next track's start plus the first track's padding, so 1624 to 2176 samples at the boundary depending on where the split falls (37 to 49 ms). An extension-less tag frame's file decodes byte for byte as the same audio with no tag frame. FFmpeg agrees on the frame except on a Xing frame with neither a frame count nor a size, which it keeps.
- iTunSMPB rests on one real file, FFmpeg's FATE `gapless-itunes.mp3` (iTunes 12.7.0.166), read as below. If another iTunes version counts the decoder's delay in its priming, as FFmpeg's reading has it, its tracks lose 529 samples at the end and gain 529 samples of near-silence at the start: about 12 ms at the boundary, still less than the 37 to 61 ms of playing it untrimmed.
- A tag whose delay and padding are both zero is left alone but for its frame; FFmpeg and mpg123 cut their 529 there, which is no nearer exact.
- A track cut from a longer stream so that its first frames need a bit reservoir left in the part before stays as stock: libmad drops those frames, one frame short for each, and the trim then takes real audio. Such a start is rare, and silencing them would add silence to untagged cuts, and was exact only when the reservoir didn't fit in the tag frame. A cutter that keeps the reservoir in the Info frame (pcutmp3) decodes as it is.
- `lame --nogap` tracks carry the signal across files in the encoder and padding under 529. Padding below 529 is clamped to zero; recovering those samples would mean feeding an extra compressed frame.
- Real MPEG frames after the tag's count, or a frame sync in a tail that isn't ID3v1 or APEv2 and decodes, play as audio and move the trailing trim: 1152 samples each. FFmpeg and mpg123 play them too. The tag's frame count isn't used to stop: on `cat a.mp3 b.mp3` that would lose b. ponytail: an APEv2 tag ahead of Lyrics3 isn't found either.
- Free-format MP3 isn't interpreted, and a file with 64 KB of junk or zeros ahead of its first frame failed to open in stock's demuxer under Unicorn (1 KB is fine).
- The handoff depends on the length estimate demo reads (`mclAutoChange`, `len - pos < 6`). With a Xing or Info frame's count that is exact, whatever is in the tail. Without one, stock takes (end of file - first frame) / the first frame's bitrate: at 320 kbps a 300 KB tail tag reads 7.4 s long, and a VBR file opening on a 32 kbps frame read 3.3 s for 1.5 s, in proportion for a longer track. Over by more than the 6 s window, the track ends before demo sends `set-gapless`, the next starts cold, and a filter or resampler carry (`pts > LENGTH - 2`) doesn't happen either. Those files have no gapless data to begin with; a true length for them needs the whole-file walk that `VBR_SCAN` removed. A track of under 3 s (`len >= 3`) is never preloaded, whatever its format.
- This removes encoded padding; physical track handoff still uses stock's Gapless setting and output pipeline.
- Each file is encoded alone, so the encoder saw silence past its end and a split's samples differ from an unsplit encode's near the boundary, as in any player, with none lost or inserted.
- With a tail tag, stock's CBR seek lands late: its byte rate and `movi_end` include the tail. On a 6 s file at 128 kbps with 60 KB behind it, a seek to 2 s landed about 1.25 s late, in proportion to the tail's share of the file. The byte rate is fixed at open, so setting `movi_end` to the audio's end changes nothing (measured); a true rate would need the seek to redo stock's sum.
- On Bluetooth a seek doesn't reset the resampler's state, as it was: output after a rewind has the same length, not the same samples.

`test/mp3_gapless.py` covers parser bounds, optional fields, malformed tags and PCM blocks from one sample to a whole track, and compares 16 generated LAME/FFmpeg files with FFmpeg's gapless output sample for sample. It feeds compressed frames directly to libavcodec for the untrimmed reference, avoiding FFmpeg demuxer's automatic metadata-frame removal, and verifies libmad's output lengths and sample alignment too. `test/mp3_gapless_player.py` executes the built MIPS hooks with modelled libc, fd I/O and downstream filters, including preload isolation, seeks before/after the first block, untagged bypass, allocation failure and cleanup. It checks that steady playback does not repeat file I/O or allocations, and that zero-tail trimming uses no PCM copies or buffers. Its `real_chain` then runs the built player's own open, demuxer, codec selection (it picks `mad`) and filter chain with the firmware's `libmad.so` under Unicorn, on files `lame` makes at test time: other encoder names and a tag revision, with and without their CRC, a `Lame3.100` file with a large ID3v2.4 cover and APEv2 then ID3v1 behind it, tag frames with no extension, iTunSMPB in each ID3v2 form, ID3v2 with a footer twice, an Info frame's header unlike the audio's, ID3v1 and APEv2 tails, wrong and unconfirmed APEv2 footers, padding beyond one frame, and rewinds on CBR, VBR and a padded tag frame. Each must equal the plain file's output byte for byte, and that output the untrimmed decode's samples at its offset. Both suites run during release validation. Hardware listening and battery measurements are still required to assess actual track handoff and power use.

## Lossy gapless playback

MP3's trim needs its own parse, because `demux_audio` keeps nothing of the tag.
