Everything else lossy goes through libavformat, FFmpeg 3.0's (Lavf 57.25.100) built into `hciplayer`, which has already read the numbers. `patch/lavf_gapless.c`, called from `mp3_filter` at the same `af_play` hook (`0x47f8e0`, before every filter), turns them into one window of stream timestamps per track, and cuts what each block has outside it. Each block's place comes from the decoder's own bookkeeping, as `written_audio_pts` counts it: `sh_audio` `pts` (+0x20, the last packet's), plus `pts_bytes` (+0xd0, decoded since), less what's left in `a_buffer` after this block (`a_buffer_len`, +0x6c). So a seek needs nothing: priming goes because a block's timestamps are before the start, wherever it came from; a mid-track seek lands past it; and there's no held tail, no copy and no allocation beyond the window. The window lives after `lavf_priv_t`, whose `calloc` (`0x4aea48`, its delay slot `LAVF_PRIV` in `tools/peq.py`) grows by 32 bytes: `lavf_check_file` makes one per demuxer, stock frees it, a preload has its own, and the first block of each track fills it in.

`demux_lavf` copies each packet whole with `memcpy` and has no side data of its own, but it doesn't lose it: `av_read_frame` merges `AV_PKT_DATA_SKIP_SAMPLES` onto the packet's end and `avcodec_decode_audio4` splits it off again. So what stock's decoder already did (below) is left to it.

| Format                                             | Start                                                                                                                  | End                                                                                                                                                                | Comes from                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| -------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| M4A's AAC, iTunSMPB (with or without an edit list) | stock: `mov_read_custom`'s `start_pad` goes to the decoder, and `mov_read_seek` sets it again for a seek to 0          | stream's end less the padding: stts total − priming − the tag's total, or the tag's remainder if its total is 0                                                    | the tag, in `AVFormatContext.metadata` (+0x484)                                                                                                                                                                         |
| M4A's AAC, edit list only                          | timestamps below 0: `AVStream.start_time` is minus the edit's media time                                               | the edit's duration, in the movie's timescale (`MOVContext` +8), or the stts total if that's less                                                                  | `MOVStreamContext.elst_data` (+0x38), `elst_count` (+0x3c): one entry only. `mov_read_trak` freed it; `MOV_EDITS` in `tools/peq.py` removes that call (`0x5168f8`), and `mov_read_close` frees it as it always tried to |
| Ogg Opus                                           | timestamps below `start_time`: `opus_packet` starts the first packet at minus the pre-skip, though `start_time` says 0 | stock: `opus_packet`'s end trimming                                                                                                                                | RFC 7845's pre-skip, bounded through `AVCodecContext.delay` (+0x78)                                                                                                                                                     |
| Ogg Vorbis                                         | nothing to cut                                                                                                         | `start_time` + `duration`: the last page's granule, from `ogg_get_length`. `vorbis_packet` only shortens the last packet's duration, which the decoder never hears |                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

Why a window and not `AVStream.first_discard_sample`, lavf's own: `read_frame_internal` counts the discard from `pkt.duration`, and for FFmpeg's M4As (short last stts entry) and Vorbis that's already short, so it computes nothing. And why not `mp3_trim`'s held tail: that needs the padding before the end, and Vorbis's isn't known until the last packet. A tag's priming isn't taken over: the decoder's skip runs across packets (a 2112 priming empties two, and part of a third, whose `pts` is then 64 frames off until the next), so the window has no start in that case. The end is only ever cut off the last packet's frames, where `pts` is exact again.

**An end is believed only inside the last packet.** Real padding lies inside the last packet, so an end at or before that packet's timestamp is wrong, and so is one with a packet still to come. A block past the end keeps every frame unless the end is above the block's packet's `pts` (strictly, since a last delta of 0 is a whole frame nobody meant to cut) and `ds_get_next_pts` (`0x485ea8`, stock's, called at open) finds no packet behind it. Its answer is ignored, since a packet without a timestamp returns the same as no packet: the test is the queue (`ds->first`, +0x48) being empty and the current packet (+0, +4) being fully read, as a sample holding two AUs isn't until its second is decoded. It runs the demuxer's fill directly, so it can't set `ds->eof` (+0x1c; only `ds_fill_buffer` does), and a packet it queues is the one the next decode would read. So it blocks no longer than that read would, and it touches only this track's demuxer, not a preload's. It runs only on the last packet, so steady play gains no I/O.

**The first block.** A window is built on a track's first block, so that block can veto it. Vorbis with all its audio on one Ogg page (a short track, or one cut with `-c copy`) has a first page that's also the last: the first packet decodes to nothing and the first block starts at 128, not at `start_time`, so its end can't be placed and the track plays as stock does. HE-AAC's packets decode to over a frame: if `pts_bytes` says so, the track plays as stock does, whatever its timescale says, because a timescale of the output rate with stts deltas of the core's and a tag of a little padding would cut 1054 frames, and that shape can't be ruled out. Its cost is HE-AAC's gap, which stays. HE and HE+PS from `libfdk-aac` at both timescales, and in that shape, come to stock's frames. A priming is cut from a block only if the block starts no further below the start than the priming is long: a chained Opus's block holding the end of one link and the next's pre-skip starts further below, and keeps both.

**Sanity.** Timestamps must be in units of the block's rate (`time_base` 1/rate), or the track is left alone. An iTunSMPB whose priming lavf refused or is 0 (AAC always has some, so its count can't be right), or whose padding comes out negative or a frame or more (Apple's TN2258 has it under a frame) isn't used, and the edit list is tried; an edit whose end is a frame or more from stts's isn't used either, and the end is stts's, whose short last delta is how FFmpeg writes padding. An edit list with more than one entry, or a media time over 16383, isn't read. Pre-skip over half a second is ignored. What's ignored plays as stock does. No silence detection: silence in the music stays.

**Crossfade and Bluetooth.** The hook comes before every filter, so the resampler's carry and the crossfade's hold only see a shorter track. Crossfade's position is `written_audio_pts`, which a trim doesn't move, and its window is from the length: it holds whatever arrives, so a track's last frames being gone shortens what it holds by them and nothing else; before, the fade ran down into that padding. The suite measures both: fading 1 s between 4 s M4As, the next track starts exactly 44100 frames before the first's true end; through Bluetooth's 48 kHz resampler, an M4A pair's seam is 0 against a FLAC pair of the same music.

Limitations:

- ADTS `.aac` has no metadata: it keeps its priming and padding (1024 + whatever the encoder left, about 43 ms from FFmpeg). WMA goes through the native ASF demuxer with no numbers to read; its priming (2048 frames measured, plus padding of up to a frame) is a property of the codec, not the file, and fixing half a seam in a rare format isn't worth a demuxer hook.
- Matroska and WebM's Vorbis and Opus aren't read: `mov` and `ogg` are the only input formats checked. HE-AAC plays as stock does, with stock's gap.
- An end early by less than what's left of a real last packet, from a tag or a granule wrong by that little, can't be told from padding and loses that much. Early by more, and it's ignored and loses nothing.
- An edit's duration is in the movie's timescale. A file with whole frames in stts and a timescale other than its rate has its end taken from the end of that tick, so it keeps up to 45 frames at 1000 and 74 at 600, of padding, and loses none. FFmpeg's M4As end on stts, which is exact.
- A tag whose padding is a frame or more, as some encoders may write, keeps it as stock does. A Vorbis track with all its audio on one page does too.
- A file with a chapter track last and iTunSMPB isn't exact: `mov_read_custom` gives `start_pad` to the last stream, so the audio keeps its priming and the tag's count doesn't match. It plays as stock does and loses nothing.
- A fragmented M4A from FFmpeg, whose moov has no edit list, keeps its priming of 1024 and has its padding of 888 cut, losing no music. On a stream that can't seek it keeps both.
- A chained Ogg Vorbis keeps the padding of every link but the last. Chained Opus comes to its links' true total, whatever the first link's length.
- Stock's own bug, left as it is: on an HE-AAC file with a tag at the output rate's timescale, stock skips twice the priming and loses 5058 frames at the head.

`test/player_seam.py` runs the real player with its real demuxers and decoders in MIPS under Unicorn (ALSA modelled, the clock virtual), on pairs it generates with `ffmpeg` and `lame`, through a real `set-gapless` preload, and finds each source in the output by correlation. It asserts the priming kept, the seam and what's left after the second track, per format, for seeks to 0 and to 1 s, for tracks of different kinds in one preload, for Crossfade and Bluetooth, and that an end wrong by more than the last packet (stts, tag, edit, granule, chained links, fragments on a stream that can't seek), a short track and HE-AAC (`test/fixtures`, made with `libfdk-aac`) come to stock's frames. Stock's player fails every format fixed here: give it as the second argument to see the numbers. It runs during release validation.

## MP3 ReplayGain

Stock **Playback → ReplayGain** (`mcl-replaygain`) scales by the gains FFmpeg's `ff_replaygain_export` (`0x538764`) finds, which the vendor changed to store them in a global (`0xafc790`: track gain, track peak, album gain, album peak, gains in 1/100000 dB, `INT32_MIN` for none) that the replaygain filter's setup (`0x428260`) takes at the next file. lavf demuxers call it, but MPlayer probes MP3 with its own `demux_audio` first, whose `demux_audio_open` hands a leading ID3v2 tag to a vendor frame walker (`0x48e6b0`, called at `0x48f704` and `0x4923d8`). That walker stops after 2 KB of frames, reads ID3v2.4's syncsafe frame sizes as plain ones and keeps a UTF-8 frame's encoding byte at the front of the TXXX description, so `\x03REPLAYGAIN_TRACK_GAIN` matches nothing. Embedded cover art or a long comment ahead of the gains, or a UTF-8 TXXX (what most ID3v2.4 taggers write), and the track plays without its gain; only Latin-1 or UTF-16 gains near the front of the tag reach the filter.

The build points both calls (`ID3_CALLS` in `tools/peq.py`, pinned) at `id3_gain` in `patch/peq_player.c`. It runs the stock walker, then reads the whole tag again through the stream's fd, as `mp3_seek` does, restoring the fd's offset: ID3v2.3 or 2.4, an extended header skipped, frame sizes syncsafe in 2.4. Each TXXX of up to 256 bytes is decoded by `id3_txxx` (Latin-1, UTF-8, UTF-16 with either BOM, UTF-16BE) and put into an FFmpeg dictionary with stock's `av_dict_set` (`0x6ea744`), which goes to stock's `ff_replaygain_export` as the walker's does, so the gains are parsed the same way and stored only when one is there. Tags at the end of the file (APEv2, as `mp3gain` writes, or ID3v1) and the LAME header's gain are not read. `test/id3.py` runs the built player's walker, dictionary and export under Unicorn on tags of each kind.

## MQA detection

At each audio (re)init, `reinit_audio_chain` (`0x42e834`) probes an eligible stream: FFmpeg FLAC or non-AIFF PCM (codec types 13 and 11 at `sh_audio+0xd4`), stereo, 16 or 24 bit, at 44.1, 48, 88.2, 96, 176.4 or 192 kHz. The probe is skipped only when `mcl-mqadecodemode` stored 2 at `0xb10e44`; demo's sender for that command (`0x5ab554`) has no caller, so stock always probes. The probe (`0x42e5dc`) pre-decodes `0xc00` bytes (`0x20000` on output way 2), looks for the MQA sync word (`0x319207d5` in L xor R) and returns 2 for MQA, otherwise 1. Either result creates the vendor PCMDecoder (handle `0xb10d74`) and is stored as isMQA (`0xb10c54`).

Stock `fill_audio_out_buffers` (`0x42f3f4`) then passes every chunk through that decoder (`0x72f9fc`, the library's decoder and renderer) whenever isMQA > 0 (`blez` at `0x42f8c0`). It plays the library's output only when isMQA == 2 (`0x42fc48`). With 1 it plays the original `a_out_buffer`, and the converted buffer is never read. All that work does is let the library's status (`0x42f6b8`) switch a track to MQA mid-play. On the Q2, playing ordinary 16-bit 44.1 kHz FLAC, the side chain was 79% of the player thread's samples in a ptrace PC profile, and hciplayer took 12.8% of a core.

The build changes the gate to isMQA == 2 (`MQA_GATE` in `tools/peq.py`, five words), so non-MQA streams skip the side chain and what reaches ALSA is unchanged. With that, the same playback takes 2.3% of a core. MQA tracks run the decoder as before, so MQA now has to be found at the start of the track. The detector (`0x730354`) searches at most 96000 frames before it settles on not MQA, so the probe now reads that much (`MQA_PROBE`, `0x300000 >> (codec ^ 15)` bytes instead of `0xc00`): `0xc0000` for FLAC, which FFmpeg decodes to 8-byte frames at 24 bit (98304 frames, 2.2 s at 44.1 kHz), well within its `a_buffer_size` (1,732,608 bytes). PCM gets `0x30000`, as ad_pcm's `a_buffer_size` is only `0x30800` and a longer probe would fail its prebuffer and leave isMQA 0. The probed audio is played, not discarded; the probe runs once per track, not on seeks. ponytail: an MQA PCM file whose signal starts after `0x30000` bytes, or any MQA file whose signal starts after 96000 frames, plays as plain PCM; probe longer if one turns up.

The long probe runs inside `reinit_audio_chain` before the track's first block goes out, at a gapless change too, so decoding and searching about 2 s of 24-bit FLAC there can outlast the audio still buffered and leave a short gap between tracks (#76). **Playback → MQA** (`mqa_row`, `mqa_click` in `patch/crossfade.c`, stock's `playset_dsdoutput` icon, after DSD Output) reads `MQA: On` or `MQA: Off` and toggles `Q2POD MQA` in `config.ini`, default 0. `mqa_sync` sends stock's own `{mcl-mqadecodemode\N}` on the player socket (`MCL_FD`): 2 while off, which makes `reinit_audio_chain` skip the probe (`0x42eabc`) and take the branch a stream that isn't probed takes, so isMQA stays 0 and nothing of the MQA library runs; 0 while on, hciplayer's `.bss` default, which probes as above. hciplayer keeps the mode until it restarts. `mqa_accept` wraps the `accept` call in stock's `mclWaitAccept` (`0x5a7da0`): after a positive descriptor, it sends the saved mode before returning the descriptor for stock to publish at `MCL_FD`. Failed accepts and stock's rejected descriptor 0 send nothing. This covers the first connection and every later one, including descriptor reuse. The old send from `ringnav_mode` during `player_initconfig` missed boot: `player_reconfig` requires the flag at `0xa3be50`, which starts clear and is set after a disconnect, not on the first accept. Thus the UI could show Off while the first player still probed, leaving gaps on wired 96/192 kHz albums until an explicit On/Off toggle. A toggle still sends at once and applies at the next `reinit_audio_chain`, normally the next track's start. With MQA off, MQA files play undecoded, as on a player without MQA. `test/playback.py` executes the actual accept loop: default, saved Off and saved On, failed accepts and repeated/reused descriptors, a toggle made while no player is connected, and the mode sent before the socket is published.

## Crossfade

**Playback → Crossfade** (`ringnav_audioset`, stock's `playset_gapless` icon, after Gapless in the order below; `patch/crossfade.c`) reads `Crossfade: Off` or `Crossfade: 5 s` (`Q2POD XFADE` and `XFADESEC`, 1-15, default 5, in `config.ini`). It opens stock's Boot volume page re-dressed: `playset_bootvol_page_init` (`0x4b4a80`) binds `img_bootswitch`'s click (`on_bootvol_switch_onclick`), `img_dec`'s and `img_add`'s (`0x4b4624`, `0x4b45a8`) and `slider_bootvol`'s `EVT_VALUE_CHANGING`/`CHANGED` (`0x4b48f4`, `0x4b4300`), each with the window as context; `xfade_open` unbinds them with `widget_off_by_func` and binds its own, so Boot volume is never written. The title reads Crossfade and `view_bootvol` (the slider, the minus and plus and `label_vol`, "Off" or "5 s") is always shown and enabled (stock disables it with the view hidden while Boot volume's switch is off, which left touch dead on the slider and the minus and plus, #140): its slider runs `slider_set_min`/`max`/`step` 0, 15, 1 (AWTK takes doubles), 0 being Off, so the page needs no switch. A drag shows and plays each value at once but saves only at its release (`slid`: the slider's value-changed, not value-changing), or when the page closes mid-drag, so a quick drag is one config write, not one per value. Stock's slider sends no value-changed for a drag released where it began or an aborted press, so any later step, the switch, or the close saves a value a drag left unsaved (`set_value`'s `held`). That matters on iPod, where stock creates the title and `img_bootswitch` inside `view_navbar`, which `navbar_only` hides; the switch still toggles where it shows. Going Off keeps the length, which Centre brings back. `xfade_key`, from `ringnav` after `spot_key`, gives the page Centre (a single press toggles, a double press turns the screen off as on any page) and the wheel (a second a tick, down to Off). The page's close (`gone`) stops a single press still waiting for its double (`stop_timer(&xf.press)`): the timer belongs to no window, so it ran after the page had gone, with `xf.ctx` back at 0, and flipped Crossfade for a press meant for Night Shift's switch.

**Settings order.** Playback and System are restacked by use after stock builds them and the payload adds its rows (`reorder`, `playset_order` and `sysset_order` in `patch/navigation.c`): Play Mode, EQ, Gapless, Crossfade, Memory Playback, Resume Songs, Gain, ReplayGain, Max and Default Volume, Balance, Mono Audio, Swap L/R, Enlarged Art, Digital Filters, DSD Output, MQA, USB Audio and Library & Folders; and Wireless, Network, Display, Controls, Power, Date & Time, Language, App Store, Update, Reset, About. Stock names each row's button by its table id (`"%d"`) and its click handler (`0x4b9364` for Playback) reads the id back from that name, so moving a row keeps its action; a page with an unexpected row count is left as built.

**Library & Folders** (`library_open`, `playset_sub`) opens stock's Playback page again, as Display's sub-pages do, keeping stock's Folder Album Cover and Folder Skip rows (13 and 14, jump table `0x76ab60`) and dropping the others; the main page drops those two instead. The two instances share nothing: `playset_playset_page_init` (`0x4b98d8`) writes no global or heap memory, its click handler (`0x4b9364`) gets the row as its context and finds its image inside that row, its only global writes being saved-setting bytes (`g_bigcovermode`, `g_foldercover_flag`), and the window's `timer_idex` is a property of each window. It holds Artists, Artist Album Order, Year Order, Folder Root, Folder Skip and Folder Album Cover.

**Rows moved off System.** In-Vehicle Mode (Power), Key Tone and Buttons Lock (both Controls) stay on System's page hidden and leave stock's page whole; their new rows keep stock's translation keys. Their new rows do what stock's click did at `0x4cc240`, `0x4cc2dc` and `0x4cc368`: In-Vehicle flips `g_carmode`, saves `SYSSET CARMODE` and clears `g_carmode_state`; Key Tone flips `g_keytone_flag` and saves `SYSSET KEYTONE`; Buttons Lock opens `systemset/keylock_page`. Both settings keep their saved values.

**Policy.** `xfade_poll`, from `playback_poll` each UI pass, writes the next change's fade in ms to `/tmp/q2xfade` (an `int`, made whole under `.tmp` and renamed like the visualizer's tap, then mapped shared), only when its inputs change: Crossfade on, stock's Gapless on (the byte `0xa3be54`, `mclGetGaplessMode`), and the next track another album's (`album_cmp`, so an album, a CUE image or an untagged folder stays gapless) and playable (`fades_into`, only when the inputs change: not `.dsf`, `.dff` or `.iso`, any case, as DSD skips the filter, and for a path on the card not missing or 0 bytes, as that file never plays and so nothing would mix the tail). The next track is `playback_next`: `playback_successor(1)` while advanced play runs, else `pos + 1` in modes 0 and 3, and in mode 3 position 0 at the queue's end, which is what stock preloads, except with the jump-folder flag (`0x5a8cb8`), where that's a cold start into the next folder (`mclAutoChange` preloads under 6 s from the end, too late to decide on, so the poll predicts it instead). The file's upper bits (`XF_STOPS`) count stops: a pass that finds `mclGetPlayStatus` 1 moves the count, once per stop, unless it's a file error. A natural end never reads as stopped: hciplayer's `exit` reply clears only the running byte for a local queue, and `mclAutoChange` goes no further at status 1. Status 1 comes from `mclStop` (also a queue's end, `0x5add94`), `mclOrderStop`, a lost socket (`0x5a8ec8`), an empty path (`0x5ad90c`), `exit` at queue type 2 (`0x5a8770`), and hciplayer's `error` reply (`0x5a87e4`). That last one is no stop: hciplayer touches `/tmp/notfile` or `/tmp/notsupport` first (`0x4234d8`, `0x4234e8`), and on a local queue `on_wm_timer_dialog` removes the file and shows `dialog/msginfo_dialog`, then calls `player_next_music` (`0x4eaa84`). That toast is modal: its init leaves `to_modal` set, so `navigator_to_with_context` runs `dialog_modal` (`0x4eae44`) for its 2 s, and UI passes run under it with the status still 1 and the file gone. hciplayer touches the file before its `error` reply, and Next's send stores status 2 (`0x5ad094`) on the UI thread as the toast ends, so no pass finds status 1 with neither file nor toast. So a pass counts a stop only with neither file there and no `msginfo_dialog` open (those two syscalls and the walk run once per stop, only while stopped). The missing-file check above means a song before a missing or empty file plays whole with no hold; a next with junk bytes is found out only by trying it, so its hold is finished and the file after it mixes the tail, up to `XF_COLD_NS`, after the toast. Stock Shuffle (mode 2, Now Playing's mode button or Play Mode) draws its next at the change, and the fade is set whenever Crossfade and Gapless are on: a shuffled next is another album's in all but name. So Crossfade can ask about the file, `playback_next` draws that next ahead (`shuffle_peek`, once per track, with `rand_below`): `mcl_shuffle_pick` (`0x5a8120`) takes the current index out of the pool `MCL_POOL` and sets the position to a random one left there, which stays in the pool until it is current, so `ringnav_shuffle` overrides the position after the pick with the draw, a uniform one of the same set. It doesn't touch the pool. It holds while the position and both records are unchanged, so a queue edit or a new queue draws again; a Play next (`shuffle_forced`) and Previous's way back go first, in the policy as at the pick; a manual Next takes the draw too. With under 2 in the pool stock refills it, or Folder Skip jumps (`-16813`), so that one draw per cycle stays -2, unknown. Stock preloads nothing in Shuffle, so that change is a cold start (`mclNextSong` → `mclStartPlayer`); the held tail waits for the new file's first block (`XF_COLD_NS`), so on the jacks the fade runs on over the open PCM when the file's format matches, and after the reopen's short gap when it doesn't ([The old track's end](#usb-output-buffer)). Before 1.6.0 stock Shuffle never faded (the poll wrote 0), which is what #164's reports describe. While a fade is set, each pass puts the PEQ filter in the chain (`peq_chain`) when PEQ is off, as the visualizer does.

A queue edit is read the same way: a Play Next changes the next record, so the next pass rewrites the flag and the filter sees it on its next block. Asked before the window, the track gets the whole fade. Inside it, the hold takes half of what the decoder has left, about half a second ahead of the sound, so an edit 3 s before the end fades about 1.25 s, and one in the last half second joins plainly. Taken back before the window, nothing is held; taken back inside it, the hold finishes and what's held mixes.

**Fade.** The fade runs in `patch/peq_player.c`'s filter, after the PEQ and before the visualizer's tap. Each block reads the decoder's position, `written_audio_pts` (`0x44641c`, a leaf of `sh_audio` `mpctx+0x2c` and `d_audio` `mpctx+0x34`; `playing_audio_pts` at `0x42a134` subtracts the output's delay from it), and the length, `0xb10e84`, the whole seconds `{media-info}` reports (`mclGetPlayTime`). From `2 × fade + 2 s` before the end the filter keeps what comes in in a 6 MB ring and puts out half as many frames, oldest first, until the fade's length is held, then as many as come in: the decoder runs at twice real time meanwhile, and nothing is skipped or reordered. The next track's filter is a new instance (the player re-adds it at each file; each gets `player_state.id`), which mixes the held tail under its own first frames, `in × sin(p) + out × cos(p)` over the held length, so the sum keeps its power. Should the player keep one instance across the change, a position that starts over near 0 right after the end counts as the next track as well; the hold keeps the length it began with. A hold once begun is finished: the song's last seconds exist only in the ring, so when demo's fade clears or shortens during it (Crossfade off, Gapless off, a queue or mode change, even to a same-album next), the hold only stops growing, every frame goes on in order, and the next track mixes what is held. That's a choice, not forced: `decode_audio` grows its buffer for a filter that puts out more than came in (`0x47fa00`-`0x47fa38`), so the tail could be played out unmixed while blocks run, but no block runs past the file's end, so the rest would still need a next track, and a same-album next gets one fade instead of a change in the output's timing. A position jump over 1.5 s while holding is a seek and drops the held audio. A stop since the hold began drops the tail, however soon another track starts (the count above; the filter compares it at the next block, as nothing runs while stopped). Otherwise a tail whose file ended by itself waits up to `XF_COLD_NS`, 30 s, for a next file's cold start (stock Shuffle, a declined or failed preload, a slow card, a length that overstates the audio); one cut short waits up to `XF_STALE_NS`, 3 s, so a long pause and then Next, or Standby, does not bring it back. The holder's `uninit` tells them: every file's end runs `uninit_player` (`0x421804`, mask `0xff7a` and so `0x400`, `uninit_audio`) before the idle loop clears the flags (`0x42da10`), with `NEXT_FLAG` (`0xb10e6c`) or `STOP_FLAG` (`0xb10e70`) up at a skip or a stop, and neither at a file's end. The count stays beside that, as a stop with nothing running never reaches hciplayer. A block that bypasses the filter (DSD, or a format it leaves alone) drops the tail, so it can't come back under a later track. Once the mix runs, a pause of its track only waits, while a stop, or a track starting over 3 s after the mix's last block, drops what is left; a track shorter than the fade hands the rest to the next, retimed at another rate. A next track at another rate plays the tail at its own rate, linearly interpolated (`xf.step` held frames an output frame, the mix's length scaled to match): a tail that is fading out, so that is enough. A next track of another channel count maps it: stereo under mono is each frame's mean, as Mono Audio's; mono under stereo goes to both sides. The ring is read with the holder's channels (`xf.nch`). The hold is capped by the ring: 15 s up to 48 kHz stereo, 7.5 s at 96 kHz, 3.75 s at 192 kHz; a longer fade at those rates just fades over what fits. While a fade is held, the elapsed time runs ahead by what is held, so it reaches the length as the next track fades in. DSD passes through the filter untouched, so a fade out of DSD holds nothing and the next track plays whole, and the policy asks none into it. ponytail: a manual skip during the hold or the mix mixes the held tail into the chosen track; on the headphone DAC a rate change at the switch still reopens the output, so the tail follows that gap; holding it past the fade would mean running the new track at the old rate through the fade and reopening inside it. Limits: the once-per-cycle Shuffle draw that stays unknown, if it lands on a DSD or missing file, loses the hold, and so does a next opening over 30 s late; a junk file in a chain of bad files can pass 30 s; turning off Repeat All or removing the next tracks during the hold of the last one leaves no next, so that hold is lost; a track shorter than what is left of a fade hands the rest to one starting over 3 s late, which drops it; a CUE track's end, which `mclAutoChange` calls by its own clock, has the next flag up, so a next opening over 3 s late loses the tail. A stop and Play with no UI pass between them, or a stop while a toast is up, isn't counted, and the 30 s or 3 s rule applies instead.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build of the filter): the hold window, half-rate emission and order, the equal-power mix and its length, no fade asked, a seek while holding, another rate after (interpolated), a mono track after (mapped), the flag clearing in the hold, a stop since the hold, the same instance starting over, and an unknown length; `test/xfade_player.py` runs the built MIPS filter under Unicorn with numbered frames through the hold, the mix (same and another rate, the ring wrapping at 15 s, stereo into mono and mono into stereo, after a cold start up to 29 s, through a pause, handed on mid-mix at another rate), the flag clearing or shortening in the hold, a track shorter than its hold, the ring's cap at 192 kHz, DSD going out and DSD between, Next in the hold, a pause in the decoder's last 1.5 s then Next, a file ending far from its length, the stop (before and mid-mix), stale (31 s, and 4 s for a tail cut short) and mid-mix drops and no fade, so a compiler change shows (1.5.7's clang 22 and 1.5.8's clang 23 builds both pass it); `test/patch.py`: the row and its saved length; `test/playback.py`: the policy, the fade written for another album's next track and at Repeat All's wrap, none for the same album, a DSD, missing or empty next, with Gapless off, Repeat One or at the queue's end, the stop count and a file error's status not moving it (status and files set by hand), stock Shuffle's draw (asked about, played, uniform, the pool left alone, a new queue, Previous), no file while Crossfade is off, and the filter put in the chain.

## Mono Audio

**Playback → Mono Audio** (`ringnav_audioset`, stock's `playset_balance` icon, below Balance; `mono_row` and `mono_click` in `patch/crossfade.c`) reads `Mono Audio: On` or `Off` and toggles `Q2POD MONO` in `config.ini` (default 0). `mono_sync`, from `playback_poll` each UI pass and on each toggle, writes it to `/tmp/q2mono`, an `int` made under `.tmp`, renamed and mapped shared, as `/tmp/q2xfade` is. Nothing is created until Mono Audio is first turned on, and a missing file reads as off. Every reader maps the file and picks up a change within one block:

- **hciplayer** (local music, Tidal; DAC, Bluetooth and USB): the PEQ filter's `play()` sets both channels of each stereo frame to their mean, after `peq_process` and before the crossfade and the visualizer's tap. Per-channel bands and the EQ's balance shape each side first. Stock's Balance acts after the mix. The file is looked for once a second of audio until it appears (`watch`, shared with `xfade_ms`). Boot leaves the filter out with the EQ off, so `mono_sync` calls `peq_chain` while Mono Audio is on, as a fade does. ponytail: `peq_chain` waits for play (status not 1, stopped) for all three callers, since whether an attach while stopped sticks is unknown.
- **q2video** (Videos, Internet Radio, Spotify's sink): `writer` mixes each S16 chunk after the soft volume (`mono`), looking for the file every hundredth chunk until it appears.
- **Spotify on the headphone DAC** normally plays through `aplay`. With Mono Audio on, `spot_output` writes `plughw:0,0 hVOLUME` to `SPOT_OUT`, so `aplay.sh` runs `q2video -s` there instead; the `h` keeps the volume on the DAC, and `sound_open` readies the DAC (`DAC_PCM`, `DAC_MUTE`) for any `plughw:0,0`, volume or not.

ponytail: DSD, DoP and native DSD output pass through bit-exact (`dsd()`) and stay stereo, and so does stock's AirPlay receiver. On the headphone DAC, switching Spotify's sink between `aplay` and `q2video` waits for the sink's next start (pause and play), like a new output device does. q2video already playing on another output follows the switch at once.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host builds): the filter's mix once the file appears, live, and exact again when cleared; `mono` on S16 at both ends of the range. `test/patch.py`: the row, its place after Balance, the saved key, and the shared int set live, mapped once.

## Resume

`ringnav_sleep` (the UI thread) polls at most once a second. It reads the queue's playing record (`queue_now`) and `player_playtime_and_length` (`0x514128`): elapsed and total track seconds, CUE-relative, and -1 while nothing plays. A track is long when its total is at least **Playback → Resume Songs** (`Q2POD RESUMEMIN`, minutes; `crossfade.c` `resume_min`, read once), or its extension is `.m4b` (case-insensitive) anywhere on the card, or it is under [Podcasts or Audiobooks](#podcasts-and-audiobooks), and its record has no CUE start or end (`+0x48`, `+0x4c`). A CUE track (either set) is long only when spoken (`.m4b`, or under those folders), so a music CUE image is left alone. Places are kept by the path's FNV-1a hash in a ring of `RESUME_SLOTS` (512, a 4 KB file), most recent first, as file seconds: a CUE track's place is its start plus its elapsed seconds, so a book's chapters share one place, and the same file played whole resumes there too. The ring is read from `/mnt/data/ringnav-resume` on the first long track and written whole, to a `.tmp` then renamed. That happens every `RESUME_SAVE_S` (60) seconds of movement, once the time stands still for two polls (a pause), and on a track change. A place within `RESUME_EDGE_S` (30 s) of either end of the file is forgotten. A CUE track saves nothing within its own first `RESUME_EDGE_S`, keeping the book's place, so a chapter played becomes the book's place once past its first 30 s; only the last CUE track (no end set) knows the file's end, where the book is finished and its place forgotten. A track change, `listen_key` (the path's hash with a CUE start mixed in, so moving to the next chapter counts), waits one poll before it counts, so the previous track's time never belongs to the new one. When a long track with a saved place first plays (elapsed at least 1 s) with under `RESUME_START_S` (5 s) elapsed, `player_seek_time` jumps there, as Scrub commits. A track paused at 0 waits for Play, so Memory Playback's paused boot does not seek, and its Location restore, already under way, is left alone. A CUE track never jumps when played: a chapter picked is played from its start. Instead the [Queue menu](#queue-menu) of a spoken CUE song row whose file has a place starts with **Resume** (`book_place`): it copies the page's songs (type 8) and plays them as a library queue (`play_folder(..., 0xf001)`, as Most Played) from the last chapter of that file starting at or before the place, with the seconds into it waiting for that chapter's `listen_key`, for up to 8 s. The poll seeks there once that chapter plays under `RESUME_START_S`, so the previous position the player still reports, or a queue that switches a poll or two late, does not consume it; the chapter already playing works the same way. With no place left at the pick, the menu says "Queue unchanged". The ring's format is unchanged, so places saved before stay valid: an earlier release's 32-slot file loads as the first 32 places, and an earlier build reads the first 32, the most recent, of the bigger one. A hash collision only means a wrong resume point.

A restart of the same file reaches the poll as a move to the start, which forgets the place, so hooks stop one before it happens: Previous and Next ask through `restart_refused`, and a pick of the playing file starts nothing. On a track holding a place worth keeping (`resume_held`: over `RESUME_EDGE_S` from either end, at the player's live time, or the last polled one once stopped, and not a CUE track, whose restart leaves the book's place alone), in a local queue, such a press does nothing but toast "Press again to restart", through `playback_poll` since a headset key can arrive off the UI thread. Only a second press of the same key (Previous or Next, `restart_key`) within `RESTART_ASK_MS` (3 s) goes ahead, and the place is forgotten. Another key asks again and arms for itself, so Previous can't confirm what a stray Next asked, and a press that wouldn't restart ends the window. The toast is stock's `msginfo_dialog`, up for 2 s of the 3, and its own key-up handler (`0x49a478`) stops Previous, Next and Play; `on_wm_keyup_fun` comes after the window's children in AWTK's dispatch (inferred from that return value, not run). So `ringnav`, ahead of the windows, sends the armed key on to its quick click while a dialog is on top. ponytail: the toast stays up its time over a restart, as closing it early means its private timer's cleanup, and another key under it is still stopped, so it doesn't ask again until the toast has gone. A headset press goes straight to `player_prev_music`, past the dialog. A refused press stamps nothing for the [press gate](#a-press-not-a-natural-change); a confirmed one does as usual. A long track here is what Resume keeps a place for: at least **Resume Songs** minutes (30 by default, 20-120 or Off), an `.m4b`, or under Podcasts or Audiobooks. There is no other length test, as a shorter track has no place for the guard to protect.

- **Previous** (`ringnav_seek_start`, `player_seek_start`, `0x5143a8`). Stock's `player_prev_music` (`0x515600`) calls it first on anything playing or paused (`mclGetPlayStatus`, `0x515670`). Past 10 s of `player_playtime` it seeks to the track's start and returns 1, in any queue and mode, so `player_change_music` is never reached; a refusal returns 1 too. Under 11 s it returns 0 and Previous changes track as below. `player_prev_music` is the seek's only caller. Getting from a long track to the one before takes three presses now, as the place has to be given up first: ask, restart, move.
- **Next, and Previous when stopped or under 11 s** (`ringnav_change`, `player_change_music`). `change_restarts` says when a press would start the playing file again. With more tracks stock moves on, Repeat One too, so outside advanced play that is a one-track queue, unless Folder Skip's sibling folder (class 1, not an M3U, and not stock Shuffle's Previous, which goes back to its last position first) or List Play's stop at a library queue's end takes the press (`test/playback.py` holds it to stock's `player_change_music` over the play modes, classes, Folder Skip, M3U and that position); in advanced play, the track's successor or history entry being the playing one. Stock's skip past a file it can't play (`on_wm_timer_dialog`, `press_natural`) is automatic and never asks.
- **A pick of the playing file** (`ringnav_player_start`, `player_start`, `0x515e38`; `playback_keeps`). Stock's `player_start` reloads the queue and starts the file at 0, and no caller reads its result. The hook asks whether the entry is the file playing or paused: the same path, CUE start and CUE end as the queue's playing row, so a chapter row while the file plays whole is another track, on a track holding a place. A stopped player is never refused, since `resume_held` survives a stop (`resume_poll` returns early on -1), and neither is a short song, which keeps stock's restart. The same row of the same list (index, size and class equal to the queue's) starts nothing, and what follows goes by the caller, told by its return address:
  - `playing_page_init` (`0x52cc14`, returning to `0x52cc1c`), which `play_folder` and stock's lists fill: the page goes on and opens itself.
  - The album pages' play (`0x4a5d54`, `0x4a9c30`, in handlers such as `0x4a5c6c`), which stay on their page after a start: `playback_np` opens Now Playing, class 0xff, as `on_backplay_click` does.
  - SyncLink (`0x51b734`, `0x51b904`), off the UI thread: silent, opening nothing. The track keeps playing, which is what the phone asked for.
  - `testmode_test_key` (`0x4d0d80`) goes the same silent way if it ever names the playing long file; `ringnav_boot` can't, as no track holds a place at boot.

  From the first two, a paused file also goes on (`player_play_pause`). Its Play waits for the DAC by itself (`dac_resume`), so it needs no stamp. A refused pick stamps nothing. The same file from another list, such as Era's Play All, Cover Flow's All albums, or a track in its album while the queue is that one file, isn't refused: stock loads that list and `resume_return` sets `rs_go_key`, as a Resume row does, so the poll goes back to the second it was at once the file starts over, with a short gap in the audio. Not presses, and left alone: an app's `.play` (`press_natural`), Shuffle Albums' own load (`s.grouping`), and DLNA and online classes. Restarting a long track is Previous twice.

- **A queue row of the playing file.** View Queue's (`0x52993c`, returning to `0x529944`) and Up Next's (`un_play`) open Now Playing as the album pages do. `player_start_queue`'s other callers (a queue delete, DLNA, URL plays) keep stock's start, at the cost of one more return address.
- **A shuffle drawing it** (`shuffle_play`, Shuffle Songs and the Queue menu's Shuffle). Not a pick of it: it draws another index, before anything changes, or with one song opens Now Playing.
- **A start after a stop.** `resume_poll` notes a stop (`rs_stopped`: time -1 with `mclGetPlayStatus` 1, not a load's or seek's -1), and a later start of the same key under `RESUME_START_S` goes back to the second it stopped at, whether by a pick, by Play or after List Play's Next stopped it, instead of forgetting the place. A confirmed restart clears it (`resume_restart`).

test/playback.py drives these from `prev_quick_click` and `next_quick_click` (`0x4e81c0`, `0x4e8228`, from `on_wm_keyup_fun`) through stock's real `player_prev_music` and `player_seek_start`, since a test entering at `player_change_music` runs below the seek and never sees it.

ponytail: other ways back to the same file's start still forget the place, none of which can be told before the press: stock Shuffle at the end of its pool, which refills with the playing track and can draw it again (`mcl_shuffle_pick`, 1 in n), and Folder Skip with no sibling folder, which falls back to a wrap (`mclNextSong`). The hooks' class filter keeps a DLNA or online queue's Previous and Next stock's; `dmr_mainscheduling` only sends a Next with 2 or more rows, so it moves on anyway. SyncLink's Previous and Next arrive as keys (`main_loop_post_key_event`) and can't be told from the Q2's own, so they ask too, on the Q2's screen. A stop isn't noticed if the player stops and starts between two polls, about a second apart: the place is forgotten as before.

Memory Playback's snapshot takes precedence over a track's place only when it has a time to give (`playback_resumed`). **Track** gives none, so a long track there resumes at its own place instead of starting at 0 and then overwriting it, and **Location** with a saved time of 0 does the same.

**Resume Songs** (`ringnav_audioset`, stock's `playset_memplay` icon, after Memory Playback in the reorder) reads `Resume Songs: 30 min` or `Resume Songs: Off` and opens Crossfade's re-dressed Boot volume page (`xfade_open` ctx 2): no switch, the slider and wheel stepping five minutes through 20-120, below 20 Off; each change is saved (a drag at its release, as Crossfade) and live on the next track. `RESUMEMIN` is 30 unless `0` (Off) or a multiple of five in 20-120; anything else reads as 30. Off stops music tracks keeping a place; spoken audio (`.m4b`, Podcasts, Audiobooks) still resumes at any length. The default was a fixed 20 minutes before this row, so a track of 20-30 minutes no longer resumes until the row is lowered.

## Folder entry

Folder opens directly at `/mnt/mmc`, layer 2, when `g_otg_status` is clear and the card is present on entry (`/tmp/mmc_add` readable, stock's `check_tfstatus` at `0x4f8978`). After stock initializes the page, `ringnav_folder` stores `/mnt/mmc` in `media_root`, sets the path and runs the existing reload, title and refresh functions, the same block a shortcut uses at layer 3. Back where `g_folder_path` equals `media_root` sets layer 1 before stock Back decrements it to 0 and leaves the page, even if OTG was connected meanwhile. With OTG connected or no card on entry, the storage selector and stock navigation remain, and `media_root` is cleared. Nested folders and playback use the existing handlers. Podcasts/Audiobooks shortcuts take precedence and retain their own root exit.

## Folder Root

**Playback → Library & Folders → Folder Root** (`ringnav_audioset`, stock's `playset_folderjump` icon, before Folder Skip in `library_order`) reads `Folder Root: Card` or `Folder Root: <name>`: the string `Q2POD FOLDERROOT` in `config.ini` (`toolsReadConfig`, `toolsWriteConfig`), missing or empty for the card. Centre or tap (`root_click`) reads `/mnt/mmc` once and saves the next top-level directory (`d_type` 4, or 0 unknown, as `media_find`) after the saved one in `strcasecmp` order (FAT and exFAT hold no two names differing only in case), dot-folders left out; "" (Card) gives the first, after the last it saves "", and a saved name no longer there moves on to the next name after it. ponytail: names over `ROOT_MAX` (200) bytes or starting or ending with a space are not offered, since stock's config line (`toolsWriteConfig`'s `sprintf` into 257 bytes, `toolsReadConfig`'s 255-byte `fgets`, then trimmed) can't keep them.

On a Folder visit with a card (`/tmp/mmc_add`), `ringnav_folder` opens `/mnt/mmc/<name>` at layer 3 through the media roots' block when it is a directory (`__xstat`; a name with `/` or a leading dot is ignored); a pending Podcasts/Audiobooks root wins, and without the folder the visit is as in [Folder entry](#folder-entry). `media_root` stays `/mnt/mmc` without a drive, so the root is an ordinary folder: stock Back (`folder_back`) strips the last name to `/mnt/mmc` at layer 2, titled `SD` as stock's, and Back there leaves the page; with a drive it reaches stock's storage selector. Every root's open (the card, Folder Root, Podcasts, Audiobooks) first runs stock's `folder_reset_variable` (`0x50727c`), as Back to layer 0 does: layer, path, `g_m3u_path` and the `g_yoffset_save`/`g_index_save` stacks cleared, and `g_navbar_status` and `g_allselect_flag` zeroed (the multi-select state, which stock's `folder_enter`, `folder_back` and page inits clear too). A visit left without Back (Home, a song opening Now Playing) keeps them, and `folder_back` pops a non-empty stack into the page's position and, inside an M3U (`g_m3u_path` set), reloads the same path instead of stepping up, so Back from the root would have landed on a stale position or stayed in the root a layer short. With the stacks empty the step up keeps the back handler's zeroed position, the top of the card's list.

Checks in `test/patch.py` (stock `folder_back` and `folder_reset_variable` executed): the root at layer 3 after a visit left deep and inside an M3U, Back to `/mnt/mmc` (layer 2, title, position untouched), then out of the page or to the selector with a drive; a gone, file, hidden or path root, no root and no card as before; Podcasts' own root; the row's cycle, order, skipped names (a UTF-8 name with a space kept), saved value and a gone root.

## Podcasts and Audiobooks

Folder's row-name formatter treated names as printf formats, crashing on `99% Invisible` even with Folder Album Cover off. `FOLDER_NAME` in `tools/build.py` changes the call at `0x522394` to `tk_strncpy(buffer, name, 511)`, keeping its 512-byte buffer terminated and displaying percent signs literally in folder and file names. The real row callback is exercised in `test/patch.py` with percent signs, format directives and an overlong name.

`ringnav_localmusic` reads `/mnt/mmc` (`opendir`/`readdir`) and adds a row for a directory (`d_type` 4, or 0 unknown) named `Podcasts` or `Audiobooks`, ASCII case aside, last, with build-added 52 px icons drawn in stock's style in `assets/icons/` (`local_podcasts`, purple with a microphone, and `local_audiobooks`, orange with headphones). A press (`media_click`, also Home's Shortcut set to Podcasts or Audiobooks) looks the folder up again and `navigator_to("folder_page")`s, as Home's Folder card (`on_home_folder_onclick`) does; with the folder gone it toasts "No Podcasts folder on the card" instead. Stock's folder browser keeps its place in globals: `g_folder_layer` (1 the storage list `folder_load_top` builds, 2 a storage root, 3 on deeper folders), `g_folder_path`, `g_navbar_name` (the title) and the `g_yoffset_save`/`g_index_save` stacks, which `folder_enter` pushes and `folder_back` pops. `folder_page_init` (`0x523330`, hooked as `ringnav_folder`) always starts at layer 1 (`folder_enter(-1, 0)`). After it, a pending press copies the path in, sets layer 3 and runs stock's `folder_reload_data` (`toolsLoadDirectory` into `p_deque_showlist`), `folder_reinit_navbarname` (layer 3 and on: the path's last name) and the page's navbar and table rebuild (`0x52176c`, `folder_refresh`), as stock's back and refresh do. Every check on the layer elsewhere is `< 2`, so the root is an ordinary folder: entering, song presses (`player_load_songlist(..., 1)`, folder play), covers and the refresh on return from Now Playing are stock's. `folder_back` (`0x507ac8`, `ringnav_folder_back`) has one caller, the page's back handler (`0x5218e4`, the title arrow and the Return key); at layer 3 on that root it sets layer 1 first, so stock's step down reaches 0, resets the browser and the handler closes the page, back to Local Music. Any other folder_page open clears the root, so the Folder view's own `/mnt/mmc/Podcasts` climbs as before.

An M4B track anywhere on the card, or a track whose path is under either folder (`/mnt/mmc/<name>/`), is resumed at any length (a CUE track by its file's place and the hold menu's Resume, see [Resume](#resume)) and skips the listen count, so it never reaches Most Played or the scrobble log. The stock speed control (`PLAYSPEED` in `playerjumpinfo_page`) now saves its 0–3 mode by exact parent path; a track with no folder (a Tidal stream) takes it for that track only, unsaved. `ringnav_speed` hooks `mclSetPlaySpeed`; calls returning to `player_initconfig` at `0x51339c` restore rather than save. `ringnav_start_player` (`playback_track_speed`) sets the folder’s mode and sends it to the running decoder before opening a track, since the decoder otherwise keeps the previous track’s speed, and `ringnav_open_preload` declines gapless across a speed boundary. Files under `/mnt/data/ringnav-speed-<path hash>` contain the full, zero-terminated 1024-byte parent path and a 32-bit mode; reads validate both, writes use `blob_io`’s temporary-file rename. Missing or invalid preferences default to normal. Failed saves show a toast and reload the saved preference. The old global speed is not migrated because it does not identify a folder. shortcut: genre-only Podcast/Audiobook tags do not grant spoken-audio behavior; add a genre source if needed. Every local track start (`ringnav_start_player`) and gapless preload (`ringnav_open_preload`) first checks `.m4b`, `.m4a` and `.mp4` files with `mp4_fits` (`patch/chapters.c`). FFmpeg's mov demuxer, which hciplayer uses for MP4 and `/usr/bin/ffmpeg` uses for Chapters, indexes every sample before it plays: a 24-byte `AVIndexEntry` and a 4-byte `stsz` size each, so a 47-hour book's millions of AAC frames outgrow the Q2's 128 MB and the device stalls, again at boot through Memory Playback. `mp4_index_bytes` walks `moov/trak/mdia/minf/stbl` with `fopen`/`fseek` and sums the `stsz`/`stz2` sample counts at 28 bytes each. A file `fopen` cannot open because it is 2 GB or more (`EOVERFLOW`, demo has no large-file I/O) counts as too long. The index must fit `MemAvailable` from `/proc/meminfo` less `MP4_SPARE_KB` (16 MB); a failed start returns -1, as stock's own checks do, a gapless preload is declined with stock's failed-attempt state so automatic change ticks do not repeat the scan, and the next UI pass (`playback_poll`) toasts it, since `mclStartPlayer` can run off the UI thread. Chapters refuses the same files with its own toast. ponytail: the estimate leaves out chunk tables and the decoder's buffers, and MemAvailable is read while the previous track may still be open; tune `MP4_SPARE_KB` from device measurements. `test/chapters.py` checks the estimate on synthetic and real MP4s; `test/patch.py` checks the Chapters refusal.

All Library music views (Songs, Albums, Artists, Composers, Genres, Hi-Res, Frequent, Recent and Recently Added, including their detail lists) exclude `.m4b`, `/mnt/mmc/Audiobooks/` and `/mnt/mmc/Podcasts/` paths with the same case-insensitive SQL predicate as Coverflow (`MUSIC_WHERE`). `ringnav_localclass` scopes `ringnav_library_query` around category loads, split-album queries and Unknown-row checks; the artist page and music album staging use the same scope. The query hook substitutes a filtered `songtable` subquery before grouping, so shared music/spoken-audio tags keep music artwork and spoken-only Unknown categories disappear. Favourites and playlist contents bypass `songtable`: Favourites keep spoken tracks; `music_records` filters only playlists' displayed song copies with `spoken`, preserving music order, playlist containers and every stored entry. Most Played, Long Songs, category Play Next/Add to Queue and Shuffle Songs, Albums and Folders use music-only loads too. Scoped loads restore the caller's filter state, including empty or failed queries. Ratings, podcast and audiobook folder browsing, playback and folder queue expansion stay unfiltered. My Fav, Frequent and Recent (`0xf00a`, `0xf008`, `0xf007`) list stock's copies of songs as added or played; a copy with an empty title, as a song played from Folder has, takes the library's title by path (`fav_titles`), so its row no longer reads as the file name with its track number.

## Buttons lock

Buttons Lock's rows (Controls) and the quick-settings lock tile offer Off, Everything, Buttons only and Volume only. They save stock's `SYSSET LOCKFLAG` and `LOCKMODE`: 3 for Everything, 2 for Buttons only, 1 for Volume only. Stock's key-down and key-up filters already apply these modes only while the screen is off: mode 1 rejects wheel keys 172/173; mode 2 rejects Return, Play/Pause and skip keys 222/223; mode 3 rejects both sets. A mode outside 1–3 is saved as 3. Centre remains the wake key. `KEY_LOCKOUT` is independent, the short debounce after a button press. `ringnav_keylock_rows` replaces the stock row builder; `ringnav_quick_lock` replaces the lock tile callback, and `ringnav_quick_settings` labels the current choice on open.

The lock is the buttons' alone. SyncLink (Eddict Player) has no path of its own to the player: `synclink_mainscheduling` returns a play control as `0x65`, `0x66` or `0x67`, and `on_wm_idle_status` posts it as a press and release of Play/Pause (171), 222 or 223 with `main_loop_post_key_event` (`0x68b5fc`, its only caller), so stock's dark filters dropped them like the buttons (#113). `ringnav_post_key` hooks the poster and keeps each post's key and the `time_now_ms` window around it, which the queued event carries as its stamp (`EVENT_TIME`, low word; four posts kept, a match spent); `ringnav_input` dispatches an event matching one with `g_keylock_flag` cleared, then restores it. A button event never matches: it needs the same key stamped within the same millisecond or two.

## Photos

The iPod classic keeps Photos on its main menu; here it is the last Local Music row, **Photos**, made like Podcasts' (`media_find` kind 3, stock's 52 px `playset_covermode` copied as `local_photos`) for a top-level `Photos` folder, case aside. iPod Home is seven fixed rows filling the client area and the Stock build's carousel needs card art per entry, so neither takes more entries without a new layout; [Books](#books) and [Videos](#videos) follow it. A press calls `photos_open` (`patch/photos.c`), which opens a runtime `photos_page` (`window_create`, `CTX_DYNAMIC` in `contexts.inc`, as `coverflow_page`) with three views, shown one at a time:

- **Albums**: Coverflow's `page_list`, "All Photos", then each subfolder holding a photo, by name (`qsort`, at most 200; deeper folders are not albums). Without subfolders the page opens on the grid.
- **Grid**: the album's photos (`.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.png`, ASCII case aside, not dot files such as macOS's `._` copies), by path, at most `PHOTOS_MAX` (500, under ringnav's 512 rows); All Photos is the folder's own, then every album's. A bare `scroll_view` (a `list_view` would lay its children out as full-width rows) holds `PH_COLS` (4) `s_listitem_black` tiles of `PH_TILE_W` × `PH_TILE_H` (93 × 80), three whole rows under the title; where the bar holds the title (Classic), the view runs on to the page's foot and shows part of a fourth. The wheel moves through them in order; iPod's bar and the Stock outline frame the tile, round a thumbnail of at most `PH_THUMB_W` × `PH_THUMB_H` (85 × 72), `PH_GREY` until it exists.
- **Viewer**: a `slide_menu` with an empty `image` child per photo, as Coverflow's covers: items the page's height square with the spacer making the stride 375, so ringnav's carousel step (`carousel_page`) moves one photo a tick with its retargeted animator, and touch drags it. The paint hook draws the photo at the live offset (`visual`) and, mid-slide, the one coming in, fitted into 375 × 290 without enlarging, over the black page. Centre (the child's click) toggles a caption, "3 of 40" and the file name, on a rounded `#000000B3` band clear of the glass corners; "Loading…" and "Can't open this photo" show without it. Return goes viewer, grid (`ringnav_select` puts the bar on the photo last shown and the grid scrolls to it), albums, Local Music.

**Cache.** Opening a grid starts one pthread, as Coverflow's art build. For each photo, the viewer's first, then its neighbours, then the rest in order, it reads the first 4 KB for the EXIF orientation (APP1 `Exif`, IFD0 tag `0x112`, either byte order), then, unless cached in `/mnt/mmc/.photos/<fnv(path)>`, writes `.jpg`, Coverflow's `thumb` (`toolsThumbSpecCover`, or ffmpeg alone for a large PNG or progressive JPEG) to `PH_SHOT_W` × `PH_SHOT_H` (sides swapped for orientations 5 to 8, so the upright photo fits the screen), and `t.jpg` from that copy at the thumbnail size, each through `.tmp` and a rename, under `parse_cover_mutex`. Stock fits landscape sources to the width and portrait ones to the height, keeping the aspect. A file neither stock nor the ffmpeg fallback can decode gets an empty `.bad` marker; a vanished card leaves none. Below 16 MB free (`statfs`) nothing new is written. A 250 ms timer repaints as photos finish and joins the thread; leaving the grid or the page cancels and joins it, a wait bounded by one photo. ponytail: keys are paths, so an edited photo under the same name keeps its old copy, and copies of removed photos stay; deleting `.photos` rebuilds it.

**Drawing.** `photos_paint`, called from `ringnav_paint` like `coverflow_paint`, decodes a cached copy with `widget_load_image`, turns it upright into a payload `bitmap_create_ex` RGBA8888 slot over black (`photo_orient`: orientations 5 to 8 swap the sides, 2, 3, 7 and 8 mirror x, 3, 4, 6 and 7 mirror y), unloads it at once and draws the slot with `canvas_draw_image` (`photo_place`: fitted, centred, never enlarged). Thumbnails keep `PH_TILE_SLOTS` (32) slots and the viewer three, the photo farthest from the one wanted giving way; the viewer decodes both neighbours once it settles, so a slide draws from memory. About 2.6 MB of slots at most, freed with the grid.

Checks in `test/patch.py`: file names, EXIF orientation (byte orders, other segments, truncation, PNG), all eight orientations against their EXIF meaning, channel order and alpha, placement; then on a mocked card the Local Music row, albums, the grid's tiles, the worker's sizes and order and the bad marker, the upright thumbnail, the viewer's fit, a mid-slide frame, the caption, the wheel step, Return's selection and the slot release.

## Books

**Books** is the last Local Music row (`media_find` kind 4, `assets/icons/local_books.png`, tan with an open book), for a top-level `Books` folder, case aside. `books_open` (`patch/books.c`) opens a runtime `books_page` (`CTX_DYNAMIC`) with two views:

- **List**: Coverflow's `page_list`: the folder's `.txt` and `.epub` files and those of its subfolders (one level, not dot files), by path, at most 500, each named by its file name without the extension. ponytail: an EPUB's `dc:title` is not read.
- **Reader**: a bare `view` (the sheet) the paint hook fills, and Photos' caption band. `books_paint`, called from `ringnav_paint` like `photos_paint`, sets the system default font at 20 px (`canvas_set_font(canvas, NULL, 20)`, as `draw_centred`) and white text over the black page, then lays out `BOOK_ROWS` (10) lines 26 px apart from y 6, 24 px in from either side, every line clear of the glass corners, with `canvas_measure_text` per character and `canvas_draw_text` per line. Lines wrap at the last space, else mid-word; a line break at the top of a page and a byte order mark are dropped; `\f` starts a new page. Text is UTF-8, and any byte that is not valid UTF-8 is read as Latin-1. The same paint finds the page before: pages laid out again from a line start up to 3 KB back until one reaches this one (`book_back`), so paging back may realign to a paragraph start. `ringnav()` hands the reader's keys to `books_key` before the surface lookup (the sheet is not a list): the wheel moves to the next or previous page, Centre toggles the caption (how far in, as a percentage of the file, and the title), and Return (the page's key-up, as Photos') goes back to the list, its bar on the book.

The file is read through a 32 KB window starting 8 KB before the page, read again once the page is within 4 KB of its start or 8 KB of its end, so no book is loaded whole. The page last read, a byte offset, is kept per book (FNV-1a of the path) in `/mnt/data/ringnav-books`: 64 key/offset pairs, the most recently read first, written whole through coverflow.c's `blob_io` when the reader closes, and reopened there.

**EPUB.** The first open converts the book on one pthread into `/mnt/mmc/.books/<fnv(path)>.txt` (through a `.tmp` and a rename), showing "Preparing…" in the caption; a 250 ms timer joins it, and later opens read the text at once. Leaving cancels between documents and joins. demo imports nothing from zlib (`libz.so.1` is loaded, but neither `inflate` nor `dlopen` is in its GOT), so `book_inflate` is a raw-deflate decoder after zlib's `puff`: stored, fixed and dynamic blocks, every length and distance checked against the output and the input. The zip's end record (in the last 64 KB) gives the central directory (2 MB at most); an entry is read by name through its local header, stored or deflated, 8 MB at most either way; encrypted and ZIP64 entries are refused. `META-INF/container.xml`'s `rootfile` `full-path` gives the OPF; each spine `itemref`'s `idref` is looked up in the manifest (`item`, any namespace prefix, either quote), and its `href`, relative to the OPF, `%XX` decoded and without a `#fragment`, is read. Images in the spine (`media-type` `image/…`) are skipped. `book_xhtml` turns each document into text in place: tags dropped; `p`, `div`, `br`, `li`, `tr`, `hr`, `dt`, `dd`, `h1`-`h6`, `blockquote` and `section` as line breaks (at most one blank line); `head`, `style`, `script` and comments skipped; `&amp;` `&lt;` `&gt;` `&quot;` `&apos;` `&nbsp;` and numeric entities as UTF-8; white space runs as one space. Documents are joined by `\f\n`, so each starts a page. A book with an `encryption.xml` naming a content document (DRM; obfuscated fonts alone are fine), or whose container, OPF or a spine document cannot be read, says "Can't open this book". ponytail: each itemref scans the manifest, quadratic in the items; an id index if large books prepare slowly.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build of `books.c`): inflate against zlib at every level and strategy, a short output and a truncated stream refused, garbage that never runs out of bounds; XHTML to text; EPUB conversion from stored and deflated zips with a prefixed OPF, a `%20` href, a fragment, a cover image and an empty document; obfuscated fonts against DRM, a missing container, truncated and bit-flipped zips, cancel; UTF-8 with the Latin-1 fallback; wrapping, `\f`, and paging back exactly in a short text and to a page reaching the current one in a long one. `test/patch.py`: the Local Music row, the list, the reader's lines (white, default font, inside the glass at every row), the wheel's pages both ways, the caption, Return, the saved page, an EPUB converted by the MIPS worker and read, and a broken one.

## Videos

**Videos** is the last Local Music row (`media_find` kind 5, `assets/icons/local_videos.png`, blue with a play screen), for a top-level `Videos` folder, case aside. It opens Folder view at that folder (as Podcasts and Audiobooks do, `media_click`), so each subfolder is its own row and nothing is flattened into one list. Its `.mp4`, `.m4v`, `.mkv`, `.avi`, `.mov` and `.mpg` files play from there by a row click: its row click (stock's `0x521bc4`, hooked as `ringnav_folder_click`; it reads the row's index into `p_deque_showlist`, then `folder_enter`, whose 8, a file, it would queue as songs, so a video failed as one) hands a song record whose `REC_PATH` names a video to `books_video`, which plays it through `video_play`, reading the resume marks first since no Books page holds them; select mode (`g_navbar_status`) and every other row stay stock's. Only other extensions are left to stock, which queues them as songs. Stock's folder listing (`toolsLoadDirectory`) takes audio extensions only, so those rows exist only because its extension check (`ringnav_audio_extension`, at its `0x5c2054` call, told apart by the return address) also takes a video's while `g_folder_path` is in the card's Videos folder (`folder_videos`); elsewhere, and in the library scan, videos stay unlisted. Without it a Videos folder showed stock's empty-folder "File Not Found" (#128). A song played there would queue them too, so `ringnav_load` hands stock's `mclLoadPlayList` a copy of the tracks without videos (`no_videos`), the start index moved to the same song, and leaves the caller's showlist whole; a list of videos alone loads an empty audio queue. Folder hold actions also exclude videos, and video rows have no audio hold menu. A row (`play_video`, `video_play`) calls stock `player_stop` (`0x515b94`: hciplayer keeps its PCM, `hw:1,0`, open while paused), readies the headphone output on the DAC as stock's `player_start` does before each track (`dac_take`, shared with Spotify's `spot_take`: `config_outputchannel(g_headset_output, 2)`, which powers the DAC with its firmware in headset mode, or `mclSetDacPwr(1)` for a line out the DAC-off timer powered down; then PCM mode, unmuted, and the volume), then `fork`s and `execl`s `/usr/bin/q2video DEVICE PATH [VOLUME]`, the path a plain argument: nothing passes through a shell. `DEVICE` is `plughw:0,0`, the headphone DAC (the CS43131, the only ALSA card; there is no card 1), whose hardware volume (`mclSetVol`, ioctl `0xc0044d00` on `/dev/shanling_dac`) stays the music's. When `mclGetOutputWay` is 1 (Bluetooth) it is `plug:bluealsa`, the PCM hciplayer opens for that way (`0x439858`, string `0x88ed30`): demo writes `defaults.bluealsa { device "XX:XX:..." profile "a2dp" }` for the connected device to `/mnt/data/asound.conf`, which `alsa.conf` loads, so the name needs no address. There the volume is hciplayer's soft volume: `device_set_volume` (`0x4f44d4`) calls `mclSetBtVol(v, v)`, sending `{mcl-setvol\%d}` with `v << 8 | v`, and hciplayer's handler (`0x42c770`) calls its `mixer_setvolume` (`0x428744`), which sets an `af_volume` level of `0.1 + 0.018 (v - 50)` from 50, `v * softvol-max (20) / 10000` below, and removes the filter at 100 (no `snd_mixer` is imported, so the soft path is the one taken). So the payload passes the effective `headset_gain()` as `VOLUME` (full gain after accepted headset volume, otherwise `g_volume`, 0-100), and the helper scales its samples by the same curve (`bt_gain`, in 1/65536) and leaves the DAC's mute alone. With a USB DAC (2) `DEVICE` is `usb`, which the helper turns into `plughw:N,0` for the card `/proc/asound/cards` lists as `USB-Audio` (card 1 behind the headphone DAC's 0, as stock's `mclUsbAudioSetVol(…, "hw:1")` has it; hciplayer's `hw:2,0` string, `0x43975c`/`0x88ed40`, is no card on the Q2), and the plug converts the helper's 48 kHz stereo to what the DAC takes. Its volume is `device_set_volume`'s: `mclUsbAudioSetVol(v, v, "hw:1")`, or 100 when `g_usbvol_mode` (Playback' USB volume) is 0, fixed. That sets the DAC's own volume control; a DAC without one leaves `USB_MIXER` (`0xa3be6c`, no symbol) at -2 and sends hciplayer `{mcl-softvolflag\1}`, whose set-volume handler then applies the same `mixer_setvolume` curve as Bluetooth's. So `VOLUME` is plain `g_volume` (the helper's `bt_gain`) only for a variable volume on a DAC without its own control; otherwise it is `h` and the volume, which the helper shows on its bar and never applies.

While the helper runs, demo's loop goes on (timers, threads, the status bar) but `ringnav_input` drops every input event before the window manager, sending a key's release (`0x114`) to the helper as a datagram on its `AF_UNIX` socket `/tmp/q2video.sock` (`video_key`): Return `q`, Play/Pause `p`, the side buttons 222/223 `b` and `f`. The wheel is the volume, as on Now Playing: a tick steps `g_volume` as stock's `volume_dialog` keys do (`0x4a2044`: by 1, up only below 100 and `g_maxvolume`, down to 0), calls `device_set_volume(v, 1)` (the DAC's `mclSetLocalVol` ioctl, so the DAC follows at once, and for Bluetooth `mclSetBtVol`, so hciplayer has it after) and `write_int_config(v, "PLAYSET", "VOLUME")`, without stock's dialog, and sends `v` and the volume byte, at an end too. Centre toggles the wheel to seeking (`s`, then `b` and `f` per tick) and back (`v`); `video_poll` also puts it back on the volume `SCRUB_MS` (3 s) after the last tick or Centre, like Now Playing's scrub. And `ringnav_wm_paint` skips the window manager's paint. `video_poll`, from `ringnav_sleep`, calls `waitpid(WNOHANG)` every pass, meanwhile `reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 1)` (`0x4f43dc`, the screen, standby and auto-power-off counters `on_wm_idle_status` and `check_to_poweroption` count down) and zeroes `g_dacoff_time`; a pause that lasts `VIDEO_PAUSE_QUIT_MS` (30 s) without a key sends `q`, as Return, since input belongs to the helper and the hold would otherwise keep a paused video (a dropped headset pauses it) lit until the battery runs out. At the helper's end it closes the socket and repaints the whole screen. Music stays stopped. A video picks up where it was stopped: `play_video` writes the second kept for its path (FNV-1a, in Books' `BOOK_MARKS` ring, through the same `mark`) to `/tmp/q2video.place`, which the helper reads as its first `-ss`; on its end it writes there the second a Return left it at (`resume_at`: 0 within `EDGE_S`, 30 s, of either end, as [Resume](#resume)'s `RESUME_EDGE_S`, and 0 when it played to the end), and `video_poll` keeps that for the path, unless it and the start were both 0, so a video never resumed takes no slot from the books. A seek still pending at the Return counts.

**q2video** (`patch/video.c`, about 10 KB) is linked by `tools/build.py` against the rootfs's own `libc-2.28.so`, `libpthread` and `libasound.so.2` (no MIPS sysroot: the file declares what it uses), `nan2008`, `fp64` and `/lib/ld-linux-mipsn8.so.1` as the stock binaries, with its own `__start` like glibc's. It runs stock `/usr/bin/ffmpeg` (FFmpeg 4.2): `-ss` the start, `-vf scale=375:320:force_original_aspect_ratio=decrease:flags=fast_bilinear,format=bgra,pad=375:320:(ow-iw)/2:(oh-ih)/2,transpose=clock` (the picture fitted into the landscape 375 × 320 view, then turned clockwise onto the portrait framebuffer, as the boot logo is stored), `-r 25 -f rawvideo pipe:3`, and `-ac 2 -ar 48000 -f s16le pipe:4`. `/dev/fb0` is 320 × 375, 32 bpp with red at bit 16 (ffmpeg's `bgra`) and two pages: `soc_fb.ko`'s `init_var_info` gives every 32-bit format red 16, green 8, blue 0 and `yres_virtual` = `yres` × `layer0_frames` (2 in `module_driver/soc_fb.sh`, `is_rotated=0`); stock `display_logo` (`0x400bf0`) refuses under 18 bpp and stores each pixel as the word `0xff000000 | R << 16 | G << 8 | B` (`0x400f2c`), the 320 × 375 JPEG unturned; demo's AWTK flush thread pans between the two pages (`FBIOPAN_DISPLAY` at `0x6265bc`, then `FBIO_WAITFORVSYNC`). The helper exits without drawing if `FBIOGET_VSCREENINFO` reports anything else. A thread writes the sound with `snd_pcm_writei` in 512-frame chunks (200 ms buffer) and keeps the frames heard (written less `snd_pcm_delay`); frame n goes to the framebuffer at n / 25 s of that clock, a whole frame late is dropped, and it draws the page demo is not showing and pans (`FBIOPAN_DISPLAY`), panning back to demo's page at the end. Nothing counts as heard while ALSA still holds all that was written: through its first fill, at each start and seek, `snd_pcm_delay` is everything written (more at a start through Bluetooth, whose plugin adds the link's own delay, `0x5b64` in `libasound_module_pcm_bluealsa.so`), and counting that as heard ran the clock up to the buffer's 200 ms ahead, so the frames due in it were dropped as late and the picture then stood until the sound caught up. The picture stays on its first frame until the sound starts. Nor does what ALSA's ring still holds count as heard, whatever a plugin answers: the delay is floored at the ring's own count, the buffer (`snd_pcm_get_params` at open) less `snd_pcm_avail`. After a seek's `snd_pcm_drop` and `snd_pcm_prepare` while playing, bluealsa's plugin answers the link's delay alone through the refill: it counts its queue from the pointer its I/O thread last saved (`+0x20c`, written only at `0x6c20` and `0x6c58`), which its stop (`0x6c64`) and prepare (`0x643c`) leave and only its underrun path (`0x740c`) clears. The kernel's delay (the DAC, USB) is never below the ring, so there the floor changes nothing; a failed `snd_pcm_avail` (an XRUN) leaves the delay alone. The video pipe is 4 MB (`F_SETPIPE_SZ`), so ffmpeg is paced by the sound. A `v` sets Bluetooth's gain to `bt_gain` of its volume. `s`, `b`, `f` and `v` show a bar for 1.5 s (`OVERLAY_MS`), drawn on the page after each frame's copy and before the pan, and redrawn with the next frame when paused: `BAR_H` (8) px high, `BAR_Y` (22) px above the picture's bottom and `BAR_X` (40) px in from its ends, clear of the 80 px glass corners, white for the volume of 100 or the position (start plus frames shown plus pending seek) of the length, black for the rest, by byte fills (white and black in BGRA). The length comes from a second `ffmpeg -nostdin -hide_banner -i PATH` started at once, its stderr read on the main loop's poll until it ends (or 4 KB) for `Duration: HH:MM:SS`; without one the position bar is not drawn. Pause holds the writer (the sound buffer plays out, then the clock stops); a seek restarts ffmpeg once 400 ms after the last tick, at the position plus 10 s per tick; without a sound stream ffmpeg fails at once and it runs again silent on a monotonic clock. After hciplayer's stop it opens the PCM (retrying 2 s; bluealsa takes one client per PCM, so this also waits for hciplayer's close), sets the DAC to PCM (`0xc0044d1b`, 0, as hciplayer does per PCM track, so a DSD track's mode is undone) and unmutes it (`0xc0044d1f`, 0); at the end it mutes it again, as a stopped hciplayer leaves it. ponytail: the frame rate is fixed at 25 and decoding is software, so sources much above the screen's size can fall behind; a smaller encode then plays smoothly.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build of `video.c`): ffmpeg's argv with and without sound, the frame pacing, Bluetooth's gain curve and sample scaling, the `Duration` parse, `resume_at`'s edges, and the bar's pixels on a padded-line frame. `test/patch.py`: the Local Music row, the list, the child's `execl` argv after `player_stop`, the DAC power-on, Bluetooth's device and volume, silent USB, every key's datagram, the volume steps and their limits through `device_set_volume` and `write_int_config`, Centre's seek mode and its timeout, no other input or paint while playing, the held timeouts, and the end.

## Games

**Games** is the Library row after the media rows, always shown (card games follow its own four: [Card games](#card-apps)), with a 52px controller icon (`assets/icons/local_games.png`) in the same centred slot as the neighboring Library icons.
