Its purple disc becomes grey in Minimal through the existing category-icon mapping. `games_open` (`patch/games.c`) `calloc`s the whole state, opens a runtime `games_page` (`CTX_DYNAMIC`) with Coverflow's `page_list` chooser (Sudoku, Vortex, Solitaire, Snake) and a bare `view` sheet, and frees it all when the page closes; the scratch keeps only the pointer. `games_paint`, called from `ringnav_paint` like `books_paint`, draws the sheet with `canvas_fill_rect`, rounded boxes and frames (`canvas_fill_rounded_rect`, `canvas_stroke_rounded_rect`, square where the canvas declines), `caption` (Solitaire's suits through `draw_centred`, the default font's ♠♥♦♣) and, for Vortex and Snake's apple, vgcanvas arcs and dots, in `accent_tone(2)` (so every theme), and puts the fill colour back. Everything stays clear of the glass's 80 px corners ([Rounded corners](ipod.md#rounded-corners)): Sudoku's centred board already does; Vortex's score, best, level and lives sit `HUD_X` (24) px in; Solitaire's 45 px cards start `SOL_X` (18) px in and `SOL_Y` (6) down, and its two outer columns close their fan up to end `SOL_LOW` (35) px above the bottom instead of 4.

**Keys.** `games_key` runs in `ringnav()` right after the rating picker, before stock's key filter, while a game is showing and the screen is usable. It takes every key, so the wheel, Centre, Play/Pause and the side buttons never reach the volume, `media_key` or the double-Centre screen-off. A held Return's release (`RETURN_RELEASE_LATCH` set) and Power stay stock, so a long Return still goes Home. In the chooser it takes nothing; Return there is the page's key-up (`return_key`), back to the Library.

- **Sudoku**: the wheel moves through the cells in reading order, Previous/Next move a row, Centre on a blank or entered cell opens the digit (the wheel picks blank or 1-9, Centre writes it, Return cancels), Play/Pause starts a new game (asking first over an unfinished one), Return goes back to the chooser. Clues are on grey; a digit that repeats in its row, column or box is red and underlined. Every cell holding the cursor's digit, or the digit being picked, shows it dark on the tone.
- **Vortex**: Centre launches the ball from the paddle, Play/Pause pauses and resumes, Return pauses with Continue/Quit (the wheel picks, Centre confirms), and after the last life Centre plays again.
- **Solitaire**: the wheel walks the 13 piles (stock, waste, the four foundations, then the seven columns) and Previous/Next jump between a top-row pile and the column under it (the gap's column to the waste). Centre on the stock draws (an empty stock takes the waste back); on another pile it picks up its top card, or a column's whole face-up run, framed in the tone, and the cursor turns white. Centre on another pile drops what fits there, and nothing happens where nothing does; Centre on the same pile again sends its top card to a foundation, or puts it back. Return drops the held cards, then goes back to the chooser. Play/Pause starts a new game, asking first unless this one is won.
- **Snake**: the side Next button turns right and Previous turns left, one turn a step and a second waiting for the next (any more are dropped). The touch wheel is ignored during play and in the pause menu; Previous/Next pick Continue/Quit. Play/Pause pauses, Return opens Continue/Quit, and after a crash Centre plays again.

**Sudoku.** 24 bundled puzzles, 4 bits a cell, each with exactly one solution. A new game picks one and relabels its digits, shuffles rows within bands, columns within stacks, the bands and the stacks, and maybe transposes it, all of which keep the solution unique. ponytail: fixed puzzles, no generator, pencil marks or hints.

**Vortex.** Three rings of 16 bricks round the centre (40-76 px out) and the paddle, a 45° arc at 118 px. All integer: positions and velocities in 16.16 px per 1/60 s step, angles 1024 a turn, sine and arctangent from tables, so the same inputs give the same game. A brick reflects the ball off its radius (ponytail: never off a brick's side); the paddle sends it back towards the centre, turned up to 45° by how far off-centre it hit. Missing the paddle costs one of three lives, shown as grey balls under Best. A broken brick flashes white and fades over `POP_MS` (240 ms), and the ball trails its last `TRAIL` (4) frames' positions in the tone; `frame()` records both, so the game logic is unchanged, and repaints only while the ball moves or a flash fades. The game-over banner says "New best!" when the game raised Best. Clearing every brick is the next level: one of three layouts in turn (ponytail: fixed layouts), each 15 px/s faster from 120 to a cap of 270, under a ring's 12 px a step, so the ball never crosses a ring without touching it.

**Solitaire.** Klondike, draw one, unlimited passes through the stock. 13 piles of up to 24 bytes, a card `suit * 13 + rank` plus `UP` when face up. A column turns its top card when the one on it leaves. A held run drops the one part of it that fits (only one card in a run can go on a given card, and only its King on an empty column), so there is no depth to choose; a pile at its 24 takes nothing, and a save's cards are checked on load (0-51, face up or not) with its pile lengths and cursor, as its seal (`games_ok`) is unkeyed and a hand-made save must neither read past the suit table nor write past a pile; a foundation takes only the top card, any foundation an Ace. Once the stock and waste are empty and every card is face up, `sol_finish` sends everything home at once (the lowest card left is always a column's top). Columns fan face-down cards 6 px and face-up ones 20 px (the rank and suit's strip), closed up to fit the sheet, the two outer columns `SOL_LOW` short of it; ponytail: at the deepest (6 down, 13 up) a label shows 12 px of its 16 px in an inner column, 9 px in an outer one. ponytail: no undo, score, timer or draw three.

**Snake.** A 20 × 20 board of 11 px cells under the score and best, clear of the glass's corners (`SN_X`, `SN_Y`). The body is a ring of cell indices from the tail, the head last; a step moves the head one cell, and the tail follows unless it ate, so moving into the cell the tail is leaving is allowed. A wall or the body ends the game. An apple adds one to the length and the score and lands on a free cell picked with `rand_below` over the free count. Vortex's `frame` timer runs it: Snake's branch adds the milliseconds passed and takes a step per `snake_ms` (160 ms, 4 ms less an apple, 70 at most), two a frame at most, so the speed doesn't follow the frame rate. Only a record is saved (`/mnt/data/ringnav-snake`, `SNK1`, sealed as Vortex's best); an unfinished game isn't. ponytail: no wrap mode, levels or obstacles.

**Wheel.** Ticks are too coarse for a paddle (about seven a turn), so while Vortex plays `wheel_raw` is set (as on Now Playing's lyrics page, [Lyrics wheel](#scrub-ipod)) and `wheel_sample` (`patch/playback.c`) posts no ticks: it adds the raw travel (200 units a turn) to `wheel_travel` atomically, and nothing is queued for a menu meanwhile. Each frame reads and zeroes it; `VORTEX_GAIN` (percent, 100 a turn for a turn) is the paddle's calibration knob.

**Frame timer.** A 33 ms `timer_add`, only while Vortex plays, runs 60 steps a second (at most 4 a frame) and repaints only when the steps changed something (the paddle, the ball, the state), so waiting to launch paints nothing; only the wheel turning holds the screen timeout off, so a ball left in play lets the screen dim and go off like any page, which pauses it. A key the game ignores paints nothing either (`games_key` compares the state before and after). The screen going off or another window on top pauses the game, clears `wheel_raw` and ends the timer; so do Play/Pause, Return's menu, leaving and closing.

**Saves.** `/mnt/data/ringnav-sudoku` (the grid and the cursor) and `/mnt/data/ringnav-solitaire` (the piles and the cursor) are written only when changed: a written digit, a move, a draw or a new game marks its game dirty and arms one `SAVE_MS` (2 s) timer from the first unsaved change, not per move, and leaving to the chooser or closing the page (Home too) writes whatever is dirty, a moved cursor included; ponytail: a power-off loses the moves of the last 2 s, and a cursor moved since the last write (only moves arm the timer). `/mnt/data/ringnav-vortex` holds the best score, written when a game ends above it. All go through `blob_io`: each a struct whose first word is a magic and last the FNV-1a of the rest, so a short read, another magic or a bad checksum is a fresh game. A failed write shows "Couldn't save".

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build of `games.c`): every bundled puzzle and 200 shuffled ones have exactly one solution; clues refuse writes, conflicts by row, column and box, completion; the save seal; Vortex's paddle wrap, launch, bricks both ways, the paddle's return, lives to game over, the next level, the speed cap, every launch angle at full speed hitting the outer ring first, and the same inputs giving the same state; Solitaire's deal, draw and redeal, foundation and column rules, a run's part that fits, turning, home, the finish, and 100 seeded random games keeping every card once and every pile sound (some won). `test/patch.py`: the Library row, the chooser, Sudoku's keys and save with no key reaching stock's filter or `media_key`, the confirm, Return game → chooser → Library, Vortex's timer and raw wheel through Play/Pause, screen-off and Quit, Solitaire's draw and save, cursor, pick up, refused and good drops, Centre twice home and put back, Return, the confirm, and closing mid-game, the Solitaire resuming. `test/playback.py`: `wheel_raw` posts no ticks.

## Spotify

**Spotify** is Streaming's row after stock's (`ringnav_stream`, after `stream_page_init`'s rows in `scroll_view_streamsset`, a `library_into` row with `assets/icons/stream_spotify.png`, Spotify's logo as a 52 px palette PNG with `list_tidal`'s inode metadata). It opens the browser (below; without `librespot` on the card, `spotify_page` saying so), a runtime window (`patch/spotify.c`), for librespot, a Spotify Connect receiver built from the [q2-librespot](https://github.com/DiamondBond/q2-librespot) fork (branch `shanlingq2`; hard-float, linked against the stock glibc, 19.5 MB, so it stays on the card). Nothing of it is in the firmware: the card's `/mnt/mmc/.spotify/` holds `librespot`, its launcher `run`, the sink `aplay.sh`, the saved login `cache/` and the logs.

**Starting.** `spot_launch` runs `system("/bin/sh /mnt/mmc/.spotify/run")` once per boot, when the row is first pressed, and only then (while [Smart Wi-Fi](#smart-wi-fi) brings the radio back, once its link is up: `smart_hold`): until it has, `spot_poll` returns at once and nothing reads the card or the state file. The login (`cache/credentials.json`, written after the first Connect handoff) only saves the phone's pick. `run` passes `--cache-size-limit` from `.cache-size` ([Streaming configuration](#streaming-configuration)), `2G` without it. `run` backgrounds a loop that restarts librespot after any exit, waiting 5 s and doubling to a minute while runs keep failing (mDNS needs Wi-Fi up), and leaves the loop's pid in `/tmp/q2-librespot`, which also makes a second run exit. librespot's process is not demo's child, as bluealsa's isn't. Once it has played, `SPOT_IDLE_MS` (30 s) without playing (paused, stopped or handed to another device) while the browser is shut makes `spot_poll` stop it as Home's Rockbox row does (`spot_stop`: the loop's pid, `killall -9 librespot aplay`, the pid file, the state file and `SPOT_OUT` removed), so the keys are local music's again; the row starts it again. Before it has played, the wait is `SPOT_CAST_MS` (150 s), time to cast to the Q2 from the phone; it counts from the launch or the last poll with the browser open, whichever is later. An open browser (pairing prompt and Now Playing on it included, and so any request in flight) restarts the wait every poll while the screen is on, but holds no timer and no DAC: only playing does (`spot_take`). Left open with the screen off, it holds nothing: once the wait runs out, `spot_poll` closes the browser (`window_close`, as Back would) and then stops librespot, so a Q2 left dark on it, with Standby off, doesn't keep librespot connected and its state files read twice a second. ponytail: waking after that finds Streaming, not the browser; its row starts librespot again. Without `librespot` on the card `spotify_page` says so and nothing runs.

**Contract.** librespot runs with `--backend subprocess --device "/bin/sh ./aplay.sh" --volume-ctrl fixed --initial-volume 100 --control-socket /tmp/q2-librespot.sock --status-file /tmp/q2-librespot.state` (the last two are the fork's):

- The status file is rewritten whole (a `.tmp`, renamed) on each player event: `state=` `none` (no Connect session: the phone hasn't chosen the Q2, or chose another device), `stopped`, `paused` or `playing`; `track=` (the Spotify id), `title=`, `artist=` (joined by `, `), `album=`, `duration=` and `position=` in ms, `at=` the `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` ms the position was taken at, and `cover=1` once `/tmp/q2-librespot.state.jpg` holds this track's cover, which librespot fetches itself over its own TLS (the smallest Spotify image at least 160 px wide: 300 px). The payload does no TLS.
- The socket takes `AF_UNIX` datagrams, sent `MSG_DONTWAIT` as Videos' are: `p` play/pause, `P` play, `s` pause, `n` next, `b` previous, `S` and a position in ms to seek. librespot ignores them without a session.
- The subprocess sink starts `aplay.sh` when playback starts and kills it when it stops, pause included, so the DAC's PCM is free while librespot is paused. `aplay.sh` is the stock `aplay -D plughw:0,0 -f S16_LE -r 44100 -c 2`, retried for 3 s: aplay opens the PCM before it reads its input, so a busy PCM costs no audio. Each try first reads `/tmp/q2-librespot.out` (`SPOT_OUT`): when it holds a device and a volume, it runs `/usr/bin/q2video -s DEVICE VOLUME` instead (below). Without the file, which older Q2 Pods never write, it is aplay as before.
- **Output.** Every poll `spot_output` asks `output_device` (books.c, Videos' and Internet Radio's choice through `video_start`) for the output in use: none on the headphone DAC, `plug:bluealsa` with `headset_gain()` for Bluetooth (`mclGetOutputWay` 1), `usb` with `g_volume`, or `h` and it when the DAC applies the volume, for a USB DAC (2). When the line changes it writes `DEVICE VOLUME` to `SPOT_OUT` (a `.tmp`, renamed) or removes the file, and sends `v` and the effective gain to `/tmp/q2sink.sock`. `q2video -s` opens the device at 44.1 kHz, S16 stereo, with Videos' `sound_open` (retrying 5 s while hciplayer lets go), plays stdin through Videos' writer at `bt_gain` of the volume, as hciplayer's soft volume would (the DAC's mute is left alone), and takes `v` and `q` on that socket as radio's helper does. It ends with its input (the sink's kill closes librespot's pipe) or when a write fails even after `snd_pcm_recover`, a headset gone: librespot's subprocess sink then restarts `aplay.sh` once, which reads the file again. ponytail: otherwise an output switched while playing takes over at the sink's next start (pause and play), as Videos and Internet Radio keep their device until they end.

**Polling and power.** `spot_poll`, from `ringnav_sleep` every `SPOT_POLL_MS` (500 ms), reads the file and parses it when it changed. On the change to `playing` (`spot_take`) it does what stock's AirPlay page does for its receiver (`airplay_page_init`, `on_airplayset_onclick`): `player_stop` unless hciplayer is stopped (it holds the PCM even paused), `config_outputchannel(g_headset_output, 2)` (what a headset insert runs: `mclSetHeadsetMode`, `mclSetDacPwr(1)`, which also loads the DAC's FPGA firmware, and `g_dacoff_time` 0), `mclSetPcmMode`, `mclSetMute(0)` (`player_stop` mutes) and `device_set_volume(g_volume, 1)`. With Bluetooth or USB (`g_headset_output` 2 and up) it only powers the DAC on if `check_dacoff_state` turned it off; the sink plays there itself (Output, above). While `playing`, every poll calls `reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 0)` (`0x4f43dc`: its arguments are standby, auto power-off and the backlight, in that order) and zeroes a counting `g_dacoff_time`, so neither standby nor `check_dacoff_state` ends playback, while the screen still turns off on its timer. On the headphone DAC the volume is the DAC's own: librespot's is fixed at 100%, and `device_set_volume` (stock's volume dialog, the screen-off wheel) sets the DAC with `mclSetLocalVol` whether or not hciplayer plays.

**Keys.** `ringnav()` hands Play/Pause, previous and next (171, 222, 223) to `spot_media` right after stock's key-lock filter (`on_wm_keyup_before_fun`), on any page and with the screen off, while Spotify is the active source: playing or paused, and no local track has played since. They become `p`, `b` and `n`, and stock's own key-up never sees them. On the page Centre works as on Now Playing's [scrub](#scrub-ipod) (`spot_key`): a release arms the same `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` (250 ms) timer (`center_press`, shared with Now Playing), and a second one before it ends starts a scrub; the wheel then moves it `SPOT_STEP_MS` (5 s) a tick and the bar turns white, and a centre press (at once, swallowing a quick second one, as on Now Playing), Return or `SCRUB_MS` without a tick seeks there with `S`. Outside a scrub the timer, a single press, replays the release to stock `on_wm_keyup_fun`, so the screen turns off. Otherwise the wheel is stock's volume and `spotify_page` is not in `contexts.inc`, so `ringnav()` leaves it to stock.

**Hand-over.** Local music → Spotify is `spot_take` above. Spotify → local music: `mclStartPlayer` (`0x5ad6ac`), which every local track's start runs before hciplayer opens the PCM, is hooked (`ringnav_start_player`). With librespot playing, `spot_yield` sends `s` and waits, 20 ms at a time up to `SPOT_YIELD_MS` (1.5 s), until the file no longer says `playing`. The fork's sink stops (aplay killed and reaped) before the `Paused` event, so the PCM is free once it does. The keys are local music's again from then on. Home's Rockbox row kills the loop and librespot (`/tmp/q2-librespot`'s pid, `killall -9 librespot aplay`), as Rockbox takes ALSA.

**Page.** `spot_open` builds Now Playing's layout from `tools/ipod.py`'s `NP_*`: "Spotify" in the top row with a play or pause glyph at its end, the art 166 px at x 16, y 50, the title (22 px white), artist and album (16 px `#AAAAAA`) in a 165 px column at x 194, the bar at `NP_BAR` and the times under it. The art is librespot's cover sized once a track by Coverflow's `thumb` (`toolsThumbSpecCover` under `parse_cover_mutex`) to `/tmp/q2spot.jpg` and loaded with its `image_show`, `default_album_flow` until there is one; it rounds its corners as Now Playing's (`paint_cover`). The position is `position + (now - at)` while playing, clamped to the duration. A 250 ms timer, idle with the screen off, writes the times when the second changes and repaints the bar, which `spot_paint` draws as Now Playing's capsule: `TRACK_COLOR` with the played part in the accent's light tone (iPod) or stock red (Stock). The remaining time is whole seconds less whole seconds, as Now Playing's. Only a `login` reply or a sign-out shows "Open Spotify on your phone / and choose Q2"; a playing or paused track, or any `ok` reply, confirms the login, and `state=none` makes it unknown again. A successful play shows "Loading…" until a track plays: the one asked for, or any other than before it (a relinked id). After `SPOT_WAIT_MS` (15 s) it says "Playback didn't start", without sending play again, until any track plays. A refused key ends the wait sooner. The play's `ok` notes the length of `.spotify/spot.log` (librespot's stderr, which `run` appends to), and while it loads `spot_poll` reads what was logged since (the last 4 KB of it, re-reading 14 bytes so a line written in two parts still matches). Once that holds `error audio key` ([librespot#1649](https://github.com/librespot-org/librespot/issues/1649): Spotify refused the track's key), it says "Spotify refused this account / Its tracks can't play on the Q2". It also runs `killall -9 librespot aplay`, because librespot otherwise skips through the whole list, a key refused each time, and a pause (`s`) doesn't stop it while it loads. `run`'s loop starts librespot again with no track, and for `SPOT_SIGNOUT_MS` the browser takes a missing socket for that restart (`spot_restarting`). Any track that plays later still clears it. The three labels scroll without focus or an ellipsis and restart when their text changes; `field` cuts only at a UTF-8 character boundary. It sits on the browser, so Return goes back to the list it was opened from. ponytail: the cover is sized on the UI thread, one decode a track; a worker if it stutters.

Under Classic `stream_ipod` (navigation.c) lays the page out as local Now Playing's, from the `NP_IPOD_*` places `tools/ipod.py` checks against the page: the art's widget in the slanted art's box (178 px, taller by the reflection, so `paint_cover` slants it; the cover is thumbnailed at that width), title, artist and album in the local rows (the title in `ROLE_NP`'s face), the times beside the bar in Classic's face, and `spot_paint` hands the bar to `ipod_bar` with the position and `ui.scrub` for the scrub's diamond. The "Spotify" label and its play glyph hide: the title bar names the page. The art's `EVT_DESTROY` (`stream_gone`) clears the page's pointers and closes the slant's frame.

**Browser.** `spotbrowse_page` (`CTX_DYNAMIC` in `contexts.inc`, so the wheel moves its rows) is one `page_list` rebuilt per level, as Internet Radio's, its rows' clicks run from a timer. A level is a Q2SPOT/1 request ([librespot.md](librespot.md#browsing-protocol-q2spot1)): its op, its arguments and cursor (escaped, `spot_escape`) and its reply, cut in place by `spot_reply` into the header's 4 fields and 6 a row (at most 50), unescaped; a bad escape, `%00`, a NUL, a line of the wrong width or too many rows makes it malformed. IDs, URIs and cursors are sent back exactly as received. The base level asks `account`: `ok` shows the menu (Now Playing, Queue and Playback Settings only with a track), `login` the pairing prompt ("Open Spotify on your phone and choose Q2", "Both on the same Wi-Fi"), which asks again every `SPOT_PAIR_MS` (2 s) and becomes the menu once paired. Liked Songs, Playlists, Albums and Artists list 64 px rows (title over subtitle); an album, playlist or artist row opens its own level (an artist's top tracks, then its albums), and a non-empty cursor adds **More…**, a level of its own, so Back returns to the page and the row left. Up to `SPOT_DEPTH` (8) levels are kept, the oldest past the base dropped; only the top `SPOT_KEEP` (3) keep their replies (64 KB each at most), and Back to an older one fetches it again. Search lays peq_ui.c's T9 edit over the title; its OK with text opens Tracks, Albums, Artists and Playlists for the term, and empty text or the box's blur leaves the menu as it was. A track row sends `play CONTEXT POSITION URI` (`play URI` without a context; a Queue row `skip POSITION URI`) and opens Now Playing once it is `ok`, else toasts the message. Play/Pause held on a track row (`ringnav_keylong` → `spot_hold`) sends `enqueue URI`, its release latched as the radio's, and only toasts a failure message; held on a playlist row it sends `download URI`, as the open playlist's Download Playlist row does, and toasts the reply (it starts, or another playlist is already downloading). Liked Songs has a Download Liked Songs action when the backend advertises saved collections. The persistent Downloads hub, capability negotiation, ordered offline playback and storage controls follow [downloads.md](downloads.md). `caps` is asked once, after the base's `account`; a request that cancels it (any other) leaves `caps_checked` clear, and the page's 250 ms tick asks again once the menu is idle at the base, re-laying the menu with the reply only when no click is pending on its rows. A removal preview is its own act (`A_PREVIEW`), so only its reply opens **Remove Downloads?**; one dropped by Return can't turn a later action's reply into a removal. Download Progress polls `jobs` every 2 s only while the browser is on top with the screen on, and a failed poll keeps the list. With an older backend (no saved collections, but `download`), Downloads keeps **Download Status** (`downloadstatus`, its message toasted). Playback Settings shows Shuffle as a switch (`list_toggle`) and Repeat (Off, All, One); a press sends the next value and reads them again. Account shows the name and Premium, and Sign Out asks first; after `signout` the pairing prompt returns. Loading ("Loading…"), empty ("Nothing here", unless a cursor offers More…) and errors are page states: the backend's message as the title, with **Retry** for `offline`, `throttled`, `error` or no usable reply. Any `login` reply returns to pairing.

**Worker.** `request` writes `Q2SPOT/1`, the id (`wk.id`, never reused) and the op into a 64 KB buffer and starts `worker` on a pthread: an `AF_UNIX` `SOCK_STREAM` (2, MIPS numbering) to `/tmp/q2-librespot.browse.sock`, the request sent `MSG_NOSIGNAL`, the write side shut (`spot_shut`, the o32 `shutdown` syscall 4182, since demo imports no `shutdown`), then `select` 100 ms at a time and `recv` into the same buffer until EOF, `SPOT_WAIT_MS` (15 s) or a cancel; more than 64 KB fails. One request at a time: a new one, Back from a loading level or the page's close cancels the one before and joins it within 100 ms. `wk.done` is the worker's to set once its thread runs, as it may have finished before `pthread_create` returns: `request` sets it only when no thread started (card apps' `req_send` is the same). The page's 250 ms timer takes the finished reply on the UI thread; one whose header doesn't echo the id, or for a level since left (`level.id`), is dropped. Actions (`play`, `skip`, `enqueue`, `shuffle`, `repeat`, `signout`) are never sent again by themselves. No socket within `SPOT_OLD_MS` (3 s) of librespot's start, or `SPOT_SIGNOUT_MS` (15 s) after a sign-out (librespot exits and its launcher restarts it), is librespot starting: "Loading…" (or the pairing prompt) asks again every 500 ms. After that, a state file means an old add-on: Now Playing and "Update Spotify on the card / to browse on the Q2", still asking every 2 s. Without either, "Spotify isn't running" with Retry. ponytail: a title is cut at 255 bytes by the backend and a search term at 63 by the page; nothing is cached across opens.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build): `spot_reply` on escapes in any case, UTF-8, a short header, every malformed shape and the row limit; `spot_escape` and its bounds. Checks in `test/patch.py`: the row and its icon, the page without librespot (nothing starts), the start once per boot from the row and never without it, saved login or not, the session prompt, `spot_take`'s calls and their order and Bluetooth's exception, `SPOT_OUT` and the sink's volume on Bluetooth and its removal on the DAC, the held timers, the art, the labels moving on from `at`, every label inside the glass, the bar's rectangles and colour, the keys on another page and their return to local music, `mclStartPlayer`'s wait and its limit, the scrub and Return; the idle stop only with the browser shut. The browser against a fake backend on the socket: the first request's bytes, the menu with and without a track, loading, errors with and without Retry, empty pages, More… on an empty page, a wrong id, a bad escape and an oversized reply, playlists by URI, tracks played in their context (with their URI) or alone and Now Playing only on `ok`, a refused key (the note, one kill, no play sent again, a line from before the play ignored, a line cut between polls found, no kill without one), More… and Back to the row left, Back while loading, an artist's and album's pages and a refetched evicted level, pairing and its polling, an old add-on and a missing librespot, search's empty, left and escaped terms, Add to Queue on hold and its failure, Queue's skip, Playback Settings and an unrepeated failure, Sign Out's confirmation, the restart after it, a lost login, and the idle stop after the browser shuts and the relaunch.

## AirPlay

Stock's receiver is `/usr/bin/shairport`, Shanling's Shairport 1.1.1 (`1.1.1-22-shanling`): AirPlay 1 (RAOP) only, with its own mDNS responder, started and stopped by demo (`airplayStartService`, `airplayStopService`) from Network service's switch (`on_airplayset_onclick`, `0x51e8f0`), with its now-playing page `airplay_page`. Streaming's **AirPlay** row (`ringnav_stream`, stock's `netservice_airplay` icon) only opens `systemset/netservice_page`. ponytail: the row doesn't toggle the service itself; stock's `airplay_switch` (`0x50ef44`) is the way in if it should. AirPlay 2 would be a shairport-sync 4.x add-on with nqptp, an add-on on the card like librespot.

## Internet Radio

**Internet Radio** is Streaming's last row (`ringnav_stream`, `assets/icons/stream_radio.png`, a broadcast glyph on coral, 52 px palette PNG with `list_tidal`'s inode metadata). It opens `radio_page`, a runtime window (`patch/radio.c`, `CTX_DYNAMIC` in `contexts.inc`, so the wheel moves its rows), one `page_list` rebuilt per level: the menu (Now Playing, when a station has played this boot or `/mnt/mmc/Radio/.last` exists; Favourites; Featured; Top Stations; By Country; By Genre), a country or genre list, and a station list. Rows rebuild the page from a timer (`later`), never inside their own click. Return goes up a level, from a "Loading…" or failure caption back to the list it covered, and from the menu back to Streaming. Each level keeps its selected row.

**Lists.** Favourites are `/mnt/mmc/Radio/favourites.m3u`, then every other `.m3u` and `.pls` there, read whole and parsed as one text (`playlist`): `#EXTINF:…,NAME` names the next line, `FileN=` (a `file://` URL whose name holds an `=` is no key) is a URL and `TitleN=` names the one before; a line without `://` is no station. Favourites start empty: opening the page only creates the `Radio` folder. The six built-in stations (`STARTERS`, each checked against stock-like ffmpeg) are Featured, parsed from the payload. Search is the page's pull-down ([Pull-to-search](#pull-to-search)) on any level: `radio_search` lays a T9 edit (`edit_create`, shared with the EQ editor's value) over the title and focuses it for the keyboard; its OK (`EVT_VALUE_CHANGED`) fetches `/m3u/stations/byname/TEXT` (%-encoded, a substring match), and an empty result says "No stations", and Return goes back to the level it was pulled over. Empty text, or the box's blur (`EVT_BLUR`, `0x10f`) without a search, rebuilds the page as it was. An empty OK closes the keyboard with neither event, so the keyboard's OK (`EVT_IM_ACTION`, `0x13a`) runs the same handler. Return empties and blurs the box, which shuts the keyboard before the box is destroyed: destroying it under an open keyboard crashes demo. With the keyboard up, Return never reaches the page: `search_key` (`ringnav`, ahead of stock's filter) takes it for the input method's edit when that is one of `edit_create`'s, named `EDIT_NAME` (`q2_edit`): it empties it, blurs it and detaches the keyboard, so every one of them (Radio, Spotify and card-app search, the EQ value and preset name) cancels, and the next Return goes back. Stock's search dialogs and the playlist editor's search, named `search_edit`, are blurred and detached without emptying, the editor's search applying its text on that Return. The directory is [radio-browser.info](https://api.radio-browser.info)'s round-robin `all.api.radio-browser.info`, fetched by `fetch_worker` on a pthread through demo's libcurl, over verified TLS with the firmware's `/etc/scrobble-ca.pem` (ISRG Root X1 signs it), 10 s to connect, 30 s in all, at most 256 KB: Top Stations is `/m3u/stations/topvote/100?hidebroken=true`, By Country `/csv/countries?order=stationcount&reverse=true&limit=60` then `/m3u/stations/bycountrycodeexact/CODE`, By Genre `/csv/tags?…` then `/m3u/stations/bytagexact/TAG` (%-encoded, `url_escape`), each with `?order=votes&reverse=true&limit=100&hidebroken=true`. The m3u is the favourites' parser's; the CSV (`choices`) is read from each line's end, so a quoted name may hold commas. The countries, the genres and a country's or genre's stations are sorted A–Z once fetched (`list_sort`, from `fetch`'s `az`): `ringnav_sort_key`, the library's rule, so "The Netherlands" sorts under N, then `strcasecmp`; an insertion sort, stable, so equal names keep the vote order. The server's order still picks which 60 and which 100 come back. Top Stations and Search, from any level, keep the vote order, and the side buttons step through a list in the order shown. Without Wi-Fi (`get_wifisignal`) the page says "No Wi-Fi"; a failed fetch "Directory unavailable". Return while loading sets a cancel flag that the transfer's progress callback returns, so the join takes at most about a second. A list holds `RADIO_MAX` (200) entries.

**Favourites.** Play/Pause held on a station (`ringnav_keylong` → `radio_hold`, the row from the list's `_ringnav_index`, or on Now Playing the station playing) rewrites `favourites.m3u` (a `.tmp`, synced and renamed) without it when its URL is there, else with it appended, and toasts which; its release is latched as the queue menu's (`st.qm_press`), so it does not stop the radio. A file already at `RADIO_MAX` is never rewritten, so nothing past it is lost.

**Playing.** A station (`radio_play`) copies the list it is in, so the side buttons step through it later, stops a playing q2video, pauses librespot (`spot_yield`), and starts `/usr/bin/q2video -r DEVICE URL [VOLUME]` through `video_start` (books.c), Videos' own output choice: `player_stop`, the headphone output readied (`dac_take`), `plughw:0,0`, `plug:bluealsa` or `usb`, and the volume for Bluetooth's and soft USB's gain. The station goes to `/mnt/mmc/Radio/.last` as a one-entry `.m3u`. The keys and their output stay demo's: `radio_poll`, from `ringnav_sleep` every 500 ms while it runs, reads `/tmp/q2radio.state`, reaps it (`waitpid(WNOHANG)`), calls `reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 0)` and zeroes a counting `g_dacoff_time`, as Spotify's poll does, and sends `v` and the volume byte on `/tmp/q2radio.sock` when `g_volume` changes, so stock's volume (wheel, dialog, screen off) reaches the soft gain. `radio_media`, next to `spot_media` after stock's key-lock filter, takes Play/Pause (stop, or play again), 222 and 223 (the previous or next station, round the ends) on any page while the radio is the active source: it played last and no local track, video or Spotify has played since. Stopping (`radio_halt`) sends `q` and waits up to `RADIO_QUIT_MS` (1.5 s), then `kill -9` on its pid (librespot also uses q2video), and removes the state file. `mclStartPlayer`'s hook, `play_video`, `spot_take` and Home's Rockbox row call `radio_stop`, which also hands the keys back. Nothing starts at boot.

**q2video -r** (`radio` in `patch/video.c`) opens the PCM and readies the DAC as Videos does (`sound_open`), then runs stock ffmpeg with `-loglevel verbose -icy 1 -reconnect 1 -reconnect_streamed 1 -rw_timeout 15000000 -i URL -vn -ac 2 -ar 48000 -f s16le pipe:4` (HTTPS works: ffmpeg's `tls_verify` is 0), the sound to the same writer thread, its stderr read a byte at a time on the main loop's poll. libavformat's http logs `Metadata update for StreamTitle: …` only at verbose; the input dump gives `icy-br` and `Stream #0:0: Audio: CODEC`. `meta` takes a line's value after `KEY :`, so `icy-notice2 : … Network Audio Server` is no codec. The state file is rewritten whole (`.tmp`, renamed) on each change: `state=` `connecting`, `playing` (once the writer has played a frame) or `error`, `title=` (the StreamTitle, which the page splits at `-` into artist and song), `codec=` (upper case), `bitrate=` (kbit/s), `error=` (when `sound_open` found no device: `open:` or `params:` and `snd_strerror`'s reason, which the page shows as "Can't play: …"; Bluetooth and USB get 50 tries, 5 s, the DAC 20, at open and again at `snd_pcm_set_params` while it is busy, since bluealsa claims its PCM there; or `write:` when a write fails past `snd_pcm_recover`, which ends that ffmpeg run, as it ends Spotify's sink; Videos drop such a write instead, so their picture goes on) and `at=` (`CLOCK_MONOTONIC` ms the sound started, for the elapsed time). When ffmpeg ends anyway it starts again after 1 s, 2 s, … up to `RADIO_TRIES` (5) starts in a row without sound, then writes `state=error` and exits. A start with no frame heard after `RADIO_SILENT_MS` (20 s) means the output is stuck (the writer may be blocked inside ALSA, so it can't be joined): it writes `state=error` with `error=no sound from the output` (or the writer's), kills ffmpeg and exits, so the page never stays on "Connecting…". `q` kills ffmpeg, unlinks the state, closes the PCM and mutes the DAC. No FM: the Q2 has no tuner (the device tree's only i2c device is the axp2101, and stock hciplayer was built with `--disable-radio`).

**Page.** `radionp_page` has Spotify's Now Playing layout: "Internet Radio" above, the row's icon unscaled in the art box, the station's list name, the StreamTitle's artist and song, then the codec and bitrate ("Connecting…", "Stopped", "Can't play: REASON" or "Can't play this station" instead). Local Now Playing's heart sits at its place (x 303, y 176, 50 px, `play_fav` / `play_unfav` centred): filled when the station playing is in `favourites.m3u`, which is read again only when `rd.serial` changes (each start and each toggle), and a tap toggles it as the held Play/Pause does (`fav_toggle`), with the same toast and the elapsed time, refreshed on a 500 ms timer idle with the screen off. It is not in `contexts.inc`, so the wheel is stock's volume and Centre stock's screen toggle. ponytail: no station logos; the directory's m3u has none, and a logo would cost another fetch and a decode per station.

Under Classic the page takes local Now Playing's places (`stream_ipod`, navigation.c, as Spotify's page does; see [Slanted art](#slanted-art)): the art's widget moves to the slanted art's box and shows the build's `classic_light_station` (`tools/art.py` `station`, `playing.station`'s colours, `NP_IPOD_ART` square), the station, artist and song take the title's, artist's and album's rows, the stream "3 of 12"'s, the heart the local one's, and the running time the elapsed time's box, in Classic's face, sized for "-59:59" until the stream has played an hour, then for the hours (`ipod_times`). `stream_ipod` adds a bar of its own, last so over the art's box, insensitive, which `paint_scrubber` paints with `ipod_bar` always full; the wheel stays the volume. The name's label hides: Classic's title bar names the page.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host builds): `meta` on ffmpeg's lines; `playlist` on CRLF, BOM, radio-browser's extra tags, `.pls` keys and the limit; `choices` on both CSVs and quoted commas; `url_escape`. `test/patch.py`: the row and its icon, empty favourites, Featured, a 1.0.1 seed removed and an edited one kept, the pull-down's search box, its query, its empty result and its cancel, the pull gesture on `radio_page`, the headphone output readied before the fork, the error's reason, the save from Now Playing and its heart, the favourites from every playlist, the child's `execl` argv after `player_stop`, `.last`, the page's labels connecting and playing, the held timers, the volume datagram, the media keys and the side buttons round the ends, `mclStartPlayer`'s hand-over, the kill after `RADIO_QUIT_MS`, the error, the favourites toggle and its latched release, Top Stations' fetch and TLS options, No Wi-Fi, genres with an escaped tag, countries by code, both A–Z with their stations, a search over them in vote order, a failed fetch and Return through every level.

## Tidal cache

Stock's Tidal stream thread (unnamed, after `tidalGetFavoritesIdsThd` at `0x4863a0`) waits for `tidalfurlstart`, which `get_tidal_trackurl` sets after copying the record's song id into `tidalsongid`. It copies that id to its own `malloc` in `$s2` (`0x48647c`), sets `tidalStreamingLevelNow` from `tidalStreamingLevelNext` (at most 3), and asks `playbackinfopostpaywall` for that quality. It then base64-decodes the manifest (`toolsBase64Decode`) and passes its `urls[0]`, a direct file URL, to `mcl_tidalStartPlayer` (`0x5ad240`, called only at `0x486d88`), which sends it to hciplayer. A skip during the fetch rewrites `tidalsongid` but not the thread's copy.

Its idle poll (the `jalr` at `0x486404`) is `ringnav_tidal_poll` (`patch/tidal.c`), which waits on a semaphore with no timer, screen on or off, whatever plays. Only `get_tidal_trackurl` (`0x483188`, store at `0x483278`) sets `tidalfurlstart`, and stock's loop polls before it reads the flag, so `ringnav_tidal_track` posts the semaphore once stock's `get_tidal_trackurl` has returned: the thread wakes once per track asked for (a queue restored at power-on, a pick, the next track), fetches it, and waits again at its next poll. A post made during a fetch is counted, so it only adds one pass that finds the flag clear. Baidu's stock thread (`baidu_pthread_playurl`, poll at `0x47f824`) calls `ringnav_baidu_poll`, which waits on a semaphore nothing posts. The thread is not exited, since a `pthread_exit` from the payload's frames has no unwind tables to walk.

`mcl_tidalStartPlayer` is hooked by `tidal_start` (`patch/tidal.c`), two instructions that pass `$s2` on as the song id; the build pins the `$s2` set-up and the call. With the extension of the URL's path (`.flac`, or `.mp4` kept as `.m4a`, which stock's file lists take), the card's `/mnt/mmc/.tidal/<id>-<quality> <artist> - <title><ext>` (`tidal_name`: the queue row with that id at `REC_TIDAL_ID` names it; FAT's reserved characters become `_`, and a long label is cut at a whole UTF-8 character) plays in the URL's place when it exists, found by its `<id>-<quality>` prefix and extension. Otherwise the URL plays, and a detached pthread downloads it with demo's libcurl to `.tmp`, flushes and syncs it, and renames it into place on HTTP 200. A skip (a `tidal_play` with another id; a re-fetch of the same id leaves its download running) or Stop aborts it from curl's progress callback, and one under 1 KB/s for 30 s gives up, so the `.tmp` is removed. Stop is inferred, not signalled: the callback reads stock's play status (`mclGetPlayStatus`, 1 stopped) and aborts once it has read stopped for `TIDAL_STOP_MS` (3 s) running, so the stopped state between queued tracks keeps the copy; ponytail: a stream that sits stopped longer than that before it plays drops its copy (cached on a later play). Only one download runs at a time: a track that starts while a skipped one's transfer is still ending leaves its URL as the job the same thread takes next, the latest track's only, and none is queued below `card_space`'s 16 MB. After each download the oldest files by mtime are removed until `.tidal` holds at most the cap chosen in [Streaming configuration](#streaming-configuration) (2 GB by default), read once per trim. Peer verification is off: the firmware's CA bundle lacks Tidal's CDN roots, and hciplayer streams the same URL unverified (ffmpeg's `tls_verify=0`). Tidal's files carry no tags, so before the rename the copy is remuxed by stock `/usr/bin/ffmpeg` (`tag`: `-c copy` to `<file>.tmp.tag.tmp`, renamed over the `.tmp` on exit 0 with a non-empty file) with the queue row's title, artist and album; a FLAC also gets the row's cover (`REC_COVER`, `+0x28`, the `/tmp/tidal/*.jpg` that `player_get_id3info` hands the cover thread as type 2), fitted to 640 px by `thumb` into `/tmp/.tidal_cover.jpg` as the job starts, since stock removes a page's covers when another opens, as an attached picture. A track played from Tidal Downloads then shows its artist and art from its own tags. ponytail: no art in an `.m4a`, as ffmpeg 4.2's mp4 muxer writes no cover; a row whose cover file is already gone (a queue restored at power-on) gets tags but no art; any ffmpeg failure keeps the untagged copy; copies cached before this stay untagged until Clear All Downloads; the remux writes the file once more on the card. Only the URL sent to the player changes; the queue record, art and Now Playing are stock's. The API request still runs first, so a cached track started from Tidal's pages needs Wi-Fi but streams nothing. **Streaming → Tidal Downloads** (`tidal_downloads` in `library_lists.c`) lists `.tidal` through `toolsLoadDirectory`, newest file first, by the label after the prefix, and folder-plays a row as local music, so it needs no Wi-Fi; its page name starts `tidal*`, so the playback keys stay with it (`media_focused`). ponytail: oldest download goes first (demo imports no `utime`to touch a replayed file); a`.tmp` left by a power-off stays until that track is cached again, or Clear All Downloads. The list ends in **Clear All Downloads…** (`td_clear`), which, from a 0 ms timer as a row's click may not rebuild its list (`td_show`), puts **Remove Downloads?** in the list's place (the track count, Cancel, Remove), worded as Spotify's Storage Management; Cancel or Return (`td_keyup`) lists the songs again, on the Clear row. Remove (`tidal_clear`, tidal.c) unlinks every name in `.tidal`not starting with a dot: finished copies and`.tmp`ones, nothing outside the folder;`.cache-size`stays. There is no index to keep in step, as the list is`toolsLoadDirectory`of the folder, read again after. It runs under`td.lock`, drops a queued job and counts `td.cleared`; the download thread renames its finished `.tmp`into place under the same lock only if that count hasn't moved since its job began, so a download running across the clear is dropped rather than left behind. A play after it caches as usual. ponytail: on the UI thread, holding the lock, a moment for a full 8 GB cache. The confirmation is the same page rebuilt, not a pushed one: a new runtime window would need its own`contexts.inc` entry, builder allowance and close handling.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build): `tidal_ext` on a query string, a dot only in the query, a trailing dot and an extension too long for the buffer; `trim`'s eviction at 2 GB, 4 GB and 512 MB caps and a failed `unlink`.

## Tidal track lists

Stock keeps a Tidal track list in `stTidalTracks` (16 bytes: total, limit, offset, the record deque). `init_infodata` (`0x4d9888`), run as the album/playlist page (`tidal_albuminfo`) opens, clears the deque and sets limit 50, offset 0. `tidalPlaylistDetailThd` (`0x48b3d8`, `playlists/%s/items?...offset=%d&limit=%d`) and `tidalAlbumDetailThd` (`0x48b59c`, `albums/%u/tracks?offset=%d&limit=%d`) request one page from the struct's offset and limit into `/tmp/tidal_<thread>` (`httpRequest`; the thread's own id) and set `tidal_albuminfo_thread_run` to 2. The page's timer polls that flag, then parses the file (`parseTidalPlaylistDetailJson` `0x48b8b0` or `parseTidalAlbumDetailJson` `0x48c500`, by the pthread id it started), which appends the records and writes back `totalNumberOfItems` and the reply's `limit`, removes it and lists the deque. Neither page ever asks for another page, so a list stopped at 50. My Collection's Tracks (`tidalColTracksThd`, `0x4882d4`, `users/%u/favorites/tracks?...limit=50&offset=%d`, its offset the thread's argument, done flag `tidal_collection_thread_run`) does page, parsed the same way (`parseTidalColTracksJson`, `0x48a300`): the page's scroll handler (`0x4d68a8`) adds 50 to the offset and starts the thread again when a scroll ends near the bottom of a list with pages left. That takes a touch drag to the end; inferred from the issue's report (favourites stop at 50), not traced, the wheel's selection moves don't reach it.

The three threads are hooked (`ringnav_tidal_playlist`, `ringnav_tidal_album`, `ringnav_tidal_favs`, `patch/tidal.c`) and their done stores (`0x48b57c`, `0x48b70c`, `0x488434`, `sb $3,0($2)`) become `nop`s (`TIDAL_PAGE_DONE`). `tidal_pages` runs the stock thread and parses its reply itself with the page's parser, so the deque and total grow per page (without that parse the deque never grew, so 1.4.8's first build stopped at 50), then, while the offset plus the limit is short of the total, adds the limit to the offset (in the struct, and as the argument for favourites) and runs it again; after the last page the hook writes `{"items":[]}` over the reply and sets the done flag, so the page's own parse adds nothing (it returns 1 on an empty `items`) and lists the whole deque once. A first page that fails to parse is left for the page, so its error handling (`subStatus` 11003, a sign-in that ran out) is stock's; a later one ends the run with what loaded. Favourites leave the offset at the last page's, so the scroll handler's check (page `offset/limit + 1` against `ceil(total/limit)`) finds no page left. A run ends early when a page adds no record (a failed request: the parser leaves the deque alone) or the struct's offset is no longer the one it set (`init_infodata` reset it: the page was opened again). ponytail: `TIDAL_MAX_TRACKS` (2,000) caps a list at 40 sequential requests, the page's spinner meanwhile; a page opened again while a request is in flight can still take that page's records, as stock's single request could. Limits: still 50 a request, stock's own size (Tidal's maximum per request isn't known here); favourite albums, artists and playlists and the user's playlist list keep stock's 50 and touch paging.

## Streaming configuration

Streaming's last row, **Configuration** (`stream_config` in navigation.c, `stream_config.png`: `assets/icons/stream_config.png`, a grey disc with a sliders glyph whose strokes stay crisp at the 40px rows (stock's `system_netservice` globe frayed there), with `list_tidal`'s inode metadata, resized on iPod as the other Streaming icons), opens `streamconf_page`, a `page_list` of four rows and then a check row per service. It and Tidal Downloads are listed in `patch/contexts.inc` (`CTX_DYNAMIC`), so the wheel moves the selection and Centre presses the row, as on the other payload lists.

**Cache sizes.** Tidal Cache and Spotify Cache step through 512 MB, 1 GB, 2 GB, 4 GB and 8 GB (`512 << i` MB) on each press. Each is a file, `.tidal/.cache-size` and `.spotify/.cache-size`, holding `<MB>M` (`2048M`), librespot's `--cache-size-limit` form; the folder is made if missing and the file written with `blob_io` (a `.tmp`, synced and renamed). `stream_cache` reads it back with `state_read`; missing, or anything but those five sizes, is 2 GB. Files, not `config.ini`: `trim` reads Tidal's on the download thread, and the card's Spotify launcher reads Spotify's. The dot keeps Tidal's out of `trim`'s count and `cached`'s matches, and Tidal Downloads lists only songs. A smaller Tidal cap applies at the next download's `trim`. The launcher (q2-librespot's `contrib/shanlingq2/launcher.sh`) reads `.cache-size` before each librespot start, falling back to `2G` when it is empty or not digits with at most one trailing `K`, `M` or `G`, so a new Spotify size applies when librespot next starts. Older launchers ignore the file and keep `2G`.

**Erase Spotify Data**, under Spotify Cache, wants two presses in a row, as the App Store's Remove: the first (`st.sc_armed`) makes the row read "Press again to erase the login and cache"; any other row's press or leaving the page (`page_closed`) drops it. The second runs the [App Store](#q2-shelf)'s erase job (`shelf_erase` in netupdate.c, on its one job thread, so it says "Wait for the one in progress" while an install, remove or check runs): `spot_stop`, then `rm -rf .spotify/cache .spotify/.cache-size` and `rmdir .spotify` (gone only when Spotify isn't installed), `sync`, `spot_stop` again, and "Spotify's login and cache erased". The row reads "Erasing…" meanwhile; at the end `stream_erased` reads both rows again, so Spotify Cache shows 2 GB, `.cache-size` being gone. The row is there with or without `.spotify`, as Spotify Cache is.

**Stop Streaming Services** (`sc_click`) runs `spot_stop` (`SPOT_KILL`: the restart loop, librespot and its `aplay`, the state and `SPOT_OUT` files; `sp` cleared, so `spot_poll` idles, `spot_status` is 0 and the keys and `media_owner` are local music's), `radio_stop` (q2video `-r` quit or killed, `rd.active` 0) and, when the queue is a stock stream (`MCL_TYPE` neither 1 nor `0xf0xx`: Tidal) and hciplayer isn't stopped, `player_stop`. A local queue is left as it is; the next local track readies the DAC itself (`player_start`). It toasts "Streaming stopped" and is harmless with nothing running. Without it, a paused librespot stays the active source (`sp.active`) until a local track plays or `SPOT_IDLE_MS` passes, so Play/Pause, the side buttons, Bluetooth controls and the status bar follow Spotify, and a phone that casts again stops local music (`spot_take`). ponytail: AirPlay's receiver keeps running; its switch is on Network service.

**Shown services.** After Stop Streaming Services, a blank row (a bare `list_item_create`, no click, so the wheel passes over it) leaves a gap above one switch row per service `ringnav_stream` lists (`svc`: stock's TIDAL row, Spotify, the card apps in `addon_scan`'s order, Tidal Downloads, Internet Radio, AirPlay), each with Shanling's `switch_on` (shown) or `switch_off` (`list_toggle`, at screen x 302 as the settings rows'). Centre (`svc_toggle`) flips it and saves the hidden ones in `Q2POD STREAMHIDE` in `config.ini`, comma separated: a fixed row's upper-case key (`TIDAL`, `SPOTIFY`, `DOWNLOADS`, `RADIO`, `AIRPLAY`) or a card app's id, which is lower case, so the two never collide and a hidden app stays hidden when another app changes its place. A missing key hides nothing. `ringnav_stream` tags each row it builds with its service (`SVC_PROP`, `ringnav_svc`) and hides the saved ones with `widget_set_visible`; a toggle finds the Streaming page underneath (`scroll_view_streamsset` from `window_manager`) and shows or hides the tagged row there too, then lays the list out again, so Return shows the change. Configuration has no switch and always stays. The comma list is `csv_find`/`csv_toggle`, shared with Spotify's Remove selection; an empty key is never added. ponytail: an uninstalled app's id stays in the key (the app is hidden again if reinstalled); once the value would pass `ROOT_MAX` (200 bytes, far beyond the five fixed keys and eight app ids; not the 260-byte buffer, as stock's config line is a 257-byte `sprintf` and a 255-byte `fgets`), a toggle that hides refuses with "Could not save setting".

Checks in `test/patch.py`: the row and its icon, the page's labels, each cache row's step, wrap and file, `stream_cache` on a size not in the list, every payload list page on the wheel's allowlist, Erase's two presses (dropped by another row and by leaving, refused while it runs, the erase and Spotify Cache back to 2 GB), and the stop row's kill, `player_stop` for a stream queue, its toast, and radio and Spotify no longer owning the keys; the service rows' ticks, `STREAMHIDE` and the Streaming rows they hide, at once and on the next open.

## Album artists

Stock has two artist settings. `artist_type` (`0xa38acc`, `PLAYSET ARTISTTYPE`, read by `read_artistype_value`) is the artist page's own switch (`btn_artistswitch`), which picks its albums and songs by album artist (`getAlbumByAlbumArtist`, `getMusicByAlbumAndAlbumSonger`) or by artist. `g_artist_type` (`0xa38ae4`) picks `GetAllAlbumArtist` for the Artists list and the album-artist queries for an artist's albums in `load_localclass_list`, but nothing in stock writes it, so Artists always grouped by the Artist tag and a guest artist split an album. `ringnav_localclass` copies `artist_type` into `g_artist_type` before every list load, so Artists, an artist's albums, the artist page and the [Queue menu](#queue-menu) follow one setting. `GetAllAlbumArtist`'s row callback (`0x4fbc28`) fills the name at `+0x18` as `getAllArtist`'s does, and also at `+0x24`, so the rows show and expand the same way. **Playback → Artists** (`ringnav_audioset`, after stock's 15 rows in `scroll_view_playset`) toggles `artist_type` and writes `PLAYSET ARTISTTYPE`, as the artist page's switch does; the lists take it on their next load. Album Artist is the default: stock's read, `read_artistype_value` (`0x4f4724`, `atoi` of the key with `"0"` as its default), is replaced by `ringnav_read_artistype`, which reads a missing key as 1. Stock calls it only from `localmusic_localclass_page_init` and `localmusic_artistinfo_page_init`, so until a Library list or artist page opened, `artist_type` stayed 0 for Playback' row, boot resume (`memeory_startplayer`) and an artist page opened from Coverflow; `ringnav_bootmode` (`config_init`'s play-mode call, before Home restores the queue) reads it once at boot as well. Stock's `config.ini` template has no `ARTISTTYPE`, and only the two toggles write it, so a saved Artist (0) stays Artist. Stock's album-artist queries (`GetAllAlbumArtist`'s `group by albumsonger`, an artist's albums' and songs' `albumsonger like '%s'` and `albumsonger=""`, search) only match the tag, so in Album Artist mode an album without one was missing from its artist and listed under Unknown Artist. `ringnav_library_query` (the `toolsQueryDbTable` hook) rewrites every `albumsonger` in those queries to `ifnull(nullif(albumsonger,''),songer)`, as `ringnav_content_query` does on `toolsQueryContentDbTable` (`0x5c863c`, the same with a row argument and the name sort on the stack), which an artist's albums (`getAlbumByAlbumArtist`) run through, so such a song goes by its artist on both tabs; Unknown Artist keeps only songs with neither tag, its albums as its songs. `select *` records keep the real tag at `+0x24`, and Coverflow's own grouping (`ALBUM_SQL`, recognised by its `ifnull(albumsonger`) is left as is, so an untagged compilation stays one album there. Stock's libtag reads a Vorbis album artist only as `ALBUMARTIST`, so FLACs tagged `ALBUM ARTIST` (foobar2000's spelling) had an empty `albumsonger` and listed under Unknown Artist. `toolsGetMusicInfo` (`0x5c5f40`, called by the library scan, `player_get_id3info` and `get_songid3_info`) is hooked: when stock leaves its album artist (`info + 0x580`, `MUSIC_INFO_ALBUM_ARTIST`) empty, `ringnav_music_info` reads a `.flac`'s VORBIS_COMMENT block itself, seeking past the other metadata blocks, and copies `ALBUM ARTIST`, at most 255 bytes. Ogg Vorbis and Opus are not read for it. Before stock's read, `crash_note` writes the path to `/tmp/q2-scan-file` and, every 500th read, appends the count, demo's VmRSS (`/proc/self/statm`) and the path to `/tmp/q2-scan.log` (each demo starts it afresh, stopped past 64 KB), which a crash log includes ([boot.md](boot.md)), so an OOM kill during Update Local Music names its file and shows whether demo grew.

<a id="oversized-ogg-tags"></a>**Oversized Ogg tags.** TagLib reads an Ogg file's comment header (packet 1) whole: `Ogg::File::packet` joins its pages into one buffer grown by doubling, and `XiphComment::parse` decodes each `METADATA_BLOCK_PICTURE` from base64 beside it, about 4 times the header. MusicBee converting a FLAC to Opus keeps its pictures there, and a 20 MB header got demo OOM-killed (92 MB resident) in Update Local Music. Before stock's read, `ringnav_music_info` measures the header of a `.opus`, `.ogg` or `.oga` from its page headers alone (`ogg_tags`, `patch/xiph.c`: lacing values, seeking past the data, stopped at EOF, a page without `OggS` and version 0, or `OGG_PAGES_MAX` pages). Up to `OGG_TAGS_MAX` (4 MB: a 3 MB cover's 4,000,000 base64 bytes stay under it, about 16 MB at TagLib's peak) stock reads the file as before. Over it, stock's TagLib is not called: `xiph_parse` streams the comments, reading at most `XIPH_VALUE` (1 KB) of each and seeking past the rest, the pictures included, and fills `toolsGetMusicInfo`'s fields as TagLib 2.0.1 and Shanling's getters would: title (`+0x280`), artist (`+0x100`), album (`+0`), genre (`+0x200`, through `ringnav_genre_copy`), composer (`+0x480`) and `ALBUMARTIST` (`+0x580`), each key's values joined with `" / "`, through stock's `toolsFixId3ErrorCode` with libtag_c's Latin-1 copy and `isLatin1` test (`utf8_latin1`); the year (`+0x680`, `DATE`, else `YEAR`), Shanling's packed track, disc (`DISC`, else `DISCNUMBER`, else 1000) at `+0x684` and track (`TRACKNUM`, else `TRACKNUMBER`) at `+0x688`; `MQAENCODER`'s flag (`0x100` in `+0x68c`); and, when audio properties are asked for, the sample rate at `+0x690` (Opus 48000, Vorbis its identification header's) and 16 bits in `+0x68c`. A key matches case aside, a value ends at its first zero byte, and an empty or invalid UTF-8 value is dropped, as TagLib's `addField` and `String` do. A header its pages cut short, or Opus without `OpusHead`, writes nothing, as TagLib's invalid file. The FLAC fallback above reads `ALBUM ARTIST` with the same `xiph_parse` in its raw mode, which keeps the earlier reader's result exactly (the first value with a byte in it, its first 255 bytes as they are, UTF-8 or not, no count check), since albums are grouped by it; the folder tag refresh, lyrics from tags and Long Songs skip TagLib for these files too. Limits: a joined text field keeps 511 bytes, so the GBK test sees only those; UTF-8 is checked in a value's first 1 KB; numbers go through `strtol`, not `wcstol`; the codec is told by the header's magic, not by the extension as `FileRef` does; Ogg FLAC and Speex over the cap still go to TagLib; Long Songs gets no length for such a file and lyrics from tags none. Embedded art goes through TagLib too: `toolsGetAlbumCover` (`0x5c4444`) calls `taglib_album_picture` (`libtag_c` `0x12508`, its only caller), which gives an `.ogg` to `get_oggfile_albumpic`, `XiphComment::pictureList` after the same whole parse; `.opus` and `.oga` have no picture reader there. Its four callers (`player_parsecover_thd`'s Now Playing art at `0x512fe0` and the three `GRID_COVER` sites) and Coverflow's `build_art` all reach it, so its entry is hooked: `ringnav_album_cover` (`patch/coverflow.c`) returns no art for an `.ogg` whose header `ogg_tags` measures over `OGG_TAGS_MAX`, without calling stock, and each caller's fallback hands the file to ffmpeg (`ff_thumb`), whose child the kernel kills first (`oom_score_adj` 1000): Now Playing's through `coverflow_player_cover` (folder images first, then the embedded picture), the grids' through `grid_cover`, Coverflow's after `beside_art`. Any other `.ogg` goes to stock as before, so stock's peak for one is still about 4 times a header of up to 4 MB.

**Artists rows.** Library Artists, Composers and Genres share one row template (built at `0x4b0efc`, bound at `0x4b0bc0`); each bind calls `0x4afc14` (`class_icon`), which sets `img_icon` to `default_artist_small` for Artists, a genre icon for Genres, or a `.downpic` picture. That call (`CLASS_ICON`, `0x4b0cec`) is a `jal` to `ringnav_class_icon`, which hides `img_icon` on Artists so the name takes its width (iPod's `compact_row_layout` skips hidden siblings; Stock keeps its 200 px label) and shows it again, through stock's setter, on the others.

**Songs rows.** The Songs template's bind (`0x4a90e4`) copies `REC_ARTIST` into `scrlabel_artist`, trimmed; an empty one gets stock's "Unknown Artist" through `getFormatString`. That call (`SONG_UNKNOWN_CALL`, `0x4a9320`) is a `jal` to `ringnav_song_unknown`, which leaves the buffer empty under a set without text-row artwork, so a two-line row with no artist tag has an empty second line, and a record that holds "Unknown" reads so. Every other set calls stock's.

**Row faces.** Stock's long lists are virtual tables (`table_client`: Artists, Composers, Genres, Songs, Albums and the rest), a pool of rows bound to their entries as the list scrolls. Its loader (`0x5c9efc`, which `table_client_reload`, `table_client_set_yoffset` and `table_client_ensure_children` all reach) sets each row's index, shows and moves it, and calls the page's `on_load_data` (`0x5ca0fc`), which writes the row's text. No relayout follows, so `compact_row_layout`'s `theme_font` never sees the new text: under Classic a row rebound from a Latin name to a CJK one kept `classic-light`, which has no CJK, and drew blank, and one rebound the other way kept stock's face. That call (`TABLE_LOAD_CALL`) is a `jal` to `ringnav_table_load`, which calls `on_load_data` (from the client's `TABLE_LOAD_DATA`) and then `row_faces`: each label in the row that `theme_font` gave a face (its `_font`, which keeps the role above the answer) is fitted again to its text, at its role's size. A label never themed is left alone, so Minimal's and Modern's rows, which take no face, bind as stock's do.

## Charge Limit

The charger is the AXP2101 PMIC's integrated one (the device tree's `x-powers,axp2101-power-supply`, driven by `drivers/power/supply/axp2101_charger.c`, which registers `battery` and `axp2101_charger`; there is no bq25890 driver in the kernel or rootfs), set to 1000 mA of charge current, the 2000 mA USB input limit (`pmu_usbpc_cur`), a 4.35 V target (`pmu_init_chgvol` 0x10fe) and 100 mA of termination (`pmu_iterm_limit` 0x64). The fuel gauge is the CW2015 on I2C-0 (`/sys/devices/i2c-0/0-0062/cw2015_capacity`, `module_driver/cw2015.sh`). `toolsGetChargeStatus` (`0x5bca7c`) reads `/sys/class/power_supply/battery/status` (1 Charging, 2 Full, -1 Discharging or anything else, 0 unreadable); the `bq25890_1_status` paths it probes and the `echo %d > .../bq25890_1_otg_enable` helper are for the other Shanling players that share `demo`. `switch_charge_enable(on)` (`0x4f3cfc`) sets the active-low charge enable on GPIO `PE22` through `toolsGpioSetValue` (libgpio, no shell); stock uses it only for USB mode's and AirPlay's charge choice (`g_usbdac_chargeflag`; `usbmode_page_init`, `usbmode_exit`, `airplay_page_init`). With it high, charging is off and the player runs from USB while the battery holds. `get_battery_capacity` (`0x4f4afc`) keeps `g_power_capacity` (0-100) and `g_power_chargestate` (a negative status is not stored).

**System settings → Power → Charge Limit** (`ringnav_powermanager`, after stock's rows in `scroll_view_powermanager`; `Q2POD CHARGELIMIT` in `config.ini`). Every `CHARGE_POLL_MS` (5 s) `charge_poll`, from `ringnav_sleep`, stops charging at `CHARGE_STOP` (80%), stops it again if anything turned it back on meanwhile (USB mode's exit, AirPlay) while the charger reports charging, and hands it back at `CHARGE_RESUME` (75%) or when the limit is turned off: on, or `g_usbdac_chargeflag` while `usbmode_page` or `airplay_page` is open. ponytail: only while demo runs; a powered-off Q2 charges as stock.

## Smart Wi-Fi

**System settings → Power → Smart Wi-Fi** (`Q2POD SMARTWIFI`, off by default; the row after Charge Limit). With it on and the user's Wi-Fi switch on, `smart_poll` (from `power_poll`, every `SMART_POLL_MS`, 1 s) turns the radio off once nothing has needed the network for `SMART_IDLE_MS` (5 min), and back on as soon as something does. An associated radio with nothing to do still keeps its SDIO bus busy (about 90 interrupts a second at a few packets a minute, measured with local FLAC and the screen off, before 1.3.0's power save), so local listening pays for a link it never uses.

Both directions go through stock's own switch, `wifi_switch` (`0x50e508`), as Quick Settings and the Wi-Fi page call it (`wifi_switch(1)`, a toggle of `g_wififlag`). Off removes `/tmp/wpasupplicant_initsuccess`, stops SyncLink's server (`stop_wifisynclink`, which needs `g_wififlag` still set), clears the switch's flags and `wifi_name`, runs `networkStop` and sets `wifilink_timeout` to 30; stock's `network_checkstatus` then sends `rtwpriv wlan0 pm_set ips=1`, as after the user's switch. On runs `networkStart(1)`, the background `wifi_connect.sh &`. The switch's last act, both branches', is its tail call `write_int_config(g_wififlag, "SYSSET", "WIFI")`; the `write_int_config` hook (`ringnav_write_config`, `smart_config`) drops that write for Smart Wi-Fi's own toggles and passes any other on, so `config.ini`, a reboot and the user's own switch stay stock's. A `SYSSET WIFI` write it passes is the user's (Quick Settings, the Wi-Fi page): Smart Wi-Fi forgets it had the radio off and counts `SMART_IDLE_MS` from the tap. With the row off, or the user's Wi-Fi off, a radio Smart Wi-Fi had off comes back, unsaved, and nothing more happens. Rockbox's own Wi-Fi stop ([Rockbox from Home](boot.md#rockbox-from-home)) is unaffected: demo restarts and reads `WIFI` from `config.ini`, which Smart Wi-Fi never wrote.

`smart_ready` keeps to stock's own conditions for its switch: `networkStart` and `networkStop` act only when `initNetworkModule`'s type (`NET_TYPE`, `0xa3bf04`, no symbol; `NET_TYPE_READS` in `tools/build.py` pins both reads) is 2, Wi-Fi; the first scan taken (`wifi_initstatus` 0) and no connect half done (`wifi_connectstatus` not 1), as Quick Settings and the Wi-Fi page require; and never in test mode, while powering off or in the first-boot guide, where `on_wm_timer_networkstatus` also leaves the network alone.

Needing the network (`smart_needed`): a USB cable in; AirPlay or DLNA on (Network service), SyncLink over Wi-Fi (`g_synclinkflag` 2) or a SyncLink session, Wi-Fi transfer; a stock stream queue of the types `on_wm_timer_networkstatus` (`0x4eabd4`) stops once the link drops (`mclGetPlayType` 2, 3, 0xf1 and 0xf2: Tidal's), playing, paused or stopped, as stock would otherwise stop a paused one and lose its place; Spotify's librespot running (`spot_running`); an Internet Radio station; a card app started (`addon_running`: launched, or restarting after Retry, until it stops by itself, Stop Streaming Services or Rockbox; one stopped for USB storage counts once it starts again, as the cable holds the radio meanwhile); a scrobble upload, the user's or the automatic one (`scrobble_busy`); Network Update's download or an Apps job (`netupdate_busy`); Download Album Lyrics (`album_lyrics_busy`); a Tidal download (`tidal_busy`); an action held for the link (below); a time sync pending on a link (`g_syncnettime_flag`, cleared after one attempt); and a network page open anywhere in the window stack (`net_page_open`: Streaming and its services' pages but Tidal Downloads, a card app's page and its QR code, System settings' Apps, AirPlay's, Network service, SyncLink, the Wi-Fi page and its dialogs, Wi-Fi transfer and the online lyrics and cover search). A new link counts as a use, so a fresh connection gets `SMART_IDLE_MS` before Smart Wi-Fi acts, time sync first. A radio that never associates (out of range, scanning) goes off after `SMART_IDLE_MS` too.

Waking. Streaming's page (`ringnav_stream`) and the Wi-Fi page (`systemset_wifi_page_init`, hooked as `ringnav_wifi_page`, before it builds its switch from `g_wififlag`) bring the radio back at once. Users that check the link once when they start would fail during the 3 to 8 s reconnect, so they wait for it through `smart_wifi_wait`, up to `SMART_CONNECT_MS` (15 s), then go on as they would have. The link is up once both the status bar's bars (`get_wifisignal`, which the payload's own actions check) and the network monitor thread's connect status (`networkGetConnectStatus`, which stock's check) say so: each comes from its own poll, about a second apart. Internet Radio's directory fetch and a station's start wait that way ("Connecting to Wi-Fi…", "Connecting…"; a station started from the side buttons too). An automatic scrobble upload due while Smart Wi-Fi has the radio off reads `[SCROBBLE] AUTO` from the card, wakes the radio if it is on, and goes at the next minute's check; a wake that brought no link, or `AUTO` off, waits `SA_RETRY_MIN` as a failed upload does, so an unreachable network is tried every half hour, not held on. A press that needs the link is held (`smart_hold`; Network Update, the Apps rows, Download Album Lyrics and Upload Scrobbles through `need_link`, which says "Connect to Wi-Fi first" when no link is coming): it toasts "Connecting to Wi-Fi…", and a 500 ms timer runs the action once the link is up, or `SMART_CONNECT_MS` on (to fail as it would have), if the page it was pressed on is still the top window past stock's self-closing overlays (toasts, `msginfo_dialog`, and the volume, `volume_dialog`, which a wheel turn opens over a toast) once no page is sliding; otherwise it is dropped. One waits at a time: a press while one waits takes its place, so the action runs once, and it runs unheld (`sw.held`), so a radio that could not come back fails it as before rather than holding it again. The payload's are Spotify's row (librespot never retries a Connect login or an mDNS registration that failed at its start), a card app's row, Upload Scrobbles, Network Update, the Apps page's rows and its opening check, and Download Album Lyrics. Three stock actions that check the link once and show `msg_notnetwork` without it are held the same way, each hooked at its entry (none has a symbol): Streaming's Tidal row (`0x52ee8c`, the row click `stream_page_init` binds, on Streaming's page and in Home's Split Streaming list, whose Library list beside it names its rows by index too, so the row is found again in `scroll_view_streamsset`), Network service's Wi-Fi transfer (`0x4c6728`, row 2 only: DLNA and AirPlay switch at once, and stock starts their services once there is a link) and Now Playing More's Download Cover & Lyrics (`0x5281f8`, given More's window; More is no network page, so only this press wakes the radio). None reads its event, and the two rows are found again by name (stock names each row's list_item and button by its index and reads only that name), so nothing pressed is kept but the page. Quick Settings shows Off while Smart Wi-Fi has the radio off, as it is; a tap turns it on and saves it, as stock's does. The Wi-Fi page wakes it first, so its switch shows the user's setting (Off only when `smart_ready` refuses the wake).

`test/patch.py` runs stock's `wifi_switch` from the payload's toggles against the user's tap (the same calls in the same order, without the write), stock's `network_checkstatus` after it, the row, each keep-alive case, the Wi-Fi page, the automatic upload's wake, the held stock actions (stock's own code for the Tidal row and Wi-Fi transfer, and the Tidal row on Home), a press that joins the wait for Upload Scrobbles, Network Update, an Apps install and Download Album Lyrics, Spotify's and Internet Radio's waits. ponytail: on Home the held Tidal row runs while Home is still the page in use, as Spotify's row does, even if the Streaming list has given way to another of Home's lists. ponytail: `network_checkstatus` sends `ips=1` only once its latch (`0xa39bc9`) is set, after a connect, so a radio that never associated is left in whatever power state the driver chose. While stock refuses its switch (`smart_ready`), a wait that outlives `SMART_CONNECT_MS` can start one more.

## Watchdog

The X2000's hardware watchdog (`module_driver/soc_watchdog.ko`, loaded at boot; `/dev/jz_watchdog`, libhardware2's `watchdog_start`, `watchdog_feed` and `watchdog_stop`) is never started by stock, so a demo whose UI loop stalls, or a stalled kernel, stays dark with every button dead until a 15 s Centre hold cuts the power: stock's `checkappprocess.sh` only reboots once demo is _gone_, and a hung demo still answers its `pgrep`. `wdt_poll`, from `power_poll` on every UI loop pass, opens the device on the first pass and starts it counting `WDT_MS` (60 s, `patch/offsets.inc`; ioctl `0x8004570d`, an `int` of milliseconds by pointer), then feeds it (`0x2000570f`) every pass, so a stall that long reboots the Q2. The driver counts on the RTC clock, through a suspend, so `ringnav_standby` stops it (`0x2000570e`) before stock's suspend and starts it again after. The driver's `close` is a no-op, so a demo that ends would leave it running: `ringnav_clean_end` stops it on a clean end (`into_poweroff`, through the hooked `save_memoryplay_info`; `device_reboot` and the firmware update dialog, through `reboot_save` in `patch/playback.c`; `rockbox_open`), and nothing is fed or recorded after. No device, or a refused start, leaves stock's behaviour. The driver's prescaler caps a start at about 23 minutes. ponytail: the UI loop is the only feeder, so a stalled worker thread is not caught; a legitimate pass longer than `WDT_MS` would reboot. The known long ones, on the UI thread, are `importPlayList` reading every song's tags (stock's Import Playlists row and card apps' imports, through `addon_m3u_dir`) and a folder add loading each directory (`qm_tracks`): `ringnav_wdt_long` restarts the count at `WDT_LONG_MS` (15 min) as they begin, and the next pass, after they return, at `WDT_MS` again. Another long pass found later needs the same call. A hang inside Standby's suspend or resume is not caught: the watchdog is stopped across it.

**State record.** `state_poll` (the same pass) keeps `/mnt/data/q2-state`: one line, `screen:on|off power:running|suspending cable:in|out battery:N play:stopped|playing|paused out:jack|bluetooth|usb`; rewritten (flushed and `fsync`ed) only when it changes, and once more, as `power:suspending`, just before Standby's suspend. A clean end removes it. One still there at boot means the last session ended in a forced restart (or the battery ran out), and `S90play` puts it in `q2-crash.log` ([boot.md](boot.md)) before that is copied to the card, so a report of a dead Q2 says what it was doing. A Coverflow open that never finished leaves `/mnt/data/q2-cf-trace` ([Build](#coverflow)), which goes in after it as `== cover flow`; boot removes that file either way. On a `--dev` build, `WHEEL_LOG_HOOK` appends `/mnt/data/q2-wheel.log` (the wheel's stale-press notes, `stale_note`, [Hooks](#hooks)) to the card's `q2-wheel.log`, kept apart from the crash record so a note never replaces one.

**Stall record.** After the watchdog's reset, `q2-crash.log` says only that demo did not end, which reads the same as a stalled kernel, a 15 s hold or a flat battery. So once `wdt_poll` has started the watchdog, it starts a thread (`stall_watch`) that wakes every `STALL_POLL_MS` (5 s, `patch/offsets.inc`). `wdt_poll` counts the passes; when the count hasn't moved for `STALL_MS` (20 s), and again at `STALL_AGAIN_MS` (45 s), the thread writes `/mnt/data/q2-stall` and `fsync`s it (`stall_write`; the second appends). It counts from its first wake after the last pass, so up to 5 s short. Each record has these lines:

- `age N ms`, since the pass.
- `toasts N`: `toast()`'s dialogs open, nested ones counted (`st.toasts`).
- The UI loop's thread, which is demo's main thread, so `/proc/self`'s: `state` (the letter after the last `)` of `stat`), `wchan`, and `syscall` (the number and arguments, then `sp` and `pc`; `-1 sp pc` outside a call; `running` on the CPU, with no registers).
- `MemAvailable` from `/proc/meminfo`, and `pgmajfault` and `workingset_refault_file` from `/proc/vmstat`.
- When `syscall` gave an `sp`, the stack words from there that could be code addresses, of the 160 up to the main stack's top (`stat`'s `startstack`), as a `q2remote --prof` line (`tid state pc ra words`, `ra` 0). `tools/q2remote.py prof` names only the samples it takes live, so a saved line's words are looked up by hand: `llvm-nm` on that build's `patch.elf` and `demo` names those in demo (linked at `0x400000`). They are read in place: from a blocked thread's `sp` to that top is its own stack, mapped, and an `sp` 8 MB or more below it is not the main stack's (`mmap`'s area starts at least 128 MB down), so it is left alone. demo imports no `lseek64` for `/proc/self/mem`.
