A live level (`refresh`) never pages, its 2 s refresh asking for its first page alone. An action, a held Play/Pause, or Back from a deeper level reloads the level from its first page and pages it again; the selected row (`lv_t.sel`) comes back when the page holding it arrives, unless the user has moved off the trailing row meanwhile; once reached or left it is the selection, so a later page never moves it. A last page with no rows leaves the selection on the last row, not past it. The cap is 500 rows because ringnav navigates at most `MAX_ENTRIES` (512) rows of a `scroll_view` and walks every row per wheel event and paint (as Books and Photos cap at 500); past that, More… asks for the rest as a level of its own. Only the top level keeps a reply (push and Back let the others' go), so the shown reply lives in `pg`: up to ten replies (`pg.buf`, each `realloc`'d to its own size before `spot_reply` cuts it, where the request had 64 KB) and 500 rows of fields (`pg.f`, 12 KB, calloc'd with the page). Progress rows belong in live lists, which never page.

**Sections.** An app's first level can be a row of sections, such as a music app's Home, Albums and Playlists, the way a phone app has tabs. A first-level reply whose message is `sections` makes each of its `list` rows a section: its uri the op that fills it, its title the section's name and its context the op's argument (other kinds and a `list` row with an empty uri are left out, and so is a context too long for a level's arguments; at most `ADDON_SECTIONS`, 8). Sections are taken once per page: a section whose own reply says `sections` shows its rows as an ordinary list, so a confused app can't make the page ask for the first section again and again. `sec_take` keeps them (`pg.sec`, a level each, calloc'd, freed with the page) and `sec_open` puts the first in the first level's place at once, asked for and titled with its name and, when there are two or more, its position (`Albums 2/3`), so the reply with the names is never shown. A sideways swipe on the page opens the next section (to the left) or the previous one (to the right), its list pushed in from that side under Classic as a deeper level's is; there is none past the first or the last. `swipe`, bound on the page's pointer events before its rows see them, works as Pull to search's pull (`pull_event`): a move of `SWIPE_CLAIM_PX` (24 px) sideways and at least twice as far as up or down takes the touch, the rows are sent a pointer abort so the lift clicks nothing, and the moves and the lift go no further; at the lift, `SWIPE_PX` (60 px) sideways and more than up or down changes the section, on the next timer pass (`G_SECTION`), as a row's press does. A move of `SWIPE_CLAIM_PX` up or down before that is the list's scroll, however far it went sideways. Only the first level swipes: a level opened from a section is an ordinary level, and Back returns to the section, asked for again. Leaving and opening the page starts at the first section again. ponytail: the wheel never changes section, on purpose (it moves the selection, as on every list), so the sections past the first are reached by touch alone. A swipe shows no movement until the lift, and a section is asked for again each time it opens (only the shown level keeps a reply), its selected row kept across the swipe; a section paged by `page` rows (**Windows**, below) comes back to its first window.

**Covers.** An app gives a row a cover by leaving a JPEG at `/tmp/q2-<id>.art/<key>.jpg` before it replies: the key is FNV-1a 32 (`hash_bytes` from `FNV_SEED`) over the row's uri, a tab and its context (as the app sent them, before Q2SPOT/1's `%` escapes), written as 8 lowercase hex digits (`art_file`). When `show` draws a level's rows it reads each row's file's header (`art_rows`, `picture_size`, stopping only at a progress row, whose bar belongs to the plain row, with the whole first page counted); if any row has one, the whole list is drawn as stock Tidal's lists are (`art_row`): `ART_ROW_H` (78 px, stock `tidal_*_page.bin`'s `list_view` `default_item_height`) rows, the cover `ART_PX` (64 px) square at the theme's text column, centred in the row's height and scaled to fit (draw type 4, as Cover Flow's), and the title over the subtitle beside it, with the chevron for a row that opens a page and the trailing More… or Loading… row in the same height. A row without a file keeps the cover's room empty, so the titles line up. The app sizes its covers: one over `ART_MAX` (128 px) either way, or whose size can't be read, counts as no cover, as each decoded bitmap stays in demo's memory; one up to 128 px is scaled to fit the 64 px. A row's bitmap is unloaded from the image manager before its image is set, so a file changed since is decoded again. Stock never releases decoded bitmaps, so a list a user scrolled would keep up to 500 x 128 x 128 x 4 (32 MB) until it closed: `art_window`, in `show`, `drop` and the page's 250 ms `tick`, keeps at most `ART_KEEP` (24, about 1.5 MB at `ART_MAX` px) rows' covers decoded around the list's real scroll position (`yoffset` over the row height, so wheel, touch and a fling all count). `[pg.art_lo, pg.art_hi)` names the rows whose bitmaps may be decoded; each tick the range is widened to all a move crossed since the last and the rows now outside the window are unloaded by name (`image_manager_unload_bitmap_by_name`), so the cost is the rows crossing, not the list's length. It is reset wherever the rows are rebuilt (`show`) and when a level's reply is let go (`drop`, unloading only that range while `pg.f` still has the rows, with no dependence on `l->art` and no file read); no row build decodes a cover. A row scrolled back paints by name again: stock's `image` widget paint (`0x614ddc`) keeps only the name (`image_base + 0x78`) and calls `widget_load_image` (`0x65c1ec`) into a `bitmap_t` on its stack each paint, which `image_manager_get_bitmap` (`0x644ccc`, `0x644900`) fills from the cache or by decoding, so a released bitmap leaves nothing dangling (the same as `np_art_poll` and Home's cover, `navigation.c`, `coverflow.c`). ponytail: at most `ART_KEEP` covers plus what a fling paints in one 250 ms tick; a cover scrolled back decodes again on the UI thread. A list with a `progress` row is drawn as before, since its bar belongs to the plain row. The protocol is unchanged: an app that leaves no files, or firmware without the `art` word, shows the plain rows. Not covered: the choice is made when the list is drawn, so in practice the first page decides: pages appended later (`append`) keep it, and Back or an action reloads from the first page, so a later page's covers don't count then either (a list rebuilt whole with its pages there, a short first page or the search box closing, counts them); and a cover written after the reply counts only once the list is drawn again, which a live level does only when its rows change, though a row already drawn reads its file again whenever it scrolls back into the kept rows, so an app that deletes the file gets a blank there, the size being checked only when the row is built; and a theme with separators between two-line rows draws none between covered rows, whose height isn't its two-line rows' (`paint_separators`). Checks in `test/patch.py`: no file, another row's key and a progress row leave the list as it was; a cover's row, a row without one and a chevron's, in 78 px rows; and later pages joining as 78 px rows; `test/fw/test_addon.py` checks the key (a `%` in a uri too), the 128 px limit, the choice, and the bitmaps let go.

**Windows.** An app can keep a long list to a window of it at a time instead (the `window` word, below): a card app such as Jellyfin sends a window of a few `pages` replies, the last ending with a **Next page** row instead of a cursor, and a later window's first reply starting with **Previous page**. A row of kind `page` has a cursor of the level's own list as its context (empty: the list's start, no argument sent); a press asks for the list from it (`page_to`: the list's own arguments up to `base` and that cursor) **in the level's place**, not as a level over it, so Back leaves the list instead of stepping back through its windows, and the firmware holds at most one window, which Back from a deeper level asks for alone. uri `prev` slides the window in from the left and selects its last row once its pages are in; any other uri (the app sends `next`) slides it in from the right and selects row 1, the row after its Previous page (past a Search and a Sort row kept as rows, under a theme without a title bar). A page row is a plain list row with an empty subtitle: `row`'s usual path and a list's row height (`row_h`), so in a list drawn with covers it is 78 px with the cover's room empty, as the trailing row. Without the word an app pages as before (up to 500 rows, then More…).

**Capabilities.** Every Streaming scan writes `/tmp/q2-addon.caps` (`ADDON_CAPS`), so a stale or half-written file never outlives the next scan: one line of words, the card-app features this firmware has, as a set (an app tests for a word, and order means nothing). Firmware without a feature lacks its word. `/tmp` is empty after every reboot and firmware update, so the file always describes the running firmware. Older firmware never writes it, so an app that finds no file knows it is on older firmware and falls back.

Words: `playlist` (Stream mode plays an `.m3u`), `progress` (the progress row), `pages` (full lists), `window` (`page` rows, **Windows**, above), `library` (**Library update**, below), `sections` (**Sections**, above), `art` (**Covers**, above), `urls` (**Stream mode: URL queues**, below), `sort` (the `sort` row, **Sort in the title bar**, above).

**Stop and Retry.** A page that can't show the app's rows ends in **Stop <name>** whenever the app may be running (launched, a restart pending, or its loop's pid file there): an error page under its Retry, and a level loading or starting for `SLOW_MS` (3 s; a frozen app holds a request up to `ADDON_WAIT_MS`, 15 s). It ends the app as Stop Streaming Services does, a TERM to its loop (`app_stop`), so a frozen app, which can't answer a stop op of its own, stops too; then it toasts and leaves the page. Retry on "<name> isn't running" while the loop's pid file is there (the app running, its socket gone) restarts it: the loop is asked to end, the page waits as for a start (a pending `restart` counts as starting), and `poll_one` starts the app again once the pid file is gone, or after `RESTART_MS` (20 s) regardless; q2-soulseek's launcher kills an app still there 15 s after its TERM. Otherwise Retry asks again, starting the app first if it isn't running.

**Stream mode.** An app that plays as well (q2-deezer's Stream mode) writes `/tmp/q2-<id>.play`, "<seq> <folder>": a hidden card folder (under `/mnt/mmc/.`, no `..` segment) it fills with a list's songs named in play order ("01 - Title [id].flac"), each renamed into place once whole. `stream_poll`, every `ADDON_POLL_MS` while the app runs, follows it: a new one is played from its first song once one is there (a 0 ms timer: the folder's songs by name, `app_album_songs`, through `play_folder` as a local album, Now Playing opening); while the queue playing is still the folder's (its first song's path in it), songs named after the last one queued join its end through `playback_queue_insert`, as Add to Queue; and the path of the one playing goes to `/tmp/q2-<id>.playing` (`blob_io`), from which the app fetches a couple ahead and deletes the ones long played. Once another queue plays, the session is left; a new seq starts again. A session is one app's (`sm.app`): each app's `.play` is kept hashed in its slot (`app_t.play`), and only a change to an app's own starts one, so two apps' files left in `/tmp` (q2-deezer and q2-subsonic keep theirs while they run) never take the queue from each other at every poll. The folder is listed only when its mtime changes (`stream_newer`, `sm.seen`), not every poll; an mtime within a second of now is scanned again on the next poll, as a file could land later in that second. A song that is added and then rejected (no `stSongInfo` for it yet) is picked up only at the folder's next change. Songs are queued by name after the last one queued, so a song that finishes downloading before an earlier one is never queued. The library scan skips hidden folders (`SCAN_DIRS`: the folder walk's `"."` test is `ringnav_scan_dir`, which skips any name starting with a dot, as Folder view's list does), so streamed songs never join the Library.

**Stream mode: a playlist.** `.play` may name a playlist file instead, "<seq> <path>.m3u" (`.m3u` in any case): it must be on the card: any path under `/mnt/mmc/` without a `..` segment, a hidden folder or not. A `.play` path of 160 bytes or more (`sm.dir` holds 159), or a `.play` of 190 bytes or more (`raw` holds that much, so its path may be cut), is refused in either mode, as cut it would name another file or folder. q2-subsonic writes one per queue, `/mnt/mmc/.subsonic/stream/queue-<seq>.m3u`. It is UTF-8 with one absolute card path a line; `#` lines (`#EXTM3U`, `#EXTINF`) and blank lines are ignored, and a leading BOM and CRLF line ends are taken. Its songs are queued in its order, wherever on the card they are (synced ones in their album folders, streamed ones in the app's folder). `m3u_songs` reads the file whole (up to `M3U_MAX`, 256 KB; of a longer file, it reads one byte more and drops the last line whose newline it didn't read, so a cut line is never taken as a path or waited for) and goes on from the entries it has already passed (`sm.next`); an entry not there yet (`access`) is waited for (`sm.want`), and nothing after it is queued before it, so order holds while streamed songs land out of order. A line off the card (not under `/mnt/mmc/`, or with a `..` segment: `/../`, a trailing `/..`; a name with an ellipsis, "Hello... (Live).flac", is fine, `dotdot`) is passed over, as is a file there that its folder's listing (`app_album_songs`, `REC_TYPE` 8) doesn't take as a song; neither is waited for. Nor is a line of 512 bytes or more (`sm.want`, `sm.first` and `sm.playing` hold 511), so a path the buffers would cut is never waited on forever. The app changes the file only by writing it aside and renaming it into place. Entries are counted, not compared: the ones already passed (`sm.next`) are never read again, so the app keeps every entry before the one waited for as it was (appending is always safe), and may rewrite the rest. An entry the firmware waits for (a card path under 512 bytes, its file not there) is never passed while it is missing, so from the first such entry on, the app may drop or replace a song that won't come (a download that failed, a synced file deleted since), and the songs after it then play. To change an entry before it, the app writes a new seq. At most `M3U_BATCH` (32) folders are listed a poll (a song's folder, `app_album_songs`, unless the song before it was in the same one), so a long list's start doesn't stall the UI, however many albums it spans; the rest follow on the next polls. The first batch plays as a folder's first song does; later ones join the queue's end through `playback_queue_insert`. The session is identified by its first song's path (`sm.first`) and by its queue's load: `playback_loads` counts `mclLoadPlayList` calls (`ringnav_load`); `stream_poll` notes it as the session starts, and `stream_start` again after its `play_folder`, whose load is synchronous. Once the queue's first song is another, or any queue has been loaded since (a Library or Folder play of the same album is the user's, even though it starts with the same file), the session is left, before its first song too (`S_WAIT`, and `stream_start` checks again): a song that lands later never replaces a queue the user started meanwhile. A stock reload of the same queue ends it too: it errs toward leaving the user's playback alone. Folder mode keeps its own test (the queue's first song in its folder) and no load test: a hidden folder can't be played from Library or Folder view, so the test would gain it nothing, and q2-deezer's sessions end as they always did. The file is read again only when its mtime changes, the song waited for lands, or a batch filled (`m3u_ready`). Folder mode wakes for any new name that isn't hidden or a `.part` or `.tmp`, so `.m4a`, `.opus`, `.ogg`, `.wav`, `.ape` and `.dsf` stream too; whether a file is a song is stock's listing's call (`ringnav_audio_extension` over stock's format list). In either mode no folder is listed while a library scan runs (`library_scanning`, coverflow.c: its thread started and not done), the scan dialog's own loop included, as a listing (`toolsLoadDirectory`) refills `tools_pdeq_directory`, which the scan's thread fills too; nor while its window (the user's Update Local Music page, `updatemusic_page`, or its dialog, `updatemusic_dialog`, which a library request opens without the page) is still open after it, as `play_folder` would open Now Playing over it (`scanning`): nothing is armed or queued until both are over, a `stream_start` that finds one running clears `sm.seen` so the next poll after it looks again, and `.playing` still follows the song playing.

**Stream mode: URL queues.** An app that streams (a Jellyfin app, say) can have stock's Tidal player play its songs instead of saving them to the card. It writes `/tmp/q2-<id>.urls` whole (temp + rename) and names it in `.play` as `<seq> /tmp/q2-<id>.urls`; only the app's own file there is taken (`sm.urls`). The file is `#Q2URLS/1<TAB>start=<index>`, then one line a song, `<url or card path><TAB><title><TAB><artist><TAB><album><TAB><cover url><TAB><seconds>` (no field holds a tab or a line break; a field missing is empty; a line with no URL is passed over, and so is one whose first field is neither a web URL, `http://` or `https://`, nor a card path, under `/mnt/mmc/` with no `..` segment, the rule a Stream mode playlist's lines pass: a server's string reaches stock's player as nothing else; a cover is fetched by stock's cover thread with curl, so one that isn't a web URL is dropped; `seconds` is optional and not read yet), read up to `AU_MAX` (256 KB, a last line cut there dropped) and kept up to `AU_SONGS` (5,000 songs, the rest of the file passed over; `start=` counts the songs kept, so a skipped line doesn't move it). A line whose URL or card path is `AU_PATH` (1,024) bytes or more, each `}` counted as five, is passed over: stock's `mcl_tidalStartPlayer` and `mclStartPlayer` both `strcpy` it into a `0x600`-byte stack buffer (`0x5ad2a0`, `0x5ad7d0`), then expand every `}` to `%0x7d` in place, unbounded (`toolsReplaceString`). `stream_poll` starts it on a 0 ms timer, as a folder's first song (`urls_start`, not during a scan or its dialog), and the session ends there: the queue is stock's. `app_urls_play` (`patch/tidal.c`) builds stock Tidal records as `load_titaltracks_ex` (`0x48596c`) does: an 88-byte `stSongInfo` zeroed, `REC_TIDAL_ID` `app:<gen>:<n>` (the queue's generation and the song's place), `REC_NAME` and `REC_TITLE` the title (`Unknown` without one, since stock prints them with `%s`), `REC_ARTIST`, `REC_ALBUM` and `REC_COVER` when given, `REC_PATH` empty for a URL or the card path, `REC_TYPE` 8 and `+0x38` `0xf1`, each `malloc`'d. `_deque_push_back` copies the record and `toolsFreeStSongInfo` frees the original, as stock does. It plays them as a Tidal page does, `play_folder(q, start, 0xf1)` (Now Playing with `{q, start, 0xf1, 2}`), and keeps the URLs (`au`) by generation.

For a `0xf1` queue `mclStartPlayer` calls `get_tidal_trackurl` (`0x483188`, hooked as `ringnav_tidal_track`), which for an `app:` record (the queue's record at `MCL_POS`) does what stock does around a track itself (`app_track`), so the id never reaches Tidal's API or stock's stream thread: offline it returns `networkGetConnectStatus()` (`0x4831b4`); online it calls `tidal_playurl(0)` and `mclStop()` (`0x483258`), then on a detached thread hands the URL to stock's `mcl_tidalStartPlayer` past the `tidal_start` hook (`stock_tidal_trampoline`), and then, as the stream thread does after it (`0x486d98`, `0x486dc4`), pauses if `savestartbt` was set and sets `transposition` to 1, which arms Now Playing's online tick (`get_onlineid3info`, its notifications and auto-advance). These threads start one URL at a time (`au.start`), as stock's one thread does, and a later start drops one still waiting (`au.play`); an id from a queue replaced since has no URL, and the player stays stopped. A start also drops one still waiting, and a stream thread's Tidal fetch still in flight (`tidalfurlstart`, as stock at `0x4831bc`), so the last Now Playing asked for wins; a job waiting is dropped too when a queue has loaded since (`playback_loads`). A card-path record arms stock's auto-advance flag itself (`tidal_playurl(1)`: `mclAutoChange` only moves on with `-0x426c` above 0, which the stream thread's `transposition` sets for a URL), waits for a stream still inside stock's start to finish (`au.start`, its 100 or 150 ms start sleep), and returns, and `mclStartPlayer`, which has no `mclStop` on its local path, writes its own play command after it, playing `REC_PATH` as a local file, so its Now Playing is a local file's: no `get_onlineid3info`, no cover by URL. Next, Previous and a song's natural end come through `mclStartPlayer` the same way. Only stock's idle tick (`player_mainscheduling`, on the UI thread) reads the flag, and a UI-thread start is running before the tick's next pass; ponytail: a start from another thread (`player_save_start`'s jack and Bluetooth restarts) could in principle be passed by that tick while the player is stopped. `tidal_play` never sees an `app:` id, the trampoline being past its hook ([Tidal cache](#tidal-cache)). The cover: `get_onlineid3info` calls `player_set_coverinfo(REC_COVER, REC_NAME, 4)` at `0x492968`, and type 4 fetches from Tidal's image server with Tidal's session; `COVERINFO_CALL` (`tools/build.py`) turns that `jalr` into `jal app_coverinfo_call` (`move $a3, $s1; j app_coverinfo`), which passes type 2 for an `app:` record instead: curl to `/tmp/dlnadownpic.jpg` on stock's cover thread, scaled to 320 px. The stock routines this calls by address (`APP_URL_CALLS`, checked against the demo's symbols at build time) are not in `FUNCTIONS`, so `test/patch.py` runs them as stock's. Stock gives the rest: Now Playing's spinner shows while a `0xf1` stream buffers (`mclGetCacheBuffer() <= 0`, `0x52b75c`), and the player seeks with HTTP ranges (a Jellyfin `/Audio/{id}/stream?static=true` URL answers them). None of `get_tidal_trackurl`, `mclStartPlayer`, `mcl_tidalStartPlayer` or `get_onlineid3info` checks for a Tidal session, so no Tidal sign-in is needed. The capability word is `urls`. ponytail: a stream that fails leaves the player stopped, and it doesn't skip on (`mclAutoChange` returns at status 1, `0x5ae6e0`); online match and scrobbling skip `0xf1` queues, as for Tidal; an app's queue isn't kept for the next power-on; there is no gapless playback for `0xf0`–`0xf2`; Now Playing's heart is stock Tidal's, so it doesn't favourite an app's song on its server. Checks in `test/patch.py`: a queue's records and start, another app's or a stray `.urls` never read, a start offline and online (the URL on its thread, the pause and tick after it, a later start or a queue loaded since dropping an earlier one), a card song arming the advance, a Tidal fetch stopped, a Tidal track still stock's, a replaced queue's old ids, files that aren't a queue, over-long URLs and card paths passed over, and the cover's type.

**Stream mode: lyrics for a URL queue.** Stock's lyrics loader and the tag lookup in `ringnav_lyric` (`patch/lyrics.c`) skip a `0xf1` queue, so a streamed song had none. The app can leave each song's lyrics, as its server gives them, in `/tmp/q2-<id>.lyrics/<n>.lrc` beside its queue, `<n>` the song's place among the songs kept (counted from 0, as the id's). The app empties the folder before naming a new queue and writes each file whole (temp + rename): a file carries no generation, and it is read once. `app_urls_play` keeps that folder with the queue (`au.lyr`: the queue's path with `.lyrics` for `.urls`), and for a `0xf1` track whose id is `app:<gen>:<n>` of the latest queue, `ringnav_lyric` reads `<n>.lrc` (`app_lyrics`, `file_text`: its first `LYRIC_TAG_MAX` bytes, a UTF-8 mark skipped) with the parser a tag's lyrics take, synced or plain. An id from a replaced queue, a Tidal track or a song with no file gets none. ponytail: the file is read once, as the track loads; a song whose file the app hasn't written yet (a skip far ahead just after the queue starts) shows none until it is played again. Checks in `test/patch.py`: synced and plain lyrics, an older queue's track, a song with no file and a Tidal track.

**Library update** (`patch/addon_library.c`). After a sync an app can ask for Update Local Music and the import of its playlists, so the user does neither by hand. It writes `/tmp/q2-<id>.library` whole (temp + rename): `<seq>` on its first line (at most 23 bytes; the file's end ends it when no newline follows), then `scan`, `import <path>` and `add <path>` lines, LF or CRLF, each path a `.m3u` (any case) directly in `/mnt/mmc/_explaylist_data/`, with no `/` in its name (so never `..`; a name with an ellipsis is fine), that exists, and whose playlist name (the file's name less `.m3u`) is at most 63 bytes (`LIB_NAME`). That limit is stock's: `ringnav_playlist_create` cuts a longer name into `char title[64]` and skips its duplicate check, so the stored name would never match the file's again, `import` would never replace it and `add` would never find it, and every sync would add another copy (a name in Chinese, Japanese or Korean reaches 64 bytes at 22 characters). `import` replaces a playlist of the same name, for a name the app owns (one it imported before); `add` imports the file only when no playlist has its name yet, and otherwise leaves that playlist alone, so a playlist of the user's with the same name is never replaced.

`addon_poll` calls `library_poll` once a pass, every `LIB_POLL_MS` (500 ms), for every app on the card (`addon_id`) whether it runs or not, so an app that exits right after its sync is still served, and no app's poll drives another's request. It reads each app's first line alone (the file's first 62 bytes); a seq that app hasn't had taken (each app's last one is kept, by id, in RAM; a seq `.done` already answers, as after demo restarts for Rockbox from Home with `/tmp` kept, is taken as done) reads the whole file once, and that read's own first line must be the seq: a file gone between the two reads, or replaced by a newer seq, is looked at again at the next poll, so no seq runs another's lines and none is answered without its own. The request is then checked whole: an error is written at once and nothing runs. A seq of 24 bytes or more, or an empty first line, is never run: it is answered `<seq> error Seq too long`, or ` error Missing seq` (no seq, so the line starts with a space), written again only once `.done` no longer holds that answer. One request runs at a time; a newer seq is taken once it is done.

The answer goes to `/tmp/q2-<id>.library.done`: `<seq> ok`, or `<seq> error <why>`, where `<why>` is `No memory card` (`/tmp/mmc_add` missing, or the import returned -2), `Unknown request: <line>`, `Not a playlist in /mnt/mmc/_explaylist_data: <path>`, `Name too long: <name>`, `Not found: <path>`, `Request too long` (the request fills `LIB_MAX`, 32 KB: more than 300 imports of the longest names), `Couldn't start the scan` or `Couldn't import <name>` (both below); then one `exists <path>` line for each `add` left alone, after an error too. It is built whole in one buffer and written with `blob_io` through `/tmp/q2-library.tmp`, renamed only once all of it is written and synced (`/tmp` full would cut the `exists` lines, and the app would take a user's same-named playlist for its own); otherwise `<seq> error Couldn't write the answer` is written the same way, and if that fails too, the temp is removed and nothing is published. The request is read into heap (`libup.list`, `LIB_MAX` for the request and `LIB_ANSWER` after it for the answer), taken at the first request and kept; its files are then moved to its start, each as its op (`i` import, `a` an add not looked up yet, `e` an add left alone) and its name, NUL-ended.

The scan and the import each wait for the same check (`library_safe`): nothing plays, as `ringnav_sleep`'s quiet check has it — the player, Spotify, the radio or a video (`media_playing`), AirPlay (`airplayGetFlag` 2) or the Bluetooth receiver (`bt__recv_pageflag` 2) — since the scan dialog's init, `dialog_updatemusic_dialog_init`, calls `player_stop` unconditionally at `0x4a172c`, which stops paused playback too and loses its place: so the scan alone also waits while a local song is paused and the screen is on (`mclGetPlayStatus` 3 and `g_backlight_status`); with the screen off it goes ahead and stops the song, as Library → Update Local Music does; no scan runs (`library_scanning`, coverflow.c's: `SCAN_THREAD` set and `SCAN_DONE` clear); and every open window, the window manager's children less the status bar (`system_bar`), is on an allowlist of windows that show no library records: `home_page`, `stream_page`, `streamconf_page` (Streaming's Configuration), `addon_page` and `qrcode_page` (a card app's page and its QR code), `playing_page` (Now Playing, which plays its own copies, `mcl_pdeqplaylist`), and System and Playback settings' own pages, `sysset_page`, `display_page`, `backlight_page`, `lighttime_page`, `keylock_page`, `language_page`, `datetime_page`, `autotime_page`, `manualtime_page`, `screensaver_page`, `standby_page`, `powermanager_page`, `sleepshutdown_page`, `about_page`, `bluetooth_page`, `btquality_page`, `btadjustvol_page`, `wifi_page`, `wifiset_page`, `netservice_page`, `playset_page`, `audiosetting_page`, `playmode_page`, `replaygain_page`, `filter_page`, `dsdoutput_page`, `memplay_page`, `usbvol_page`, `maxvol_page`, `bootvol_page`, `balance_page` and `equalizer_page`. Any other window holds the request until it closes, so the check fails safe: a dialog (`library_poll` also runs inside every modal loop, a toast's or a confirm's, and no request starts there); stock's Library pages, whose rows are records of the lists the import reloads (`p_deque_playlist` and `p_deque_showlist`, `load_localclass_list(0xf00b)`), so a press on one afterwards would take the wrong record; Q2 Pod's own library pages, which keep records read from the database the scan rebuilds; the user's own Update Local Music (`updatemusic_page`, awaiting its OK: no second dialog); USB storage; the lock screen; firmware update, reset, Wi-Fi transfer and the App Store; and any page added later until it is listed.

A request with neither line reports `ok` at once and runs nothing. The scan opens stock's own `dialog/updatemusic_dialog` (a zeroed 16-byte context, as stock's four callers do), a modal dialog: its init starts the scan's thread, and its own loop runs until it closes. The state is `L_SCAN` before the call; a `library_poll` from inside that loop (`libup.busy` set) runs nothing but notes the scan it sees (`scan_seen`: the thread running, or `updatemusic_dialog` open), as does a check right after the call. The scan is over once it was seen and is gone; one never seen (the dialog didn't open) is answered `Couldn't start the scan`, never `ok` without a scan. Then the payload loads Library → Playlists' list itself (`ringnav_localclass(0xf00b)`), looks each `add`'s name up in it as stock's replace does (`library_adds`: the file's name less `.m3u`, compared whole with each playlist's), drops the ones found from the import, and calls stock `importPlayList` (`0x50da18`), as the Import Playlists row does; with nothing left to import it is not called, and no toast follows. Stock stops at a create that fails (-1: a database error, or the 100 playlists' cap) and returns 1 as if done, so afterwards each imported file's name must be in the reloaded `p_deque_playlist`; the first that isn't gives `Couldn't import <name>`. The result is written to `.library.done` first, then toasted ("Playlists updated", or the error): stock's `msginfo_dialog`, like Update Local Music's dialog, runs a loop of its own that polls again, so `library_poll` is guarded (`libup.busy`) and a nested poll runs nothing. For each file stock looks its name up in `p_deque_playlist` (`checkPlayListByName`, a `strcmp` against each playlist's name) and deletes a match, header (`deletePlaylist`) and entries (`deleteAllMusicByPlaylist`), before it creates the playlist again (`createPlayListItem`, Q2 Pod's `ringnav_playlist_create`) and adds the file's songs, their tags read from the files, so a playlist of the same name is replaced, not doubled. `p_deque_playlist` is empty until Playlists first loads it, hence the load: without it the name is missed, the create fails on the duplicate name (-2) and stock adds the songs to playlist -2. The playlist takes the file's name without its extension (`toolsGetDirectoryM3u8` cuts it at the last dot; paths are `/mnt/mmc/_explaylist_data/<file>`), and absolute `/mnt/mmc/...` entries are accepted. Stock lists the whole folder; `importPlayList`'s `toolsGetDirectoryM3u8` call (`M3U_DIR_CALL`, `0x50dab8`, a `jalr` with `addiu a0, a0, 0x12e0` in its delay slot, which stays) becomes `jal addon_m3u_dir`, which keeps only the records whose path is `/mnt/mmc/_explaylist_data/` and an `i` name of the request while `libup.only` is set, around the app's import alone. Stock's own Import Playlists row is unaffected and still imports the whole folder. Limits (`ponytail:`): a scan cancelled with the dialog's Back still reports `ok`, as stock keeps no cancel flag; the import reads every song's tags on the UI thread, as stock's Import row does, so a long playlist freezes the UI for a while.

## Q2 Shelf

**System settings → App Store** (titled **App Store**; just above System Update; `shelf_open` in `patch/netupdate.c`, the `apps_page` runtime window) installs, updates and removes the apps in [`sources.list`](../sources.list) from their GitHub releases. The firmware names no app but its own three. The list is fetched from `raw.githubusercontent.com/DiamondBond/q2-pod/main/sources.list`, which doesn't count against the API's rate limit, and kept as `/mnt/mmc/.q2/sources.list` so the page works offline. The first row, **Check for Updates** (`ROW_CHECK`), runs a check when pressed, so with no list yet it is the one row and fetches the list.

Each line is `owner/repo [Name]` (`#` starts a comment), and `shelf_sources` reads up to 16 (`SH_MAX`). The owner is `A-Za-z0-9-`, the repo also allows `._`, and the name defaults to the repo's. A line is skipped when any of these fail or its id repeats an earlier one's. The id is the repo's name less `q2-`, and must be 1 to 15 of `a-z0-9-`, addon.c's rule, since it goes into shell commands. A card app's folder is `.<id>`, its binary `.<id>/q2-<id>`, and nothing in it is kept from unzip.

Q2 Pod's own apps are matched by the repo's name, so a fork is the same app; another repo whose id would be one of theirs is skipped:

- `q2-rockbox` → `rockbox`: binary `.rockbox/rockbox`, keeps `.rockbox/config.cfg`.
- `q2-rockbox-themes` → `themes`: folder `.rockbox`, no binary, keeps the same file.
- `q2-librespot` → `spotify`: binary `.spotify/librespot`, keeps `.spotify/cache/*` and `.spotify/.cache-size`.

The app rows follow the list's order. Each one shows its app's state as the row's detail:

- **Installed** with a tag in `/mnt/mmc/.q2/apps/<id>.tag`.
- **Update** when that tag differs from the latest release's (string equality, not order), or when the app's binary is there without a tag (a manual install).
- Otherwise **Install**.

Rockbox Themes is listed only while `.rockbox/rockbox` is a regular file. The rows are built when the page opens, and again when a job's end changes which (`sh_rows`). Themes says "Install Rockbox first" without Rockbox.

ponytail: a card app keeps nothing from an update. Its settings survive only because its release zip doesn't ship them; an app whose zip ships its settings file overwrites the user's copy on every update. The fix belongs in that app's release, or in a `keep` list here if one ever needs it.

See [the user guide](manual/guide.html#apps) for install, removal, confirmation and cancellation controls.

One pthread runs one job at a time, all transfers through netupdate.c's `fetch` (the same CA bundle and TLS checks, `User-Agent: Q2Pod`, progress and cancel through its transfer callback). Opening the page with Wi-Fi checks: it fetches the list, then every app's latest release. This is skipped if every release was checked in the last 10 minutes (`SH_STALE_MS`, kept until power-off): GitHub allows 60 unauthenticated API requests an hour, a check takes one per app, and an install fetches its own release anyway (`releases/latest` of `api.github.com`: `tag_name`, and the first asset whose name ends in `.zip`, its `browser_download_url` and `size`, `shelf_release`). The worker parses the fetched list into `sh.next`, adds back any app it dropped that still has a tag (so it can still be removed), and writes the result to the card. The UI thread adopts it when the check ends, so the rows never change under a job. A list that fails to fetch or parses to nothing leaves the old one in place.

**Check for Updates** checks inside those 10 minutes, but not within a minute of the last whole check (`SH_RECHECK_MS`): then it says that check's count again without fetching, so quick repeated presses don't spend the hour's requests that installs also need. ponytail: a press every minute still costs a check each, so about six use up the hour; raise `SH_RECHECK_MS` if that turns out to matter. Its row reads "Checking…" during any check; pressing it then starts nothing, but the check's end answers it. When a check ends with the page on top (not covered, nor stopped by another row's press), `sh_told` toasts the number of apps whose tag file differs from the latest release: "1 app can be updated", "3 apps can be updated". A manual install's **Update** isn't counted, as its version is unknown. With none, after Check for Updates (`sh.manual`, cleared by the next `sh_start`) it says why the check failed, the first `SH_NET` or `SH_BUSY` from `release()` ("Can't reach GitHub", "GitHub is busy, try later"), or else "All apps are up to date". An app with no release (`SH_MISSING`) counts as checked, not failed. No list at all, on the card or fetched, is `SH_NET`. The opening check says nothing unless an app can be updated.

An install:

1. Fetches its release again, deletes any leftover `/mnt/mmc/.q2/staging` (an interrupted install is retried, never resumed), and needs the zip's size plus 64 MB free (`statfs`).
2. Downloads the zip to `.q2/staging/<id>.part`.
3. Lists it with `busybox unzip -l -qq` (stock busybox 1.31.1), restricted as the unpack is: the app's folder only (`.rockbox/*` or `.spotify/*`), never `*/._*` or the kept paths (`.rockbox/config.cfg`; `.spotify/cache/*` and `.spotify/.cache-size`). `shelf_unpacked` rejects an absolute path or a `..` component and a total over 1 GB, and needs at least one entry; the card then needs that total plus 64 MB free.
4. Unpacks with `busybox unzip -o -qq -d .q2/staging/<id>` and the same patterns, so `INSTALL.txt`, `__MACOSX/` and macOS `._` files stay off the card. busybox's unzip matches with `fnmatch` without `FNM_PATHNAME` (so `*` spans `/`), and strips a leading `/` and `../` from names itself before listing and unpacking.
5. Stops Spotify (`SPOT_KILL`) again before touching `.spotify`, in case it was started during the download, lists the staged files with `find <dir> -type f`, and renames each over its target on the card (the same file system), making folders as needed; the app's binary goes last, and only when every other file moved, so `.rockbox/rockbox` is the old file or the new one, never part of one, and never new beside a half-moved set. The list becomes `.q2/apps/<id>.files`, then `sync`, then the tag through `blob_io`. Staging is deleted after every install, whatever the outcome.

Every Spotify job starts and ends with `spot_stop` on the UI thread, so spotify.c forgets the librespot it started and Streaming → Spotify starts the new one (or says it is missing).

**Remove** unlinks the app's binary first (a remove cut short still boots Q2 Pod), then each path in `<id>.files` that no other app's list holds (two fonts are in both Rockbox's and the themes' zips), then each folder left empty, up to and including the app's own, then, for a card app (not in `special[]`), its folder whole (`rm -rf .<id>`, with the login and settings it saved there, which no release lists), then the list and tag. So `shelf_sources` refuses a card app whose id names one of the firmware's own card folders (`reserved[]`: `q2`, `tidal`, `books`, `coverflow`, `downpic`, `photos`, `sldp`), as it does Spotify's; `test/peq.py` fails when a card folder in `patch/` is missing from it. ponytail: `addon_quit` waits only `AWAY_MS` (3 s) for the app to end, so an app still saving on its TERM after that can write files back into the folder; wait on its pid file if one does. Removing Rockbox removes the themes first. Removing Spotify stops it and keeps `cache/` and `.cache-size`, which were never installed by it; [Streaming configuration](#streaming-configuration)'s **Erase Spotify Data** runs on this thread as a job too (`shelf_erase`), stops it and deletes those two.

A 500 ms timer refreshes progress and holds standby and auto power-off during app jobs; `sh_wait` joins the worker and reports its result wherever the user is, including after leaving the page.

ponytail: TLS and curl's length check are the only checks on the zip, and unzip's CRCs the only ones inside it; there is no signature. Cancel stops the download at once, otherwise it lands after the step it was pressed in (the API request, the listing or the unpack); once files move it is ignored. A power cut while files move leaves those moved so far beside the old ones, with the old tag and list; the next install starts over. Staging left by a power cut stays on the card until the next install. A release that drops a file leaves the old one on the card and off the new list. The card is FAT32 or exFAT, which hold no symlinks or special files: busybox fails on one, so nothing checks for them.

### Card apps

Every other app in the list is a card app: it installs through the same rows and runs from **Streaming** through one generic host, `patch/addon.c`. It lists up to 8 card folders `.<id>/` (id: 1 to 15 of `a-z0-9-`, since it goes into shell commands) holding `q2app` (`name=`, `search=`, `qr=1`, `goto=1`, `scrobble=0`: see [Scrobbling](#scrobbling); `game=1` and `audio=1`: see Card games below), `run` and `q2-<id>`, and optionally `icon.png` (52 px) and `icon-40.png` (40 px), each at most 128 px either way (`ART_MAX`: demo decodes it whole, so a larger one, or one whose size can't be read, is not used): Streaming's row shows the 40 px icon 1:1, else the other scaled down, else the shared `stream_app` (`addon_icon`); in the Minimal theme it greys as Streaming's own icons do (`addon_is_icon` in `ringnav_image_add`), and Classic keeps its colours. The launcher's loop leaves its pid in `/tmp/q2-<id>`; a TERM to it stops the app. `/tmp/q2-<id>.browse.sock` serves the page over Q2SPOT/1, and `/tmp/q2-<id>.state` holds `hold=1` (standby kept off) and the QR page's `addr`, `pin`, `qr`, `qrsize`, `note`. USB storage (`usbmode_page_init`'s hook, `addon_card_away`) asks every app to end at once and waits up to 3 s in all, then starts each again once the card is back. No app starts while the Apps page installs or removes its folder (`shelf_busy`). A stop removes a pid file whose loop is gone, but it trusts the pid inside: a loop that died without removing it, its pid since reused, would TERM that process (a `flock` in `run` would close this). The QR page draws up to 49 modules (`QR_HEX`).

**Card games.** A card app whose `q2app` holds `game=1` (q2-fidget, named Buddy) has no Streaming row and needs no `run`: `addon_scan` keeps it in its own by-name list, which Library's Games chooser lists after its own four (`addon_games`, `addon_game_name`; `games.c` `pick_card_game`). A row forks and `execl`s `.<id>/q2-<id>` from the card (`addon_game_start`), as Videos' q2video runs, and the game draws on `/dev/fb0` itself while demo runs on: `ringnav_wm_paint` skips the window manager's paint and `ringnav_input` hands every event to `addon_game_input` instead of stock, except Play/Pause and the side buttons (222, 223), down, up and long, which stay stock's media keys under the Games page unless the game has the sound (below). The rest go as text datagrams to `/tmp/q2-<id>.input.sock`: `k d CODE` and `k u CODE` for a key's down and release, `t d X Y`, `t m X Y` and `t u X Y` for a touch, in the 375 × 320 view's pixels (this hook sees the portrait panel's coordinates, before the window manager turns them, so X is the panel's y and Y is 319 less its x; the pointer types `EVT_POINTER_DOWN`, `_MOVE_BEFORE` and `_UP_BEFORE` less one or not, an abort as `u`). `wheel_raw` is set while it runs, and `game_poll` (from `addon_poll`, each UI loop pass) sends the travel gathered since as `w N` (200 a turn, rising anticlockwise). Return held `GAME_HOLD_MS` (700 ms, timed in `game_poll` from its key-down, since stock makes `EVT_KEY_LONG` after this hook) TERMs the game, and one still there `GAME_KILL_MS` (3 s) later is KILLed, as often as it takes. So does `GAME_IDLE_MS` (2 min) without input, unless Smart Awake (`IPOD KEEPLIT`, `ringnav_smart_awake`) is on, which holds it open and lit with no limit (asked once each `GAME_IDLE_MS`); meanwhile `reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 1)` holds the screen, standby and auto power-off. Its end (`waitpid` `WNOHANG`) clears `wheel_raw` and repaints the whole window manager. The capability word is `game`. ponytail: the game runs from the card, so USB storage's prompt, under it, can't be answered until it ends; and the screen can't go dark under it, as for a video.

**A game with the sound.** A card game whose `q2app` also holds `audio=1` (q2-groovebox, named Groovebox) owns the audio output while it runs, as a video does. Before the fork `addon_game_start` runs what `video_play` and `video_start` run: `headset_manual`, `spot_yield`, `radio_stop`, `player_stop` (hciplayer holds the PCM even paused) and, for the headphone output, `dac_take` (power, PCM mode, unmuted, the volume). The binary gets q2video's arguments: the output device, `plughw:0,0`, `plug:bluealsa` or `usb` (`output_device`), and on Bluetooth or USB the volume as a second argument (`NN`, or `hNN` to show only), since the DAC's hardware volume is out of those paths and the game applies the soft volume itself; on the jack there is no second argument. The game opens them as q2video does (`video.c` `sound_open`), which the contract in [librespot.md](librespot.md) spells out: `usb` is the card `/proc/asound/cards` lists as USB-Audio, `plughw:N,0`; hciplayer lets go of the PCM, muting the DAC, a moment after the stop, so the open is retried (2 s on the jack, 5 s on Bluetooth and USB, and up to 5 s more at `snd_pcm_set_params` off the jack, since bluealsa claims its PCM there) and, on the jack, `DAC_MUTE` and `DAC_PCM` are cleared through `/dev/shanling_dac` after it, the mute set again when it closes; `hNN` is applied by the DAC, so the game plays at unity; Mono Audio and Swap L/R are `/tmp/q2mono` and `/tmp/q2swap` (an int each), applied to its output as q2video applies them. Play/Pause and the side buttons then reach it as `k d|u 171`, `222` and `223` (stock makes a key's long press after the hook, so none arrives, and no Play-hold action of stock's runs; power-off is Centre's hold, which stock still has). A headset's buttons do nothing meanwhile: `media_key` and `media_set_playing` (headset.c) return while `addon_game_audio` says a game has the sound, as otherwise, with video, radio and Spotify all stopped, a headset's Play (AVRCP PLAY, 200 and 207, which `headset_command` sends to `media_set_playing`, not `media_key`) would reach `player_play_pause` with the player stopped and start hciplayer over the game; a pause still passes, Play/Pause's included, for a Spotify a phone resumed meanwhile. Every other start of stock's player meanwhile fails with -1 as a too-long track's does, silently (`ringnav_start_player`, mclStartPlayer's hook: a jack or USB DAC change and a Bluetooth drop come through `player_save_start`), and a headset's link coming up starts nothing (`ringnav_bt_connect_start`, Resume on Connect either way). Stock's `player_save_start(1)` ignores the -1 and still marks the player paused, on nothing, so the game's end stops it when it finds it paused (`game_poll`), and Play then starts the queue as from a stop. A launched card app's first song waits for the game's end (`stream_poll` keeps `S_WAIT`); stock's own steps before the hook (`config_outputchannel`, the volume) still run. `game_poll` zeroes a counting `g_dacoff_time` each pass, as `video_poll` does, so the DAC stays on, and skips the `GAME_IDLE_MS` close: only the game knows whether it is playing, so the idle close is its own (Groovebox ends itself after 5 minutes stopped and untouched). Return's hold still ends it. Music stays stopped when it ends, as after a video, and the DAC powers off on stock's count. Standby needs the screen dark, which a game keeps lit, so nothing else changes; `media_playing` does not count a game. The capability word is `audio`. Home's Shortcut can open such a game by id (`addon_game_open`, -1 when no card game has the id, which `home_shortcut_open` toasts as not on the card, or as unable to start from it when the binary is there without its `q2app`'s `game=1` or a slot; a failed fork toasts as a failed exec does: Groovebox, [Home](ipod.md#home)). ponytail: a headset's Play could be forwarded as a datagram instead of ignored; a live volume change while it runs is not mirrored (Videos' `video_sync` does that), the game has its own volume gesture; a Spotify a phone resumes meanwhile opens its own PCM beside the game's, as beside a video's.

**Go to on a card app's song.** The hold menu's Go to Album and Go to Artist (`qm_goto`) on a song under `/mnt/mmc/.<id>/` first ask `addon_goto`: when that app's `q2app` holds `goto=1` (the app rewrites the line from its own setting; `goto=0` hides the rows again, as below), its page opens with the Q2SPOT/1 level `goto` (`album` or `artist`, then the song's path) as its first level, titled by the record's album or artist tag (`card_tags` fills the playing song's), and the app answers with that album's or artist's own level; More… keeps both arguments, and Back from it closes the page as from a menu. An app page already open (the song played from it) is closed first, as Coverflow's scoped page (`closed` then `window_manager_close_window_force`); `closed` ignores the late destroy of a page it no longer holds. An app not yet scanned this boot is scanned for its name. A song in an app's folder whose app doesn't have `goto=1` (Tidal's downloads too) has no Go to rows (`addon_goes`): the Library never scans those hidden folders, so stock's pages would be empty. `goto` in `/tmp/q2-addon.caps` tells an app the firmware has it. Checks in `test/patch.py`: the request and its title, More…, Back, the replaced page and its late destroy, and no page for an app without `goto=1`, a song outside a card folder or an id that can't be one.

**Tags.** A card app's songs come from its folder's listing (`card_songs`, `toolsLoadDirectory`) or an `.m3u`, which read no tags, and play as a library queue (`0xf001`). `player_get_id3info` parses only a folder play's file (`MCL_TYPE` 1) and copies any other queue's record into `g_play_id3_info`, so such a song read as its file name by an Unknown artist. `card_tags`, from `ringnav_id3_path` as each song starts, gives the playing record (`queue_now`, its path the parsed one, under `/mnt/mmc/`, no album yet, not a CUE track, whose tags are its sheet's) and the parsed tags its file's title, artist, album and album artist, and the record its track and disc, read as the library scan reads them (`toolsGetMusicInfo`): one TagLib open as a song starts, once a queue, since the record then has its album; a song on the card without an album tag played from a list (Shuffle Songs, Most Played) is read at each start, as stock reads every folder play's. A song without tags keeps its record's. ponytail: Up Next and an app's lists show songs not yet played by their file names, and holding a song in an app's list has no Go to rows (Now Playing's has); reading a whole folder when it's queued froze the UI for seconds on a few hundred songs.

**Sleep timers.** An app's `/tmp/q2-<id>.sleep` (q2-podcasts' Sleep Timer) holds `until <time>` (seconds since the epoch) or `end`; `sleep_read`, in the app's poll, takes a new request (its contents hashed per slot), and its removal by the app cancels the one it set. Q2 Pod keeps the timer, so it fires once the app has stopped too: `until` is kept as the time left on the boot clock (`boot_s`, `CLOCK_BOOTTIME`), which counts Standby and doesn't move when Wi-Fi sets the clock (stock's NTP), so neither shifts it (a time already past pauses at once; no digits, more than 11, anything after them, or more than a week away, `SLEEP_MAX_S`, is dropped without pausing; a time the Q2 slept through by more than `SLEEP_LATE_S`, a minute, is let go without pausing). `end` waits on the track playing (its place, `MCL_POS`) and follows its elapsed time and length (`player_playtime_and_length`, a pass reading -1 between tracks skipped) every pass: ending by itself (`mclNextSong`'s automatic call, counted in `auto_nexts` by `ringnav_next`, so a VBR file whose length is overstated still counts) or leaving its last `SLEEP_END_S` (2 s) for another place pauses, the next track waiting at its start, and so does starting over in place (repeat one); a move from anywhere else (another track picked, Next, a seek back) waits for that track's end instead, since stock refills the same deque for a new queue (`mclLoadPlayList`), so a pick and Next look alike. A length unknown counts any move on as the end. The player stopped for `SLEEP_STOP_MS` (3 s, as Tidal's: stock reports stopped between tracks for a moment), or another one taking playback (`media_owner`), lets it go. `sleep_check`, every `addon_poll`, pauses with `media_set_playing(0)` (whatever owns playback: the local player, Spotify, radio or a video) and removes the file, the app's sign the timer is over. One timer at a time: the latest request, from any app, replaces the one before; another app's removes the earlier one's file. `sleep` in `/tmp/q2-addon.caps` tells an app the firmware has it. Checks in `test/patch.py`: a timer before and at its time, cancelled, already due, slept through, the clock set a day on, a bad or far-off time, a time replaced by `end`, and `end` at the next track, at a natural end before the length's last seconds, at repeat one, after another track picked and a seek back, and at a stop.

## 2038

The rootfs is glibc 2.28 on 32-bit MIPS, so stock's `time_t` is 32 bits, and the root certificates in `assets/scrobble-ca.pem` expire between 2035 and 2046.

**TLS.** Every verified libcurl handle (Network Update, Q2 Shelf, Internet Radio, scrobbling, lyrics and covers) sets `CURLOPT_SSL_CTX_FUNCTION` to `tls_untimed` (`patch/peq.h`). It sets `X509_V_FLAG_NO_CHECK_TIME` in the `SSL_CTX`'s `X509_VERIFY_PARAM`, which each connection inherits, so chains still have to reach a pinned root, but no certificate's dates are checked. demo doesn't import `SSL_CTX_get0_param` or `X509_VERIFY_PARAM_set_flags`, so the callback writes OpenSSL 1.1.1f's fields itself (`SSL_CTX->param` at `0x100`, `->flags` at `0xc`). `tools/build.py` checks that both functions in the rootfs's `libssl`/`libcrypto` still load those offsets. ponytail: an expired or retired server certificate is accepted too, and there is no revocation check. That is the price of working with a firmware nobody can update. Tidal's downloads already run unverified, as its stream does.

**Clock.** Linux 5.10 keeps 64-bit time, and glibc 2.28's `time()` truncates it to 32 bits. `wall_time` reads that unsigned, which is right until 2106. Scrobble timestamps use it. The iPod status bar takes it back in 28-year steps, a span over which dates and weekdays repeat, before stock's 32-bit `localtime`, which is right until 2100. ponytail: stock code stays 32-bit. Its clock screens and its network time sync (`ntpdate`, then `hwclock -wu`) will show or write wrong dates after January 2038, and NTP's 2036 era rollover depends on stock's `ntpdate`. Stock's own HTTPS calls skip verification. Card apps' folder poll (`addon.c`) compares a 32-bit `st_mtime` with `time()`; both wrap together, so only the second of the wrap misjudges a change. librespot carries its own fix (DiamondBond/q2-librespot).
